# Raffica – Decisions

Log of architecture and technology decisions. Newest entries on top.

**When an entry is due:** choice of framework, library or version, architecture and interface decisions, deliberately rejected approaches, workarounds with consequences – anything you would otherwise discuss again in half a year.

**Rules:** Do not rewrite entries afterwards. If a decision is revised, add a new entry and note "Superseded by: <title>" in the old one.

## 2026-11-27 – Raffica Write: the fields limit (M4.5b)
**Context:** The cases of `--measure-fields` and `--measure-typing-fields` were measured for the first time on the Windows PC (Intel Core i5-9500T, Native AOT app) in the last task of milestone 4.5b ("Raffica Write: fields, captions and tables of contents (M4.5b)"). The spec asks for a fixed limit set from the first measurement (budget times 1.25, the method of "Raffica Write: the objects limit (M4.5a)") and typing within 16 ms with a table of contents of 100 entries in the document.
**Decision:** `measure.cs` judges the metric `fields`, the worst median of the four commands of `--measure-fields` (a caption, a page cross-reference, a table of contents of the levels 1 to 3 and a bookmark inserted), against a fixed **75 ms** on every platform (`CheckCommand.FieldsBudget` 60 × 1.25). The value is the CPU cost of the command (key handler, the render pass and the page draws of that frame), reduced as the objects metric is (the median over the launches per row, then the largest); the frame latency, the split, the refresh cases and the settle rows are recorded and never judged. First measurement (three launches, 2026-11-27, build 1 of the paragraph **Builds** below): worst median **52.79 ms** (the table of contents; its launches 45.4, 52.8 and 55.4), rounded up to a 60 ms budget (the M4.2 rule for time budgets). The final build (build 2) gave 47.96 ms. The 100 ms aim is met on this PC. **No limit is entered for the settle** (the plan records it; the spec says it is judged only against a limit of its own once measured, which is the owner's call). `typing-fields` counts into `typing` (the fixed 16 ms). The Linux laptop (mains power) and the Intel Mac runs of `measure.yml` (cases `fields` and `typing-fields`) are the owner's gate; the limit is unproven on them (the worst objects command took 2.7 times as long on the Linux laptop as on the Windows PC before a fix, so the headroom of 75 ms is the first thing to look at there): `gh workflow run measure.yml --ref dev -f platform=<linux|macos> -f target=write -f note="<machine>"` after the merge (`measure.yml` builds only commits on `main` or `dev`). Never loosened to make a run pass.
**What the runs do:** both first prepare the same document from the thesis, untimed: heading numbering on (the thesis has it already), a table of contents of the levels 1 and 2 with page numbers before the first chapter (122 entries beside the file's own list of 32), one caption and one page cross-reference at a paragraph in the middle. The refresh runner gets a clock that never fires, so the run decides when a refresh happens (`FieldRefreshRunner.Flush` is the tick of the 250 ms timer). Every sample is undone and the document must be the prepared one afterwards, or the run fails.
**Builds** (Native AOT publishes of 2026-11-27, Windows PC; every number below is of build 2 unless it says otherwise). Build 1: the milestone's code with the measuring code, before the changes the Word check of the same day brought; it gave the first measurement (the limit's basis) and the far-view experiment. Build 2: the final product code of the milestone (branch commit 63a39d3): the Word-rule fixes, the numbers of a chapter heading worked out only for field results, and the paragraph a removed table of contents leaves. A build between the two (the Word-rule fixes alone, in which the layout formatted a numbered label a second time) was measured too; its numbers are superseded and not recorded.
**Numbers (build 2, ms, the three launches' values, sorted per column; "command or first refresh" median and p95, then the UI time of the refresh passes that complete the change and the time until the layout is complete again):**

| Case | median | p95 | refresh passes, UI | until at rest |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| caption-insert | 5.85 6.11 8.53 | 7.10 12.05 14.78 | 57.7 58.3 60.7 | 283 286 296 |
| xref-insert | 4.57 5.97 6.38 | 6.89 10.78 11.14 | 43.4 44.3 45.5 | 108 108 121 |
| toc-insert (the worst) | 47.40 47.96 48.40 | 62.64 71.35 98.43 | 182.6 188.3 190.1 | 705 706 709 |
| bookmark-add | 5.01 5.78 5.99 | 6.92 7.26 7.93 | 43.1 43.7 50.7 | 110 112 117 |
| refresh-heading-edit | 77.00 78.15 82.14 | 80.58 106.28 115.21 | 77.0 78.1 82.1 | 112 126 128 |
| refresh-pages | 64.68 65.57 70.09 | 89.83 97.94 105.05 | 80.9 87.5 87.7 | 146 151 160 |
| first-settle | 76.04 77.53 84.88 | 109.92 111.29 120.40 | 76.0 77.5 84.8 | 119 126 135 |
| refresh-heading-edit-far-view | 78.05 83.84 93.83 | 107.54 139.83 149.88 | 77.6 83.0 93.4 | 135 136 136 |

The first refresh of the thesis as opened (`fields-open`, its own table of contents is stale and gains two entries) held the UI thread 54 to 60 ms and was at rest after 483 to 513 ms (two passes). **Typing** (`typing-fields`, 100 keys per place and view, a refresh after every tenth key): worst p95 6.56 ms (median of the launches per row; per-launch worst 5.8, 6.6 and 6.9), every row inside 16 ms. **The refresh at a pause** (UI thread, median of the launches' medians, page view / writing view): in a paragraph without a field 6.3 / 5.9 ms (the page results are skipped), in a caption 47 / 41, in a table cell 43 / 42 (a nested change walks the whole body), at the end of a numbered heading the list shows 59 / 41. The engine alone (`write-layout-bench edit` of build 2, one run of 8 per case): caption 2.4, cross-reference 5.2, table of contents 45.3, bookmark 3.4, refresh after a heading edit 50.3, after a pagination change 49.9, first look 45.2.
**Measured and decided (carried from the tasks):**
- *The whole-body page pass* (`refresh-pages` and every burst that touches a heading, a field, a bookmark or moves a page): 65 to 90 ms on the UI thread 250 ms after the burst (the medians; the 95th percentile of the first look is 110 to 120 ms). Kept as it is: it runs only at a pause, only in a document with page results, and the skip covers the common burst (task 11, on its own build, measured the stable skipped pass at 6.0 to 6.3 ms against 45 to 51 ms with the skip off). Cutting it needs an incremental page pass with its own oracle case; backlog "Write: a cheaper field refresh".
- *The timer path lays out from the changed block through the page in view on the UI thread.* In the app's far-view case the thesis converges right after the list (medians 78 to 94 ms against 77 to 82 ms near). **When the rewrite moves the pagination and the view is at the end it is nearly a full layout:** a temporary bench build (build 1 with an extra command, not committed) gave 465 ms (max 469) to tell the layout of a list that gained 60 entries and made the thesis 18 pages longer, with the view at the last page, against 8.4 ms with the view at page 2; one added entry that moved no page cost 7.2 against 0.7 ms. Kept and recorded as a limit; the same backlog item.
- *The first whole-body settle* costs 50 to 85 ms on the UI thread plus the background layout, once per opened document with fields, after the first sharp page.
**Start cost** (build 2 against a publish of the commit before 4.5b, d7af73e; three interleaved rounds of ten runs per case, the order alternating; medians of the rounds' medians, before against after): cold 226.3 against 220.9 ms (-2.4 percent), warm 238.9 against 241.6 ms (+1.1 percent), thesis 245.6 against 249.4 ms (+1.5 percent): no regression beyond noise (single rounds differ in both directions by up to 33 ms). The published exe grows from 59.31 to 60.02 MB. `--measure-typing` against the same commit, two pairs of three launches each, in alternating order: worst p95 6.60 and 7.39 before, 7.28 and 6.04 after; the mean over the 16 rows' p95 4.00 and 4.16 before, 4.45 and 4.19 after (higher after in both pairs, by 0.45 and 0.03 ms); every value is far inside 16 ms. The `section-break` rows are not comparable to older ones: since 4.5b both pickers skip a paragraph inside a table of contents, so they type in another paragraph.
**After the whole-branch review** (build 3: Native AOT publish of branch commit c1c2f52, the product code with the review fixes, Windows PC, 2026-11-27, three launches each through `measure.cs`; the fixes touch a measured path, the commands now read bookmark ids and names from everything the document holds): `fields` worst command median 50.11 ms of 75 (table of contents, launches 48.74, 50.11, 50.46); bookmark-add 6.60, 6.91, 7.89 against 5.01, 5.78, 5.99 in build 2 (the longer walk), cross-reference 4.14, 4.68, 5.09, caption 5.87, 6.22, 6.49; the refresh cases 69 to 98 ms as before. `typing-fields` worst p95 5.39 ms of 16. `measure.cs check` judges both rows ok (the check as a whole fails for the cases that were not run). One launch takes 53.1 s (`--measure-fields`) and 34.8 s (`--measure-typing-fields`) of the app's 240 s watchdog on this PC; the laptops are two to three times slower, so the first would take up to about 160 s there (unmeasured). `write-layout-bench edit` on the thesis (build 3) runs to its end and exits by itself after 313.6 s (`measure.yml` has no step timeout; the job's is 90 minutes).

Measured again after the table of contents entry fixes (2026-11-28, Windows PC, Native AOT publish of 675aeca, three launches): `fields` worst command median 68.5 ms of 75 (table of contents insert, launches 68.5, 74.8, 65.89). A control the same day, the build of 1f7ea71 and this head run alternately, six launches each, gave a table of contents insert of about 60.4 ms against about 67.4 ms, so the fixes cost about 7 ms. The limit holds with little room; the Linux laptop and the Intel Mac are unmeasured.
**Rejected alternatives:** a limit from a later run (the worst medians of later builds, about 48 to 51 ms, round to a 50 or a 60 ms budget by chance; the rule says the first measurement); a limit on the refresh cases (their cost is one design decision, recorded above, and the plan judges the commands).

## 2026-11-27 – Raffica Write: fields, captions and tables of contents (M4.5b)
**Context:** A thesis needs numbered figures and tables, references to them and a table of contents, and Word makes the user update them by hand. 4.5b makes them in Write and keeps them current (goal 8 of the Write spec). Spec: `Docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-25-raffica-write-4-5b-fields-captions-and-tables-of-contents-design.md`, plan: `Docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-26-milestone-04-5b-bookmarks-captions-cross-references-and-tables-of-contents.md`. The owner decided on 2026-11-25: Write recomputes only the fields it fully understands and leaves every other field's stored result untouched and marked, and refreshed results live in the model.
**Decision:**
- **A derived refresh in the model.** `TextEditor.RefreshFields(host, scope, touched)` rewrites results in the model; it raises `DocumentEdits.DerivedVersion` (not the revision), attributes the changes to the snapshot tracker (`AttributeDerived`) and reaches the layout through `ApplyEdit`. It never dirties, records no step of its own and writes no recovery copy. `DocumentEdits.Stamp` (revision and derived version) is the key of every memo that reads content; the dirty flag, the history, the save stamp and autosave stay on the revision.
- **Structural changes** (a table of contents that changes its number of paragraphs) are `DerivedRecord`s attached to the newest history step (`EditHistory.AttachDerived`): undo reverts them before the step, redo replays them after; such a step takes no more typing; while a redo is pending the list is left as it is and the refresh looks again later.
- **The host.** `IFieldHost` (editing, model only) gives the number label, whether a tab follows it, the chapter number, the geometry of a text on a paragraph's first line (`LineGeometry` of the model: indent, end of the text, tab stops), the page and the printed number of a paragraph, a page signature and the note marks; the app implements it over `FieldLabels`, `LineMeasure`, `FieldPages` and the layout.
- **The runner** (`FieldRefreshRunner`, one per tab, made on the first edit, the first complete layout of a document with fields or a save of one): 250 ms after a burst, never in a key handler, never during a save, an IME composition, a prefix document or a stale layout; page results only from a complete pages layout, at most three passes (then the last result stays and is counted); the timer path never completes the layout on the UI thread, a save does (`SettleAsync`). A burst of plain edits that moves no page skips the page pass (`PagePassCanBeSkipped`: equal page signature and no touched block carrying an anchor). Page results owed after passes in the writing view are settled when the pages view is back, and by a save there: it makes a pages layout of its own under the lease (`LayoutRunner.CreateDetachedPageLayout`), lays it out to its end one page at a time (a cancelled save or a closed tab stops it there) and answers the page questions from it, within the same three passes, without switching the view or touching its layouts; changed results leave the tab's pages layout stale, so a switch back makes it anew. The runner goes by its own flag for owed page results, which can be set although no page result can be written (see *Saved page results* under the known limits); the timer never makes such a layout, and nothing of it exists at start. A failure of that layout is a counted refresh failure, never a faulted document. (Amended 2026-11-27, whole-branch review: the task 11 ruling that such a save keeps the stored page results rested on a wrong premise, that a pages layout needs a view switch, and is withdrawn.)
- **Fields.** Supported: `SEQ` (`\* ARABIC` and the roman and letter formats, `\n`, `\c`, `\r n`, `\s n`), `STYLEREF 1 \s`, `REF` (`\h`, `\n`, `\r`, `\* MERGEFORMAT`, `\* CHARFORMAT`), `PAGEREF \h`, `NOTEREF` (`\h`, `\f`), `TOC` (`\o`, `\u`, `\h`, `\z`, `\n`, `\c`). Anything else keeps its stored result byte for byte and is counted: one bar per document session, and the status line when the caret touches it.
- **Commands** (References tab, key tip `R`, no keys): Bookmarks (list, Go to, Add, Delete; Word's name rule with ASCII letters), Caption (label, text, above or below, chapter number), Cross-reference (heading, caption, footnote, endnote, bookmark; hidden `_Ref` bookmark), Table of contents and lists of figures and tables (levels, page numbers, right alignment, leader; hidden `_Toc` bookmarks), Remove table of contents. A table of contents is protected as a whole (a partial edit is refused with a reason, a covering delete removes it), and there is always a place to type around it (amended 2026-11-27, whole-branch review: a list last in its body, cell or content control left none, so a new document could not be typed in after it): Insert puts an empty paragraph after a list that would be the last block of its list of blocks (kept markers aside); Enter at the end edge of a list makes a plain paragraph after the field (it takes the section break of the paragraph that holds the field's end); Enter at the start edge of a list that is first in its body, cell or content control makes a plain paragraph above it (controller's call: a title goes there). The caret goes to the new paragraph, the field is not touched, a content control of one paragraph keeps refusing, and so does a paragraph where one list ends and another begins. A paste keeps complete `SEQ` and `STYLEREF` fields and flattens the others; a bookmark whose whole range is deleted goes with it.
- **Measuring.** `--measure-fields` (seven cases and the far-view variant of the heading edit), `--measure-typing-fields`, the cases `fields` and `typing-fields` of `measure.cs` and `measure.yml`, seven engine rows of `write-layout-bench edit`. Limits and numbers: "Raffica Write: the fields limit (M4.5b)".
**What Word does, found in the Word check and followed (2026-11-27, Word 16.0 build 16.0.20625.20142; table in `Docs/write-word-check.md`).** Write's first engine differed in four ways; each was fixed with a failing test first, and the independent oracles (`ReferenceFields`, `ReferenceToc`, `ReferenceNumbering`) changed to the same rules:
1. `STYLEREF 1 \s` is the heading's number from the first to the last placeholder of its level text, without the text around it (`Chapter I:` gives `I`, `(1)` gives `1`, `Sec 1 - 2)` gives `1 - 2`); Write showed the whole label without a trailing period, also in the chapter number of a new caption. `IFieldHost.ChapterNumber`, over `NumberingCounter.Next(paragraph, out numbers)`, which only `FieldLabels` calls: the layout formats each label once, as before.
2. `SEQ \s n` restarts only when a heading of level n or higher stands between the field and the previous field of the label (Write restarted at the next `\s` field after a heading of exactly level n); `\c` shows 0 at a new start and counts nothing, `\n` wins over `\c`; `\r` with no number sets 0; an unreadable `\s` restarts nothing.
3. Word counts every `SEQ` field of the label that is not deleted or moved away: also hidden ones, other formats, nested ones and ones in a tracked insertion or moved-in text (not one that names a bookmark or has an unknown switch). Write counted only the fields it writes, so the numbers after one of the others differed (`SeqCounter.Counts`, `FieldInstruction.ArgumentCount`, `FieldInfo.InDeletion`). This supersedes the task-5 choice that a `SEQ` in a tracked change does not count.
4. A list of figures or tables holds the captions in tracked insertions and moved-in text and not the hidden ones.

A second check the same day, over files for Remove table of contents and Delete bookmark, found one more: removing a table of contents could leave a table last in a table cell, which Word does not open, or two tables together, which Word reads as one. An empty paragraph now stays where the list was when a table would otherwise end the body, cell or content control or stand right before another table (`TocEditingTests.RemoveTocNeverLeavesATableLastOrTwoTablesTogether`). Delete bookmark needed no change.
**Found by the whole-branch review (2026-11-27) and fixed before the merge**, each with a failing test first: no place to type after a list that ended its container, `?` page numbers in a file saved from the writing view (both above), and a new bookmark that could take the id or the name of a bookmark Write keeps only as content (between blocks, in a table row) or of another story: ids and names are now read from everything the document holds (`BookmarkOperations.AllNames`, `NextId`). The oracle step added for the first (`InsertTocAtEnd`) changed the random sequences and exposed one more: a new cross-reference could take the generated name of a deleted bookmark that an older reference still pointed at, so the old reference silently came to mean the new target; a `_Ref` name now also avoids every generated name a field or a link points at. In the same oracle a chapter caption is now tried only below a Heading 1 (it had depended on the draw), and the check before the refresh after a new cross-reference judges the new field only (an older reference whose bookmarked text the new field stands in is stale until that refresh, which the step then checks).
**Found by the visual Word check (2026-11-27) and fixed before the merge**, each with a failing test first. The scripts of the earlier Word check compare the text of the fields only, so they passed while the lists looked broken: the pages were looked at for the first time after the whole-branch review (images of Write's drawing, of the file as Word shows it and after Word's update).
1. **An entry of a numbered heading was broken**, in Word and in Write: it had only the right tab stop of the page number, so the tab after the number jumped to it, the title stood at the right edge and the page number wrapped onto a second line. Word writes a left stop into each such paragraph. Word's rule, probed with about 100 documents made in Word (number widths, levels, sizes, fonts, indents, style tab stops, hanging indents): the stop is the left indent of the first of the styles TOC1 to TOC9, in that order, that lies behind the end of the number plus 240 twips (a style the document does not have counts with Word's own indent, 220 twips per level above the first), else that place itself; the number is measured with the font of the entry's style, from the first line's start. `TocTabs` writes it, with the width from `IFieldHost.MeasureLine` (`LineMeasure`: the layout's resolver and shaper), and omits it when a stop of the style or a hanging indent already takes the text behind the number (the corpus thesis keeps its look). A number that a space or nothing follows (`w:suff`) is written with a space and gets no stop.
2. **A refresh keeps the tab stops of each entry** (before: the stops of the first paragraph went to all, which rewrote a list Word had made). An entry is paired with the old paragraph that points at a bookmark of its heading, else with the one of its style that says the same line, else with the one in its place (`TocPrevious`; a second style of one built-in name counts as the first). A paired entry of the same level keeps its stops, with exceptions that each take away a stop which would catch the tab before the page number of a short entry, or push its title out of the line (`TocTabs.ForExisting`, amended 2026-11-27 after the task review, after its re-review and after the second re-review). (a) A left stop of the paragraph that stands behind a stop of the entry's style, or of its hanging indent, which is itself behind the number goes, unless it stands at Word's place for the number (within 20 twips, with the number's end taken 20 twips shorter and longer too): the style's stop takes the tab after the number, and Word writes its own stop also there. Behind a hanging indent that takes the number on a first line at the margin or in front of it, the stop goes also at that place: Word writes none there (third re-review, below). (b) The number's stop is the left stop of the paragraph that took the tab after the old number. When the number differs and the tab after the new number would not go to that stop, or the stop stands where Word puts one for the old number and not where it puts one for the new, it goes; so does a left stop far to the right of Word's place, in the right half of the line, that leaves the title and the page number no room (one written for much larger letters). Word's place for the new number gets a stop in its stead, unless a stop of the style or of the hanging indent takes the text then, or one of the paragraph at or before that place, or one that stood in front of the stop that went. A stop of the user's that still takes the text behind the new number stays. (c) When no tab follows a number any more, the stop that took it goes with any left stop before it. (d) When the tab after a number would go to the right stop (a list saved in the broken shape, a number or a font grown past the stop), the stop is added and the left stops before the number's end go. An entry without a number keeps all its stops but those (c) takes and, when its number just went, every left stop behind a stop of the style that took the number (Word writes none without a number). A new entry takes the right stop of its neighbours and its own left stop. Before the first amendment a refresh removed every left stop of an entry without a number whose line changed, and left a stop behind each number that grew, so that after the number shrank again the page number of a short entry went to the second stop. The first amendment still did that over a style with a left stop of its own: a stop written for a long number came back in front of the style's when the number was short again, or stayed behind it when the letters grew and shrank, and the page number was drawn after the title. A random-sequence test (`TocTabSequenceTests`: 600 sequences of one to three changes between two refreshes, in Write's layout) failed in 104 of them before the second amendment and found three more causes, each fixed with a test of its own: `LineMeasure` rounded the end of a number up to whole twips, so a stop less than a twip behind a number was not taken by Write although the layout takes it (a stop behind a very wide number can now be 1 twip further left); a stop was added in front of one of the paragraph's own that already took the new number (letters and number changed at once); a stop written for much larger letters stayed far to the right when letters and number changed at once or the number stayed, and the page number wrapped. Word's "Update table" writes the stops anew: one stop for the current number (also when it shrinks), none when the numbering is gone, and the user's own stops are dropped; Write keeps the user's unless (a) to (d) take them.

   *The second re-review* found that rule (a) as first written (every stop behind a style or hanging stop) rewrote lists Word had made: where a hanging indent takes the number, Word usually still writes its stop behind the indent or at it (a left indent of 1440 with a hanging indent of 720: `left:1540` at level 3; 580 with 360: `left:800` at level 2). Such a stop can draw the page number of a short entry at the stop, in Word as in Write: that is Word's look and stays (in a list Write itself makes, no stop is written there). A stop left over from larger letters or a longer number goes when it does not stand at Word's place for the number as it is now; one that stands there stays. Checking Word's own lists found two more rewrites, each fixed with a test: a changed number removed Word's stop although it was Word's stop for the new number too, and a refresh wrote `PAGEREF` without `\h` into a list without links (`TOC` without `\h`), where Word writes `\h` all the same. **Proof on Word's files** (a scratch program, not in the repository): 217 files with a list that Word made itself over the probed shapes (numbers and chapter texts, letters of 8 to 48 points, several fonts, style stops, hanging indents; German style ids renamed to the English ones in memory, the recorded limit) and the corpus thesis, one refresh each with nothing changed: no entry's stops changed (33 entries in 21 of the 194 newest files changed before). After renumbering four of these lists and letting Word update them, Write's refresh of the same change gives Word's stops (a few twips apart behind a wide number) or, where the hanging indent takes the new number, none: the title then stands where Word's does or at the indent, and the page number at the right edge, where Word often draws a short entry's page number at its stop.

   *The third re-review* (2026-11-28) found that after the letters of TOC1 grew and shrank again, a stop Write had written for the large letters stayed when it stood at Word's place for the small number, and then took the tab before the page number of a short entry. Word was asked (headings "A" and "Beta", numbers `%1.` and `%1.%2.`; `Docs/write-word-check.md`). **Over a left stop of TOC1 at 600 twips**, with a TOC2 indent of 1200 or 1320, Word's own list, new and after "Update table", has the single stop `left:1200` (`left:1320`) on the level-1 entry and draws its page number there, in the middle of the line. The stop Write keeps is the one Word writes for that document, so Write draws what Word draws: Word's look wins, the rule is unchanged and tests pin it. **Over a hanging indent of TOC1** of 400 twips Word writes the right stop only and the page number stands at the right edge, while Write kept `left:1200`: a defect, fixed with failing tests first. Sixteen further documents made in Word showed where the two differ: behind a hanging indent that takes the number on a first line at the margin or in front of it, Word writes no stop (levels 1 and 2, also with a tab stop in the style); where the first line starts behind the margin it often writes one and sometimes none (among the sixteen new documents a first line 20 twips behind the margin got none, and earlier Word files also hold entries with a first line 100 to 840 twips behind the margin and no stop); that condition is not worked out. Write now removes a stop behind such a hanging indent only in the first case (`LineGeometry.FirstLineStart`). A refresh with nothing changed changes no stop in the 217 files of the second re-review and in the 22 documents of this one.
3. **"Right-align page numbers" off did not last.** Write now writes `\p " "` as Word does (`TOC \o "1-3" \p " " \h \z \u`, `TOC \p " " \h \z \c "Figure"`) with a space before the page number, and the right stop with dots that Word writes into every list (also one without page numbers). `\p` is supported with Word's reading: the first character of its value, a space for an empty value, a tab without a value; another control character leaves the list "not updated".
4. **An empty numbered heading was missing.** Word lists a numbered heading without visible text as its number, followed by the page separator and the page, with no stop of its own: an empty one, white space, tabs, line and column breaks, a note or comment reference, bookmarks, hidden runs or deleted text. It leaves out a heading whose paragraph mark is hidden, one that is only a page break (with or without spaces), and an unnumbered one. A picture, a symbol or a non-breaking hyphen it copies into the entry; Write leaves such a heading out (the recorded picture difference). Cross-reference targets, lists of figures and bookmarks are unchanged.
5. **A tab in a caption was read as a space** (found after the re-review). In a list of figures Word keeps the first tab of a caption, with a left stop behind the text before it (the same place rule as behind a number), reads a later tab as a space and trims tabs at the start and the end; in a heading it reads every tab as a space. Write now follows: the text before the tab stands where a number stands in the entry. Before, a refresh rewrote a list Word had made without its tab.

**Built-in styles are found by name, as Word finds them (2026-11-28).** The field code knew headings and entry styles by the English style ids, so in a file of a German Word (`berschrift1`, `Verzeichnis1`, named `heading 1`, `toc 1`) a table of contents without `\u` was replaced by the sentence of an empty list, a list of figures lost its style, `SEQ \s` never restarted and headings and chapter numbers were missing; it happened by itself after opening, and a save wrote it. One definition, `FieldStyles` (`Raffica.Write.Editing`), holds Word's rules, probed with Word 16 (`Docs/write-word-check.md`, "Built-in style names"):
- A built-in style is a paragraph style (no `w:type` counts) that is **not** `w:customStyle` and whose name is exactly the built-in name in any case (`heading 1,Kapitel` is not). The style of a paragraph is the paragraph style its `w:pStyle` names, else the default paragraph style.
- A heading is a paragraph of `heading 1` to `heading 9`; only these restart `SEQ \s`, give `STYLEREF 1` and are cross-reference targets (not a custom style with an outline level, one based on a heading or a direct `w:outlineLvl`).
- The style outline level is decided by the first style of the `basedOn` chain with a `w:outlineLvl` (9 is body text) or that is a heading style. `TOC \o` lists a heading at its level (whatever the paragraph says), else at the style outline level; with `\u` a paragraph that is no heading is listed at its own `w:outlineLvl` when it has one.
- Entry styles (`toc 1` to `toc 9`, `table of figures`) and the caption style (`caption`) are found by name; of two of one name the first counts. Where the file has none, Write makes one under an id no style has (`TOC3`, or `TOC31`), based on `Normal` by id, else the default paragraph style. A refresh makes an entry style too, when a list is about to name a level the file has no style for (not when its paragraphs name that id already, and not for the sentence of an empty list); like a result it is no history step and an undo leaves it, and `FieldRefreshResult.StylesAdded` makes the owner start its layout over (`DocumentLayout.Restyle`) and the session rebuild its format.
- A chapter number needs the style named `heading 1` to carry a list (`CaptionRules.HeadingsAreNumbered`), not the state of the numbering switch.

A style sheet is read once per operation or pass (a dictionary by id and by name), never searched per paragraph. Measured (2026-11-28, Windows PC, Native AOT publish of the fix, three launches): `fields` worst command median 63.97 ms of 75 (table of contents insert, launches 68.2, 62.78, 63.97), the refresh after a heading edit 96.9 to 99.5 ms; no worse than the runs above.

**Cost.** `fields` on the Windows PC after the fix (Native AOT, 2026-11-27, three launches): worst command median 63.99 ms of 75, the table of contents insert; a control build of the commit before the fix (1f7ea71), measured the same day, gave 55.9 to 56.1 ms for it, and build 3 of the fields limit 50.1 ms. The editor's own phase of the insert is unchanged (26 ms against 26 ms); the layout after it took 34 to 38 ms against 27 to 30 ms (the cause was not isolated). The refresh after a heading edit took 98 to 101 ms against 91 to 94 ms. The limit is unchanged; the headroom on this command is now 11 ms.

**The leader after Word's "Update table".** `TableOfContents.Update()` writes the leader the running Word has last set in any document (dots in a new session), not the one in the file, also in a list Word made: checked with Word's own list and with Write's (after a hyphen leader was set on another document, every list got hyphens). `Fields.Update()` keeps it. Nothing in the file changes this.

**Rationale:** One text for the screen, the editor, the recovery copy and the file; a refresh that is not an edit keeps the undo list and the dirty mark honest; computing only what Write fully understands means a number in a saved file is Word's number.
**Rejected alternatives:** an overlay computed by the layout with a materialise step at save (the editor, the recovery copy and the screen would differ); every refresh an ordinary edit (opening would dirty the file); re-deriving a structural change on redo (replayed instead); completing the layout on the UI thread on the timer path (a half-second freeze).
**Known limits** (each deliberate or deferred):
- *Saved page results.* A save in the writing view that owes page results pays a full pages layout of the document on the UI thread: about 0.5 s on the thesis (the settle alone, not the writing of the file: median 506 ms after a typed letter and 518 ms after an edit that moves pages, worst 609 ms; 60 saves in three launches of a temporary Native AOT build on the Windows PC on 2026-11-27, the fix wave's code with an uncommitted measuring step over the prepared thesis of the fields run); the writing view itself shows the stored results (a `?` for a new reference) until a save or the switch back. A save made while an input method is composing skips the settle, so it writes the stored results, a `?` among them. The flag for owed page results can stay or become set although nothing can be written, and a save in the writing view then lays out the whole document all the same (about 0.5 s at thesis size). Cases: the only `PAGEREF` or table of contents is removed in the writing view (a pass over the touched blocks leaves the flag set; the next save pays once). A first `SEQ`, `REF` or `NOTEREF` field arrives in the writing view in a document that had no field, and the pass looks at the touched blocks only (the flag still has its start value; paid once). `PAGEREF` and `TOC` fields are counted whether Write can update them or not, so a document whose only page field is `PAGEREF \p` pays at every writing-view save that follows an edit.
- *Pages.* No page results on screen in the writing view; a paragraph nested in a table or a content control answers with the page of its top-level block's first line (a long table can differ from Word), as does a paragraph with a page break inside; a chapter prefix of the page number (`w:chapStyle`) is not given; page numbers differ from Word where the pagination differs.
- *Where fields are refreshed.* Body, table cells and content controls only. Fields in notes, headers, footers and comments and fields in tracked changes keep their stored results (Word updates them); nested fields are never written and not counted by the bar or marked in the status line. The numbers of the `SEQ` fields after them agree with Word's.
- *Differences from Word kept on purpose.* A `REF` to a bookmark that holds the reference keeps its stored result (Word writes "Error! Not a valid bookmark self-reference."); two references that show each other are not detected and end at the pass bound; a heading that is only a picture, a symbol, a non-breaking hyphen or another object is left out of a table of contents (Word lists it with a copy of it); a table of contents whose styles have tab stops still gets Write's right stop with the chosen leader (Word then writes none of its own and, with a single left stop in the style, writes its stop after the number although the style's would serve); `REF` does not copy the source formatting; `STYLEREF` other than `1 \s` is not updated; Word writes the sentence of an empty table of contents in the default paragraph style, Write in the entry style of its first level.
- *Built-in styles outside the fields.* The heading numbering switch, promote and demote, the footnote and endnote styles and `TableGrid` are still found by their English ids (4.4a and 4.5a), so in a file with other ids the switch is unavailable, promote and demote refuse and Write adds its own note and table styles; the outline pane goes by outline levels, so a heading style without one is listed by a table of contents and not by the pane.
- *The three-pass bound.* A table of contents whose length depends on its own page numbers, or fields that move each other, stop after three passes (counted); tested with a fake host only.
- *Tables of contents.* A switch such as `\t`, `\w` or `\x` makes it "not updated"; a list whose entry paragraphs carry a section break is not updated at all (not its page numbers either); a Word list in one paragraph with text beside it is not updated; a refresh writes no right stop into a list that has none; an entry whose level changed takes the stops of its new neighbours and loses its own left ones; the page number of a short entry does not end at the right edge when two stops stand behind its number (or, without a number, one behind its title) that Write keeps: two stops of the style or of its hanging indent (as in Word, which draws an entry without a number the same way over one such stop); a stop of the paragraph's at Word's place for the number behind a stop of the style, or behind a hanging indent whose first line starts behind the margin (Word writes and draws such a stop; Write keeps one Word wrote, and one of its own that stands there after the number or its letters changed, also where Word would write none over such a hanging indent); where a hanging indent starts the first line at the margin and Write's measure says the number just fits before it while Word's number just passes it, a refresh would remove the stop Word wrote (found by reading the code; no probed file is near that boundary); two of the paragraph's own, as when the user adds one beside the number's (also once the user's stop in front takes the number and the number or its letters change: which of the two is the number's cannot be told, so the other one can stay); a stop of the paragraph's in front of a stop of the style (the user's, or one left in front when the user moves a style's stop behind it); and an entry that holds two left stops of its own from before the amendments is not repaired while its number is unchanged; the width of a number is measured by Write and can differ from Word's by a few twips (Word then writes its stop a little elsewhere on its own update); a stop Word wrote behind a style stop is taken for a left-over one, and removed by a refresh, when Write's measure of the number differs from Word's by more than that (a font that is missing and replaced) or when the list is stale (the letters or the number changed in Word without an update); where a changed number lets the style's or the hanging stop take the tab, Write writes no stop although Word's update would (the title can then stand at the indent instead of at a stop of Word's in front of it); after a first heading of a deeper level Word counts the following level-1 heading as 2 and Write as 1 (found in lists Word made, a numbering difference, not fixed); a number about as wide as the text area leaves the title at the right edge; the structural refresh waits while a redo is pending (the old entries show meanwhile); removing a list drops a foreign bookmark marker inside it; an entry leaves out hidden text only where it is hidden by direct formatting, not by a style.
- *Protection and editing.* The refusals of the table of contents show after the attempt (a picture picker opens first); formatting and styles on its paragraphs are allowed and a refresh may drop direct formatting on entries; moving a caret or a selection end into a list snaps to its edge; a double or triple click inside collapses oddly; a field in one paragraph is not protected as a list; promoting or demoting a range that touches a list is refused.
- *Commands.* Bookmark names are ASCII letters, digits and underscores (Word also takes letters such as `Ü`, so `Übersicht` is refused); Add outside the body gives a general note; deleting a bookmarked link text can leave an empty link. A caption's place-dependent refusals (no Heading 1 above for a chapter number, a section end) show after the card; a caption with the caret in a table cell goes inside the cell; no keep-with-next on a caption above; the chapter separator is a hyphen; the blank document's language is fixed to `en-US`, so a German user's new document gets "Figure". A cross-reference is made in the body only, does not replace a selection, shows `?` for a page until the refresh, lists at most 2000 targets and no heading without text and number.
- *Cost.* A burst that touches a heading, a field, a bookmark, a table cell or a content control, or that moves a block to another page, walks the whole body on the UI thread at the pause (40 to 90 ms on the thesis on the Windows PC; about 188 ms after a table of contents is inserted); a view far below a rewritten list that moves the pagination pays nearly a full layout there (465 ms); a burst that skips the page pass still walks every paragraph once (about 6 ms on the thesis), against the spec's "never the whole document"; the Linux laptop and the Intel Mac are unmeasured.
- *Waiting.* The refresh waits longer than the spec says: the whole refresh waits while a redo is pending after it found a structural change, and while page results are wanted the order-based results wait for a complete layout too. A save during the wait for the redo refreshes nothing, so it writes the stored results, the page results among them, possibly `?`.
- *Undo, redo and the caret.* The redo of a typing step that carries a structural change of a table of contents leaves the links of its entries to a changed heading dangling until the next refresh or save (the heading's bookmark is a content change that is not recorded). `FieldPositions.Follow` pairs old and live paragraphs by their index in a rewritten block range, so a caret on the end edge of a list whose last paragraph holds an entry and whose number of paragraphs changed is mapped against another old paragraph (fewer paragraphs: the offset is clamped) or not at all (more paragraphs: the offset stays), and can then stand inside the list, where typing is refused. A list whose end mark has a paragraph of its own (Word's shape) is not affected, and neither are the paragraphs around a list, the one Insert adds and the ones Enter makes among them: they are outside the rewritten range. Room to type around a list does not change this.
- *Around a table of contents.* No place to type between a table or a content control and a list that directly follows it (Enter at the start edge makes room only for a list that is first). Backspace in the paragraph Enter made after a list, and Delete in the one it made above a list, select the list as before instead of taking the paragraph away (Undo takes it back). Deleting a whole list by selection leaves an empty paragraph in the style `TOC1`. A table directly before a content control that holds a list, a table and a paragraph is left next to the control's table after Remove table of contents (not checked in Word). A `SEQ` field in the second or a later paragraph of a table of contents is counted (`SeqCounter.Counts` looks at the parent fields only), against its doc comment. When a table of contents is wrapped in a content control (Word's usual shape), Enter at its end edge makes the new paragraph inside that control: text typed there belongs to the control, and Word's "Remove Table of Contents" might remove it with the list (not checked in Word).
- *Bookmarks.* A bookmark Write holds only as kept content (between blocks, in a table row, in a wrapper or text box) or in another story is not listed, cannot be deleted in Write and keeps its name taken. A new cross-reference never takes a generated name a field or a link still points at; a new table of contents bookmark may (`_Toc` names are taken from the bookmarks alone, so that the links of a list heal after a redo), so a `PAGEREF` outside a list to a deleted heading can come to point at a new one. Deleting a bookmark clones every top-level block between its start and its end.
- *Hostile files* (milestone 4.8): the search for tables of contents starts over up to 256 times for unclosed fields, the search for bookmark ends is quadratic (`BookmarkOperations.Scan`) and a delete looks forward from every bookmark start (`EditRules.BookmarksInside`).
**Proof.** `FieldOracleTests` with `FieldSteps` (every field command and edit step, undo and redo, over the corpus and generated documents; each step ends in `LayoutOracle.AssertEqualToFull`, the independent counts of `ReferenceFields`, `ReferenceToc` and `ReferencePageResults`, and the bookmark checks; a structural refresh after an undo or redo that restored the state is asserted never to happen), `SnapshotCacheOracleTests`, `DerivedHistoryTests`, `TocStructuralRefreshTests`, `FieldRefreshTests`, `PageRefreshTests`, `FieldViewTests`, `BookmarkEditingTests`, `CaptionEditingTests`, `CrossReferenceEditingTests`, `TocEditingTests`, `TocEntryShapeTests`, `TocEntryLayoutTests`, `TocTabSequenceTests`, `TocProtectionTests`, `TocGuardTests`, `TocEdgeTests`, `BookmarkReuseTests`, `FieldClipboardTests`, and in the app `FieldRefreshRunnerTests`, `FieldSettleTests` (also `TheRealRefreshOnTheThesisSettlesAndTheLiveLayoutEqualsAFreshOne`: the real runner on the thesis, the live layout against a fresh one), `DerivedStampConsumersTests`, `SaveSettleTests`, `StartupCreatesNoFieldWorkTests`, the card and command tests. **Every new raise site needs an attribution and cases in both oracles; a new field rule changes the engine and the independent oracle apart from each other, from what Word does.**
**Word check:** all 49 files of `WordCheckFieldFilesTests` (36, then 37 with the foreign-bookmark probe, then 44 with the table of contents cases of the visual check, then 47 with the renumbered lists of the task review, then 49 with the cases of its re-review) open with no repair prompt; `tools/word-field-results.ps1` reports no difference but page numbers and the recorded ones, and its negative control reports every wrong stored result (`Docs/write-word-check.md`). The comparison is text only and cannot see layout: the lists are also exported to PDF as saved and after Word's update and looked at beside Write's drawing.
**Tooling note:** the `dotnet format` warning "required references not loaded" for `Raffica.Write.Tests` and `WriteLayoutBench` came from a stray empty file `bench/write-layout/obj/Debug` in the local build output (the Debug load of the bench failed, so analyzers did not cover the two projects); with it removed the format check loads everything and passes. `ImageCacheBudgetTests` moved into the wall-clock test collection: its 10 s waits on pool work failed once in the full run beside the parallel classes (bounds unchanged).
**Follow-ups:** hyperlinks and find and replace (4.5c); a cheaper field refresh (backlog); table of contents entries for headings that are only objects; non-ASCII bookmark names.
**Owner gates (milestone 4.5.2 stays `in-progress`):** (1) **Word:** done by script on the Windows PC on 2026-11-27 (above); open: the owner's acceptance of the recorded differences and a person's look at the rendered files (`RAFFICA_WORD_CHECK_DIR=<folder> dotnet test tests/Raffica.Write.Tests -c Release --filter-class "*WordCheckFieldFilesTests"`, then `pwsh tools/word-open-check.ps1 <files>` and `pwsh tools/word-field-results.ps1 <files>`). (2) **A look at the Native AOT app on a real display** on Windows, the Linux laptop and the Intel Mac: the References tab, the four cards, the bar, the status text and a table of contents that follows an edit. (3) **`measure.yml`** target `write` with the cases `fields` and `typing-fields` on the Linux laptop (mains power) and the Intel Mac, after the merge to `dev`: `gh workflow run measure.yml --ref dev -f platform=<linux|macos> -f target=write -f note="<machine>"`. The IME check is not part of 4.5b.

## 2026-11-25 – Raffica Write: the objects limit (M4.5a)
**Context:** The ten commands of `--measure-objects` and the pointer moves of `--measure-gesture` were measured for the first time on the Windows PC (Task 14 of milestone 4.5a, "Raffica Write: tables, pictures and notes (M4.5a)"). The spec asks for a fixed limit set from the first measurement (budget times 1.25, the method of "Raffica Write: the sections limit (M4.4b)") and a pointer move of at most 16 ms at the 95th percentile.
**Decision:** `measure.cs` judges the metric `objects`, the worst median of the ten commands of `--measure-objects` (a 5 by 5 table inserted, a row added, two cells merged, borders set, a picture inserted, pasted from a clipboard, resized and moved, a footnote inserted and the reference of one deleted, all on the thesis in the page view) against a fixed 12.5 ms on every platform (`CheckCommand.ObjectsBudget` 10 × 1.25). The value is the CPU cost of the command (key handler, the render pass and the page draws of that frame; the frame latency, the split and the refresh of the ribbon states are recorded and never judged), reduced as the structure metric is (the median over the launches per row, then the largest). First measurement on the Windows PC (Intel Core i5-8500T, mains, Native AOT app, three launches, 2026-11-25): worst median 8.24 ms (picture-paste; picture-insert 7.66, the other eight 1.6 to 4.5 ms). 8.24 ms is rounded up to a 10 ms budget (the M4.2 rule for time budgets), so the limit is 12.5 ms. It is a regression guard, not an aim: the commands are far inside any perceptible delay. The metric `gesture` (worst 95th percentile of the pointer moves of six drag kinds: a column border, the right edge of a table, a row, a cell range, the resize of a picture and the move of a floating picture, 100 moves each) is judged against the fixed 16 ms (budget 12.8 × 1.25) like typing; first measurement 1.91 ms (cell-range, the worst kind). `typing-objects` (typing at the end of a picture in a paragraph and in a table cell, both views) counts into `typing` (worst p95 6.5 ms). The Linux laptop (mains power) and the Intel Mac runs of `measure.yml` (cases `objects`, `gesture`, `typing-objects`) are the owner's gate: `gh workflow run measure.yml --ref dev -f platform=<linux|macos> -f target=write -f note="<machine>"`; the limit is unproven on them. Never loosened to make a run pass.
**Numbers (first measurement, Windows PC, ms; medians of the three launches, then p95 of the three launches):**

| Case | median | p95 |
|---|---|---|
| table-insert | 4.25 4.45 4.56 | 9.56 11.02 11.40 |
| table-add-row | 2.95 3.27 3.31 | 6.55 8.55 8.74 |
| table-merge | 2.67 2.78 2.84 | 3.44 5.98 6.98 |
| table-border-set | 2.92 2.92 3.18 | 6.25 7.13 7.34 |
| picture-insert | 7.16 7.66 7.87 | 9.43 10.37 10.96 |
| picture-paste (the worst) | 7.81 8.24 9.40 | 12.74 12.99 14.77 |
| picture-resize | 2.62 2.74 2.76 | 4.07 5.28 5.81 |
| picture-float-move | 4.30 4.30 4.35 | 7.64 8.10 8.24 |
| footnote-insert | 2.47 2.53 2.66 | 5.27 6.15 13.27 |
| footnote-delete | 1.56 1.60 1.60 | 3.72 3.93 3.94 |

The gesture p95 per drag kind over the three launches is 0.5 to 2.2 ms (cell-range 1.79 1.91 2.19, column-border 0.52 0.52 0.59, picture-float-move 0.85 1.06 1.14, picture-resize 0.64 0.66 0.67, row 0.57 0.60 0.84, table-edge 0.70 0.79 0.81). Per-launch noise on picture-paste was 7.8 to 9.4 ms, so the limit is 33 percent above the worst launch. The existing cases on the same publish stayed inside their limits (typing worst p95 7.35 ms, format 7.77, structure worst move median about 6.2, sections worst median 406 to 426 ms of 600).
**Rejected alternatives:** a limit from the 95th percentile of the commands (a single noisy launch would set it, as the 13 ms p95 of one footnote-insert launch shows); the plain 1.25 times the first measurement (10.3 ms, below the noise of picture-paste); a limit per platform before the other two machines have run.

**Amendment (2026-11-25, the Linux laptop, the Intel Mac and the cost of a picture insert):** the first runs of `measure.yml` target `write` (Linux laptop on mains at 3.7 GHz, run 45684539847; Intel Mac, run 45684544347) showed that the limit set from the Windows PC alone was too tight for a picture insert: the Linux laptop's worst command median was **22.55 ms** (picture paste 20 to 24 ms, picture insert 15 to 20 ms; all eight other commands 1.8 to 7 ms) and the Intel Mac's picture insert was 11.3 to 13.1 ms (12.47 as the median of the launches, 0.03 ms under the limit). The time was in the key handler's *operation* phase, not in drawing (draws 0.4 to 0.7 ms): the engine-only bench had the same insert at 5.2 ms, and timing the steps inside `InsertPicture` on the thesis gave two document-wide walks of 3.6 and 3.9 ms (Windows PC, JIT): `PictureOperations.NextDocPrId` (every picture insert, to find an id above every `wp:docPr` of the document) and the `EditHistory.PruneMedia` scan that runs when an insert drops a redo step (the measurement undoes every run, so every timed insert dropped one; in normal use it happens after an undo and a new insert). Both walked the document through nested `yield` iterators that allocate several enumerators per paragraph (about 2.9 MB per insert). **Fix (PR 137):** `TextEditor` walks for the first id only and counts on from it (`TextEditor.NextPictureId`; a picture enters a document only through this editor or the file it was read from; an undo never frees an id for reuse; the walk is taken again when the first id was a free one below a hostile file's highest id, because counting on from it could collide, test `IdsAfterAFreeIdBelowAHostileFilesHighestNeverCollide`), and `PictureOperations.DrawingRoots` and `BlockRoots` collect into one list with indexed loops (same results, same order). **Measured:** `InsertPicture` on the thesis 7.8 to 2.8 ms (JIT, Windows PC); Native AOT on the Windows PC picture insert 8.5 to about 5.0 ms and paste 9.1 to about 5.0 ms; on `dev` after the fix (runs 45704076541 and 45704080285, every check step green) the **Linux laptop's worst command median is 10.36 ms** (picture paste 9.8 to 10.5, picture insert 4.9 to 6.1, gesture p95 1.33, typing 12.57) and the **Intel Mac's 5.81 ms** (gesture p95 0.64, typing 7.11). **The limit stays 12.5 ms on every platform** (the numbers were not used to relax it); the Linux laptop's headroom is about 2 ms, so a picture command that gets slower there is the first thing to look at. What remains in the operation phase of a picture insert is the `PruneMedia` walk after the measurement's undo (about 2 ms on the Windows PC), which a real session meets only after an undo and a new insert. **Rejected for now:** a per-platform limit (it would have hidden a real, fixable cost; the PDF budgets have per-platform entries because their cost is platform-bound, this one was not); a cache of the drawing ids on the model (a model change by another path would stale it silently; the editor is the one place pictures are made). **Process lesson:** `measure.yml` builds only commits that are on `main` or `dev`: dispatched with the `ref` of a feature branch, its "Check that the ref is code of this repository" step fails and the job still measures the checked-out `dev` and then fails the size rows, so such a run proves nothing about the branch (two runs of 2026-11-25 did exactly this); merge to `dev`, then measure `dev`.

## 2026-11-25 – Raffica Write: tables, pictures and notes (M4.5a)
**Context:** The first milestone of the long-document tools (the old 4.5 was split into 4.5a, 4.5b and 4.5c by the owner on 2026-11-24). A thesis needs tables, figures and notes; before 4.5a Write could open, lay out and edit the text of these elements but could not make one, and the pointer knew only text. 4.5a adds the commands, the objects the pointer can act on (full direct manipulation, the owner's call) and the package support the new content needs (a picture is a new part, a first note is a new notes part), plus pasting a picture from the system clipboard (added to the scope by the owner). Spec: `Docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-24-raffica-write-4-5a-tables-pictures-and-notes-design.md`, plan: `Docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-24-milestone-04-5a-tables-pictures-and-notes.md`.
**Decision:**
- **Notes first.** `NotesParts.Ensure(document, NoteType)` makes the footnotes or endnotes part on first use with the separator (id -1) and continuation separator (id 0) notes and names both in `w:settings` (`w:footnotePr` or `w:endnotePr`, placed by `SchemaOrder`; a settings part is made when the file has none); the existing created-part path of `DocxFile` (user save and `SaveSnapshotAsync`) needed no change. **`NoteType { Footnote, Endnote }` is the choice of the plan's `NoteKind`**, which already means the kind of one note (normal, separator, ...). `TextEditor.InsertNote(caret, NoteType)` places the reference run in its own run (a reference at a paragraph end or in a paragraph with only hidden content goes on the paragraph itself so a tracked change cannot hide it), takes the id above the highest of the part, makes the styles `FootnoteReference` and `FootnoteText` (or the endnote pair) on first use as a style change that the session answers with `Restyle` (created styles stay after an undo, like a numbering part; a document without a style sheet gets a direct superscript), and puts the caret in the new note. A delete, a replacing insert or a paste that removes a reference removes its note in the same step (`RemoveNotesOf`); a merge that keeps the reference keeps the note. A dangling reference deletes silently. A note change is `ObjectChange` (`NotesAdded`, `NotesRemoved`, `MediaAdded`, `MediaRemoved`) routed through `EditResult.Objects` and `EditHistory` like `Sections` and `Style`: undo and redo check applicability before anything changes, removed notes are live objects (never mutated, re-inserted in id order, so a file whose notes were not in id order is saved sorted after an undo). `EditHistory.Record` gained an `after` point because the caret of an insert lies in the note story while the block change is in the body. Refused (`NoteRefusal`) in a header, footer, note, comment, a prefix document and an inline text, combo box, drop-down or date control; the writing view draws no note, so there the commands say so and a switch into the writing view with the caret in a note first returns to the reference. Commands `note.footnote`, `note.endnote`, `note.back` (Insert tab, group Notes, digit 3), no keys (Word's Ctrl+Alt keys cannot exist, AltGr is text); a double click on a reference jumps into its note (`NoteLinks`, `PageView.Notes`) and on the note's number back, Esc and "Back to the text" return; the jump puts the caret at the start of the note text, Back behind the reference mark.
- **The object foundation.** `ObjectMap` (per `LaidOutPage`, immutable, built with the page at emit time, no second pass; pages without objects share `ObjectMap.Empty`) holds `PictureBox`es (the identity of a plain `pic:pic` drawing of the body; the box has no stored id: `Id` is derived per read from the anchor because a memoized id on a retained stale page diverged from a full layout after paragraph inserts) and `TableGrid`s per table piece (column borders including both outer edges, row edges, `CellRect`s with the model row index, `TableGrid.BlockIndex` = the top-level body block or the enclosing block content control). `LayoutOracle.AssertEqualToFull` compares the maps, so every oracle run of 4.3a to 4.4b now covers them. `ObjectId(Story, Paragraph, Run, Content)` is the identity of a picture (ordinals in document order, depth cap 32; a run inserted before a picture shifts its id, so a caller holds the resolved `Picture` object or re-derives the id). `DocumentLayout.HitObject` gives, by priority, a handle of the selected resizable picture, a picture (last placed; behind-text pictures only with `includeBehindText`, then last), the nearer of a column border and a row edge, a table margin (row, column or table select), a cell; pictures in headers, footers and notes and shapes, text boxes, VML and alternate content get no box (they stay marked placeholders). `EditSession` has an object selection beside the text range (`PictureSelection`, `CellSelection`; setting one collapses the text range, a text selection change ends it; `ReconcileObjectSelection` keeps it across an edit that leaves the same drawing; every rebind or foreign model ends it), announced as text (`ObjectAnnouncement`). `ObjectDrag` is the one drag machine (Idle, Pressed, Dragging after 4 DIP, Releasing; a drag edits once on release; cancelled by Esc, capture lost, a save starting, a document change, a view change or dispose; a click commits nothing), `ObjectHandles` the pure geometry (8 handles of 8 DIP with 12 DIP hit areas). Esc order: a drag, then a plain object selection, then the old roles. Handles, guide lines, the drop line, the cell tint and the dashed outline are drawn by `EditOverlay` over the page and never enter a `LaidOutPage`; a render pass touches no visual. Object selection and every gesture exist in the pages view and the body only.
- **Tables.** `TableGridMap.Of(Table)` is a read-only view (spans, vertical merges, `RowIndex` = index in `Table.Rows`) that reads a table as the layout's `TableReader` does; a table with a wrapper row or cell (sdt), a tracked-deleted row, `gridBefore` or `gridAfter` or a short row is **irregular and refuses every command except Delete table** (`TableRefusal.UnsupportedStructure`; the corpus has 27 tables, 1 irregular, 0 inconsistent). Every operation runs on a **clone** of the table (`TableOperations`, `TableFormatting`, pure), validates it and replaces the table block as one `BlockChange` (one history step; notes whose reference left the table go with it); `RefusalFor<Op>` companions run the same on a clone and commit nothing (so a ribbon refresh asks once per shown command and change, never per key or frame, and `TableFactsSource` memoizes). `InsertTable(at, columns, rows)` makes the table the width of the text (one column's width in a multi-column section, at least 100 twips per column, remainder spread over the first columns so the grid sums exactly), fixed layout, `tblLook 04A0`, a dxa `tcW` per cell, one empty paragraph per cell and the `TableGrid` style (made on first use as a companion style change, Word's own definition; a document without a style sheet gets the single 0.5 pt `w:tblBorders` directly; the 24 blank-document styles are unchanged). At the start of a paragraph the table goes before it, at the end after it plus a new empty paragraph unless a paragraph follows, in the middle between the two halves of the split paragraph; a paragraph is kept between two adjacent tables; so the body never ends in a table. Refused: header, footer, note, comment, a cell (no nested creation), a text, combo box, drop-down or date control, a prefix document, more than 63 columns (`TableRules.MaxColumns`, pinned to the layout's `TableLimits`) or 500 rows. Add and delete rows and columns inside merged regions extend or shrink them (a continuation cell goes, the head's span shrinks, a deleted row that holds the head passes it on); Merge **widens the range to whole merged cells** and joins the content as paragraphs (empty ones skipped, nothing lost); Split only gives a merged cell back as its original cells (the content stays in the first); move row up and down refuses a cut merge and the header boundary. Borders: `BorderSpec` (sides as flags, style solid, double, dashed or dotted, width in eighths of a point, colour or automatic, presets None, All, Outside, Box and per side with "off"); **Outside is the outline only (inside lines untouched, Word's "Outside Borders"), Box is the outline and the range's inside lines removed (Word's dialog "Box")**; every shared border is written on both cells it belongs to (the neighbour outside the range gets the opposite side, because the layout draws the wider of two); a side element keeps its name and other attributes. Shading merges into `w:shd`; null removes exactly that element. The header row marks row 0 only on, and clears the whole leading run off (Word and the layout repeat leading rows only). **Widths:** every width write recomputes the grid, `tblW` dxa, `tblLayout fixed` and a dxa `tcW` on every cell (a spanning cell sums its columns), so the first write converts an autofit or percentage table as Word does (reading never converts, `ReadingNeverConverts`); the width operations take `drawnColumnTwips` from the object map so **the first conversion freezes the drawn widths**, not the model's; `SetTableWidth` keeps proportions with largest-remainder rounding so the sum is exact, `DistributeColumns` uses a dxa `tblW` or the grid sum. Colours come from the app's fixed list (`ColorChoices`; the editor takes `uint?` 0xAARRGGBB and the app validates). A formatting operation takes the session caret and puts it in the first paragraph of the first touched cell (the paragraphs are new objects).
- **Table commands, Tab and gestures.** 20 `table.*` commands (Table tab, key tip `T`, order Home, Insert, Table, Layout, View; groups Rows, Columns, Cells, Table; always present, disabled with a reason outside a table) plus the Insert group Table: the grid picker is a `Flyout` with the app's `TableGridPicker` (10 columns by 8 rows, the shell's `RibbonPicker` cannot show a hover grid) and `TableInsertCard` for any size up to 63 by 500; borders and shading are `PickerDefinition`s; `TableBordersCard` and `TablePropertiesCard` (width, every column, row height rule, header row; cm or inch by region; one history step through `TextEditor.SetTableProperties`, `ReadTableValues`). In the writing view Distribute columns and Table properties are refused with a reason. **Tab rule:** in a list item the 4.4a list rule decides first at offset 0 (empty item or not, Shift+Tab at level 0 and Tab at level 8 change nothing, the list rule never falls through); elsewhere in a table cell Tab moves to the next cell and in the last cell adds a row, Shift+Tab moves back; in a nested table Tab is a tab character; the plan said "with text", the code keeps 4.4a's behaviour (Word does the same). Gestures: a column border and the right table edge (the neighbours change and the table width stays; the right edge may grow until the page edge; minimum column 284 twips (0.5 cm), a column already narrower is not made narrower), a row by its left handle (a drop line; one `MoveRow` step; a cut merge or the header boundary is a note, dropping on its own row does nothing), a cell range by pointer (live while the pointer enters other cells of the same page piece, sticky once entered; widened to whole merged cells; Shift+click and Shift+arrows extend; Esc ends it), selection by the row, column and table margins. A gesture never starts on a nested table, in a prefix document, in a save or with another button; Delete or Backspace on a cell range is claimed and shows a note (Word clears the cells).
- **Pictures and the package.** `MediaStore` (document level, `WordDocument.Media`, lazy; ids `rId<N>` above every id of the main part's relationships and every id given, never reused; at most 50 MiB per entry and 256 MiB in all; reads take no lock) holds the bytes of a new picture until the next save; **the Open XML SDK 3.5.1 names a new image part `/media/imageN` outside `/word`, so media is not made through the SDK**: `MediaPartWriter.AddTo` adds `word/media/image<N>.<ext>`, the image relationship in the main part's rels and a `Default` for the extension (an `Override` when the package already maps the extension to another type) as zip entries after the package is closed, stored uncompressed; existing entries are untouched; the guard knows created media (the saved content type, exactly one main relationship with the intended id, the image type and a resolving target) and refuses a tampered one. **Used-ness is computed from references at save time** (`MediaPartWriter.Used`, one XML pass over the serialized main part, only when the store has entries, after the lease is released): an entry that no drawing refers to is not written, so a deleted picture or an undone insert leaves no part, an undo of a deletion brings the picture back with its bytes, and every save (also Save as and the recovery copy) writes one part per picture. **Pruning:** undo and redo never touch the store; `EditHistory` collects the media of every step it drops (a redo stack cut by a new edit, `Clear`, `Trim`) and removes an entry only when no attribute in the relationships namespace of the current model (every story) and no remaining step snapshot refers to it (`MediaReferences`, the one definition of "reference" that save and prune share; a candidate still referenced when its step drops is held and re-checked). `PackageImageSource` asks the pending media first for the main story. The size of an insert is its pixels at 96 DPI (9525 EMU) scaled in proportion to the text width of the section it lands in and Word's largest side (20,116,800 EMU, 1584 pt); in a fixed-layout, non-nested cell it is capped to the cell's content width (autofit and percentage cells fall back to the section width; the layout shrinks an inline picture wider than a fixed cell). The drawing is Word's inline `w:drawing`; `wp:docPr` ids are above every id of body, headers, footers, notes and comments, the name is `Picture <N>` (never from a file name), alt text is `descr` on `docPr` and `pic:cNvPr` (at most 4096 characters, invalid XML characters dropped). Bytes are untrusted: size, then the managed header probe `ImageHeaderProbe` (PNG, JPEG, GIF, BMP; the layout's `ImageHeader` stays Skia-based and a test pins that both state the same size for every corpus picture), then at most 32,000,000 pixels; the checks run before anything changes and no message holds bytes, names or alt text (one `PictureRefusal` member and one worded reason in both languages each).
- **Picture operations (all one history step, one `BlockChange`, attributed).** Insert (`InsertPicture`; it does not replace a selection, see the limits), `ResizePicture` (only `wp:extent` and `a:ext` change, so a crop, the effect extent and unknown children stay byte for byte; a rotated picture refuses), `ResetPictureSize` (pixels at 96 DPI, a cropped picture shows its crop at that scale; bytes through the `PictureBytes` provider or the store), `SetAltText`, `SetPictureWrap` (inline to floating writes Word's defaults: `distT` and `distB` 0, `distL` and `distR` 114300, `simplePos` 0, `relativeHeight` above every drawing, `behindDoc` per wrap, `layoutInCell` 1, `allowOverlap` 1, column and paragraph offsets 0; floating back to inline restores the inline form (`DeepEquals` with the original); between floating wraps only the wrap element and `behindDoc` change; tight and through are read as square and writing square replaces them; refused in a table cell), `MovePicture` (the run goes to the **start** of the target paragraph, the offsets are written as given and clamped, an alignment keyword on an axis is replaced by the offset; refused for an inline picture, a cell, another list of blocks (`AnotherPlace`) and 20,000 blocks or more (`TooFar`)), `NudgePicture` (offsets by points × 12700; consecutive nudges of one picture less than a second apart are one step, a sliding window, injected `TimeProvider`; refused for an alignment keyword on a moved axis), `DeletePicture` and `SetPictureProperties` (the card's one OK: wrap, then size, then description, one step, a refusing part refuses the whole call, a part already at its value is skipped). `PictureView.Of` reads everything from the XML on each call (no second storage). The layout reads back exactly what the editor writes (`FloatRoundTripTests`).
- **Picture commands, cards and gestures.** Five commands (`picture.insert`, `picture.properties`, `picture.wrap` with the five wrap payloads, `picture.reset`, `picture.delete`; Insert tab group Picture, digit 2; no keys). Insert: the system picker (PNG, JPEG, GIF, BMP; TIFF is not offered, see "Final review fixes" below), the file length checked against 50 MiB before any read, a bounded read on a pool thread (an error says "could not be read" and never the name or the system message), then `PictureInsertCard` (one optional description; Cancel aborts the whole insert), then insert at the end of the text selection. `PicturePropertiesCard` (width and height in cm or inch by region, proportions kept by default, back to the original size, description, wrap; only changed parts come back). Handles resize (a corner keeps the ratio by the side that moved more, Shift frees it, each side 8.5 pt to 1584 pt; the commit and the dashed outline use the same function, `PictureGestures.Resize`); a floating picture drags (no-drop over a table cell, a header, footer, note or the desk; the target paragraph's first line is tinted; the offsets come from the pointer minus the grab offset against the origin each axis is measured from, `PictureGestures.MoveOffsets`; a drop on another page keeps the line's top; the commit re-selects the moved picture in the same pass) and Left, Right, Up and Down nudge it 1 pt (Shift 10 pt). Avalonia has no diagonal size cursors, so corners use the corner cursors, edges the size cursors and a floating picture `SizeAll`.
- **Paste a picture (owner's addition).** Precedence: Write's own fragment, then text, then an image (**a clipboard that holds both an image and text, as a spreadsheet cell range does, keeps pasting the text**; the order differs from the spec's wording "fragment, image, text" and equals its stated intent). `AvaloniaEditClipboard.ReadImageAsync` takes `image/png` as a platform format first (bytes untouched), else the system bitmap (`DataFormat.Bitmap`) converted to PNG by Skia on a pool thread after a pixel check; DPI metadata is never read. `TextEditor.PastePicture(selection, bytes)` is ONE step: the media is added, the selection is deleted as a text paste would (a note whose reference goes is removed), the picture is placed where it was and the caret is after it; a collapsed selection is `InsertPicture`. A refusal (header, footer, note, comment, a control, bad bytes, a bitmap over 32 Mpx, a conversion that failed) pastes nothing, shows the reason and never deletes the selection (a zero-byte image counts as an image). A copied fragment never carries a picture: `FragmentCleaner.Keep` drops every picture and drawing, so a pasted fragment never holds a dangling `r:embed` (`ClipboardTests.FragmentWithTableAndPictureAndFieldPastesAsText`); a picture copied inside Write therefore does not paste as a picture, a deviation from the spec's "a copy of a picture inside Write goes through Write's own fragment" (a backlog item).
- **During a save and prefix documents.** A prefix document refuses every command of the milestone. **Table, picture and note commands and keys are disabled while a save runs** (with a reason), and a pointer press that would start a gesture only selects: a deviation from the spec's "queued and replayed by the save queue" (the spec's own 4.4a precedent disabled the structure commands and queued only the drop); a paste during a save is queued as before.
- **Final review fixes (2026-11-25, the whole-branch review of 4.5a).** (1) **TIFF is dropped** from the picture types (`ImageHeaderProbe`, `PictureRules.CheckBytes`, `MediaStore.ExtensionOf`, the picker filter, the wording): SkiaSharp 3.119.4 cannot decode a TIFF, so an inserted one was an unselectable grey placeholder; the spec's own rule "whatever Skia decodes and Word reads" excludes it (the plan's constraint that listed it was wrong). A TIFF already in a document stays untouched and is drawn as before (a placeholder); `InsertedPictureFormatTests` proves that PNG, JPEG, GIF and BMP each get a picture box after an insert. (2) **A picture is selectable without a pointer.** `TextEditor.PictureOfSelection(range)` names the picture the picture commands act on when no object is selected: the one character of an inline picture that a text selection covers exactly (Shift and an arrow next to it, either direction), else the only floating picture anchored in the paragraph of the selection (a floating picture has no character in the page view's text, so no range can cover it; two floating pictures in one paragraph are ambiguous and name none); `EditSession.TargetPicture()` is that or the pointer's `PictureSelection`, and `PictureFactsSource`, `PageView.PictureOperation` and `PicturePropertiesAtSelection` read it, so Picture properties (alt text, size, wrap), Text wrapping, Reset size and Delete picture work from the keyboard, through the ribbon, the command search or the menu; the reason "Select a picture first" says how. Nudging with the arrow keys and the eight handles stay pointer features. (3) **Selecting a picture puts the caret right behind it** (`TextEditor.PositionAfterPicture`, set by `EditSession.SetObjectSelection`; the press, the end of a drag and the measurement runs all go through it), so typing, Enter and a paste land next to the picture and not at an old place that may be pages away and not drawn; typing does not replace the picture (a Word-like replace would be a compound operation, recorded as a limit).
