(4) **A copy of a picture is no longer silent.** The clipboard of this editor still carries a picture in its fragment and a paste flattens it away (`FragmentCleaner.ForPaste` counts what it leaves out: pictures, old-style `w:pict`, objects and alternate content; `TextEditor.LastPasteDroppedPictures`): the paste now shows the note "Pictures and drawings in the pasted content were left out" (also when nothing else was left and the paste changed nothing), and Ctrl+C or Ctrl+X with a picture selected show "A picture cannot be copied or cut in this version" instead of doing nothing (the ribbon and menu Copy and Cut stay disabled with the reason "no selection") (`PageView.TakesClipboardKeyOfSelectedPicture` lets the key past the rules that disable copy without a text selection). The comment in `TryPressObject` that said an inline picture "moves by cut and paste" was false and is corrected: an inline picture has no move in this milestone. (5) **A note leaves the part only when no reference of the whole document names it** (`NoteOperations.DocumentReferenceKeys`: body, headers and footers, by kind and id): a foreign file with two references to one id lost the note and kept a dangling reference (a Word repair prompt) when one was deleted. (6) `NoteOperations.NextId` returns null when the highest id is `int.MaxValue` (a hostile file) and `InsertNote` returns null before anything changes (`TextEditor.NoteIdsUsedUp`; the view says why: "highest possible note number"). (7) `ReasonPictureTooMuchMedia` says that saving and opening the document again frees room (the 256 MiB counts pictures added since the file was opened, saved or not). (8) A second table in one block content control would have left both without a `TableRef` (every command refused); the insert is refused instead (`TableRefusal.ControlHoldsATable`, wording in both languages), so it is no longer a limit. (9) **Refusal checks on a big table, measured** (`TableFactsCostTests`, a table of 500 rows by 3 columns, the sixteen checks that `TableFactsSource` asks for the Table tab at a caret in the middle, Windows PC, JIT, median of 15 rounds after 3 warm-ups): **about 42 ms per refresh of the Table tab, 2.6 ms per check** (one clone of the table, 2.7 ms, and two grid maps, 0.6 ms each, per check; the clone is 70 percent of it). The refresh runs at a typing pause (100 ms debounce) and only while the Table tab is shown; it is above the 4 ms the review set for a cheap change, and the change is not small (a clone of the structure without cell content is safe because no refusal depends on content, but it touches three Model classes and needs a proof that the refusals stay equal; a fingerprint of the structure that skips unchanged tables needs an invalidation rule), so the number is recorded and the cost stays: the follow-up is "Write: cheaper refusal checks on a large table".
- **Proof.** `ObjectOracleTests` with `ObjectSteps` (every note, table and picture step kind, forced order over every corpus file plus generated documents with cropped, anchored, turned and tabled pictures; each step ends in `LayoutOracle.AssertEqualToFull`, now also comparing the object map, and checks drawn note numbers against the references in document order; the kinds-ran guards fail when a kind never changed anything), `SnapshotCacheOracleTests` (every raise site: the cache's bytes equal a full write after the operation, every undo and every redo; the store's bytes unchanged through them), `MediaPartTests`, `MediaPruningTests`, `NoteEditingTests`, `NotesPartCreationTests`, `TableStructureTests`, `TableCorpusStructureTests`, `TableFormattingTests`, `TableFormattingConsistencyTests`, `TablePropertiesTests`, `PictureEditingTests`, `ImagePasteTests`, `FloatRoundTripTests`, `ObjectMapTests`, and in the app `ObjectSelectionTests`, `ObjectDragTests`, `TableGestureTests`, `PictureGestureTests`, `PageViewNoteTests`, `PageViewPasteTests`, `NoteCommandTests`, `TableCommandTests`, `PictureCommandTests`, `StartupCreatesNoObjectWork`. **Every new raise site needs an attribution (`result.Attribute(document)`) and cases in both oracles.**
- **Measuring.** App switches `--measure-objects` (ten cases), `--measure-gesture` (six drag kinds, each timed sample one real `PointerMoved` routed event, the judged time handler plus overlay render pass plus page draws; `PageView.OverlayProbe` lets the probe see overlay-only frames) and `--measure-typing-objects` (typing at the end of a picture in a paragraph and in a table cell); `write-layout-bench edit` prints the ten engine rows (a picture the bench inserts has no bytes in the package the bench layout reads, so its layout draws a placeholder; first run medians 0.6 to 11 ms); the `objects`, `gesture` and `typing-objects` cases of `measure.cs` (`selftest` 813 of 813) and three steps of `measure.yml`. Limits and numbers: "Raffica Write: the objects limit (M4.5a)". Every run is undone outside the timing and a run fails ("a measured command could not be undone") when an undo does nothing; the tests compare a fingerprint of the thesis before and after.
- **Start cost.** Nothing of tables, pictures, notes, the new pickers, the grid picker or the object map is built before first use (`StartupCreatesNoObjectWork`); the Home band's width hint is unchanged. `--measure-startup` against the commit before 4.5a (4b00f9b, Native AOT publishes, three interleaved rounds of ten runs, Windows PC): cold 236.5 against 238.2 ms (+0.7 percent), warm 245.3 against 243.1 ms (-0.9 percent): no regression beyond noise (round spread 3 to 5 percent); the published exe grows from 58.32 to 59.31 MB. The Native AOT publish logs hold no `IL[23][0-9]{3}` hit.
- **A real-display check found what the headless tests could not (2026-11-25).** The Insert table grid never appeared: the window hands the ribbon's tab row and band to the frame (`Frame.TabRow = Ribbon.TabRow`, `Frame.Band = Ribbon.Band`), so the `RibbonView` itself is in no visual tree, and a `Flyout` anchored to it was "open" (`IsOpen` true) with no top level, no size and nothing drawn. It now anchors to the Insert button in `Ribbon.Band`, or to the band itself when another tab is shown (command search, menu); `TheGridPickerIsOnScreenUnderTheInsertButton` and `TheGridPickerIsOnScreenWhenTheInsertBandIsNotShown` assert `TopLevel.GetTopLevel`, `IsLoaded` and a non-empty `Bounds` (a test that asserts only that a popup control was built, or `IsOpen`, proves nothing about it being on screen). PR 136.
**Word check (Windows PC, Word 16.0 build 16.0.20625, Microsoft 365, 2026-11-25, read through Word automation, not by a person looking; rows in `Docs/write-word-check.md`).** All 21 files of `WordCheckObjectFilesTests` (3 note files, 7 table files, 11 picture files, each with a facts file of what the model holds) open with no repair prompt (`tools/word-open-check.ps1`, exit 0), and `tools/word-object-counts.ps1` finds **0 differences**: Word's table count, row, cell and column counts, footnote and endnote counts, inline and floating picture counts and, per picture, the wrap kind (square, top and bottom, in front, behind, inline), the size in points (within 0.6 pt) and the description equal the model's. A person's look at the rendered pages is not done. A finding about Word itself: with a hidden document window (`Documents.Open(..., Visible:=False)`, as `word-open-check.ps1` opens) Word returns an empty `AlternativeText` for every picture; the new script opens the window visible. Not covered by a file: a cropped picture the user made (Write has no crop command; the file adds `a:srcRect` to a drawing and checks resize and reset keep it), pictures in headers or footers, a rotated picture, and a picture pasted from a real clipboard.
**Rationale:** The object map, the object selection and the overlay (approach A of the spec) fit the rules of the code base: immutable pages, no visual touched in a render pass, one history step per operation, an oracle that compares the incremental layout with a full one. Operating on a clone and replacing the block makes a table operation atomic and keeps the position ints honest; computing media use from references at save time means undo, redo and pruning need no coordination with the package. Putting the dxa widths of a first write from the drawn columns means a table never changes its look when it is first edited.
**Rejected alternatives:** the spec's approaches B (the model changes live during a drag: every pointer move would be an edit and a layout pass, and multiplies the states the oracle sees) and C (handles as real controls: a table grid is dozens of moving parts to reposition after every layout change). Media through the Open XML SDK (it names the part outside `/word`). Calling `MediaStore.Remove` on undo (a redo would find no bytes; a hand-made reference count would be a second storage). Sharing one part between two pictures with the same bytes (a `Remove` would dangle the other; used-ness from references makes it unnecessary). A `NoteKind` parameter (taken). Hit testing a picture through its id per candidate (O(paragraphs) per box): `HitObject` resolves the selection once and compares `Picture` objects. Queueing every command during a save (see above).
**Known limits** (each deliberate or deferred):
- *Pictures exist only in body blocks.* `EditHistory.PruneMedia` scans `Old`, `New` and `MediaAdded` of the steps and the whole current model, **not `NotesAdded`, `NotesRemoved` or section carriers**: a milestone that allows pictures in notes, headers, footers or comments must extend the scan, give each part its own media store and relationships, and extend `MediaPartWriter.Used`. Today a picture in a header is a marked placeholder in the layout and no command touches it.
- *Clipboard.* `DataFormat.CreateBytesPlatformFormat("image/png")` may not match the native PNG format names on Windows or macOS (the fallback system bitmap to PNG is tested headless only): the real clipboard of each platform (Snipping Tool, a browser's "copy image", an Office paste) is an owner gate: **Windows is checked** (2026-11-25, Native AOT build, the real Windows clipboard: a Windows bitmap and the registered PNG format each pasted as a picture at its pixel size), the Linux laptop (Wayland and X11) and the Intel Mac were checked by the owner afterwards (2026-11-25, reported fine and working). The size cap cannot stop the read of a clipboard bitmap (Avalonia hands the bytes over), only the check after it. A copied fragment drops pictures and a paste says so (see "Final review fixes"; the editor's own picture cannot be copied inside Write yet); an image file copied in a file manager and a dropped file are not pasted (backlog).
- *Media.* At most 50 MiB per picture and 256 MiB of pending media; entry bytes stay in memory for the life of the document (every save rewrites from the opened snapshot); the used-scan costs one XML pass per save with pictures; the cap protects against pictures nothing refers to, not against a long history that holds many (the step limits bound it); adding media rewrites `[Content_Types].xml` (it moves to the end of the zip and loses `standalone="yes"`: Word opens it); `wp14:sizeRelH` and `sizeRelV` are ignored; `tcW` that disagrees with the grid: the grid wins; table-style cell margins are not read.
- *Picture insert and move.* Insert with a text selection keeps the selected text (Word replaces it) and does not select the new picture (a compound operation would be needed); a move below the old place lands about 23 pt off because the offsets are computed on the pre-move layout; the resize outline of a drag uses view-space deltas (wrong only if the wheel scrolls mid-drag); a drop needs the target page's first line on the pointer's page; nudge coalescing is a sliding one-second window; the anchor character is not preserved by a move (the run goes to the paragraph start); the picture card's linked size field round-trips display text (cm and inch to two decimals), so an edit-and-revert of a non-round size can write a one-twip change; a picture in a cell cannot float; a picture pasted into a restricted control is refused.
- *Tables.* Irregular tables refuse every restructure (above); a table with more than one table in a block content control has no `TableRef`; nested tables are never edited by a command or gesture and a press in one starts nothing; the refusal checks clone the table (a 500-row table with the Table tab shown pays about 42 ms per refresh at a typing pause, measured, see "Final review fixes"); a second table is refused inside a block content control that already holds one; a drag that leaves the cell and returns shows a one-cell selection (Word reverts to text); no auto-scroll during a live range drag or a row drag; only pages in view are targets; the right edge is limited by the page edge, not the text area; a formatting operation that changes nothing still records a step; the vertical-merge bottom border comes from the head cell (pre-existing); "Delete" on a cell range shows a note; width and table-properties commands are refused in the writing view; Tab in a list item is the list rule.
- *Notes.* Insert and jump into an endnote on a long, not yet laid-out document does not draw the caret until the layout reaches the endnote; `NoteLinks.FindReference` walks every body paragraph per Esc or Back (O(document)); the plan cache and the oracle compare drawn note numbers by count for formats other than decimal and Roman; `InsertNote` mutates styles and the part before the reference is placed (reorder if touched again); a redo of an insert puts the caret in the body; undo or redo of a text step inside a note in the writing view can put the caret in a note; the oracle checks the main part's bytes only for the snapshot cache (the notes part is not byte-checked); undoing a first note or picture leaves the created styles and the notes part in the package.
- *Selection and keys.* The IME with an object selection is not cleared until the commit; `CollapseTextSelection` raises `SelectionChanged` before the object selection is committed; `ObjectAnnouncement.Columns` counts cells per row; no macOS key for any of the 28 new commands (3 note, 20 table, 5 picture) (menu, ribbon and command search only); the object selection is announced to assistive technology as text, a real screen reader is untried.
- *Tests.* The pointer-move timing tests of `TableGestureTests` are wall-clock p95 bounds (best of three attempts after a full warm-up pass, bound unchanged) and `ImageCacheBudgetTests.DisposeStopsPrefetchResults` and `PrefetchRaisesDecodedOnce` are timing tests of 4.3c's prefetch that failed once or twice in a loaded full run and pass alone: known load flakes. `StructureSteps.AssertReadable` allowances stay per file id.
- *After the final review (2026-11-25).* A picture deleted from a file that already held it keeps its media part and its relationship in later saves (the 4.1 rule that every other part stays byte-identical): the bytes stay in the file, which is a privacy note for someone who deletes a picture to remove it (only a picture added since the file was opened, and then deleted, leaves no part). Recovery copies write every pending picture again on each autosave and compare it, so a document with many new pictures costs time and memory every 30 seconds (unmeasured with pictures present). Selecting a picture and typing puts the text next to it and does not replace it (Word replaces it); nudging and the handles need a picture selected with the pointer (the keyboard route resolves the picture for the commands and the properties card only); a picture still cannot be copied or cut inside Write (a paste says so; the clipboard still takes a picture that arrives from outside); a floating picture is named by any selection in its paragraph, so Delete picture and Text wrapping act on the only floating picture of the paragraph the caret is in.
- *Keyboard selection of pictures (final review, 2026-11-25).* A paragraph with two or more floating pictures cannot have one selected by keyboard: the target is null and only the pointer selects them; the hint "the caret in its paragraph is enough" in `ReasonPictureNoSelection` is true for one floating picture only. A real Shift+Right or Shift+Left over a picture character is unproven: the tests set the text selection directly.
**Follow-ups:** bookmarks, captions, cross-references and tables of contents (4.5b); hyperlinks and find and replace (4.5c); pictures in headers, footers and notes with a per-part store; cropping and rotation; the Linux laptop and Intel Mac measurements of the objects limit; macOS keys for the new commands.
**Owner gates (milestone 4.5.1 stays `in-progress`):** (1) **Word, done by script on 2026-11-25** (above); open: a person's look at the rendered files. Commands: `RAFFICA_WORD_CHECK_DIR=<folder> dotnet test tests/Raffica.Write.Tests -c Release --filter-class "*WordCheckObjectFilesTests"`, then `pwsh tools/word-open-check.ps1 <files>` and `pwsh tools/word-object-counts.ps1 <file>` (one file per call). (2) **A look at the Native AOT app on a real display** on Windows, the Linux laptop and the Intel Mac: handles, the drag outline, guides and the drop line, the cursors, the grid picker, the cards (only headless captures exist). (3) **`measure.yml`** target `write` with the cases `objects`, `gesture` and `typing-objects` on the Linux laptop (mains power) and the Intel Mac: `gh workflow run measure.yml --ref dev -f platform=<linux|macos> -f target=write -f note="<machine>"` (each check step green; the limits are in "Raffica Write: the objects limit (M4.5a)"). (4) **The real clipboard with an image on each platform**: a screenshot (Snipping Tool, macOS screenshot, a Linux screenshot tool) and an image copied from a browser and from an Office program pasted into Write on Windows, the Linux laptop (Wayland and X11 where available) and the Intel Mac, recorded. The IME check is not part of 4.5a.

## 2026-11-24 – Raffica Write: the sections limit (M4.4b)
**Context:** The six section commands of `--measure-sections` were recorded and not judged until the owner's runs on the Linux laptop and the Intel Mac gave numbers ("Raffica Write: sections, page numbers and page setup (M4.4b)", owner gates). The runs are in: `measure.yml` target `write` on `dev` at ac68eb1 (the merged 4.4b), every step green: the Linux laptop **on mains power** (run 45432429309: `machine.txt` says mains 1, governor powersave with the balance_performance preference, 3725 of at most 3900 MHz, 63 °C at the start) and the Intel Mac (run 45432435118); the Windows PC numbers are the three launches of 2026-11-23 (decision above). Four commands miss the 100 ms aim of the milestone on all three machines, because `DocumentLayout.Resection` starts a fresh engine and lays out from page 1 through the caret or view page.
**Decision:**
- **The limit is a regression guard, not the aim: the metric `sections` is the worst median of the six rows, at most 600 ms (budget 480 × 1.25, `CheckCommand.SectionsBudget`), entered for every platform in `s_writeBudgets`.** It is a fixed limit like `typing`, `format`, `structure` and the Linux `engine`: it does not make a platform one "with an app row" (`JudgeWrite`). The judged value is CPU cost (key handler, render pass, page draws; the typing definition); the frame latency, the full layout (`sections-full`) and the refresh are recorded and never judged. A miss is a finding, never a loosened number.
- **How 600 was chosen:** the slowest machine's worst median (the Intel Mac, appendix prefix, 456 ms) times 1.25 is 570, rounded up to 600, the rule of the structure and 4.2 limits, so ordinary variation between days (30 percent on a laptop) does not fail a green build while a command that gets about 30 percent slower than today does. One number for every platform, as for `structure`: the Linux laptop has the most headroom (326 against 600, so a regression of up to about 80 percent there is not seen); per-platform limits (about 415, 520 and 570) would be tighter but give three numbers to maintain for a limit that is meant to go away.
- **The aim stays 100 ms.** This entry does not accept 200 to 450 ms as the goal. The incremental resection (a `Resection` that keeps the pages before the changed section and the filler state, as `ApplyEdit` does for a block) is the open follow-up; its entry cuts the limit to the aim and says so, and the roadmap criterion was reworded on 2026-11-24 to what is proven (a fixed limit set from the measured medians and met); milestone 4.4.2 is closed with the aim in the backlog (scope change of 2026-11-24).
**Numbers (worst median per command, medians of three launches of 12 runs each, ms; the Windows column is the first measurement of 2026-11-23):**

| Command | Windows PC | Intel Mac | Linux laptop (mains) |
|---|---|---|---|
| number pages from page 3 | 265 | 273, 283, 286 | 193, 206, 205 |
| appendix prefix (the worst) | 415 | 451, 456, 456 | 330, 326, 308 |
| remove marker | 4.5 | 5.3, 5.5, 5.5 | 3.5, 2.7, 2.7 |
| page setup, whole document | 225 | 249, 261, 250 | 175, 180, 176 |
| page setup, one section | 220 | 263, 242, 245 | 180, 169, 187 |
| insert page number | 24 | 24, 26, 24 | 27, 24, 20 |

The metric as `check` reads it: **456.44 ms on the Intel Mac and 325.67 ms on the Linux laptop** against 600 (ok; the `check` step of both runs and a re-run of `check --target write` on the downloaded CSVs agree). The full layout after a command is 387 to 411 ms on the Linux laptop and 540 to 597 ms on the Intel Mac (`sections-full`). One launch on the Intel Mac had a p95 of 697 ms and a maximum of 1300 ms for number pages from page 3 (its median 283 ms, and the other two launches were clean): a single noisy launch, which the median does not see. The judged typing worst p95 with the Sections pane open among the rows is 13.15 ms on the Linux laptop and 7.35 ms on the Intel Mac against 16 (the format limit 12.66 and 5.99 ms, structure 4.45 and 7.31 ms): the pane adds no typing cost that matters.
**Rejected alternatives:** leaving the limit open until the incremental resection exists (nothing would catch a regression of the commands that are slow today, and the follow-up has no date); the aim of 100 ms as the limit now (every machine would fail, so the check would be red until a follow-up lands, or the limit would be loosened later, which the rule forbids); limits per platform (above); judging the full layout after the command (it is 390 to 600 ms for a reason that is not the command, and the user sees the first pages, not the last).

## 2026-11-23 – Raffica Write: sections, page numbers and page setup (M4.4b)
**Context:** The second half of milestone 4.4 ("Structure", split by the owner on 2026-11-21; 4.4a is "Raffica Write: headings, lists and the outline (M4.4a)"). In Word, "page numbers that start at 1 on page 3" take five steps (a section break, unlink the footer, format the numbers, start at 1, delete the numbers before). 4.4b does it in one action, shows the section it made, lets a person take it back with one click, and adds headers and footers per section (own or same as previous, different first page, left and right pages), Insert page number and page setup (size, margins, orientation, columns) for this section, the whole document or from here on. The outline also moves a chapter that holds a section break. Spec: `Docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-23-raffica-write-4-4b-sections-page-numbers-and-page-setup-design.md`, plan: `Docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-23-milestone-04-4b-sections-page-numbers-and-page-setup.md`.
**Decision:**
- **The model is the document's own sections, no second storage.** `SectionList.Of(document)` lists the body's sections (each `w:sectPr` on a paragraph's `w:pPr` and the body's final one) with the break paragraph, the first and last block and, per header or footer kind and type, whether the section has its own reference or inherits (`SectionList.Resolve`, the OOXML rule: a missing reference inherits the previous section's of the same kind and type, the first section none). `SectionPageSetup` reads and writes size, orientation, margins and columns as typed twips; a missing element reads the layout's defaults. **The first-page rule is resolved as Word's documented one:** a section with `w:titlePg` and no first-page reference of its own uses the previous section's first-page reference; `HeaderFooterPlan.Resolve` in the layout already did this and equals `SectionList.Resolve` over every page of every corpus file (Task 2 checked it, the layout needed no change).
- **One command makes the section it needs (`NumberPagesFrom`, `InsertSectionBreak`, `SectionSplitter`).** The earlier half gets a clone of the old section's properties (so its pages keep their headers, footers and numbering); the later half keeps the existing end properties and gets `w:type` next page, `w:pgNumType` (`w:start` omitted for "continue", `w:fmt` for the format) and **no `w:titlePg`** (a new section never inherits "different first page" from the half it came from, which a card says). The carrier of the break is the previous plain paragraph or a new empty one. A block that already starts its section makes no split (that would be an empty section) and edits that section alone. Every footer type the section shows (the default one, the first-page one while `titlePg` stays on, the even one with left and right pages) shows the page number: a type that already resolves to a footer with a `PAGE` field is left alone, any other gets an own footer (a copy of what it resolved to plus a centred paragraph with the field); a prefix ("A-") is footer text before the field, not a section property. "From here" reaches the end of the document: a later section that sets nothing of its own follows, one with its own footer or `w:pgNumType` keeps it; the card names how many sections follow. The number field is a complex field (begin, instruction, separate, result, end), so the layout and Word both evaluate it.
- **Removing the marker merges.** Section k merges into k-1: the paragraph that carried k-1's break loses its `w:sectPr`, and k's end properties keep their object and take a clone of the whole content of k-1's (attributes, every child, unknown ones, references, `w:type`, `w:pgNumType`, `w:titlePg`); a child k-1 does not have is absent afterwards. One undo step; header and footer parts the removed setup used stay in the package unused. The pane and the chip popover name what changes when the two setups differ.
- **A section change is a history step of its own (`SectionChange`, `SectionSwap`, `EditResult.Sections`).** A step swaps live `SectionProperties` objects (old and new clone, content copied in place so every holder of the object sees it) together with the carrier paragraph, created or removed header and footer parts and the settings flag, and `EditHistory.Swap` pairs the live objects again through block undo and redo. The session answers an `EditResult` with `Sections` with `DocumentLayout.Resection(throughPage)` **instead of** `ApplyEdit`; `EditResult.Attribute(document)` is the one call every raise site makes (block changes and carriers to the snapshot tracker; carrier changes never go into `EditResult.Changes`, because more than one change makes `Record` skip the step). The invariant "a sections change implies `EditResult.Sections`" backs `EditSession.SectionEpoch`, which the status line and the chip memos key on.
- **Headers and footers.** "Own" creates a new part (new relationship id, a `w:hdr` or `w:ftr` root, a copy of the inherited content so nothing disappears); "same as previous" removes the reference and keeps the part in the package. A part made by copy takes the source's relationships (`HeaderFooterPart.RelationshipsFrom`, copied by `DocxFile`; the layout resolves pictures by `PictureOwner`); the save guard relaxes for those created relationships and does **not** compare relationship types. **Different first page** is per section and gives the section an empty own first-page part only where none resolves; **different left and right pages** is one document switch (`w:evenAndOddHeaders`, the settings part is made when the file has none and stays after an undo). `Insert page number` (Layout, group Header and footer) takes the type that applies at the caret's page and writes the field into the part that type resolves to; a shared part says in the status line which sections share it ("Footer, section 2, shared with sections 3 to 5"). `NUMPAGES` is the physical total as in Word; the picker marks the "of N" looks with the hint "N counts every page" when the numbers are not 1 to N.
- **Page setup (`ApplyPageSetup`, `PageSetupChange(Value, Parts)`, `ApplyScope`).** Only the changed parts are written. This section: the caret's section. Whole document: every section including the final one. **From here on** splits at the caret (next page, continuous for columns alone; none when the caret's section already has the values) and writes the changed part into the new section and every later section, as "this point forward" does in Word; **this overrides the "later section with its own size keeps it" rule of number-pages** (spec conflict, ruled for the settled section). An orientation change turns each section's own page (width and height swapped). Header and footer distances, the gutter and every unknown attribute are never written. Columns are always written equal (a `w:col` list is replaced), so the card warns when a target section has unequal columns and offers no spacing-only or line-only change on them. Wide margins are 5.08 cm (2880 twips, Word's value). Landscape is read as `w:orient` says landscape or no attribute and width above height. Units are cm or inch by region (`PagePresets.UsesInches`: US and CA, Letter and 1 inch, as `BlankPaper`).
- **The marker is a chip.** One per section after the first, in the margin or on the desk beside the page where the section starts, saying "Section 2" and "numbers start at 3" or "continues"; its popover has the same controls as the pane row and Remove marker. **Deviation from the spec's "drawn by the page view in a render pass": the chips are real `Button` controls in an overlay layer (`SectionChipLayer`)**, because a drawn chip has no focus, automation name or hit testing and a render pass must touch no visual. They are never part of a `LaidOutPage`, so print and PDF export never get them. They are read only when `DocumentLayout.SectionCount` is above 1 (a one-section document makes no layer, marker or timer) and lag an edit by up to 100 ms. They are not tab stops: the keyboard route is the Sections pane, the section commands and Remove section.
- **The Sections pane** (View, `view.sections`, right slot, `IRightSlotPane` beside the formatting and outline panes) has one row per section (start page, numbering, "A4, Portrait, 2 columns", own header and footer, different first page, Number from here, Remove marker) and the document row for left and right pages. Rows are compared by value and kept while equal, every control acts on a fresh read by index and identity (a stale row is refused), the disabled state shows its reason as a tooltip, nothing is read during a save, and more than 200 sections shows a notice.
- **The outline moves a chapter that holds a section break.** `OutlineTree.CheckDrop` returns the target and a reason (4.4a refused such a subtree): a move that would leave an empty final section (`MoveBlock.LeavesAnEmptyFinalSection`, including moving the final chapter above one that ends in a break) is refused with its reason, the table-last rule stays, and a row that holds a break starts a drag.
- **Never-wrong numbers are proved against an independent count.** `ReferencePageNumbers` counts the number and format of every page from the sections alone (start, format, continue, no layout, no `FieldContext`), `DrawnPageNumbers.Read(layout, document)` reads what the pages drew, and `SectionOracleTests` with `SectionSteps` run every section command (number from here with and without a prefix, breaks of each type, removal, own or same, first page, left and right, page numbers, page setup in each scope) with its undo and redo over the corpus and generated documents; after each step the layout equals a full layout exactly (`LayoutOracle.AssertEqualToFull`, which now also compares `LaidOutPage.ChromeType` and `NumberValue`) and the drawn numbers equal the reference. The saved file read again agrees. **Rules settled by the Word check of 2026-11-23** (below): a continuous section that carries a `w:start` begins the page it is first at the top of with that start, and when it begins in the middle of a page that page keeps its number and the next page is start + 1 (the layout, `ReferencePageNumbers` and the generator follow Word since the fix commit); a next-page break with a restart is not turned into an odd-page break. **Still unsettled and mirrored by the layout:** the number format of the blank page before an even-page or odd-page section, and a wrong-parity start (an even-page section that starts at 1), where Word differs from Write (below); the oracle skips those pages (counted, under 10 percent of the compared pages) and the generator avoids them.
- **Measuring.** The app switch `--measure-sections` (cases `number-from-page-3`, `appendix-prefix`, `remove-marker`, `page-setup-whole`, `page-setup-section`, `insert-page-number`, each undone outside the timing, plus `sections-full`, the full layout after a command, and refresh rows), `write-layout-bench edit` (the section rows), and `--measure-typing-sections-pane` (typing with the Sections pane open; counts into the judged `typing` metric, 16.0 ms); the `sections` case of `measure.cs` records the worst median of the six rows and **has no limit** yet (it prints "not judged" and never fails; a limit comes from the owner's runs, entry to follow, as for 4.4a). Windows PC, quiet, Native AOT, the thesis, three launches of 20 runs each (the default count is now 12 per case, see below; CPU cost: handler, render pass and draws):

| What (median ms, Windows PC) | Result | Aim |
| --- | --- | --- |
| number from page 3 | about 265 | 100 |
| appendix with a prefix ("A-1") | about 415 | 100 |
| page setup, whole document | about 225 | 100 |
| page setup, this section | about 220 | 100 |
| remove marker | about 4.5 | 100 |
| insert page number | about 24 | 100 |
| full layout after a command (recorded) | 495 to 535 (insert page number 64 to 79) | not judged |
| Sections pane rows, refresh | under 1 | recorded |

**Four of the six commands miss the 100 ms aim on the thesis.** The cause is in the code: a section result calls `DocumentLayout.Resection(throughPage)`, which starts a fresh engine and lays out from page 1 through the caret or view page (the run puts the caret mid-document, about page 150), because a split or a changed page setup changes the geometry of every later page. It is a recorded fact, not a defect of 4.4b (the spec says measure, then set); **the follow-up is an incremental resection** (keep the pages before the first changed section and relay out from its first block, with the same exact-equality oracle). A command with the caret near the top of a long document costs correspondingly less. Remove marker and insert page number do not restart the whole document. A launch with 20 runs per case took 100 to 150 s on the Windows PC and the Intel Mac's full layout is about 1.5 times slower, which risks the 240 s app watchdog, so the default is `SectionsRuns` = 12 (3 launches of 12 = 36 samples per row) and the measure job of `measure.yml` has a 90 minute timeout (it was 60; the Intel Mac runs already take 45 to 48 minutes before the two new steps). The start cost after the section commands (cold 243.6 to 249.0 ms, warm 247.7 to 244.1 ms on the AOT app, Task 11) showed no regression, and a document with one section reads no sections after the first page (`NothingIsComputedBeforeTheFirstSectionBreakIsKnown`).
**Word check (Windows PC, Word 16.0 build 16.0.20625.20108, 2026-11-23, read through Word automation, not a person looking; rows in `Docs/write-word-check.md`).** All 17 files of the first run (the 15 of `WordCheckSectionFilesTests` and `WordCheckFilesTests` with `lists.docx` and `numbered-headings.docx`; three experiment files were added after the findings) open with no repair prompt (`tools/word-open-check.ps1`, exit 0). `tools/word-page-numbers.ps1` (Word's `wdActiveEndAdjustedPageNumber` of the first paragraph on each page against the numbers Write's layout draws) agrees on every compared page for `page-three-one`, `restart-page-four`, `roman-front-matter`, `first-page`, `left-right`, `landscape-section`, `two-columns`, `continuous-start-other-paper` and, apart from the unreadable blank page 4, `blank-page-roman`; the thesis appendix is read by section (Word: section 3 starts on page 313 with restart, start 1 and the footer "A-" plus the page field, four pages long as in Write). **The "1 on page 3" and "A-1" acceptance of the spec is met in Word as intended; a person's look at the rendered page is not done.**
**Rules not yet seen in Word, now seen (the first, a continuous section with a start, is fixed in the layout; the others are recorded):**
- **A continuous section on the same paper with `w:start` (fixed, commit "a continuous section with a start restarts the page number as Word does").** `continuous-start-same-paper.docx` (a start of 5 in the middle of page 2): Word 1 2 6 7, Write had 1 2 3 4 because the layout and the oracle held that such a section never restarts. Word leaves the number of the page the section begins on (the first paragraph of the section reads page 2) and numbers the next page start + 1; `continuous-start-page-top.docx` (the section's first block at the top of page 3): Word 1 2 5 6, the page takes the start. `PageFiller` now sets the next number to start + 1 when the section's first block is placed on the page it joins and to the start when that block goes to the top of the next page; `DrawnPageNumbers` reads `StartedMidPage` and `ReferencePageNumbers` follows the same rule (cited to the Word-check rows); `SectionSteps` no longer skips or avoids a continuous start; `ReferencePageNumbersTests` has the two cases. Re-read in Word after the fix: 0 differences on both files and on `continuous-start-other-paper.docx`. Extended by reasoning, not by a Word file: a first block that goes to the next page by `w:pageBreakBefore` (the unit test) begins that page with the start like the page-top file.
- **An even-page section with a start of 1 and an odd-page section with a start of 2 (wrong parity, not fixed):** `even-page-start-one.docx` (the section at the top of page 2): Word has 6 pages numbered 1 2 3 4 5 6, Write has 7 pages numbered 1 1 2 3 4 5 6; `odd-page-start-two.docx` (at the top of page 3): Word 1 2 3 4 5 6 on 6 pages, Write 1 2 2 3 4 5 6 on 7. Word ignores a start of the parity the section type cannot have, continues the count and adds no blank page. Two experiments show the right parity is applied and Write agrees: `even-page-start-two.docx` (Word and Write 1 2 2 3 4 5) and `odd-page-start-one.docx` (1 2 1 2 3 4). The observation is a rule only for those positions (see `Docs/write-word-check.md`); the layout is **not** changed (owner ruling of 2026-11-23: record it, backlog item "Write: page numbering of even-page and odd-page sections like Word"). The oracle leaves out the pages of a wrong-parity start (counted) and the generator draws one in eight.
- **The number format of the blank page before an even-page or odd-page section is unread** (the script cannot read a page with no paragraph; the page count and the other numbers of `blank-page-roman.docx` agree).
- **A next-page restart is not turned into an odd-page break** (`restart-page-four.docx`: 1 2 3 1 2 3, no extra page): the oracle's rule holds.
- **A split after a paragraph that ends in a `w:br` page break: confirmed by Word.** `trailing-page-break-split.docx`: Word has **2 pages**, Write has **3** (an extra blank page). The known limit above stays; it is a layout-fidelity follow-up, not a numbering rule.
**Rationale:** The section is the document's own thing; making it with one command and showing it with a chip and a pane is the whole point, and putting every change through one history step keeps undo exact, whatever the command touched. Reading sections only when a second one can exist keeps the start free of them. The independent page-number count is the proof that no command leaves a wrong number, for the same reason as the 4.4a label count: it shares no code with the layout. Real buttons for the chips cost an overlay layer but give focus, automation and hit testing for free.
**Rejected alternatives:** The approaches of the spec's section "Approaches considered" (the owner chose C): **A, a Word clone** (separate dialogs, hidden breaks: the cheapest, and it repeats the pain the milestone exists to remove) and **B, a sidebar only** (Pages' Section tab: clear, but several clicks and no marker in the page; its pane became part of C). A chip drawn in a render pass (no focus or automation, and a render pass must touch no visual). A numbering prefix as a section property (there is none in OOXML; it is footer text). A separate `ApplyEdit` fast path for sections (the geometry of every later page can change, so only a full or an incremental resection is exact). Squeezing the page-setup "from here" into number-pages' "a later section keeps its own" rule (a person who says "from here landscape" means every later page). Suppressing CA1720 for `PageNumberFormat.Decimal` (the repository forbids suppressions: it is `Arabic`).
**Known limits** (each deliberate or deferred):
- *A split carried by a paragraph that ends in a `w:br` page break* shows one extra blank page (equal to a full layout of the same file; the layout draws the paragraph mark of a trailing page break on the next page, Word with compatibility 15 keeps it on the page of the break). A layout-fidelity follow-up, pinned by `AParagraphThatEndsInAPageBreakCarriesTheBreakLikeAnyOther`; the Word check has `trailing-page-break-split.docx`.
- *Even-page and odd-page sections with a wrong-parity start* (`even-page-start-one.docx`, `odd-page-start-two.docx`): Word ignores the start, adds no blank page and counts on (6 pages, 1 to 6); Write adds a blank page and restarts (7 pages). Not fixed on purpose; the oracle skips the affected pages (counted, under 10 percent) and the generator draws such a start one time in eight; backlog item. The number format of the blank page before such a section is unread.
- *Write's own UI can make the wrong-parity state:* insert an even-page break, then "Number pages from here" with a start of 1 on that section: Word ignores the start and adds no blank page, Write restarts with a blank page (7 pages against 6). A cheap guard, not built: a hint in `NumberPagesCard` when the target section is even-page or odd-page and the parity of the start does not fit.
- *The pane and the chip actions lay out through the default page.* They use `ApplyGuarded` with `ThroughPage()` (the later of the last page in view and the caret page), not the in-view variant of the outline moves of 4.4a, so acting on a section row while the caret is on a far page costs a `Resection` through that far page: slower, not wrong. It belongs with the incremental-resection follow-up.
- *The reference reads top-level breaks only.* `ReferencePageNumbers.Sections` reads the break paragraphs of the body's top level, while `SectionList.Of` also descends into block content controls (depth 32); they agree on the generated documents, and corpus files with sections inside content controls are not run through that oracle.
- *Not incremental:* `Resection` restarts the engine (the numbers above).
- *The default header and footer part only* is changed by the pane and the Layout commands; the first-page and even parts have the switches and Insert page number but no editing UI beyond entering the region.
- *Chips:* no keyboard route to a chip, an edit shows in a chip up to 100 ms late, a chip is absent when neither the margin nor the desk has room (very low zoom, narrow window; the pane is then the way), and the writing view has no chips.
- *Remove marker leaves the carrier paragraph* (an empty paragraph stays where the break was when Write made one); undo of a created final `w:sectPr` leaves an empty final section; parts and relationships a merge leaves unused stay in the package.
- *Columns write equalizes `w:col`;* a split turns off `titlePg` in the new half; a section whose later sections have unequal columns is only warned about, not blocked.
- *Number pages:* a PAGE field the prefix cannot reach (nested in another field's result) gives two numbers; a prefix typed inside a hyperlink becomes link text; the editor cannot see a prefix document (the app refuses every section command on one); `Step.KeepsBodySelection` means a body-caret page-number step has no caret of its own (the owner of the selection ignores `result.Caret`).
- *The Sections pane and the formatting pane duplicate their lifecycle* (shared only as `IRightSlotPane`); `RowItem.Previous` is not updated by page changes; the hidden-pane refresh test waits a fixed 300 ms.
- *Tests:* the section oracle adds about 2 minutes to the Write tests (the whole project ran 6 m 41 s at Task 9); a flake of `PageNumberTypeTests` (reading the page total before the background layout was complete) was fixed in the test, and the same class had passed 20 of 20 on the base commit.
**Follow-ups:** an incremental resection (the 100 ms aim), the `sections` limit is set (600 ms, "Raffica Write: the sections limit (M4.4b)"; it falls to the aim with the resection), the `w:br` trailing page break (layout fidelity), a keyboard route to the chips, the Insert tab's placement of Insert page number (4.5).
**Owner gates (milestone 4.4.2 stays `in-progress`):** (1) **Word, done by script on 2026-11-23** (above); open: a person's look at the rendered pages of `page-three-one.docx` and `appendix-a1.docx` ("1" on page 3 and "A-1"), the Roman and letter formats, the blank page format, and the wrong-parity start and trailing page-break findings above (follow-ups). (2) **A look at the Native AOT app on a real display** on Windows, the Linux laptop and the Intel Mac: the chips, the Sections pane, the popover and the cards (only headless captures exist). (3) **`measure.yml`** target `write` cases `sections` and `typing-sections-pane` on the Linux laptop (mains power) and the Intel Mac, done on 2026-11-24 (runs 45432429309 and 45432435118, all steps green) and the limit entry for `sections` is "Raffica Write: the sections limit (M4.4b)"; the command was: `gh workflow run measure.yml --ref <branch> -f platform=<linux|macos> -f target=write -f note="<machine>"` (12 runs per case by default; the measure job times out after 90 minutes). Open after that: the incremental resection if the 100 ms aim stays missed.


## 2026-11-22 – PDFium 160: the tile test allows one level of difference
**Context:** The Dependabot bump of `bblanchon.PDFium` from 157.0.8100 to 160.0.8165 (PR #90) failed `RenderTests.TilesStitchToTheFullPage` on Windows, Linux and macOS: 75 of 162,000 pixels differed, max delta 1. Reproduced locally; all 75 are one column (x = 720, y = 105 to 179) of the anti-aliased edge of a shape, where the tile (drawn at another offset) rounds one channel pair one level differently. It is not a tile seam.
**Decision:** Update to PDFium 160.0.8165 and let that one test accept a maximum difference of 1 per channel (`MaxDelta`). Every other render comparison (`AssertSameImage`) stays exact.
**Also needed:** the bump failed `licenses.cs check` on macOS, so `third-party/licenses.json`, `third-party/pdfium/README.md` (versions, archive SHA-256 of `chromium/8165`) and the notices were refreshed from the new archives; the only new licence text is `licenses/highway.txt` (Google Highway, Apache-2.0). Both archives still build with `pdf_enable_v8 = false` and `pdf_enable_xfa = false`.
**Rationale:** One level out of 255 on an edge pixel is invisible, and a tile at a wrong offset or scale gives large deltas on whole regions, which the test still catches. Pinning PDFium at 157 would only postpone the update.
**Rejected alternatives:** Staying on 157 (upstream PDFium fixes are wanted); loosening the comparison for all render tests; allowing a share of pixels with any delta.

## 2026-11-22 – Raffica Write: the structure limit (M4.4a)
**Context:** The structure rows of `--measure-structure` were recorded and not judged until the owner's runs on the Linux laptop and the Intel Mac gave numbers, and the entry that sets the limit had to say which reading the 100 ms aim of a chapter move means. The runs are in: `measure.yml` target `write` after the far-caret fix (see "Raffica Write: headings, lists and the outline (M4.4a)"): the Intel Mac on AC power (run 45014269858, app at the fix branch), the Linux laptop **on mains power** (run 45024333010 of `dev` at 44d411d: `machine.txt` says mains 1, governor powersave with the balance_performance preference, 3718 of at most 3900 MHz; the two earlier Linux runs of the same day were on battery and are not used) and the Windows PC (three launches of `--measure-structure`, Native AOT, thesis).
**Decision:**
- **The limit is the aim: the metric `structure` is the worst median of the three chapter-move rows, at most 100 ms (budget 80 × 1.25, `CheckCommand.StructureBudget`), entered for every platform in `s_writeBudgets`.** It is a fixed limit like `typing`, `format` and the Linux `engine`: it does not make a platform one "with an app row" (`JudgeWrite`). A miss is a finding, never a loosened number.
- **The reading of the aim is CPU cost**, the same as for `typing` and `format`: the key handler, the render pass on the UI thread and the page draws on the render thread, from the command to the pages in view. The frame latency (it holds the wait for the next vsync, 15.8 ms median on the Linux laptop) is recorded as `structure-split` and not judged. The numbering switch, the list toggle and the far-caret move are recorded, never judged (the far-caret row stays as the guard against the old cost).
- **Why not median × 1.25 of the runs** (the rule of the 4.2 app budgets, and what the first plan said): the medians are 2 to 8 ms, so that rule gives limits of 5 to 10 ms, which a laptop's 30 percent variation between days fails without any change in the code; the regression the limit exists for, a move that lays out the whole document (about 500 to 770 ms on the three machines before the fix), is caught 5 times over by 100 ms and the spec's own number needs no second one.
**Numbers (worst median of the three move rows, medians of three launches per row, ms):** Windows PC 6.66 (first to last 6.27, middle 2.76, last to first 6.66); Intel Mac 6.23 (6.23, 2.16, 6.09; numbering on and off 4.5 and 4.6, list toggle 3.0); Linux laptop on mains **4.10** (4.02, 1.87, 4.10; numbering 2.7 and 2.6, list toggle 1.8; frame median of a move 15.75 ms, p95 17.3 ms). Far-caret move: Windows 1.88 to 3.02, Intel Mac 2.0 to 2.4, Linux laptop 1.56 to 1.88 (before the fix 510, 550 and 743 to 775 ms). The same Linux run met every other limit on mains power: cold 290 ms (280 budget, 350 limit), engine first page 93.9 ms (100), typing worst p95 13.03 and format 13.4 ms (16), full layout of the thesis 392 ms for run 2 (3000), the check step green.
**Rejected alternatives:** a limit from the medians times 1.25 (above); a limit per platform (the spec has one aim); judging the frame latency (it can never meet the number on a 60 Hz display for an unrelated reason, as for typing).
**Not covered:** a move on a document much longer than the thesis (the layout is incremental, the numbers say nothing about a 2000-page file); the owner's real display and a real pointer drag.

## 2026-11-22 – Raffica Write: headings, lists and the outline (M4.4a)
**Context:** Milestone 4.4 ("Structure") was too large for one branch and is split (owner, 2026-11-21): **4.4.1 ("4.4a")** is headings, lists and the outline (this entry), **4.4.2 ("4.4b")** is sections, page numbers, headers and footers and page setup (the roadmap's scope change records the split; 4.5 now follows 4.4.2). 4.4a lets a person number the headings of a document with one command, make bullet and numbered lists with levels, restart and continue, and move, promote and demote a chapter in the outline pane, with the numbers on the page never stale after any of it. Spec: `Docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-21-raffica-write-4-4a-headings-lists-and-outline-design.md`, plan: `Docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-21-milestone-04-4a-headings-lists-and-outline.md`.
**Decision:**
- **Heading numbering is linked to the styles (approach A of the spec).** `structure.numberHeadings` (`TextEditor.SetHeadingNumbering(on, caret)`) writes `w:numPr` into the paragraph properties of the heading styles (`ilvl` from level 2 on, Word's spelling) and removes it again when switched off; direct `numPr` of a paragraph is never touched, and a style that already carries a list is left alone. **Amendment (found at Task 13, ruled on 2026-11-21):** the switch numbers **the heading styles that exist** among `Heading1` to `Heading9` (id `HeadingN`, name `heading N` in any case); it is `Unavailable` only without a style sheet or without `Heading1`. The plan said "unavailable unless all nine exist", which made the switch do nothing on the generated thesis and on most real files, because they define `Heading1` to `Heading3` only (the rest are latent). The `w:pStyle` link of a definition's level is written only for a style that exists (a dangling link is not written; layout and `StyleResolver` ignore the link anyway). The state is On when `Heading1` carries a list, Off when it exists with none. One history step: a `StyleChange` with `Companions` (every other style), swapped all or nothing; the session restyles the layout (`DocumentLayout.Restyle`) instead of `ApplyEdit`.
- **The numbering definitions (`ListCatalog`, one per editor as `TextEditor.Lists`).** The list a command uses is the document's own: a `w:num` whose level 0 is of the kind (the list the List Bullet or List Number style points at wins), a definition linked to the headings (`HeadingList`, found again after an undo so on, off, on adds no second one), else Write's own copied from the blank document's definitions. A document without a numbering part gets one (`ListCatalog.Required`; the DOCX layer writes the part, relationship and content type). `NewRun` makes one `w:num` per toggle and reuses only an instance Write made that has no overrides; a restart adds a `w:num` with `w:startOverride`. **A `w:num` is never removed and undo never removes a definition or an instance:** the instances stay unused after an undo, and a redo or a later command finds them.
- **Lists** (`ToggleList`, `ChangeListLevel`, `RestartNumbering`, `ContinueNumbering`, `ReadListFacts`): one `BlockChange` per operation in the container of the selection, as paragraph formatting. A toggle on paragraphs of the other kind switches them, keeping the level; a numbered toggle gets `startOverride` 1 when another paragraph already uses the same definition (so "numbers from 1" holds under Word's shared-counter rule). A normal paragraph also gets List Paragraph when the file has it. Keys: Tab and Shift+Tab change the level at the start of a list paragraph or over a non-empty selection (at the edge nothing happens, no tab fallback; elsewhere Tab inserts a tab as before); Enter on an empty list paragraph ends the list and keeps the paragraph; Backspace at the start of a list paragraph removes the list first, keeping the text.
- **Promote and demote are paragraph changes, not style changes** (`ShiftOutlineLevels(first, last, delta)`): every paragraph with an outline level in the blocks of a heading's subtree changes by one level, by its own `w:outlineLvl` (which wins and is rewritten) or by the style id (`HeadingN` becomes `Heading(N+delta)`, which must exist). A level from any other source, a missing target style or a result outside 1 to 9 refuses the whole call before anything changes. One `BlockChange`, one history step.
- **The move is a history step of its own (option 1 of the spec).** `MoveBlocks(first, count, target)` rotates the range of top-level blocks in place; `EditHistory` stores indices only (`BlockMove`; undo is the inverse rotation), so no block is cloned, and bookmarks, comment anchors and `w14:paraId` stay because the same objects move. **The `OldBlocks` of a move's `BlockChange` are the live pre-move objects, not clones, and must never be mutated** (a deliberate exception to "history snapshots are read-only copies"; the layout and `EditTracker` treat them read-only). A step costs a few bytes whatever the range (`TheHistoryStepCostsNoBlockClones`).
- **The subtree and the drop rules** (`OutlineTree` in the app): a heading's subtree is the top-level blocks from the heading to before the next listed heading of the same or a higher level. Blocked (command and drag disabled, with a reason): a subtree that holds a paragraph with a section break (4.4b lifts this), a heading inside a block content control, a heading that is not at top level. `DropTarget(headings, document, dragged, row, place)` gives -1 for a drop inside the dragged subtree, onto its own place, **between two rows that belong to one content control**, and **where the body would then end in a table** (**the table-last rule**: Word wants a paragraph after a final table and repairs a file that has none; a body that already ended in a table is not blocked for moves that keep it last). The caret goes to the start of the moved heading and the row stays selected. Move down is one row: past the next heading that is not in the chapter's own subtree, so a chapter lands between its next sibling and that sibling's first sub-heading (as Word's outline view does).
- **The layout needed no change for a move** apart from one fix. A block plan is position-independent and keyed by block, numbering and note state, so `ApplyEdit` with a change whose removed count equals its added count and the same objects relays out from the first changed block and equals a full layout; this is proved by the oracle (`StructureSteps`, `CorpusIncrementalTests.AChapterMoveStaysEqualToFullLayout` for every corpus file in both views, at every through page, with undo and redo, and a section-break case) and by mutation checks (a narrowed changed range failed 18 of 81 cases including the thesis in both views; skipping `Forget` failed existing edit cases but no move case, which agrees with the reasoning). Pages mode takes `Restart` for every move whose range holds a section-break paragraph (3 to 6 ms through page 2). The one change: `DocumentLayout.Engine` remembers the `NumberingDefinitions` its counters were built from and `Refill` restarts when the document's numbering part is another object, so a list command that created a numbering part shows numbers at once.
- **Never-stale numbers are proved against an independent count.** `ReferenceNumbering` (test support) counts labels from the model without `NumberingCounter` or `StyleResolver` (effective `numPr` own over style chain, shared counters per definition, deeper-level restart, `startOverride` once per instance and level); `DrawnLabels` reads the labels drawn on the pages; `StructureSteps` runs every kind of structure operation (move, promote, demote, delete a heading, delete over headings, insert a heading, the numbering switch, list toggle, level, restart, continue, undo, redo) over the corpus and generated documents, compares each step with a full layout (exact) and the drawn labels with the reference, and `RealEditorOracleTests` runs four structure steps per file, mode and seed. The saved file of the final state is read again and its drawn labels equal the reference of the reopened model. An allowance for pages whose text does not line up with lines (`LabelTally`, `StructureSteps.AssertReadable`) is empirical per file id (`poi-comment`, `poi-shapes-with-text` only).
- **Keys.** Alt+Shift+Up and Down move, Alt+Shift+Left and Right promote and demote (Word's keys; AltGr is text), in the pane (also from the list) and with the caret in a heading, once per press. **macOS has no key for the nine commands:** the Option+Shift arrows extend the selection by paragraph, and the chords that would be free (Ctrl+Cmd arrows) cannot be expressed by `WriteShortcut` (one base modifier plus Shift); the commands are in the Format menu, the ribbon, the command search and the pane's buttons (`ShortcutFor(true)` is null, pinned by `TheKeysAreAltShiftArrowsOnWindowsAndLinuxAndNoneOnTheMac`).
- **The pane.** A header with four icon buttons (move up and down, promote, demote; the disabled state shows the reason as tooltip and automation name), and a drag: press on a row and move 6 DIP, a 2 px drop line indented by the target's level where `DropTarget != -1`, the dragged row dimmed to 60 percent; Esc, a rebuilt list, a detach or a lost capture cancels; a drop calls `PageView.RunOutlineDrop`, which finds both rows again in the live model inside the operation. Structure commands, keys and buttons are disabled while a save runs (the still-saving reason); a drop during a save is queued and replayed by the save queue. The Home band has two new groups (lists, digit 8; outline, digit 9) with nine commands, each with its own Lucide icon (`Heading`, `List`, `ListOrdered`, `ListRestart`, `ListStart`, `ChevronsUp`, `ChevronsDown`, `ArrowLeftToLine`, `ArrowRightToLine`, Lucide 1.63.0, ISC); the Home band's width hint stays 2500 (pinned against the real band in English and German).
- **Facts are lazy and bounded.** `StructureFactsSource` reads only what the asked command needs (list facts for `list.*`, the numbering state for the switch, the headings and plan for `outline.*`), memoized by model, revision, selection and outline row; it reads nothing before an editing session exists (`StartupCreatesNoStructureWork`). Above `TextEditor.MaxFactBlocks` (200 top-level blocks) the four outline commands are offered permissively and the operation, which reads the live model when it runs, is authoritative and refuses with a visible note (never a silent no-op).
- **Bullets.** The blank document's bullets are Symbol and Wingdings private-use glyphs, which the shipped fonts lack. `NumberingCounter.BulletText` draws every such bullet as U+2022 (the Wingdings square too); level-2 `o` is unchanged. Carlito has U+2022 and U+25AA, **Caladea has U+2022 but not U+25AA**, so a square in a Caladea paragraph would be a box (`FontCatalogTests.EveryBulletGlyphTheLayoutDrawsExistsInEveryShippedFace`). This settles the "bullets may draw as a fallback until 4.4" limit of 4.3c. After the bullet change the thesis page count test (`CorpusToolTests.TheThesisHasTheNumberOfPagesWordMakesOfIt`, 309) passed in the full run and `Docs/write-corpus.md`, regenerated, did not change.
- **Measuring** (the Windows PC, quiet, Native AOT, the generated thesis; the structure rows are three launches of the run with 20 runs each, typing, format and typing with the pane are one run each, the start cost is three interleaved rounds of 10 runs; the value is CPU cost as for `typing`: handler, render pass and page draws; the frame latency is recorded and not judged): the app switch `--measure-structure` (cases `move-first-to-last`, `move-middle`, `move-last-to-first`, `numbering-on`, `numbering-off`, `toggle-list`, and the unjudged `move-last-to-first-far-caret`) and the `structure` case of `measure.cs`; `write-layout-bench edit` prints the engine-only rows. Results in the table below. The structure family was **recorded and not judged** when this entry was written; the limit of the three move rows (100 ms, CPU cost) is set by "Raffica Write: the structure limit (M4.4a)" above.

| What (median / p95 ms, Windows PC) | Result | Aim or limit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| move first chapter to last | 5.45, 5.79, 5.74 / 14.9, 11.9, 6.4 | aim 100 ms (Linux laptop) |
| move a middle chapter down (one row) | 2.28, 2.55, 2.51 / 2.9, 4.0, 7.2 | aim 100 ms |
| move last chapter to first (crosses section breaks) | 5.88, 6.11, 5.49 / 17.9, 15.4, 12.7 | aim 100 ms |
| move last to first, **caret on the last page, view at the first** (recorded; before the fix of 2026-11-22) | 498.3, 497.0, 492.0 / 532.0, 514.5, 523.3 | none: fixed, see the known limit (2.06, 1.88, 3.02 after) |
| numbering on, numbering off (thesis) | 3.86 to 4.66, 3.81 to 5.49 | recorded |
| numbered list toggle (thesis) | 2.23 to 2.51 | recorded |
| engine alone (`edit`): moves, numbering, toggle | 0.19 to 0.87, about 0.9, 0.84 | recorded |
| worst p95 of `typing`, `format`, typing with the pane open | 6.14, 7.22, 8.27 | 16.0 |
| start, cold and warm, branch against 38fe65e (10 runs, 3 interleaved rounds, median) | 237.6 against 238.1, 241.8 against 242.9 | no regression |
| ribbon refresh after a pause (thesis; a 144-block generated document) | 0.45 to 3.2; 0.49 to 0.70 | recorded |

The first measurement of last-to-first read 485 ms and was a defect of the measurement, not of the layout: the run set the caret in the same frame as the command, so `PageView.ThroughPage()` (the later of the last page in view and the caret page) was the caret page left by the previous undo (305 of 306) and the move laid out through it. The run now places the caret in frames of its own before the timed command (`through` is 2 on every run); the 485 ms row is superseded by the table above.

**Rationale:** Linking the numbers to the styles is what makes "a heading typed afterwards has a number" true, which is the stale state the milestone removes; a move that swaps the same objects keeps bookmarks, anchors and ids for free, costs no history bytes and lets the layout and the snapshot cache treat it as one range change. The independent reference count is the proof that no operation leaves a wrong number: it shares no code with the layout, so a bug cannot hide on both sides. Lazy, bounded facts keep the ribbon refresh and the start free of the new commands.
**Rejected alternatives:** *Numbering approach B*, a `numPr` on each heading paragraph (simple, but a heading typed afterwards has no number until it is applied; the stale state the milestone exists to remove). *Move option 2*, one `BlockChange` over the spanned range with clones (moving the first chapter of the thesis to the end would clone most of the body and press on the 16 MiB history limit). *Move option 3*, cut and paste through the fragment codec (loses bookmarks, anchors and ids). A numbering switch that needs all nine heading styles (the plan's wording; rejected after the thesis showed it does nothing on real files). A square bullet when the face has it (see the limit below).
**Known limits** (what remains after review; each is deliberate or deferred):
- *Undo of the numbering switch is not exact on disk.* A definition, its `w:num` and the `w:pStyle` links added to a chosen unlinked definition stay when the step is undone (undo never removes definitions), and a numbering part that was created stays. After an undo the document reads clean (no labels), but a file saved then can differ from the file before the switch (an extra numbering part, `w:pStyle` links).
- *Two definitions can link the same styles.* After a style is added in the middle of a session (or between files) and the switch is cycled, a second definition can come to link the same styles; layout ignores the link and whether Word tolerates it is unchecked. `HeadingList` is looser with fewer styles: a user's definition linked only to Heading 1 and 2 is taken as the heading list when the file defines only those.
- *Linked heading styles with `numId` but no `ilvl`* (the level named by the `w:lvl`'s `w:pStyle`, which ECMA-376 and Word read): layout and `ReferenceNumbering` agree and both ignore the link (the numbers count at level 0), so a foreign file that is built so differs from Word. Our own switch writes an explicit `ilvl` from level 2 on. Fixing it is one coordinated change of `StyleResolver`, `NumberingCounter` and the reference.
- *Localized heading style ids* (a German Word writes ids in the UI language, such as `Überschrift1`, with the name "heading 1"): promote and demote refuse them as `NotAHeadingStyle` and the numbering switch is `Unavailable`, because detection is by the ids `Heading1` to `Heading9`.
- *Backspace at a numbered heading* writes a direct `numId` 0 on that paragraph (the number goes first, as in Word), so it takes two presses to merge with the paragraph before.
- *A list toggle on a mixed selection:* paragraphs that are not yet in a list get one new instance that restarts at 1 (the others keep theirs); `StartAtOneWhenShared` resolves the list of every paragraph of the story per numbered toggle (2.2 ms on the thesis, no cost seen) and looks at direct and one-level style `numPr` only.
- *The far-caret outline move (fixed in the change of 2026-11-22).* An outline operation (the commands `outline.moveUp`, `moveDown`, `promote` and `demote` and the drop of a drag) used to lay out through `PageView.ThroughPage()`, the later of the last page in view and the **caret page**; the caret page is the page of the *old* caret, which an outline operation does not keep (a move puts the caret at the start of the moved heading, promote and demote keep the selection where it was), so a move run with the caret far from the view cost up to a full layout (Pages mode takes `Restart` across a section break): about **510 ms on the thesis** on the Windows PC in the run of this change (510.2, 512.7, 537.1 ms medians; 498, 497 and 492 ms in the first measurement of this milestone), about 760 ms on the Linux laptop on battery and about 550 ms on the Intel Mac (runs of 2026-11-22, before the fix). **`PageView.RunOutline` and `RunOutlineDrop` now call `ApplyGuarded(..., outline: true)`, which lays out through the last page in view only (`ThroughPageInView`)**; typing, formatting and the list commands still use `ThroughPage()`. The layout is exact for any such page (`StructureOracleTests.AMoveLaidOutThroughALowPageStillEqualsAFullLayout`: move, undo and redo through page 0, with and without section breaks, each equal to a full layout) and the queued path of a save carries the same value (`EditSession._queue` stores the page the view had when the operation was asked for). **What the view does after an outline move:** it follows the caret only if the layout can show the caret; `OnEditChanged` calls `EnsureCaretVisible`, which scrolls only to a caret on a laid-out page and does nothing for a caret the layout cannot show yet. A move whose heading lands in or above the last page in view scrolls to it as before; a move to a place below the view is laid out in the background (the pages are not laid out by the command), the view stays where it is and the caret is not drawn until its page is laid out; the caret sits on a page that is not laid out, the view stays, and keys typed meanwhile go into a place the user cannot see until the background layout reaches that page (under 1 s on the thesis, longer on a very long document); the view jumps to the caret only on the next key after the layout is complete. This was so before the fix for a caret in view and a destination below the view (the through-page value was the same in the old and the new code); it is no data-integrity problem (typing, Backspace, Enter, Tab, Ctrl+B, paste and undo after such a move were run by the reviewer: no fault, the text is in the moved heading's paragraph, the final layout equals a fresh one, the undo steps are right). The page of the caret (`_caretPage`) is made unknown again when the session caret has no caret box (`PageView.CaretBarBounds`), so the first key after such a move lays out through the last page in view and not through the old caret's page (before this follow-up the first key paid the whole layout, about 500 ms; `OutlineFarCaretTests.TheFirstKeyAfterAMoveBelowTheViewDoesNotPayTheLayoutOfTheOldCaretPage`). The roadmap backlog has the idea "View follows the moved heading after an outline move". Promote and demote keep the selection, so with a far caret the view no longer jumps to it (before the fix the caret page was laid out and `EnsureCaretVisible` scrolled there). Windows PC, Native AOT, thesis, three launches of `--measure-structure`, medians in ms before and after: move last to first, caret on the last page and view at the first (`move-last-to-first-far-caret`) **510.2, 512.7, 537.1 to 2.06, 1.88, 3.02**; the other six rows within noise (move first to last 5.98, 6.33, 6.28 to 6.66, 6.24, 6.27; middle 2.88, 2.52, 3.07 to 2.76, 2.92, 2.37; last to first 6.47, 6.44, 6.39 to 6.66, 6.32, 7.39; numbering on 4.19, 4.76, 5.77 to 5.72, 6.11, 5.29; numbering off 5.01, 4.68, 5.68 to 4.71, 4.28, 5.61; list toggle 2.72, 3.34, 3.15 to 3.53, 2.54, 2.91). **Open:** the numbers of the Linux laptop and the Intel Mac after the fix (`measure.yml` case `structure` of the merged change); the row stays recorded and never judged, as a guard against the old cost.
- *The drag has no auto-scroll* near the edges of the list, and only the rows on screen can be targets (the list is virtual); a long outline needs the buttons or keys for far moves. A drop queued during a save gives no feedback and a refusal at replay is silent; a drop refused by the live plan shows the note.
- *No macOS key* for the nine commands (above).
- *A move across a section break* changes the section of the moved chapter and can leave a section-break paragraph as the last body block (an empty final section: Word may show an extra blank page). The table-last rule exists because Word repairs a file whose body ends in a table; a last section-break paragraph needs no repair but is visible. The Word-check thesis file makes exactly this kind of move. 4.4b revisits sections.
- *Bookmark and comment ranges* that straddle a chapter boundary can end with their end before their start after a move (not handled).
- *A list right after a list.* Numbering a paragraph directly after an existing numbered list starts a new list at 1, where Word continues it (the spec's call, noted for the owner).
- *Untested paths:* a stale queued move at replay, `PointerCaptureLost` and a pane hide in the middle of a drag, and the `NoteStructureFailed` path of a list command have no tests.
- *Bullets.* Every Symbol and Wingdings bullet draws as U+2022, so Word's square level-3 bullet is round in Carlito paragraphs too (a fidelity loss that avoids an empty box in Caladea; the faithful variant checks the face and uses the square when it has it).
- *Facts and the editor.* `ReadListFacts` is not memoized in the editor (the app caches by revision and range); the editor's effective format is rebuilt when the numbering part changes (`EditSession.NoteNumbering`); the snapshot tracker marks every block of a moved range dirty, so the next recovery copy re-serializes the span (acceptable).
- *Tests.* The structure oracle adds about 80 s to the Write test project; the keep-with-next walk-back in `PlanEdit` was not proved by the corpus (mutation: all 84 cases passed with it disabled) and is now pinned by `AMoveUnderAKeepWithNextHeadingPullsItBackToThePageBeforeAndStaysEqualToFullLayout` (it failed under the mutation); `DrawnLabels` collapses only a repeated table header row (proved on a hand-made table) and the allowances of `AssertReadable` are empirical per file.
- *Word acceptance of a definition linked on levels 1 to 3 only* (a file whose heading definition links fewer than nine levels) is covered by the script check below, which opened the files, but no person has looked at the numbers in Word.
**Follow-ups:** the limit entry for the three move rows is done (see "Raffica Write: the structure limit (M4.4a)"), the `lvl`-pStyle reading and localized heading ids (above), auto-scroll in the drag, 4.4b (sections, which lifts the section-break block of the move).
**Owner gates (milestone 4.4.1 closed as done on 2026-11-22; the look at a real display was never done, see item 2):** (1) **Word**: the script check ran on the Windows PC with Word for Microsoft 365 on 2026-11-22 and Word opened `numbered-headings.docx`, `lists.docx` and `numbered-thesis-chapter-move.docx` without repair (made by `WordCheckFilesTests` with `RAFFICA_WORD_CHECK_DIR` set; rows in `Docs/write-word-check.md`); a person must still look at whether the numbers read in Word as in Write: set `RAFFICA_WORD_CHECK_DIR` to a folder, run `dotnet test tests/Raffica.Write.Tests -c Release --filter-class "*WordCheckFilesTests"`, open the three files (or `pwsh tools/word-open-check.ps1 <folder>\numbered-headings.docx <folder>\lists.docx <folder>\numbered-thesis-chapter-move.docx` for the repair check; the script takes explicit file names, not a wildcard). (2) **A look at the Native AOT app** (closed by the owner on 2026-11-22 without a personal look; never seen on a real display: real keys, a real pointer drag, the drop line) on Windows, the Linux laptop and the Intel Mac: drop line, dimmed row, the button size in the 240 px pane, the Alt+Shift and Tab keys, numbered and bulleted headings and lists. (3) and (4), the `structure` runs on the Linux laptop and the Intel Mac, the limit entry and the 100 ms aim, are closed on 2026-11-22 (the Linux laptop on mains: worst move median 4.10 ms; see "Raffica Write: the structure limit (M4.4a)"). The numbers read as in Write: Word's own list strings equal Write's labels on every numbered paragraph of the three files (checked on 2026-11-22 through Word automation on the Windows PC, not by a person looking; see `Docs/write-word-check.md`). Also still open and not claimed here: IME input on every system.

## 2026-11-21 – Raffica Write: the gates of 4.2 and 4.3 are closed (M4.2, M4.3)
**Context:** Milestones 4.2, 4.3.1, 4.3.2 and 4.3.3 stayed `in-progress` only for owner gates. On 2026-11-21 the owner recorded the checks as done on Windows, the Linux laptop and the Intel Mac (real-display look, Save as, the clipboard, kill and restart, the autosave box and the German label; Word checks of the saved files; the speed runs with the limits in force; the full layout and the recovery-copy cost, see the entry below), dropped the IME check from the gates, and left the call on formatted typing to a recommendation.
**Decision:** (1) **IME is not a gate.** Acceptance criterion 3 of 4.3.1 is removed from the roadmap (scope change); the IME code, its headless tests (`ImeTests`, `PreeditLayoutTests`) and the checklist `Docs/write-ime-check.md` stay, and no real input method (Windows, Linux Wayland or X11, macOS) and no German layout was run, so IME input is unverified; the first real report is a bug report, not a regression. (2) **A format command ends the typing step**, as in Word: `EditHistory.Record` gets the session's pending list as `typingFormat` and a typing step joins the open one only when both took the same pending list (none, or the same list by reference), so "hello", Ctrl+B and "x" are two undo steps, and the letters after "x" join the second. A new format command is a new list, so it ends the step too; a line break or a text with no characters takes no format. Test: `EditHistoryTests.AFormatCommandBetweenTypingEndsTheStepAsInWord`. This replaces the line "Typing and pending format" of the formatting decision (M4.3b), which left it open. (3) **The slower layout of 4.3a is accepted** (the figures of 4.3.1 item 8: about 24 percent for the full layout against the branch point): the full layout of the thesis is 393 and 552 ms on the laptops against the spec limit of 3 s and the first page is inside its limit, so it stays a follow-up, not a gate. (4) The four milestones are `done` with `completedOn` 2026-11-21.
