**Rejected alternatives:** Keeping 4.3.1 open for the IME check (it needs input methods on three systems and the owner does not want it as a gate); ticking the IME criterion without a run (it would claim proof there is none); a timer or a setting for the coalescing (a format command is a clear boundary).
**Known limits:** IME is unverified on every system. The other open items are follow-ups, not gates: the other corpus Word PDFs (`word.pdf` of the other entries, `word-accept.pdf`, `word-reject.pdf`), the US Letter blank document and a Mac in the Word check, a quiet re-run of the typing cases on the Windows PC, and the text-box groups of poi-60316.

## 2026-11-21 – Raffica Write: the engine checks run in `measure.yml` (M4.2, M4.3)
**Context:** Two owner gates asked for a run on the Linux laptop and the Intel Mac by hand: the full layout of the thesis under 3 s (`write-layout-bench check --budget-ms 3000`, spec section 5) and the cost of a recovery copy (`write-layout-bench edit --snapshot`). The owner's runners only serve `measure.yml`, and device checks are meant to go through CI runners.
**Decision:** The target `write` of `measure.yml` has two more steps on the published `write-layout-bench`: "Full layout of the thesis under 3 s (write)" (`check --root . --budget-ms 3000`; it fails the job on a finding or above 3 s, and the time of both runs is in `full-layout.txt`) and "Recovery copy cost, engine only (write)" (`edit --snapshot`, recorded in `edit-snapshot.txt`, never judged). The 3 s limit is applied on every platform, not on Linux alone: the Windows PC needs 0.4 s, so the limit costs nothing there and shows a regression early. Answering the gates is one dispatch per machine: `gh workflow run measure.yml --ref dev -f platform=<linux|macos> -f target=write -f note="<machine>"`.
**Rejected alternatives:** A limit row in `s_writeBudgets` for the full layout (the check already judges it in the tool, a second table would hold the same number twice); judging the snapshot cost (the criterion that matters, typing beside a snapshot, is judged by `typing-snapshot`).
**Known limits:** Both steps ran green on the first dispatch on the Linux laptop (run 44940794683) and the Intel Mac (run 44940797794), 2026-11-21. Full layout of the thesis (309 pages), run2: 393 ms on the laptop and 552 ms on the Mac, against 3000 ms. Recovery copy (median, p95): lease held 1.25 and 7.9 ms on the laptop, 2.21 and 11.6 ms on the Mac; whole copy 54 and 79 ms; the plain save holds the lease 13.0 and 17.4 ms and takes 98 and 122 ms; a copy allocates 7.3 MiB against 28.9 for a plain save, 0.37 MiB on disk.

## 2026-11-21 – Raffica Write: the extra room of a line spacing multiple is below the text (M4.2)
**Context:** The 4.2 gate asks that Write's layout of the generated thesis has the page count of Word's PDF of it. The owner's first PDF had 320 pages against Write's 311. It had been made without Caladea and Carlito installed, so Word had substituted Cambria and Aptos (Cambria's letters are 4 to 5 percent wider than Caladea's, read from the PDF's width table), which made the comparison meaningless. With both fonts installed Word makes 309 pages (embedded fonts Caladea, Carlito, Aptos, Arial), Write 311, and the chapters were one page off here and there. The PDFs' text positions showed why: the body style has line spacing 1.5 (`w:line="360"`, automatic). Word puts the extra half line **below** the text of every line (the first line of a page stands one ascent below the top margin, 9.8 pt, where Write had 16.2 pt) and lets it **hang below the text area** at the end of a page: the last line of a page ends at its own text height (770.5 pt against a text area ending at 770.9 pt), so it fits where Write, which counted the full 19 pt line, pushed it to the next page.
**Decision:** For `lineRule` auto with a multiple above one, the baseline of a line is its natural ascent from the top of the line (the extra is below: `baseline = min(height, natural) - descent`), `LineBox.Extra` holds that extra, and a paragraph piece fits when its height minus the extra of its last line fits (`ParagraphFlow.Split` and `LinesThatFit`). Lines still advance by the full height, so everything after a line stays where it was. A multiple below one, `atLeast` and `exact` are unchanged (not looked at against Word). Result: the thesis is 309 pages in Write, equal to Word's (mean per-page text overlap 0.83 against 0.29 before; page 2 differs because Word updated the contents with the two appendix entries and Write shows the contents as saved). Tests: `ParagraphLayoutTests.TheExtraRoomOfAMultipleIsBelowTheTextAndIsNotNeededToFit`, `PageLayoutTests.TheLastLineOfAPageNeedsNoExtraLineSpacingRoom` (fails with 4 lines against 5 without the rule), the golden record (`thesis/Pages` 311 to 309, `thesis/Flow` changed; no other corpus file changed), and `CorpusToolTests.TheThesisHasTheNumberOfPagesWordMakesOfIt`, which compares with `testdata/write-corpus/thesis/word.pdf` (committed, made by the owner).
**Rejected alternatives:** Tuning the line height or the margin until the count matches (it would hide the cause and break other documents); accepting the 320-page PDF as the reference (the fonts differ); scaling Caladea (the fonts are Write's own choice, Word's PDF now uses them).
**Known limits:** Checked against one Word PDF of one document (Word for Microsoft 365, German, Windows 11), where the page count and the chapter starts now agree. Footnote pages and `atLeast` line spacing may still differ from Word; the table-of-contents page differs because Write shows the cached contents. The PDF depends on the owner's Word and the two fonts: a PDF made without them fails the test and is not a reference (the test names the fonts).

## 2026-11-21 – Raffica Write: one paragraph in a text, combo box, drop-down list or date control (M4.3a)
**Context:** The owner's first Word check on Windows (Word for Microsoft 365) found that Word shows a repair prompt ("unreadable content") for a poi-bug54849 file that Write edited and saved. The file passed the Open XML SDK's schema validator with no new error against the original, so the schema did not say. The cause was found with Word itself on the dev machine (a script opens each file through Word's COM automation, `Documents.Open` without repair, and reports whether it opened): the original opens, the owner's saved file does not, the saved file with its two new empty paragraphs removed opens, and the original plus only those two paragraphs does not. The paragraphs were made by Enter inside a block-level plain-text content control (`w:sdt` with `w:text`). A second pass split one paragraph in every paragraph of every content control of the file, one file each: the text control (now fixed), the combo box, the drop-down list and the date control all fail the same way, the controls with no kind (rich text) open.
**Decision:** A block content control of the kind text, combo box, drop-down list or date holds one paragraph, like Word's own. In such a control `TextEditor` never makes a second paragraph: Enter (and a line break in pasted or typed text) is a `w:br` in a text control with `w:multiLine` and is dropped in every other one (several pasted lines are joined with a space), and a paste into one takes the plain text of the clipboard only, never the paragraphs of a fragment. The caret and the one history step follow the usual rules. A control with no kind (rich text) and every other list of blocks are unchanged. Tests: `ContentControlEditingTests` (the three text-control cases, the three other kinds, rich text unchanged); the Word check of the saved files is the script described in `Docs/write-word-check.md`.
**Rejected alternatives:** Fixing it on save (dropping or merging paragraphs when a control is written): the editor would show a document the file does not hold, and Write would change what the user typed behind their back. Refusing every edit in such a control: typing in it is allowed and works. Extending the rule to kinds that were not tested with Word (check box, picture, equation, group): not proven, and no corpus file has a block-level one.
**Known limits:** Only the four kinds above, found in the corpus (poi-bug54849) and checked with Word on one machine (Word 16.0, Windows 11). Paste into a nested control takes the innermost one. A copy out of such a control still copies its paragraph as a fragment. Other places a second paragraph could break Word (a footnote or table cell inside a control) were not looked at. The corpus files poi-bug54849 and poi-60316 were also edited by script in six other ways (type, Enter, backspace, forward delete, delete a range, paste, in every paragraph) and every result opens in Word.

## 2026-11-21 – Raffica Write: the reading of the 16 ms criterion (M4.3)
**Context:** The spec criteria of milestones 4.3.1 to 4.3.3 say a key and a formatting edit take at most 16 ms ("key to frame" in the editing spec). `measure.cs` has judged the CPU cost of the key handler, the UI render pass and the render-thread draws (worst p95 over all case and view pairs) and only recorded the frame latency. The owner was asked to ratify that reading, and the reading of `typing-snapshot` (typing while recovery copies are written back to back, 3.5 a second, against the real 30 s cadence).
**Decision:** The owner accepted both on 2026-11-21. `typing` and `format` are judged on CPU cost against a fixed 16.0 ms on every platform; the frame latency stays recorded and is not judged. `typing-snapshot` rows count into the judged `typing` metric at the back-to-back rate. A sanity bound on the recorded latency is kept in this entry, not in the check: the worst p95 key-to-frame latency of the typing, typing-pane and format cases must stay within two frame periods (33 ms at 60 Hz); on the runs of 2026-11-21 it was 17.4 ms (Windows PC, run 44866254218), 22.2 ms (Linux laptop, run 44866256721) and 19.5 ms (Intel Mac, run 44864148204).
**Rationale:** Key to frame on a vsynced display contains the wait for the next vsync (up to one period, 16.7 ms at 60 Hz), so a hard 16 ms on it cannot be met by any code on any machine. What the app controls is the work a key causes: when it stays under one frame period, a key never drops a frame, and the latency numbers confirm that the display adds about one frame (17 to 22 ms in total). The back-to-back snapshot rate is conservative: a pass there is a pass at the real cadence.
**Rejected alternatives:** Judging the frame latency against 16 ms (unmeetable, a permanent failure that hides real regressions); judging it against 33 ms in the check (display- and compositor-dependent, it would fail on a 30 Hz display or a busy compositor for reasons outside the app); dropping `typing-snapshot` from the judged metric (the snapshot is the one background work that runs during typing, so it belongs in the judged number).
**Known limits:** The criterion measures the app's own time and the frame it waits for, not the compositor, the display's own latency or an input device. The Linux laptop has the least margin (typing 10.9 to 11.4 ms, format 12.5 to 14.7 ms of 16.0, the table-cell cases the thinnest). `typing-snapshot` needs a quiet machine and is a step of `measure.yml` from this entry on; its first numbers on the Linux laptop and the Intel Mac are the runs that follow (open until they are in).

## 2026-11-21 – Raffica Write: speed budgets (M4.2)
**Context:** Milestone 4.2 (step 3) records the Write app's speed and size limits from the owner's runs, as milestone 2.1 did for the PDF app. Until now `s_writeBudgets` in `bench/shell-probe/measure.cs` held only the fixed engine limit (Linux, 100 ms), and the fixed `typing` and `format` limits (16 ms, every platform). The owner's three machines ran `measure.yml` with `target=write` on 2026-11-21, after the key-cost fixes of "Raffica Write: the cost of a key on the laptops (M4.3 follow-up)" (`dev` at 72adf63): the Windows PC (run 44866254218), the Linux laptop (runs 44864145294 and 44866256721) and the Intel Mac (runs 44843636813, 44852598340 and 44864148204; the two older Mac runs are from before the last key fixes, which do not touch start, scroll or memory).
**Decision:** The app limits are the median over the runs of the median of each run, rounded up as in M1 and M2.1 (time to 10 ms, memory to 5 MiB, size and zip to 1 MiB), and the check limit is budget × 1.25 (`Stats.Limit`). They are entered per platform, as the PDF app's are, and the Intel Mac's bind that machine.

| Metric | Windows PC | Linux laptop | Intel Mac |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cold start | 270 ms (265.0) | 280 ms (271.0, 272.9) | 730 ms (641.3, 722.3, 730.0) |
| Warm start | 270 ms (263.4) | 270 ms (249.1, 269.6) | 800 ms (710.6, 793.9, 806.4) |
| Thesis to the first page | 280 ms (276.4) | 300 ms (288.9, 295.1) | 800 ms (741.5, 793.8, 807.5) |
| Second file | 20 ms (12.7) | 50 ms (48.5, 49.6) | 80 ms (68.1, 72.3, 72.1) |
| Scroll p99 | 20 ms (16.8) | 20 ms (17.2, 17.3) | 20 ms (18.9, 19.7, 19.6) |
| Memory with the thesis | 115 MiB (110.1) | 210 MiB (205.9, 209.4) | 155 MiB (148.4, 151.9, 151.2) |
| Published size | 73 MiB (72.78) | 67 MiB (66.10) | 69 MiB (68.07) |
| Zip | 28 MiB (27.56) | 29 MiB (28.02) | 30 MiB (29.04) |

(The medians of each run in brackets.) The limits are therefore 337.5, 337.5, 350, 25, 25, 143.75, 91.25 and 35 on Windows, 350, 337.5, 375, 62.5, 25, 262.5, 83.75 and 36.25 on Linux, 912.5, 1000, 1000, 100, 25, 193.75, 86.25 and 37.5 on macOS (ms, MiB). The engine limit stays Linux-only (the Windows PC measured 26.8 ms, the Intel Mac 166.9 ms, both recorded and not judged), and `typing` and `format` stay at 16 ms everywhere. Measured on the same runs for the record: typing 1.6 (Windows), 10.8 and 10.9 (Linux), 5.7 ms (Mac); format 1.9, 12.5 and 14.0, 5.8 ms; the engine first page 26.8, 95.7 and 96.9, 166.9 ms.
**Rationale:** The same method as the PDF app (the entry "Raffica PDF measurement method (M2.1)"), so a number means the same in both tables and a drift shows as a failed check, never as a quietly loosened one. The median of the runs' medians, not the best run, because the Intel Mac moves by 10 percent between days (cold 641 to 730 ms); a limit of budget × 1.25 still catches a real regression (a 25 percent slower start), which a single good night's number would turn into a flaky check. The Mac's budgets come from the owner's machine and not from a hosted runner, as in "Raffica on macOS: the Intel budgets and the rendering default (M3.3)".
**Rejected alternatives:** Budgets copied from the PDF app (Write opens a larger file and loads fonts; its cold start on Windows is 265 ms against the PDF app's budget of 200 ms, and on the Linux laptop 273 against 230, so a copied limit would fail on day one, while on the Intel Mac Write starts faster than the PDF budget of 774 ms and a copied limit would hide a regression); one budget for all platforms (the machines differ by a factor of 2.5 in start); leaving the limits empty until the Windows PC and the laptop have a second run (the table then judges nothing, which is the state this entry ends).
**Known limits:** The Windows numbers come from one run (the other machines from two and three): a second Windows run should replace them if it differs, in a new entry, never silently. The runs of 2026-11-21 had no Pagewing control run (the owner chose not to run one), so a time row is a record of that day and not a verdict against the laptop's day-to-day variation of "Raffica PDF: release readiness measurements (M2.7)"; the limit's 25 percent is the margin. The Write start is slower than the PDF app's budgets on Windows and the Linux laptop; this entry records that state and sets no target to improve on it. Memory is 206 to 210 MiB on the Linux laptop against 155 MiB for the PDF app (not examined). Scroll p99 sits at about one frame period of a 60 Hz display (16.8 to 19.7 ms), so its budget of 20 ms (limit 25 ms) cannot catch a loss of less than a frame. The size metric on the Mac is the published folder of `dotnet publish` on the owner's Mac, not the bundle (milestone 4.9). The measurement with the least margin to a limit is the Linux laptop's: format 14.0 ms of 16.0 (one run, 12.5 in the other) and the engine first page 96.9 ms of 100. **Later the same day:** the owner ratified the reading of the 16 ms criterion and of `typing-snapshot` (entry "Raffica Write: the reading of the 16 ms criterion (M4.3)"), and `measure.yml` judged each platform against these limits with `check: ok` on all three machines (Windows PC run 44891528598, Linux laptop run 44891531372, Intel Mac run 44891534043; the Linux engine first page measured 91.2 ms of 100).

## 2026-11-21 – Raffica Write: the cost of a key on the laptops (M4.3 follow-up)
**Context:** The owner's runs of the 16 ms criterion for `typing` and `format` (worst p95, CPU cost of the key handler, the UI render pass and the render-thread draws) were over the limit on the Linux laptop (i5-8265U: typing 32.4 ms, format 32.8 ms) and borderline on the Intel Mac (16.35 and 17.4 ms), while the Windows PC was at about 3.3 ms. The engine bench (editor and layout without the app) takes 0.4 to 1 ms for the same edits, so the cost was in the app.
**Decision:** Find it with recorded, never judged rows instead of guessing. The typing and format rows carry the split of the handler (`typing-split` and `format-split` families of `measure.cs`: the phases of `EditSession.Apply` and, inside the change event, the steps of the view and of the tab, filled by `EditPhases`, which only a measurement run sets), the allocation per key and the collections during the handlers (`alloc-*-kb`, `gc0`, `gc1`, `gc2`, `gc-pause-ms`), and the power state in the machine notes. Three causes, three fixes: (1) the window's handler of the view's change event ended in `RefreshCommandStates`, a read of every ribbon command, on every key (4.4 to 5 ms in a body paragraph and 8.6 in a table cell on the laptop): once the tab has an editing session it is deferred through a 100 ms debounce, `ViewStatesRefresh` (before the first caret or key nothing is deferred and no timer exists); (2) `LayoutEffectiveFormat` rebuilt, for every new revision (every edit), a dictionary of every paragraph of the story, so one bold toggle in the thesis allocated 1.2 to 1.5 MB, which is 27 gen 0 and 14 gen 1 collections and 170 ms of pause in 100 keys on the laptop (each collection costs 4 to 7 ms there; the heavy tail of the formatting operation): a paragraph that is a block of its story is answered by a reference search and never earns a walk, the walk records only the paragraphs in tables and reads no geometry, and the geometry of a table is read when a paragraph of that table is first asked for; (3) none besides these. The limit stays 16.0 ms on every platform and nothing was loosened.
**Result:** Linux laptop: typing 11.4 and 10.9 ms, format 13.7 and 12.5 ms (two runs after the last change, engine first page 95.7 and 96.9 ms of 100); Intel Mac: typing 5.7 ms, format 5.5 ms; the Windows PC measured 3.3 ms before the fixes (run again after them: open). A bold toggle allocates 26 KB instead of 1247 KB (scratch test on the thesis).
**Rationale:** A key must not cost more than a frame on a laptop of 2019; the ribbon is about the state of commands, which a pause shows as well as every key does, and an index of every paragraph is wasted work for the nearly every paragraph that has no cell.
**Rejected alternatives:** A larger gen 0 budget or no-GC regions around a key (moves the pause, does not remove the allocation); a limit per platform (the criterion is one number); caching the index across revisions (a paragraph object can be replaced by an edit, so an identity-keyed index goes stale; a structure revision would be new machinery).
**Known limits:** typing still allocates about 336 KB per key (the layout, 2.2 to 2.8 ms, and its collections are now the largest part of the handler); the table-cell cases have the least margin (format 14.7 ms p95 in one run); the Mac's second-file case failed once on a night run with no first-page line in 45 s (the Mac's display or session asleep is the likely cause, unproven; the first failure of that Mac was a display asleep).

## 2026-11-17 – Raffica Write: new documents and recovery (M4.3c)
**Context:** Milestone 4.3.3 ("4.3c") lets a person start a document in Write and makes a crash lose no text: a New document command with Write's own built-in style set, untitled tabs, a recovery copy of unsaved work every 30 s, and an offer to restore the copies at the next start. It also closes the 4.2 follow-up of the picture cache (a byte budget, a pixel cap, no decode on the render thread). Creating or restyling styles, templates and "New from template", an edit journal, recovery of the PDF app and the 4.8 hardening of hostile files are not in it. The shared crash guard, which the 4.3a decision listed as a 4.3c follow-up, is **not** part of 4.3c (owner, 2026-11-17): it moves to 4.9 with the update flow, the default-app services and the print services (`Docs/roadmap.json`, "Scope changes"). Design: `Docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-17-raffica-write-4-3c-new-documents-and-recovery-design.md` (approach A, a full DOCX snapshot through the existing guarded writer; the owner's "your pick"); plan: `Docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-17-milestone-04-3c-new-documents-and-recovery.md`.
**Decision:**
- **The blank document (`Raffica.Write.Docx/Blank`).** `BlankDocument.Create(PaperSize)` writes the bytes of a minimal package by hand (10 parts: content types, relationships, `document.xml` with one empty paragraph and the final section, `styles.xml`, `numbering.xml`, `settings.xml`, `fontTable.xml`, core and app properties; the zip entry times are fixed, so the same input gives the same bytes), and the tab opens them with `DocxFile.Open(bytes)`: a normal `DocxFile` with no origin, so the 4.1 round trip, the save guard and the layout need no special case. It lives in Docx, not in the model (the model references nothing). The SDK's schema validation of the saved bytes reports no error (`BlankDocumentTests.SavedBlankDocumentPassesSchemaValidation`, which also checks the child order of the styles). Nothing of it exists before "New document" runs.
- **The style set: 24 ids** (Normal, Heading 1 to 9, Title, Caption, TOC 1 to 9, Footnote Text, List Paragraph, Hyperlink; the plan said 33 by a miscount, the list it named has 24 and `BlankStyleSet.Ids` and its test say 24), plus `DefaultParagraphFont`, `TableNormal` and `NoList` so Word and the resolver behave. Body: Carlito 11 pt, 8 pt after, line 1.1 (Word's modern default metrics; Carlito is shipped). Headings: Caladea bold, 20, 16, 14, 12, 11, 11 italic, 11, 11 italic and 11 pt for levels 1 to 9, space before and after (pt) 18/6, 16/5, 14/4, 12/3, 10/3, 10/3, 8/2, 8/2, 8/2, with keep-with-next and keep-lines: a clear hierarchy for levels 1 to 4, the deep levels stay at body size and differ by italics so they do not shout. Colours tie the headings to the Write ink of `PRODUCT.md` (direction D: the accent sparingly): Heading 1 to 3 and Title `1D4F91` (8.6:1 on white), Heading 4 to 9 `2F3F57` (about 10.6:1); a test requires at least 4.5:1 (WCAG AA) for headings and Title. Title: Caladea bold 28 pt, tracking -0.3 pt, 12 pt after. Caption: Carlito italic 9 pt, `595959` (7:1), 3 pt before and 10 pt after. Hyperlink: `0B5CAD` with a single underline (about 6.6:1; not covered by the contrast test). TOC n: left indent (n - 1) x 220 twips, 5 pt after. Footnote Text: 10 pt, no space after, single line. List Paragraph: indent 720, contextual spacing. Headings are outline levels 0 to 8 and carry **no `numPr`**: a new document shows "Introduction", not "1 Introduction"; numbered headings are 4.4's command.
- **Three numbering definitions.** `abstractNum` 0 is a multilevel decimal (`%1.`, `%1.%2.` up to nine levels) linked to no style, 1 is a bullet list (nine levels), 2 is a numbered list (decimal, lower letter, lower roman, cycling); `num` 1, 2 and 3 point at them. The spec said "the one multi-level definition"; the builder read it as one multilevel definition for 4.4's heading numbering to switch on plus separate bullet and number-list definitions for the list styles (ruled in Task 1). The bullet levels use the `Symbol` and `Wingdings` private-use glyphs as Word does; Write's layout has no such font, so a bullet may draw as a fallback glyph until 4.4 decides the list rendering.
- **Paper by region.** `BlankPaper.For(RegionInfo)`: US and CA give Letter (12240 x 15840 twips, 1 inch margins), every other region A4 (11906 x 16838, 1417 twips = 2.5 cm margins); header and footer distance 709. `WriteWindow.PaperSource` is the seam the tests use.
- **Untitled tabs.** `WriteTab.Path` is `string?`; `IsUntitled`, and `SuggestedSavePath` (the name and folder a Save as offers). The title is "Untitled", "Untitled 2" and so on, German "Unbenannt" and "Unbenannt 2", the smallest free number among the untitled tabs of the window (`UntitledNames.Next`); it is fixed when the tab is made (no re-title on a language change). A new tab is never in the recent list (`Touch` is skipped for a null path) and never a hit of `WriteWorkspace.Find`. Save on an untitled tab takes the Save as branch (`SaveCommandState.Untitled`, `WriteSaveFlow`); Save as binds it (`Rebind` clears the suggestion and the tab enters the recent list through the normal path). An untitled tab that was never edited closes without a question, an edited one asks as any dirty tab. A layout fault of an untitled tab shows no open-error card.
- **New document (`file.new`).** Ctrl+N (Cmd+N on macOS), the existing Lucide icon `FilePlus` (no other command uses it), field code and key tip `H16` (Home, File group), a description, strings in both languages, a line in `WriteMacMenu` (before Open), enabled with or without a document and disabled with a reason while the window closes. `OpenFlow.NewDocumentAsync` makes the tab; when the blank cannot be made (it never fails in practice) the window shows the open-error card naming "Untitled" instead of nothing. The Home band's width hint is unchanged (`HomeFullWidthAtLeast` 2500, `RibbonStartupTests`); `StartupCreatesNoBlankDocumentWork` pins that nothing exists before first use. The tab strip's "+" button and the start view have no New (out of scope: the "+" opens the file dialog as before).
- **A recovery copy is a pair: `<id>.docx` plus `<id>.json`** in `<AppFiles.ConfigDirectoryFor(settingsFile)>/recovery` (`RecoveryStore.FolderFor`), so `--settings-file` redirects the folder and tests and measurements never touch the user's. The id is 32 lower-case hex digits (a GUID in "N" format), checked before any path is built (`DocxPathOf` throws `ArgumentException` on a bad id, `List` ignores a file whose name is not an id), so a hostile name cannot leave the folder. **The sidecar is the commit marker**: it holds the id, the title (the tab's name, a file name or "Untitled"), the original path or null, the time written, the size of the DOCX, the owner process id and the owner's start time, **never document text** (no text goes into a file name, a log line, an exception message or the sidecar). The DOCX is written first, the sidecar second, both through `AtomicFile` (write next to the target, flush, replace); `List` shows only pairs with a readable, complete sidecar whose `Id` equals its file name and a DOCX that exists, newest first; anything else is a *problem* kept on disk (listing never deletes). A sidecar is untrusted data (`IsComplete`: a title, a size of at least 0, a process id above 0). `Delete` removes the DOCX **first** and the sidecar only when that worked, so a copy that cannot be removed stays listed and a later delete retries it. Nothing creates the folder before the first copy (`EnsureFolder` is the writer's call).
- **Who owns a copy: the liveness probe.** An entry whose owner process is alive and is not this one belongs to a running instance and is **never offered and never deleted**; a process that is gone, or whose id was reused by a process that started more than 2 seconds away from the recorded start, left a crash's copy (`RecoveryLiveness.IsLive`, `IProcessProbe`). A process that exists but whose start time cannot be read (another user, elevated) counts as **live** (`DateTimeOffset.MaxValue` is the documented sentinel; the first version mapped it to "dead", which broke the never-touch-a-live-owner rule). The single-instance rule stays the first line (a second instance hands over and does not look); this is the second, for a failed hand-over. Copies of this process (its own earlier copies) are offerable by design.
- **The snapshot (`RecoverySnapshot.WriteAsync`, UI thread only).** It writes a copy through the existing writer (`DocxFile.SaveSnapshotAsync`, the same `Save` as `SaveAsAsync` with a snapshot flag) under the **model-read lease**, which `WriteSaveFlow` and the snapshot now share (`ModelReadLease.Attach`, extracted unchanged from the save flow: the pool thread takes the editing lease inside `DocxFile.BeforeModelRead` and releases it in `AfterModelRead`, in a `finally`). It never calls `MarkSaved` and never changes the origin, the stamp or the snapshot of the `DocxFile`; a failed copy returns `Failed` (never throws, `OutOfMemoryException` excepted), is silent (no card, no dirty mark, typing never waits for it), and the exception is not shown. The revision is read inside the lease (it is the revision of what is written). Skips (`SnapshotSkip`): the tab is closed, only a prefix is open, the session is faulted, a save runs, or a snapshot of the same file runs (`Busy`); the skips that depend on thread-unsafe state are checked on the UI thread before and a second, thread-safe set (`IInLeaseSnapshotCheck`: `Tab.IsDisposed`, `Docx.IsPrefix`, `Runner.IsFaulted`, `Editor.IsSaving` through a `Volatile.Read`) again on the pool thread inside the lease. **A per-`DocxFile` in-flight marker** (`ConditionalWeakTable`, set before the first await, `IsRunning` and `WhenIdleAsync`) serializes a snapshot against a save and against another snapshot, because both ride the same `BeforeModelRead` and `AfterModelRead` hooks of one `DocxFile`: without it a snapshot overlapping a save clobbered the hooks and could fail the user's save. `WriteSaveFlow.WriteAsync` waits for an idle file (`while (IsRunning) await WhenIdleAsync`) before it takes the lease, with no await between the last check and the hook attach; the UI thread is free while it waits. The first copy of a document writes the DOCX, then the sidecar; if the sidecar write fails in this process the DOCX the call created is deleted again (a failed later copy leaves an old sidecar beside a whole new DOCX, both openable).
- **The block cache and the attribution rule (the fix that makes the 16 ms criterion hold).** The first version of the snapshot held the lease for the whole serialization of the main part (about 13 ms on the thesis, a median of 13.0 ms, p95 21.5, max 24.6 in the bench; 90 percent of it is the writer of `document.xml`, 1,488,029 bytes, and it is writer-bound: a write into `Stream.Null` costs the same; all other parts together are about 1 ms, so reusing unchanged parts could not help). `BodySnapshotCache` (Docx, internal) keeps the serialized bytes of every top-level body block keyed by block identity; a pass writes only the blocks that need it into one buffer and takes the rest from the cache, and the output is prefix, blocks, suffix (a block's XML does not depend on its siblings, only on the namespaces the root and `w:body` declare, and a stand-in first child pins the `>` of `<w:body>`; if that shape assumption fails the pass falls back to a plain full write). **The attribution rule:** `EditTracker` (Model) is made under the lease at the first copy and held weakly; `DocumentEdits.Raise` and `Restore` count a raise and **every raise is followed by `DocumentEdits.Attribute(changes)`** with the `BlockChange` list the edit already returns (the list the incremental layout relies on, which `LayoutOracle` proves exact); the editor attributes after each of its raise sites, after `Undo` and after `Redo`. A body change in `Body.Blocks` marks the blocks in `[First, First + Added)` dirty; a change of a list inside a block (a table cell, a content control) marks every non-paragraph block dirty; a change in another story (notes, headers, comments) touches another part, which is written live every time. **Safe by default:** a tracker whose raise count differs from its attribution count is incomplete and the whole body is written again, as is a failed pass or a block the cache has not seen (so an edit path that does not attribute, such as the restored copy's `Raise()` in `WriteWindow.Recovery`, costs time and never correctness). `SnapshotCacheOracleTests.TheCacheEqualsAFullWriteAcrossARandomEditSequence` runs seeded real-editor sequences (type, Enter, delete across paragraphs, Backspace, copy, paste, bold, alignment, undo, redo, in the body, tables, content controls, headers and notes) on 7 corpus files (the thesis 60 steps, the others 100 to 150), takes a cache pass after one to three operations and asserts **byte equality with the user-save writer's main part** after every pass and that blocks were reused; mutation checks (dropping the dirty marking: 6 of 7 cases fail; dropping the nested mark: 4 fail). The user's Save and Save as never read the cache. An `EditTrackerTests` set (7) and `ATypedParagraphIsTheOnlyBlockWrittenAgain` (the thesis: 1 block written, 1508 reused) pin the rule. The first copy of a session, and a copy after an unattributed edit, still write the whole body inside the lease (about 13 ms once).
- **A recovery copy has a lighter guard than a user save (deliberate, documented at `DocxFile.Guard` and `SaveSnapshotAsync`).** A user save's guard reads the written main part back into a whole model and compares; for a copy written 3.5 times a second that read-back allocated 20.4 MiB per copy (a copy 25.1 MiB in all) and, in one back-to-back run, caused 1462 collections, **100 of them gen2, and 11.4 s of GC pause**, whose pauses (about 8 ms each) hit about one key in ten and were the larger part of the remaining 7 ms of the p95 once the lease was short. A copy's guard now compares the written bytes of every model part with the package in 80 KiB chunks (no new array) and reads the main part back **as well-formed XML with a `w:document` root** (no DTD; any reader exception other than out-of-memory becomes `DocxSaveException(ModelNotReadable)`), not as a model: 7.3 MiB per copy (a plain save 28.9 MiB). **The consequence: a writer bug can now replace a good older copy with one that does not open as a model**; that is found when the copy is opened (and then reported and kept like any unreadable copy), not when it is written. `DocxSnapshotGuardTests` (12 cases, 9 of them fail when the byte compare and the read-back are disabled) pin that a copy with an altered `document.xml` (one byte more, one byte flipped) or a removed small model part is refused and leaves no file, that `ReadBackWellFormed` refuses seven kinds of bad input and accepts a good one, and that a copy's package entries equal a `SaveAsync` of the same model after four edit rounds. User Save and Save as keep the full guard.
- **The scheduler (`AutosaveScheduler`, one per window, made when the first tab becomes dirty).** A clock (`IRecoveryClock`; `DispatcherRecoveryClock` is a `DispatcherTimer` on the dispatcher captured when it is made, at Background priority) beats every **30 s** (`AutosaveScheduler.Interval`) from the first edit (never at start) and `WriteWindow` calls `TickNow` when the window is deactivated. A pass writes one copy per tracked tab whose revision differs from the last one it wrote (the scheduler tracks that itself: the stateless `WriteAsync` does not skip an unchanged revision), **one snapshot at a time** (a tick during a pass sets a rerun flag, it never stacks a second pass); a failure is counted (`FailureCount`) and tried again at the next tick. A copy is deleted when its tab is clean again (after a Save or a Save as marked the revision saved, or an undo reached the saved revision), never while a snapshot of the same file runs, and when a tab is released: **only after the close questions answered Save or Don't save, or when the tab was clean** (`WriteWindow.ReleaseRecovery`; a Cancel, a bare dispose of the window or a tab disposed without the guard keep the copy, which the next start offers). A release during a snapshot deletes what it wrote when it ends. The setting is read live at every tick. The wiring is guarded: `OnTabDirtyChanged` is skipped in a measurement run and wrapped in a try/catch that counts `RecoveryWiringFailures` (autosave must never break the edit that raised the event), and the scheduler is disposed without deleting when the window closes.
- **The setting.** `ShellSettings.Autosave` (the shell's stored value, on by default) is shown in Write's settings dialog under Write's own label, because the shell's "Autosave" would say that the user's file is saved by itself, which Write never does: English "Keep a recovery copy of unsaved work", German "Wiederherstellungskopie ungespeicherter Arbeit anlegen" (54 characters). `SettingsDialogOptions.AutosaveLabel` is a new optional member (null keeps the shell's text, so the PDF app and the gallery are unchanged). Off means no copies are written and nothing is deleted; copies that exist are still offered at the next start.
- **The offer and Restore (`RecoveryFlow`, `RecoveryCoordinator`, `RecoveryCard`).** Once per process (`RecoveryCoordinator.OfferOnce`, an `Interlocked` flag; one coordinator held by `App`), after the first sharp page of the first window or, for a window with no file, after `Opened` and an idle moment; also when the window was started with a file that failed to open and has no tab; never in a measurement run and never for a closing window; nothing of the folder is read before that. The list is read on a pool thread and entries with a live owner are dropped. One card (`CardFooter.Build(Restore, [Discard], Later)`, Esc is Later) names each copy's title and time (never text from the document), at most 20 (`MaxListed`) and then one line "... and N more"; a title is shortened to `WriteWindow.MaxShownNameLength` (120). **Restore** opens every entry through the ordinary open path on the copy's bytes (`OpenFlow.OpenRecoveredAsync`, no handle on the copy, no origin on the `DocxFile`) as a **dirty, unbound tab**: titled with the original file name or "Untitled N", no path, `SuggestedSavePath` = the original path, so Save on it is Save as with the old name and folder suggested and the picker's overwrite confirmation is the user's "yes, replace it" (a recovered text never overwrites the file it came from silently, and a restored tab has no stamp to check against). The tab is **adopted under the entry's id before the dirty mark** (`new WriteTabRecoverySubject(tab, entry.Id)`, `Adopt`, then `Edits.Raise()` and `UpdateDirty`), so the copy stays until the tab is saved or closed with Save or Don't save (a second crash does not lose it) and the next tick replaces it in place instead of making a second copy; a clean adopted copy stays until its tab was dirty once (in the real window wiring this is moot: `OpenRecoveredAsync` adopts, then `Raise` and `UpdateDirty` fire `DirtyChanged`, `OnTabDirtyChanged` calls `Track` and clears `Adopted` at once; harmless, because a restored tab starts at revision 1 against a saved 0 and becomes clean only by a save). **Discard** deletes exactly the offered entries (all of them, also those beyond the 20 listed) on a pool thread; **Later** and Esc keep everything. A copy that cannot be opened is kept and reported once: one failure is the open-error card, several are one `OpenFailuresCard` (names up to 5, then "and N more"), never one card each; the other copies are still restored.
- **The picture cache (`ImageCache`, `ImageCacheLimits`).** Budget **256 MiB** of decoded pixels (`ByteBudget`, 4 bytes per pixel assumed), cap **32,000,000 pixels** per picture (`MaxPixels`; the constructor requires `MaxPixels * 4 * 2 <= ByteBudget`, so the cap is at most half the budget, one picture can never exceed it and two of the largest allowed fit; a test pins the relation and the cache re-validates `with` copies and `default`). The values are the designer's call from the spec, set before measuring and kept: the thesis has one small inline picture and the corpus pictures are far under the cap. A picture whose header says more than the cap is not decoded and is drawn as the marked box (as a metafile is); the header is read from the first 256 KiB (`ImageHeader`, shared with `ImageProbe`). Least-recently-used eviction at insert (a linked list under the lock; `Get` and `TryGet` hits touch it); an evicted image goes to a **retired list that only `ReleaseRetired` disposes**, which `PageDrawOperation` calls **after each draw, inside the draw gate** (a draw that may hold the image has ended), so eviction never frees a picture a draw is using and the gate order of 4.3a ("closed before the cache is disposed") is unchanged. `TryGet` never decodes; `Get` still decodes (the bench, the tests and the pixel oracle use it, so "pixel output unchanged" compares pictures with pictures); `Warm` and `Prefetch` take a `CancellationToken`, decode outside the lock (the first insert wins, the loser disposes its copy) and skip a picture another thread has in flight (so two overlapping calls decode once and the budget cannot be exceeded by duplicates); `IsKnown` answers "decoded or failed" without a touch; `Clear` retires everything. The app's cache is made with `decodeOnGet: false`, so **the render thread only calls `TryGet` and `ReleaseRetired`**.
- **Pictures in the open path and the page view.** The opener decodes the pictures of page 1 on its pool thread before the first sharp page (`Warm` with the open's token; both return paths), so the first draw finds them. `PageView` asks the cache what is unknown for the pages in view and one page each side from `RaiseViewChanged` (the one place after every scroll, zoom, runner change and edit, undo and redo), off the render pass, and prefetches **one task at a time**: a range with pictures the running task does not cover is stored as the next range (the latest wins) and cancels the running task after its current picture; a decoded picture raises `Decoded` on a pool thread and the view posts one coalesced invalidation through the dispatcher it captured in its constructor. A picture not decoded yet is the marked box, drawn at once and replaced when the decode arrives. `PackageImageSource.Read` is now called from pool threads (it is thread-safe: one lock covers `Read`, `Retarget`, `Dispose` and the lazy archive). **Ownership rule:** a failed read caches null for that id, which is deterministic because the package is an in-memory snapshot and `Retarget` keeps the ids valid, so the app never calls `Clear` today; **a future "insert picture" (4.4) must call `Clear` and trigger a prefetch** (a picture id reused for different bytes is the one case the view cannot see; a picture that an edit or undo moves into view is found, because edits reach `RaiseViewChanged`). **A prefetch restarts only after a cancelled task** (`PrefetchEnded` asks again when the finished task was cancelled and no next range was kept, the case of a range that was scrolled back to before the cancelled task ended); never after a task that ran to the end, because a range holding more than the cache budget evicts what it decoded and "not known" would never end (`ARangeWhosePicturesExceedTheBudgetDoesNotPrefetchForever`).
- **Measuring.** The app switch `--measure-typing-snapshot` runs the plain `typing` plan and starts a loop that calls `RecoverySnapshot.WriteAsync(new WriteTabRecoverySubject(tab), store, token)` back to back into the folder that follows the settings file (the window's own scheduler stays off in a measurement run), waits 5 ms after a skip, fails the run on a failed copy (naming only the exception type) or on no copy at all (it waits up to 60 s on a completion signal, not a sleep, for a first copy still packaging when the run ends), and prints `snapshot n=... lease-median-ms=... lease-max-ms=... total-median-ms=... total-max-ms=...` with the blocks written per copy, whole passes and the GC counters. `measure.cs` has the case `typing-snapshot` (3 runs): its rows `typing-snapshot/<case>-<view>-median|p95` count into the judged **`typing`** metric (`WorstTypingP95`, keyed by case plus run, 16.0 ms on every platform) and the rows `snapshot/lease-ms`, `lease-max-ms`, `total-ms`, `total-max-ms`, `count`, the blocks and GC rows are recorded and never judged. The bench has `write-layout-bench edit --snapshot [--runs n]` (lease and total of a recovery copy and of a plain save). **The criterion is read as the spec asks and no further:** snapshots back to back (3.5 a second) is a harsher case than the real cadence of one copy per 30 s (a key meets a lease about once in 30 s), judged on CPU cost including the wait for the lease, with the frame latency recorded, not judged; the limit was not loosened, the read was bounded.
**Measured** (the Windows PC, Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26200; Native AOT win-x64 publishes of the app and the bench, software rendering, 0 hits of `IL[23][0-9]{3}` in both publish logs; the generated thesis, 311 pages; the implementer's figures; **the machine was noisy that day**: the tool's desktop composition warning (3.8 Hz) on every run, and plain typing at 12 to 13 ms against 4.9 to 6.9 ms on the quiet days of the 4.3a and 4.3b entries):

| What | Result | Limit or reference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `typing-snapshot`, full write inside the lease (the first version), 3 launches | worst p95 25.13, 30.57, 21.35 ms | 16.0 ms: **not met** |
| `typing-snapshot`, block cache only, guard unchanged, 3 launches | 19.96, 19.74, 21.18 ms | 16.0 ms |
| `typing-snapshot`, final build, 5 launches, back to back | 10.52, 12.37, 10.67, 9.89, 9.83 ms; judged (median over launches of each case's p95, then the worst case) 9.98 ms | 16.0 ms: ok |
| Plain `--measure-typing`, same session, 3 launches | 13.09, 12.92, 11.97 ms | 16.0 ms (quiet day: 4.4 to 4.9 ms) |
| `--measure-format`, same session, 3 launches | 13.17, 12.15, 13.70 ms | 16.0 ms (quiet day: 7.32, 7.20, 10.76 ms) |
| Lease held by a recovery copy of the thesis (bench, 30 runs, median / p95 / max) | 1.39 / 1.68 / 1.70 ms; 13.0 / 21.5 / 24.6 ms before the cache (plain save in the same run 13.1 / 19.9 / 30.6) | recorded |
| Total time of a copy of the thesis (median / p95 / max) | 95.0 / 116.7 / 131.0 ms; 135 ms before (plain save 135.5 / 161.8 / 172.0) | recorded |
| Allocation per copy | 25.1 MiB before, 7.3 MiB after (plain save 28.9 MiB); gen2 collections 13 in 60 saves | recorded |
| The copy of the thesis on disk | 0.37 MiB | |
| Cold start to the first sharp page, 10 runs | 245.7 ms (first pass 245.8) | 4.3b after its fix: 268 and 260 ms |
| Warm start, 10 runs | 241.8 ms (first pass 246.5) | 4.3b after its fix: 236, 238, 249 ms |
| Start with the thesis, 10 runs | 258.5 ms | none recorded |
| First page of the thesis (`write-layout-bench run`, median of 10 cold runs) | 51.1 ms | 49.6 to 50.4 ms earlier; limit 100 |
| First page of `poi-60329` (inline pictures), `poi-60316` (floating), `poi-shapes-with-text` | 67.0, 51.5, 45.8 ms | no earlier figures recorded |

Not measured: `--measure-format` against a picture-heavy file, the start of a restore of many copies (the 50-tab restore cost is unmeasured), the Linux laptop and the Intel Mac.

**Full proof of the milestone branch** (2026-11-17, at commit `fdfe0d5`; the commit after it changes documents only): `dotnet build Raffica.slnx -c Release`: 0 warnings, 0 errors; `RAFFICA_REQUIRE_OCR=1 dotnet test Raffica.slnx -c Release --no-build --max-parallel-test-modules 1` (the OCR library fetched by `native-fetch --rid win-x64`, so no OCR test skipped): 8422 total, 8372 passed, **0 failed**, 50 skipped in 12 min 57 s; `dotnet format Raffica.slnx --verify-no-changes` exit 0; `dotnet run tools/roadmap.cs -- check` valid and up to date; `KilledProcessRecoveryTests` 2 of 2 inside the run; the Native AOT publishes of the app and the bench were made in Task 12 (win-x64, 0 hits of `IL[23][0-9]{3}`). **All 50 skips, from the test output:** 18 measurement-only or sample-writing tests that need an output folder (12 `RAFFICA_MEASURE_OUT`: `AnnotationMeasurementTests` 3, `FormMeasurementTests`, `FormOpenMeasurementTests`, `OcrMeasurementTests` 2, `OrganizeMeasurementTests`, `RedactMeasurementTests` 2, `RedactOverlayDrawTests`, `TextEditMeasurementTests`; 6 `RAFFICA_INTEROP_OUT`: `InteropSampleTests`); 11 `MacSwapScriptTests` cases that need `/bin/sh` (not on Windows); 6 `CupsPrintServiceTests` (CUPS is Linux); 5 Unix file-mode tests (`DocxSaveTests.UnixPermissionsOfTheTargetAreKept`, `DocumentSaverTests.UnixPermissionsOfTheTargetAreKept`, `SignatureStoreTests.UnixFilesAreOwnerOnly`, `SingleInstanceTests.RestrictingASocketLeavesReadAndWriteForTheOwnerOnly`, `JsonFileLimitsTests.ConfigFilesAreWrittenForTheOwnerOnly`); 3 macOS-only (`FileIdentityTests.MacOsAnswersAndDoesNotGiveUp`, `PlatformPrintServicesTests.CupsLibraryLoadsOnMacOs`, `MacDefaultAppServiceTests.LaunchServicesAnswersOnMacOs`); 4 Unix-only (`FileIdentityTests.TwoNamesOfOneFileAreTheSameFile`, `SingleInstanceLongTempFolderTests.LongTempFolderStillHandsOverFiles`, `SingleInstanceTests.TheSocketIsOpenToTheCurrentUserOnly`, `DocumentSaverTests.TheSystemReportsAFullDiskAsDiskFull` needs `/dev/full`); `FileIdentityTests.OtherNormalizationOfTheNameIsTheSameFile` (the volume keeps NFC and NFD names apart); `PrintSupportTests.PrintingToADeviceContextIsRejectedOffWindows`; and `CorpusLayoutTests.AcceptViewMatchesWordAcceptForTrackedChanges` (the owner's `word-accept.pdf` of `poi-tika-792` is absent). None is a Write 4.3c test.
**Rationale:** A copy is a whole DOCX through the writer every later milestone (4.4 to 4.6) already feeds, so everything Write can save is recovered without a second format or a replay that has to equal the live model (the risk the "incremental equals full" rule guards against); the sidecar as commit marker and the DOCX-first order mean a crash at any point leaves either nothing listable or a whole, openable pair, and a sidecar never names a missing file; the owner check keeps two running instances from offering or deleting each other's copies; the in-flight marker and the shared lease keep a snapshot and a save from sharing hooks of one file; the block cache turns a 13 ms read into a 1.4 ms one with an attribution rule that is safe by default and proven byte for byte against the user-save writer; the lighter guard trades a read-back that was most of the allocation and the GC pauses for an open-time check; a restored tab is unbound so a crash can never silently overwrite the file it came from; a retired list disposed inside the draw gate keeps eviction from freeing a picture a draw holds, without reference counting.
**Rejected alternatives:** An edit journal replayed after the crash, or a snapshot plus a journal (every kind of edit would need a record and a replay and 4.4 to 4.6 would each extend it; rejected in the spec); reusing unchanged parts only (the main part is 90 percent of the lease and typing always changes it); a structural clone of the model under the lease with the write outside it (the clone costs about as much as the write); slicing the read with a lease per block range (a copy would mix revisions); a shorter interval or a longer lease as the fix (the criterion is not loosened); binding a restored tab to its old path (a silent overwrite, no stamp to check); showing the shell's "Autosave" label (it would say that the user's file is saved by itself); reference-counted images for the draw (the retired list and the gate are enough); deleting a sidecar-less DOCX or listing it (a live instance can be between the two writes and a sidecar-less file has no owner to check, so offering or deleting it would break the live-owner rule); a shared crash guard in this milestone (owner: 4.9).
**Deviations from the plan and rulings:**
- *Task 1:* 24 style ids, not 33 (a miscount in the plan; the list it names has 24); the numbering reading above; the document language is `en-US` and the core properties carry a fixed created and modified date, 2026-01-01 (see the limits).
- *Task 3:* when the blank cannot be made, the existing open-error card names "Untitled" (`OpenError.Other`); the "+" button and the start view have no New.
- *Task 4 and 5:* a sidecar-less DOCX is **not** surfaced by `List` (ruling in Task 5, reasons above); `RecoveryStore.EnsureFolder` is public so the writer creates the folder; the first sidecar failure deletes the DOCX the call created.
- *Task 6:* a second interface `IInLeaseSnapshotCheck` (the in-lease check reads thread-safe state only; the first version read `WriteSaveFlow`'s dictionary on a pool thread, a data race that no test can show and the thread-safety argument rules out); the scheduler is gated off in a measurement run; `Adopt` of a clean subject keeps the only restored copy until its tab was dirty once (the scheduler's rule; the window wiring clears the flag at once, see the offer above).
- *Task 8:* one failure card for many corrupt copies (a first version queued one card per copy and again at every start); the offer also runs for a window whose file failed to open; Discard deletes on a pool thread; the tests build their original paths with `Path.Combine` (three hard-coded `C:\` paths failed on Linux and macOS).
- *Task 11:* `CloseGuardTests.CardsAreBuiltByCardFooter` lacked `RecoveryCard.Create` (a regression of Task 8 that its filtered run did not see) and was fixed in the Task 11 round; the prefetch skip key `(layout, first, last, count)` left a visible picture a marked box after an edit or undo until a scroll, and was replaced by asking the cache on every view change.
- *Task 12:* the first run was **blocked** (worst p95 21 to 31 ms against 16.0); the plan's own remedy (bound the model read, never loosen the limit) led to the block cache, the attribution rule and the lighter guard above; its review added `DocxSnapshotGuardTests` and the bounded wait for the first copy of a run.
- *Final review (fix wave, 2026-11-17):* a prefetch cancelled by a newer range and then scrolled back to before it ended is restarted (`PageView.PrefetchEnded` asks `PrefetchPictures` again when no next range was kept; `ARangeCancelledByANewerOneAndScrolledBackToBeforeItEndedIsDecodedInFull`, red before the fix); a layout fault of an untitled tab asks the commands again so Save and Save as show their reason (no card: a new or restored document has no file that failed to open); `RecoverySnapshot.WriteMarkedAsync` reads the process identity, creates the folder and looks at the sidecar on a pool thread (the in-flight mark is still set on the UI thread first); `RecoveryStore.EnsureFolder` creates the folder with mode 0700 on Linux and macOS (the test skips on Windows; CI proves it); the failure cards of a restore say that the recovery copy cannot be read and that a file saved earlier is not affected (`RecoveryErrorTitle`, `RecoveryErrorReason`, `RecoveryFailuresTitle`, `RecoveryFailuresMessage`, `recoveryCopy` and `recoveryCopies` of `OpenErrorCard` and `OpenFailuresCard`), and shown names are cut without splitting a surrogate pair (`WriteWindow.ShortName`); the autosave check box has the `wrap` class (`SettingsDialogTests.ALongGermanAutosaveLabelWrapsInsideTheCard`); the end of a `typing-snapshot` run is observed and stops its first-copy timer; the killed-process harness lost dead code and its waits are bounded; the bullet glyphs of the blank document are `\u` escapes.
- *Test-first honesty:* the tests of Tasks 1, 5, 6 and 8 and of the block cache of Task 12 were written together with or after the code, so no failing-first run exists for them; Tasks 5, 6, 8 and 12 are backed by named mutation checks instead (each mutation failed the named tests and was reverted); Task 1 has neither (its first runs failed on a build error and on the style count, then were green). Real RED runs were captured for Tasks 2, 3, 4, 7, 9, 10 and 11 and for the measurement tooling of Task 12.
**Known limits** (what remains after review; each is deliberate or deferred):
- *The first copy.* A crash in the few milliseconds between the DOCX write and the sidecar write of a **first** copy loses that copy and leaves an orphan DOCX that no listing shows and nothing cleans (a sweep of aged orphans is a 4.9 candidate; a later copy of a document is safe at every point). The killed-process test cannot hit that window.
- *Cancel on a later tab.* A close that answers Don't save for one tab and then Cancel on a later tab keeps the copies of the tabs already answered (conservative: they are offered at the next start).
- *Wording.* The restore failure cards say "recovery copy"; the rest of the app's wording for the feature (the settings label and the offer card) is as in 4.3c and open to the owner. A layout fault of an untitled tab shows no card and no status text: Save and Save as are disabled with their reason, which is all the user is told.
- *A restored copy's sidecar* keeps the dead owner's process id until its first new snapshot, so a second instance on the same folder in that time could offer or discard it (the single-instance rule makes it rare). The offer-once flag is claimed before the card is answered (a window closed during the card means no later window offers until the next start). The 50-tab restore cost is unmeasured and the oldest copy becomes the active tab (the list is newest first). Copies of the own process are offerable by design.
- *The in-lease check of a copy.* `WriteTabRecoverySubject`'s check on the pool thread inside the lease reads only thread-safe state (`IsDisposed`, `IsPrefix`, `Runner.IsFaulted`, `Editor.IsSaving`). That covers the editor session's faulted state too: `EditSession.BlockedReason` reports `Faulted` only when the tab is disposed or `Runner.IsFaulted`, and the check reads exactly those, so a session that faults between the early check and the lease is not copied. `Adopted` is also cleared when a subject is seen dirty even if that tick's snapshot was skipped.
- *Temporary files in the recovery folder.* `AtomicFile.NewTemporaryPath` puts the temporary DOCX or sidecar of a write into the recovery folder itself; a process killed in the middle of a write leaves a temporary file there that holds document text, is never listed and is never removed (the orphan sweep of 4.9 must cover it). The folder is made for its owner only (0700) on Linux and macOS; an existing folder keeps its mode.
- *Liveness of an unreadable owner.* `RecoveryLiveness.IsLive` treats an owner whose start time cannot be read as live, so after a crash and a reboot a copy whose old process id now belongs to a protected process is not offered until that process ends (conservative; the text stays on disk).
- *A snapshot in flight at Release.* When a snapshot is running as its tab is released, the copy is deleted later, in the `AutosaveScheduler` `StepAsync` continuation; if the last window closes and the process exits first, the copy of a "Don't save" document survives and is offered at the next start (a privacy nuisance, not a loss of data).
- *The macOS New item* is a plain menu `Item`, not a `CommandItem`, so its state is not read from the command; `OpenFlow.NewDocumentAsync` checks `IsClosing` again.
- *Cost of the first copy.* The first copy of a session and a copy after an unattributed edit (a restore) write the whole body inside the lease, about 13 ms once. The cache keeps every block's bytes and a non-shrinking buffer for the life of the `DocxFile` (2 to 3 times the body).
- *The lighter guard* (above): a writer bug can replace a good older copy with one that does not open as a model.
- *The blank document.* The document language is a fixed `en-US` in `docDefaults` and the created and modified dates of the core properties are fixed at 2026-01-01 (so the bytes are reproducible); neither follows the user. The bullets use the `Symbol` and `Wingdings` glyphs and may draw as a fallback glyph until 4.4. The Hyperlink and Caption colours are not contrast-tested. Word's acceptance of the file is unchecked (owner gate).
- *Pictures.* A picture id that is reused for different bytes needs `ImageCache.Clear` and a prefetch (the rule for a future insert-picture feature; edits and undo already reach `RaiseViewChanged`, so a picture that an edit moves into an unchanged range is prefetched, `AnEditThatMovesAPictureIntoViewIsPrefetchedWithoutScrolling`); the cache assumes 4 bytes per pixel and has a retired list that holds evicted images until the next draw ends (not counted in the budget while no frame draws); the header read is limited to 256 KiB (the layout agrees); a duplicate concurrent decode can briefly hold two copies; the opener's `Warm` has no cancellation test (the cache's cancellation is tested). The page-1 decode now sits on the first-page path (moved from the render thread, same total work; measured above, no change on the thesis).
- *Measurement.* `typing-snapshot` is the harsher back-to-back case and the Windows PC was noisy that day (one launch of an intermediate build measured 40.5 ms as an outlier); a quiet re-run and the other machines are open. `EditTracker.Take` compares counts, not pairings, so an unbalanced attribution that happens to match counts would be trusted (the oracle covers every attribution site the editor has; a new raise site needs an attribution and an oracle case); `LastSnapshotPass` is public for diagnostics. `EndSnapshotRunAsync` of the measurement catches what it throws (the type of the exception ends the run) and stops its 60 s first-copy delay when the first copy arrives.
- *Tests.* The killed-process test proves the real scheduler, store and writer under a harness lease, not the app's `LayoutRunner` lease, the dispatcher clock or Native AOT; a kill lands at a random moment; the Linux and macOS apphost path of the harness is proven by CI only; the Deactivated-to-`TickNow` wiring is untested (a headless window cannot raise it; `TickNow` is tested). The harness's `PrintNewCopy` records `LastWritten` at lease time, not at commit time, so "the restored paragraph count is at least the last printed count" has a theoretical microsecond window in which it can flake; the second test rarely kills in the middle of a write (it kills during typing after the third copy, with no offset or loop to hit the write). Several negative assertions wait 300 ms.
**Follow-ups:** the crash guard, an orphan sweep (aged orphan DOCX files and the temporary DOCX or sidecar files that a process killed in the middle of a write leaves in the recovery folder) in 4.9; `Clear` and a prefetch with the first insert-picture feature (4.4); a shared `Measurement` class for both apps (carried from 4.2); a summary of the 50-tab restore; a deterministic test of the 60 s first-copy wait.
**Open (owner gates; nothing here is measured or decided on a real machine yet; the open gates of the 4.2, 4.3a and 4.3b entries stay open and are not claimed here):** (1) **Word opens a blank document made by Write and a recovery copy that was restored and saved** without a repair prompt and shows the style set as designed: steps and the empty result table in `Docs/write-word-check.md`, section "New document and recovery (milestone 4.3c)". (2) Kill and restart on a real display: the Native AOT app on Windows, the Linux laptop and the Intel Mac is killed during typing and the next start offers the text. (3) The autosave check box in the settings dialog on a real display, and the German label (54 characters, the label wraps, pinned by a headless layout test; a real display is still wanted). (4) `typing` (judged, with `typing-snapshot`) and the start cases on the Linux laptop and the Intel Mac: `gh workflow run measure.yml --ref <ref> -f platform=<windows|linux|macos> -f target=write -f note="<machine>" -f ref=<ref>` and `write-layout-bench edit --snapshot --file testdata/write-corpus/thesis/thesis.docx` on a Native AOT publish of that machine; a quiet re-run on the Windows PC. (5) **The owner decides** whether the back-to-back reading of the `typing-snapshot` criterion stands (see "Measuring").

## 2026-11-16 – Raffica Write: formatting and styles (M4.3b)
**Context:** Milestone 4.3.2 ("4.3b") lets a person format text in Write and answer "why does this paragraph look like that?": direct character and paragraph formatting in every story 4.3a edits, paragraph and character styles from the document's own list, the formatting inspector pane (style lines, one removable row per direct override, "Update style to match", "Clear direct formatting") and undo and redo for all of it. Creating or restyling styles in a dialog, lists and numbering (4.4), tab stops, borders and shading, table formatting (4.5) and tracked formatting (4.6) are not in it; the built-in style set comes with 4.3c. Design: `Docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-16-raffica-write-formatting-design.md` (approach B, owner's pick); plan: `Docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-16-milestone-04-3b-formatting-and-styles.md`.
**Decision:**
- **Two kinds of edit.** Direct formatting rides the 4.3a path unchanged: every operation of `TextEditor` (`ToggleCharacter`, `ApplyCharacterChanges`, `ApplyParagraphChanges`, `ApplyParagraphStyle`, `ApplyCharacterStyle`, `RemoveOverride`, `ClearDirectFormatting`) keeps clones of the touched top-level blocks as the history's old snapshots (`BlockChange.OldBlocks`), changes the live blocks in place (`EditResult.KeepSelection` depends on that: the paragraph objects the selection points at stay), raises the revision once and records one `BlockChange` (one `EditResult`, one undo step, `EditResult.KeepSelection` so the selection survives a format operation). A **style edit** is a new kind of history step: `StyleChange` (the old and the new `Style` clone) with an optional block swap, so "Update style to match" is one composite step (the style swap and the paragraph's changes together). `EditHistory` swaps the style by identity at the same index in `StyleSheet.Replace`, never coalesces a style step into typing, counts it in `SizeOf`, and Undo and Redo return `EditResult.Style` with the step's snapshots as recorded (not swapped): `Style != null` only means "the sheet changed". A style edit touches every paragraph of the style, so putting it through `BlockChange` would have put thousands of blocks into the history (approach A, rejected in the spec).
- **The layout restyles on a fresh engine.** `DocumentLayout.Restyle(throughPage)` mirrors `ApplyEdit`: under the gate it rethrows a remembered fault, calls the existing private `Restart` (a new `StyleResolver` and plan cache, because the resolver snapshots the styles at construction), counts one edit and one restart, and captures a fault; there is no second restart path. The session calls `Restyle` whenever `EditResult.Style != null` (also for undo and redo) and **never also `ApplyEdit`** for that result: the model already holds the block changes and the fresh engine reads them (a test cannot tell `Restyle` followed by `ApplyEdit` or `Restyle(0)` from the right call after the layout is complete; the choice is pinned by a code comment). A direct format needs no layout change beyond the usual one: the live paragraphs are changed in place and `ApplyEdit` re-plans them.
- **`IEffectiveFormat` lives in Model and is implemented in Layout (a deviation from the spec's section 5, part 1).** The spec offered a UI-free reader in Model that must equal the layout's `StyleResolver`, or moving the layering rules into Model. Neither was needed: `IEffectiveFormat` and `EffectiveRun` are the interface and the answer in `Raffica.Write.Model` (`Formatting/`), `LayoutEffectiveFormat` in `Raffica.Write.Layout` (`Styles/`) implements it **over `StyleResolver` itself** (document defaults, the based-on chain, the character style, the table style's conditional layers for text in a table, the numbering level, then direct formatting), with `ResolveRun` and `ResolveParagraph` public for the app. `TextEditor` takes it as an optional last constructor parameter (`Raffica.Write.Editing` still references only Model). One definition by construction: the corpus test `EffectiveFormatTests` compares it with the layout's own `BlockPlanner.PlanFor` result for every run and paragraph of every corpus file, tables and lists included, and a differential suite covers merged cells, row-level content controls before a row, nested tables (the innermost cell wins) and 80 grid columns. The table geometry (row, grid start, spans, the 63-column cap, unknown row items) is one internal helper, `TableGeometry.Of`, used by `TableReader.Read` and by `LayoutEffectiveFormat`; the first version copied it and diverged on unknown rows and the cap. The editor's format is a `RefreshableFormat` inside `EditSession`, invalidated on every style result, undo and redo included, and on rebind. **`LayoutEffectiveFormat` finds the cell of a paragraph through an index** (one walk of the story, `TableGeometry.Of` once per table, the cell format resolved lazily per cell) that it keeps for as long as `DocumentEdits.Revision` stays the same: every edit that restructures blocks raises the revision and drops it, and a paragraph the index does not know costs one more walk per revision (a hand-made model change). The first version walked the whole story from its start for every run, which was quadratic (every run of the thesis 1086 ms, the toggle of select all 1.4 to 1.8 s, a read of the last 200 blocks 510 ms on the UI thread); with the index every run of the thesis is one walk (`EffectiveFormatTests`, counter `StoryWalks`).
- **How a property is written (`PropertyBag`, `SchemaOrder`).** New children are placed by `SchemaOrder` as in 4.1. A value command merges attributes **into the existing element** (`ApplyChange`, `s_themeAttributes`): the font command writes `ascii` and `hAnsi` and drops `asciiTheme` and `hAnsiTheme` of the same `w:rFonts` (`eastAsia` and `cs` stay); the colour command writes `val` and drops `themeColor`, `themeShade` and `themeTint`; `w:ind` takes left, right, first line and hanging, and a first line and a hanging indent replace each other **in every spelling** (`hanging`, `hangingChars`, `firstLine`, `firstLineChars`; the legacy `start`, `end`, `leftChars`, `rightChars` are dropped when left or right is set); a new `before` or `after` of `w:spacing` drops its `beforeLines`, `beforeAutospacing`, `afterLines` and `afterAutospacing` spellings. A spacing `line` value without its rule throws (it would write the wrong unit); a rule alone keeps the number and changes only `w:lineRule` (a pinned decision: no unit is reinterpreted). Indents are signed, spacing is not, points are capped at 31680, an automatic multiple only has to be finite and not negative. A run is split by Write's own run-level splitter, not `InlineSplitter` (that one cuts containers such as hyperlinks and tracked changes). After Set, Toggle and Apply style, neighbouring runs with equal properties, attributes (ignoring `w:rsid*`) and kept children and text-only content are merged again (`RunMerger`; "touched" means the covered runs and their direct siblings, so turning bold off on "bc" merges back with "a" and "d"); a run that holds anything but text, tabs and breaks is never merged.
- **Toggles judge the effective value of the whole selection.** Bold, italic, underline (single), strikethrough, small and all caps, superscript and subscript: all on turns it off, otherwise on (a mixed selection becomes all on, as in Word); the selection is walked through tables and block content controls between its ends too (`VisitWholeBlock`, depth cap 64), because a first version that skipped them judged the toggle on partial data. Turning off removes the direct element and, when the effective value is still on (it comes from a style), writes an explicit off (`val="0"`, `none`, `baseline`); a run that is already effectively on is left alone on toggle-on (a double underline stays). Superscript and subscript exclude each other. A field result is formatted on the run the projection reports for it.
- **Pending format.** At a caret, a character command sets a `PendingFormat` in `EditSession` that the next typed text takes: it survives typing inserts (a typed word stays formatted), is cleared by a real selection change, any non-typing operation and Esc, and lives only in the session (never in the model, the history or the revision, as the IME preedit). In an empty paragraph the command also toggles the paragraph mark's run properties in one history step, because the layout takes the line height of an empty paragraph from them. `Apply` gained an optional `typing` flag (queued and replayed during a save like typing). **An input method commit takes it only when it is the very format object the session had when the composition began and still has** (Ctrl+B, then composing: the commit is bold and the format stays for the next one); one set, changed or cleared during the composition is stale and the commit drops it. `SelectionChanged` is a new event of the session: `SetSelection` raises it at once when the selection changed, an operation raises it once after `Changed`, a replayed batch once. It fires per typed character, so every subscriber is debounced or cheap.
- **The inspector (`FormattingInspector`, `TextEditor.Inspect`).** Rows are every direct child of the paragraph's `pPr` and the runs' `rPr` except `pStyle`, `rStyle`, `numPr`, `sectPr`, the paragraph mark's `rPr`, `rPrChange` and `pPrChange` (a mark row also excludes `ins`, `del`, `moveFrom` and `moveTo`: they are tracked-change records, not formatting). One row is one element name per scope (a font row is the whole `w:rFonts`, a colour row the whole `w:color`; a duplicate child of the same name counts as one row and all of them go); a row reads its value from `w:val`, else `name=value` pairs, and a font from `ascii`, `hAnsi`, then the theme name; an element Write has no wording for is a row named by the element, so every override has one. Over several paragraphs a row shows when it is on every touched paragraph with the same value, as partly or mixed otherwise. The mark's rows exist only while the paragraph has no text. The app reads with `TextEditor.Inspect` (the editor's hosts, so offsets are layout offsets); the static `Read` uses the plain host and can differ where field results show. **Removal never merges runs and equals "before minus that one element"**: an emptied `w:rPr` or `w:pPr` stays as an empty element, a run partly covered is split only where it carries the element, the pieces stay split, a run without the element is never split, and a refused removal (absent, structural, the mark of a paragraph with text) changes nothing and records no step. Clear direct formatting removes every row's override, keeps `pStyle`, `numPr`, `sectPr`, `rStyle` and the change records, and clears the mark's `rPr` for an empty paragraph or one whose whole text the selection covers.
- **Update style to match** (`UpdateStyleToMatch`, `CanUpdateStyleToMatch`, one shared `PlanUpdate`): works on the paragraph style of the first paragraph (a paragraph without `pStyle` resolves to the file's default paragraph style); the paragraph's direct paragraph overrides and the character overrides on every run of it are merged into the style's `pPr` and `rPr` (attribute merge as above), then removed from that paragraph, in one undo step. Refusals, each with a reason in `WriteCommandRules.ReasonFor`: the selection touches paragraphs of different styles, the style is not defined in the file, there is nothing to update. It touches neither `basedOn`, `next`, `link` nor the linked character style. Nothing else about other paragraphs of the style changes (their own direct formatting stays and may now equal the style); the pane notes once that the style is shared.
- **Bounded fact reads.** The command state and the Home pickers read facts about the selection (`FormatFacts`, `SelectionFormat`, the inspector). A read walks at most `TextEditor.MaxFactBlocks = 200` top-level blocks: beyond it the command facts are **permissive** (`FormatFacts.Unknown`, `HasStylesPart` still read; Update style and Clear stay enabled and the operation itself refuses or does nothing), the Home display shows every value as mixed and no switch pressed (`SelectionFormat.AllMixed`, `Truncated`; grow and shrink font start from the default size 11), and the pane shows "too large". Answers are memoized by model, `Edits.Revision` and range (`EditSession.ReadFormatFacts`, `ReadSelectionFormat`, `ReadInspector`, counters `FactReads` and `InspectorReads`), and nothing is read before a session exists or before one of the fact commands is asked.
- **The debounced refresh.** `FormatDisplayRefresh` (100 ms, UI thread, the timer made by the first request) refreshes the Home pickers and the pressed state from `SelectionChanged`, `Changed` and `PendingFormat.Changed`; the pane reuses it. A closed window requests nothing (`_formatClosed`). A hidden or switched-away pane detaches from the session events, cancels its pending refresh and reads once when shown again (`Hidden`, `Shown`); a pane during a save keeps what it shows and refreshes when the save ends.
- **Commands.** 32 ids `format.*` (21 plain commands and the 11 payload commands below) in three ribbon groups (Font, Paragraph, Styles) plus `view.formatting`, each with its own Lucide icon (`CommandIconTests`; `view.writing` moved to `PenLine`, because `AlignLeft` is now `format.alignLeft`), reasons in every disabled state, strings in both languages ("Sie" form), a Format menu on macOS (check and radio items; the key equivalents come from the catalog) and keys Ctrl or Cmd with B, I, U, E, L, R, J (`EditingKeys.FormatCommandFor`: the command key is the only modifier, AltGr is text, the page view owns them and a key repeat is claimed but not run). `WriteCommandCatalog.Find` skips `format.*` so the window never swallows Ctrl+B. The 11 **payload commands** (font, size, sizeUp, sizeDown, color, highlight, spaceBefore, spaceAfter, lineSpacing, paragraphStyle, characterStyle; `TakesPayload`) are ordinary catalog entries run through `WriteWindow.RunCommand(id, payload)`, which returns false for a bad payload and never throws (Ruling 1 below): size takes the target in points 1 to 1638 (the picker computes the step: the Word list 8 to 72, above 72 plus 10, below 8 minus 1), colour a `uint`, an `int` 0 to 0xFFFFFF or "RRGGBB" or "#RRGGBB", highlight a Word colour name (null or "none" removes), spacing 0 to 1584 pt, line spacing a `LineSpacingChoice(value, rule)` or a plain number (a multiple), a style an id defined in the file for that kind or one the style picker itself lists (an id that a paragraph or run uses and the file does not define; null or empty removes), a font a string of at most 128 characters without control characters. Indent more and less step the first paragraph's effective left indent by 36 pt and set it on all touched paragraphs; the four pagination switches flip the first paragraph's effective value; both read it through the session's `LayoutEffectiveFormat.ResolveParagraph`, so a paragraph in a table cell sees the table style's indent and pagination.
- **The picker item (shell).** `CommandDefinition.Picker` is a `PickerDefinition(Items: Func<IReadOnlyList<PickerItem>>, Chosen, SelectedId?, IsMixed?, SelectedLabel?, Editable, Width, MaxLength)`; `RibbonPicker` is built on the shell's `SelectBox` with `Styles/Frame/RibbonPicker.axaml` (tokens only, no new colour token).
