Tables: rows or cells inside a model-kept wrapper (`UnknownRowItem`: `w:sdt`, `w:customXml`) are not laid out; `tblCellSpacing`, floating tables (laid out inline), `textDirection`, `gridAfter` and `hideMark` are not handled; dotted and dashed borders are drawn solid; a border conflict goes to the wider, then the darker line; exact-height rows and a vertical merge taller than a page overflow without a clip; `TableReader.Read` plans every cell paragraph of a table when first needed (a huge table at the start costs first-page time); the header-repeat decision looks at the page being cut. Views: no hit test, caret or selection; `ViewAnchor` has no character offset; the writing view at fit width has no side margin (the text touches both edges; a design call); the start view shows both a "New: Open document" card and an "Open: Browse" button; the status hint is window-wide; the outline has no shortcut and no F6 pane ring. The published Write exe is 52.5 MiB (the PDF app's is 26.9 MB); what dominates it is not analyzed. The rules most likely to differ from Word (the thesis grew from 301 to 304 pages with headers, reference marks and note areas, to 311 with tables): footnote placement, separator height, the blank page of an even or odd section start, `SECTIONPAGES` at a continuous start, footnotes in columns, and the table rules (cell margins, border conflicts, repeated headers, split rows).
**Follow-ups from the review of the branch (for a later milestone):** (a) `ImageCache` has no eviction or byte budget, decodes the full bitmap on Avalonia's render thread at the first draw and has no pixel cap (decompression-bomb exposure): an LRU or byte budget, a pixel cap and off-thread prefetch belong in 4.3 or 4.8, before 4.9 at the latest. (b) The Write render operation has no crash guard and no try/catch around `PageRenderer.Draw`: the crash guard stayed in `Raffica.Pdf`; before 4.9. (c) For the 4.8 hardening list: `DocxFile.InflateMain` allocates `new byte[(int)entry.Length]` from the declared entry size and `OutOfMemoryException` is deliberately not caught, so a hostile zip can end the app instead of showing the "damaged" card; `PackageImageSource.Part` reserves from the declared size (64 MB cap); `NumberingCounter.Letters` is uncapped (a hostile `w:start` near `int.MaxValue` allocates about 165 MB). (d) The writing view's status bar counts strips as pages ("Page 12 of 900"); the start view shows both the "New: Open document" card and the "Open: Browse" button. (e) The guard of `ReplaceModel` compares only `Page.Text` (add width, height and the item count). (f) The PDF app's `ThirdPartyNotices.txt` and About card now list Caladea, the Unicode data and the hyphenation patterns although the PDF app does not ship them (scope `third-party/licenses.json` per app). (g) There is no `CHANGELOG.md` entry on purpose: Write is not packaged before 4.9.
**Open (owner gates; nothing here is measured or decided yet):** (1) the first page of the thesis on the Linux laptop, limit 100 ms (the Windows PC measured 46.0 ms): `write-layout-bench run --file testdata/write-corpus/thesis/thesis.docx` on a Native AOT publish of this branch, the CSV under `Docs/measurements/write-engine-2026-11-xx/`; (2) the speed budgets of the Write app: `gh workflow run measure.yml --ref dev -f platform=<windows|linux|macos> -f target=write -f note="<machine>" -f ref=<ref>` on the three machines (and `macos-hosted` as a record) with a `target=reference` control run of the same day, then `s_writeBudgets` filled in `CheckCommand`, the pinning lines in the selftest and the entry "Raffica Write: speed budgets (M4.2)" in this file (deliberately not written yet); (3) the thesis page count against Word: `testdata/write-corpus/thesis/word.pdf` (and `word-accept.pdf`, `word-reject.pdf` for `poi-tika-792`, and the other corpus `word.pdf` files) from the owner, then `dotnet run --project bench/write-corpus -c Release -- compare --id thesis --require-same-page-count` must exit 0 (Write's count is 311; on a different count the output names the first different page and the rules above are the suspects), then the CI step "Thesis page count against Word's PDF"; (3b) the full layout of the generated thesis, limit 3 s on the Linux laptop (spec section 5, next to the 100 ms first page): `write-layout-bench check --root . --budget-ms 3000` on a Native AOT publish of this branch prints `run2 ms` and exits 1 above the budget (without `--budget-ms` nothing is asserted, so CI is unchanged); the Windows PC measured run2 374 ms (pages=311, Native AOT, one run, 2026-11-14), the laptop's number is the binding one; (4) a look at the Native AOT Write app on the Linux laptop (Wayland) and on the Intel Mac (not seen), and the Linux and macOS AOT publish of the Write app (CI only); (5) milestone 4.2 stays in progress until (1), (2), (3) and (3b) are closed.

## 2026-11-13 – Raffica Write: document model and DOCX package layer (M4.1)
**Context:** Milestone 4.1 builds the UI-free library under Write: a document model that follows OOXML, read and written without any Avalonia type, and a round trip measured on a corpus. The spike "Open XML SDK under Native AOT (M4)" had shown that the SDK works under AOT when only the package layer is used.
**Decision:**
- **Two projects and a tool.** `Raffica.Write.Model` references nothing (the layout library of 4.2 needs the model without the SDK); `Raffica.Write.Docx` references only Model, Core and the Open XML SDK (3.5.1, central package management); `bench/write-corpus` is the corpus tool and the Native AOT proof; `ProjectGuardTests` pins the references. The compiler drops unused assembly references, so the guard sees only references the code really uses (the first version needed an anchor type until `DocxFile` used Model and Core for real).
- **The SDK is the package layer only** (parts, relationships, content types, zip). The model is read through `XmlReader` and written as raw XML; the SDK's typed DOM is never used. Relationship ids, not part objects, are remembered, so the same map finds the parts in the temporary copy. External relationships are never fetched. A `.docm` (and a macro-enabled template) is kept with its macro part (`DocxKind`).
- **Save rule.** A model part is rewritten on every save: equal content, not equal bytes (`xml:space="preserve"` on a leaf without edge whitespace is dropped on write, and `XmlEquivalence` tolerates exactly that). Every other part keeps its decompressed bytes. A guard in `DocxFile.SaveAsync` compares them, and the relationship and content-type structure, before the replace: every original entry must be present and equal, `[Content_Types].xml` may only gain an Override or Default for a part we created, the main part's relationships may only gain a relationship to a created part, any other relationships part may not change, and a written part must read back byte-equal to what the codec produced. A failure is `DocxSaveException` (`PartsChanged`, `ModelNotReadable`) before the target is touched; a file that cannot be read is a `DocxFormatException`, never a silent repair. A save over the opened file (links resolved) is refused with `SaveFailureException` (`OriginalChanged`) before anything is written when its `FileStamp` (taken before the read, as for a PDF) differs or the file is gone, unless the caller passes `overwriteChangedOriginal` (the overload `SaveAsync(path, bool, token)`; the analyzer wants the token last); a successful save over the opened file records the new stamp, a save to another path changes neither the stamp nor the snapshot, and `Open(bytes)` has no origin and no check. Three internal seams (`BeforeReplace`, `WrapPartWrite`, `AfterPackageWritten`) let tests break each step.
- **`AtomicFile` in Core.** The atomic replace moved out of `DocumentSaver` (moved, not copied), with `TargetMetadata` (Unix mode, hidden attribute, ACL, read-only refusal) and `FlushToDisk`; `DocumentSaver` and `DocxFile` both use it, and the behaviour of the PDF save is unchanged (the Core suite is the proof). A symlink target keeps the link and the temporary file lies next to the real target.
- **Kept-unknown design.** Unknown elements and attributes stay in place: `PropertyBag` holds the properties of a paragraph, run, table, cell, section or style (typed access for what is understood, `UnknownContent` captured with its prefixes for the rest); the `Unknown*` block, inline, run-content and row-item types hold an `UnknownContent` by composition (they derive their own bases, so one shared capture is the single storage). Order is position-as-read; a new child lands by schema order (`PartXml.Flush`, `PropertyBag.Set`, `SchemaOrder` in `Raffica.Write.Model`, because Model references nothing). Fields (`FieldScanner`) and tracked changes (`TrackedChanges`) are views over the stored runs, not a second storage. The vocabulary rule: later milestones add accessors, never a second storage.
- **`DocxParts` is the hook for 4.2's lazy loading.** `DocxFile.Open(path, parts)` loads only the asked-for model parts; a part that is not loaded is still located, so a model the caller sets for it replaces the part instead of adding a second relationship.
- **Corpus.** `testdata/write-corpus` holds a manifest (`corpus.json`: id, path, SHA-256, source, SPDX licence, licence file, retrieval date, feature tags). `check` finds the feature tags from the part XML (`FeatureDetector`), refuses a licence outside the allowed list (MIT, Apache-2.0, BSD-2/3-Clause, CC0-1.0, CC-BY-4.0, GPL-3.0-or-later for our own files), and fails a required feature that no file covers. The corpus has 16 files (0.63 MiB): the generated thesis, a generated right-to-left file, 13 Apache POI test documents (Apache-2.0, commit `e0658769cb7f`, licence and NOTICE kept) and one Open XML SDK file (MIT, commit `431ab05cf160`). Both public repositories state their licence for the repository, not per file; "when in doubt leave it out": after review `poi-delins`, `poi-drawing`, `poi-bib-chernigovka`, `poi-rtl` and `poi-word-with-attachments` were removed (Wikipedia, news or web-download content, embedded OLE). Files of the owner's own, if any, stay local and only their result is recorded.
- **Native AOT proof in CI.** The corpus tool publishes without trim or AOT warnings in the build job on Linux and Windows and in `build-macos` for both architectures (the steps "Publish the Write corpus tool (Native AOT, Linux)" and "... (Native AOT, Windows)", the macOS step "Publish the PDF app, the CLI and the Write corpus tool (Native AOT, both architectures)"; the log is searched for `IL[23][0-9]{3}`), and the published tool runs `check` and `run` over the corpus. Locally the win-x64 publish has no such warning and the published exe ran every row true.
- **Licence of the SDK.** `DocumentFormat.OpenXml` and `DocumentFormat.OpenXml.Framework` 3.5.1 declare `MIT` in their NuGet metadata (`<license type="expression">MIT</license>` in the nuspec of the NuGet cache; `System.IO.Packaging` 10.0.2, which the SDK pulls in, declares `MIT` too). `tools/licenses.cs` does not cover the Write projects: it inspects only `src/Raffica.Pdf` and `src/Raffica.Cli` (`PackageList.Projects`), so its green result says nothing about the SDK, and no manifest entry or third-party notice exists for it. Before Write ships, the SDK, `System.IO.Packaging` and their transitive packages need an entry in `third-party/licenses.json`, the notices and `licenses.cs` (backlog item in `Docs/roadmap.json`).
- **Comparison tool: the Word side only.** `write-corpus pdf-facts`, `compare` and `table` read the page count and the text per page of a PDF through PDFium (never printing text) and write `Docs/write-corpus.md`. Page texts are normalised (NFC, whitespace and no-break spaces to one space, soft hyphens and all Cf format characters removed, ligatures not folded) and compared as a multiset of words per page. Exit codes: 0 ok, 1 findings (`--require-same-page-count` with different counts), 2 unreadable input or an unwritable output; a missing PDFium is exit 2. Write's own layout does not exist before 4.2 (scope change of 2026-11-13).
**Rationale:** Raw XML for the model keeps every unknown element and attribute without the SDK re-serialising it, keeps the model free of the SDK, and makes "a part that was not touched keeps its bytes" a property the guard can check. A guard before the replace turns a codec bug into a refused save instead of a damaged file. The corpus with source and licence per file makes "compatible" a measured claim.
**Rejected alternatives:** The SDK's typed DOM as the model (re-serialises, drops what it does not know, ties the model to the SDK); one project (the layout library of 4.2 needs the model without the SDK); a dirty flag per part (not needed for the acceptance, 4.3 can add it); keeping files whose content licence is unclear for the sake of coverage.
**Known limits:** `DocxFile.Open` refuses a file with a relationship to a missing part (the SDK throws; Word may still open it). The guard reads every untouched part twice and the package sits in memory beside the temporary copy (not measured). Position ints on tables, rows and cells are fragile against editing and reordering read list items is not reflected in the written order: the editors of 4.3 must account for both. A `w:t` with children or other malformed model XML ends in `DocxFormatException`. The model must not change while `SaveAsync` runs. Hostile-file hardening is 4.8. `sections`, `toc`, `rtl-text` and `equations` rest on generated or single files (Word-made specimens are to be added in 4.2). (a) There is no Save-As protection against a file that appears at the target between the prompt and the save, and a Windows `.pw-*.old` leftover path is discarded by `MoveIntoPlace` (4.3 must expose it before Save As). (b) A `w:t` with leading or trailing spaces and `xml:space="default"` is written back as `xml:space="preserve"`, which can change what Word shows for a file whose producer omitted preserve; 4.2/4.3 decide whether to keep the original attribute. (c) Deep nesting (2000 nested tables or content controls, 100000 nested raw elements) ends in an uncatchable stack overflow: the readers recurse without a limit and `Open` runs on the caller's thread; 4.8 must add an explicit depth limit. (d) Strict OOXML files (another officeDocument relationship type) are refused with "no main document part", like a relationship to a missing part. (e) The broad catch in `DocxFile.Load` relabels a codec bug as `DocxFormatException` (4.8 revisits it). (f) `Docs/write-corpus.md` is not enforced by CI.
**Open:** Word's PDFs (`word.pdf` per corpus entry, `word-accept.pdf` and `word-reject.pdf` for `poi-tika-792`) are the owner's; Word's page count of the thesis; whether Word shows "1" on physical page 3 and "A-1" on the first appendix page; check with `dotnet run --project bench/write-corpus -c Release -- table --root testdata/write-corpus --out Docs/write-corpus.md`. The Unix-mode save test (`UnixPermissionsOfTheTargetAreKept`) is skipped on the Windows host and proven only by CI on Linux and macOS; the Linux and macOS AOT publish of the tool is proven only by CI.

## 2026-11-13 – Raffica Write: the spec and the first release (M4)
**Context:** Milestone 4 ended with the Write spec (`Docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-13-raffica-write-design.md`), brainstormed with the owner after the four spikes and a look at Word, LibreOffice Writer, ONLYOFFICE, Google Docs, Pages and WordPerfect. A builder check raised six questions; three were the owner's.
**Decision:** The first release of Write is "good enough for a thesis" and comes in milestones 4.1 to 4.9: document model and DOCX round trip, a fast viewer (where the speed budgets are set), editing, structure, long-document tools, review and proofing, print and PDF, a release-readiness and security check (owner's requirement), and the release as its own milestone. The owner decided in the session: a layout difference from Word in the compatibility corpus is recorded, not a failure, except that the generated thesis must have the page count of Word's PDF of it from 4.2 on (Word's count is not known yet); the corpus mixes generated and freely licensed public files in the repository with the owner's own documents checked locally (only the result recorded); Latin scripts fully, Chinese and Japanese simply (system fonts, IME, no East Asian layout rules), right-to-left in the backlog. Calls of the author, approved with the spec: autosave is a recovery copy every 30 s in Write's data folder and the user's file is written only on Save; in Write Ctrl+K inserts a hyperlink and the command search moves to Alt+Q (an app-level option of the frame, never on AltGr+Q; on macOS a ⌘ shortcut, since Option+letter types characters; Raffica PDF keeps Ctrl+K); the 100 ms first-page limit is the headless engine measure of the spike, and the app's first sharp page gets its own measured budget; field results are written from Write's own layout without `updateFields` or `w:dirty`; a document with change tracking on gets a bar saying Write's edits are not tracked; `.docm` opens with its macros kept and never run; code Write shares with Raffica PDF (print services, window host, crash guard, update flow) moves into shared projects, never copied.
**Rationale:** The research matched the owner's seven points of 2026-11-12: Word's pain is hidden structure (styles, fields that need F9, recipes for page numbers, cloud-only AutoSave), LibreOffice's is speed and DOCX conversion, ONLYOFFICE shows that fidelity comes from working on OOXML natively. Recording layout differences instead of failing on them keeps the milestones finishable while the table makes "compatible" a measured claim.
**Rejected alternatives:** Every corpus file must match Word's page count before a milestone is done (owner: record instead); right-to-left in the first release (owner: backlog, it would enlarge 4.2 to 4.4); autosave that overwrites the user's file (contradicts "never damage the original"); asking Word to update fields on open (Word would prompt on every open).

## 2026-11-13 – Raffica Write: IME in a custom text control (M4)
**Context:** Write's editor is its own control, not Avalonia's `TextBox`, so composition (preedit) for Japanese, Chinese and other input methods has to work in a control we draw ourselves. Spike 4 (`spikes/write/ime`, Avalonia 12.1.5) built `ComposeBox`: a `Control` that draws its text and the preedit underlined and answers `TextInputMethodClientRequested` with a `TextInputMethodClient` (`SetPreeditText(text, cursor)`, `CursorRectangle`, `TextViewVisual`, `SurroundingText`, `Selection`, `SupportsPreedit`). `TextBox` was the control group. The owner tried six steps in each control (compose, candidates, commit, cancel, position, Backspace) on Windows 11 (Microsoft Japanese IME and Pinyin), on Linux in a Wayland session (Fcitx5 with Mozc and Pinyin, HP ProBook, CachyOS) and on macOS 15.8.2 (Intel, Japanese - Romaji); the tables are in `spikes/write/ime/CHECKLIST.md`.
**Decision:** **Write's editor is a custom control that implements Avalonia's `TextInputMethodClient`.** No native text control and no own platform code for input methods are needed for the systems tested.
**Measured:** In every row, `ComposeBox` and `TextBox` behaved the same; compose, candidates, commit, cancel and Backspace worked, and the position step was only judged as "below the field" (see below). On macOS the log has no `commit` line at all, although the owner saw the converted text stay in the field: either the text arrived without the `TextInput` event the spike logs, or the preedit was never cleared and only looked committed, so the macOS commit stands on the owner's report and is not proven by the log. The preedit was drawn underlined in the field, converted text (`日本語`, `你好`) was committed, Esc and Backspace behaved as each input method defines them (on macOS the first Esc removes the candidates and the second removes the text; in Pinyin Enter commits the typed letters, Space or a number key commits a character). Facts for the implementation: the preedit cursor that Avalonia reports differs by system (a number on Windows, always 0 under Fcitx5 Pinyin, none on macOS), so Write must draw the preedit caret itself and not rely on it; the candidate window stood below the text field on every system, never at the caret, which cannot be judged in a small field (the owner finds the placement good); the log of the Fcitx5 Mozc run has the commit but no preedit line, although the hiragana stood underlined in the field.
**Rationale:** A custom control gets the same input method behaviour as `TextBox` on the three systems, so the editor can be written without a native text view.
**Rejected alternatives:** None tried: nothing failed that would call for one.
**Open:** IBus, the X11 session on Linux, Apple silicon, Chinese on macOS and other input methods (Korean, handwriting, emoji) were not tested. The first editor milestone of Write has to check where the candidate window stands in a page of text (it must follow the caret) and has to test with a German keyboard layout (Microsoft Pinyin reads the key positions of a US keyboard, so `z` gives `y`); it also has to see the commit on macOS in its own log, and to run IBus and an X11 session on Linux.

## 2026-11-12 – Raffica Write: the layout engine (M4)
**Context:** The suite spec left open whether Write's layout is C# or a Rust core. Spike 2 (`spikes/write/layout`) built two candidates behind one interface and measured them on a generated thesis (311 pages in the spike's own layout; `spikes/write/testdoc`, Caladea 11 pt body, 20 tables, 100 footnotes, a section that restarts the page number on physical page 3): **C#** (HarfBuzzSharp shaping, UAX #14 line breaks from Avalonia's `LineBreakEnumerator`, SkiaSharp drawing) and **Rust** (Parley 0.13.0 with default features off, so no system fonts, as a `cdylib` with a C interface called through `LibraryImport`). Page breaking, headers and footers, footnotes, tables and the incremental re-layout rule (re-lay out the edited paragraph, then refill pages forward until a page starts where it started before) are written once in C# and shared, so the two differ only in the paragraph layer and the cost of the boundary. Fonts are the shipped Carlito and Caladea only. Every number is a median of 10 runs after 2 warm-ups from a Native AOT publish (`layout-bench run`; the tool refuses to time anything else). The binding machine is the owner's HP ProBook (Core i5-8265U, CachyOS), measured at commit `b65b11c`.
**Measured on the binding Linux laptop (limits of the spec in brackets):**

| | First page (100 ms) | Full layout (3 s) | Keystroke (16 ms) | Hit test per call (50 us) | Memory |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C# | **108.9 ms** | 403 ms | 0.26 ms | 0.79 us | 86 MB |
| Rust | **110.5 ms** | 392 ms | 0.26 ms | 3.99 us | 131 MB |

Both candidates pass the five checks (fonts load from the spike folders and every character has a glyph; the reader sees 3 sections, 20 tables, 100 footnotes; the incremental result equals a full layout after 20 seeded edits, half of them long insertions that move page breaks; 1,000 seeded hit-test round trips; page 3 shows "1" and the first appendix page "A-1"), each seen to fail when the code it guards was broken. Parley and HarfBuzz break the lines identically: 0 of 1,484 paragraphs differ, 9,532 lines each (Windows, Linux, macOS arm64). Other machines (CSVs in `spikes/write/layout/results`): the owner's Windows PC (Ryzen 7 7800X3D) C# 86 ms / 451 ms / 0.34 ms / 0.73 us / 58 MB, Rust 91 ms / 429 ms / 0.27 ms / 4.3 us / 107 MB; GitHub-hosted Linux, macOS arm64 and macOS Intel are inside every limit for C# (the hosted Intel Mac measured 84.5 and 99.7 ms in two runs, Rust 114 ms; virtual machines, a record only). Native size Rust adds: 1.74 MB (win-x64), 2.19 MB (linux-x64), 2.00 MB (macOS arm64), 2.07 MB (macOS x64), plus about 50 MB of working memory. The owner's Intel Mac is not measured yet and cannot change the result.
**Why the first page misses, for both:** Laying out and drawing page 1 takes about 5 ms; the engine is not the cost. A phase breakdown on the Windows PC (`layout-bench child first-page-phases`) shows about 30 ms from process start to `Main`, about 30 ms to load all eight font files (the cover needs three), about 15 ms to open and read the DOCX, 0.6 ms of layout and about 4 ms of drawing. Loading fonts only when first used saved only about 4 ms on the Windows PC (tried, not kept in the spike), so the miss is startup work that the Write milestones have to shorten (smaller font loading, reading less of the package before the first page), not a property of the engine.
**Decision:** By the rule agreed with the owner (C# wins when both meet every limit, Rust only when C# misses a limit that Rust meets, and when neither meets a limit the spec decides with the owner), the owner decided on 2026-11-12: **Write's layout is C#.** The limit of 100 ms for the first page on the Linux laptop stays and is a startup task of the Write spec and its first milestones, not an engine question.
**Rationale:** One language, one toolchain, no second native build on three systems, no extra Mach-O signing and no per-query call across a boundary. Rust won nothing: the first page is 1.6 ms slower, the hit test five times slower, the memory 45 MB higher; its full layout is 10 ms faster, which does not matter against a 3 s limit.
**What a full Rust core would change (not measured, argued):** The measured Rust cost is the boundary of a per-paragraph interface: 4 us per hit-test and caret pair (against 0.8 us in C#) and a second copy of the text and the line data. A Rust core that holds the model and the layout and answers coarser questions (a page, a range) would remove most of those calls, but not the 50 MB of extra memory of two runtimes, not the startup cost that dominates the first page, and it would move the document model, the Open XML handling (spike 1 works in C# under Native AOT) and the print path (spike 3) across the same boundary. The numbers give no reason to pay that.
**Rejected alternatives:** Rust (Parley) as the layout engine (above); a looser limit for the first page (the owner kept 100 ms); a full layout per keystroke (the incremental rule costs 0.26 ms).
**Open:** The same measurements on the owner's Intel Mac (`spikes/write/layout/README.md`, "Open") would add a record, not change the decision. Word's page count of the test document was not reported (the owner opened it in Word and found it fine); 311 is the spike layout's own count, and how Word numbers page 3 and the appendix is open too (`spikes/write/testdoc/README.md`).

## 2026-11-12 – Raffica Write: printing per operating system (M4)
**Context:** The owner's requirement: finished Write prints itself, with its own Print command and dialog, the way Word does on each system, and never by exporting a PDF for another program. Spike 3 (`spikes/write/print`, `Docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-12-raffica-write-spikes-design.md`) drew the 311 pages of the generated test document (`spikes/write/testdoc`) three ways. The machine was the owner's Windows PC (Ryzen 7 7800X3D) with the printer "Microsoft Print to PDF", and GitHub-hosted runners with a virtual CUPS queue each (workflow `spikes-write`, a throwaway that was removed again before the PR; the owner's runner group only serves `measure.yml`).
**Decision:** **Windows: Write draws directly onto the printer's device context with GDI (route a).** Glyph runs go out with `ExtTextOutW` and `ETO_GLYPH_INDEX` (the glyph ids of the shaping engine), table borders and shading with pens and brushes, images with `StretchDIBits`; the font files are added to the process with `AddFontMemResourceEx`. **Linux and macOS: Write makes a PDF in memory and hands it to CUPS as a job** (`cupsCreateJob`, `cupsStartDocument` with `application/pdf`, `cupsWriteRequestData` in 64 KiB chunks, `cupsFinishDocument`), through a list of the CUPS queues until Write has its own print dialog.
**Measured:** Route a printed 311 pages in 11.0 s and produced 1.66 MB with real text (four embedded fonts with a `ToUnicode` map, PDFium finds the text of pages 3 and 311, page 3 shows "1", the last page "A-8"). Route b (the same pages as a PDF in memory, printed through PDFium into the device context, as Raffica PDF prints) took 460.6 s and produced 100 MB of pictures without any text. The PDF in memory is 2.17 MB and takes 0.7 s on Windows, 0.66 s on macOS arm64 and 0.85 s on Linux. A CUPS job from memory was accepted and completed on Ubuntu 24.04 (CUPS 2.4.7, `cups-pdf`) and on macOS 15 arm64; no temporary file was needed.
**Rationale:** Route a is 42 times faster, 60 times smaller and keeps text as text; it is what Word does and what the owner asked for. CUPS takes PDF as its normal job format, every GTK, Qt and Cocoa program prints that way, and macOS turns every print job into a PDF itself, so a PDF from memory is the native path there, not an export for another program. Skia's XPS backend (route a2) was not needed.
**Rejected alternatives:** PDF in memory printed through PDFium on Windows (route b, 42 times slower, 60 times larger, pictures); exporting a PDF file for the user to print elsewhere (the owner's requirement); `cupsPrintFile2` with a temporary file (not needed, streaming works).
**Open:** On the Linux laptop a real printer printed the page ranges `1-6` and `304-311` from the CUPS path with the right page numbers ("1" on page 3, "A-1" in the appendix; 2026-11-13). On Windows a real printer printed the ranges `1-6` (215 ms to the spooler) and `304-311` (162 ms) with route a, and the owner found text, page numbers, margins, tables and images fine (2026-11-13). A real printer on the Intel Mac still has to confirm sharp text, margins and page numbers (checklist in `spikes/write/print/README.md`). The no-break space after a heading number comes back as a replacement character in the text of the Windows output, because the driver's `ToUnicode` map has no entry for it; Write should print an ordinary space there or give the glyph a mapping. Questions for the Write spec: how print code is shared with Raffica PDF (the platform print services live in `src/Raffica.Pdf/Platform`), Write's own print dialog, duplex and orientation (the spike maps none of them), and the native macOS print panel (backlog).

## 2026-11-12 – Raffica Write: Open XML SDK under Native AOT (M4)
**Context:** Spike 1 (`spikes/write/openxml-aot`) asked whether a Native AOT app can open, edit and save DOCX and XLSX with the Open XML SDK 3.x (MIT) and whether the parts it does not touch survive. DocumentFormat.OpenXml 3.5.1 (latest stable), .NET SDK 10.0, test files from `spikes/write/testdoc` (the generated thesis DOCX) and from the spike itself (a plain XLSX of 1,000 rows by 10 columns, an XLSX with SUM, VLOOKUP, a cross-sheet reference, number formats, a merged cell and conditional formatting). Published for win-x64 on the owner's PC and for linux-x64, osx-arm64 and osx-x64 on GitHub-hosted runners (workflow `spikes-write`).
**Decision:** The Open XML SDK 3.x is usable under Native AOT for Write's DOCX and for XLSX; Plan B (an own thin layer over `System.IO.Packaging` and `XmlReader`/`XmlWriter`) is not needed. The spec of Write should treat the SDK as the package and part layer and keep the document model its own.
**Measured:** Zero `IL2xxx` or `IL3xxx` warnings from the SDK on all four targets (`TrimmerSingleWarn` off, `IsAotCompatible` on). Every step ran in the published program: edit a paragraph and a style of the DOCX, write both XLSX files, edit cell B2. Published size 31.9 MB on win-x64, 27.3 MB linux-x64, 25.6 MB osx-arm64, 27.5 MB osx-x64 (one single file each; the spike's own code is small, the SDK's typed classes dominate). Open, edit and save of the thesis DOCX, median of 10 runs after 2 warm-ups: 149 ms on the Ryzen PC (the first generated file, 468 KB, 393 pages in the spike layout), 101 ms (Linux) and 103 ms (macOS arm64) on hosted runners and 243 ms on the hosted Intel Mac (the committed file, 392,700 bytes, 311 pages in the spike layout); the Windows number is therefore not comparable with the hosted ones. Compared part by part on the decompressed bytes, only the two parts the code touched changed in the DOCX (`/word/document.xml`, `/word/styles.xml`) and only the edited worksheet in the XLSX; footnotes, numbering, headers, footers, settings, images and package properties stayed byte-identical. LibreOffice (Linux, headless) opens the edited DOCX and both XLSX files.
**Finding for Write:** The SDK re-serialises every part whose DOM it loaded, also when the code only reads it (`/xl/workbook.xml`, found through the workbook to locate the sheet, came back with the same content and other bytes: the namespace declaration moved). A part that is never loaded stays byte-identical. Write must keep a part unloaded unless it edits it, and must never load a part it does not understand; "equal content" is then the most a loaded part promises.
**Rationale:** The SDK is MIT, maintained, typed, and now proven under AOT, so Write does not have to own the packaging and part code; the byte identity of untouched parts is a property of how Write uses it, not of the SDK failing.
**Rejected alternatives:** Plan B first (more code to own for no measured need); another library (the SDK passed every check).
**Word and Excel (owner, 2026-11-13):** the saved DOCX and both XLSX files open in Word and Excel on Windows with every check of the list fine (checklist in `spikes/write/openxml-aot/README.md`; LibreOffice opens them too). The same commands on the Linux laptop and the Intel Mac would add real-hardware timing; the operating systems and architectures are covered by the hosted runs.

## 2026-11-12 – Raffica PDF v0.2.0: the first release with the macOS images
**Context:** The owner asked for a last security and function pass and then the release for the Mac. The pass was the full test run (6497 tests, none failed), build without warnings, `dotnet format`, the roadmap check, both tool selftests, two independent read-only reviews and a release dry run on the dev head (run 43232994234).
**Decision:** v0.2.0 (a new minor version, because the macOS images are new) was released from `main` after a merge-commit pull request. The release was first created as a pre-release, so `releases/latest` still pointed to v0.1.0 while the three build jobs and the manifest job ran (release run 43238663941, the owner approved `release-signing`); after `update-verify` accepted the manifest (six kinds, both macOS images) and the checksums of the two images matched the uploaded files, it was set to Latest. The notes are the short `[0.2.0]` section of the changelog, written for users (what it is, the first start with "Open Anyway", what was tested), not a list of internal work.
**Rationale:** A client that reads `releases/latest` before the signed manifest exists would see a failed check; the pre-release step closes that window at no cost. Short notes are what users read.
**Rejected alternatives:** Creating the release as Latest at once (the window above); long notes that list every milestone task (the v0.1.0 notes were too long for users).

## 2026-11-12 – Raffica on macOS: the security pass before the first release and the swap script
**Context:** Before the first release with the macOS images a read-only review covered the update chain, the default-app calls, packaging and signing, the workflows and the file hand-over. It found nothing of high severity. The environment `release-signing` has the owner as required reviewer and a branch policy (checked), so the signing key is not reachable from an arbitrary workflow.
**Decision:** One fix goes into the first release because a Mac that installs it runs this updater for its first update: `MacSwapScript` takes the wait (parameter 8, 30 s by default) and, when the old process is still running after it, drops the staged bundle and ends with exit code 2, without swapping and without reopening (it used to swap under a running app and reopen that process). The other findings are recorded as one backlog entry (bundle not pinned to the signed manifest, no sequence number in the manifest, no recovery after a killed swap, `LC_RPATH` not checked, `measure.yml` accepts any branch as `ref` on the owner's machines).
**Rationale:** A swap under a running app is never right and the fix is small and testable (`ARunningProcessAfterTheWaitMeansNoSwapAndNoReopen`); the rest needs a format change of the signed manifest or new tooling and is no blocker for an ad-hoc signed first release.
**Rejected alternatives:** Swapping after the wait anyway (the previous behavior); a manifest change in this release (it would change the signed vector the Windows and Linux clients read).

## 2026-11-11 – Raffica on macOS: the Intel budgets and the rendering default (M3.3)
**Context:** "Raffica on macOS: published sizes and the hosted record (M3.3)" left two things to the owner's Intel Mac: the time and memory budgets of `s_pdfBudgets["macos"]` and the question whether macOS gets software rendering as its default, as Windows did. The runner `runner-macbook-benni` (Intel Core i5-6360U 2.00 GHz, 4 logical processors, 8 GiB, macOS 15.8.2, a MacBook Pro) measured `target=pdf`, 10 runs per case, on the same evening and the same app code (documentation-only commits between): run 43089378750 with `rendering=default` (commit bd3ce05, 19:44 UTC) and run 43093704440 with `rendering=software` (commit b0a1dfc, 20:11 UTC). A first attempt at the software run (43089398159) was lost when the runner lost its connection to GitHub; it produced no data, and the rerun ran with the power adapter and `caffeinate`. The state of the power source and of the display setting during the first run is not recorded (the owner did not state it at dispatch). The CSVs are in `Docs/measurements/pdf-viewer-2026-11-11-macos/{default,software}/` with the machine column set to `macos-macbook`, the kind of name the other committed CSVs use (the real host name carries the owner's first name).
**Measured (medians):**

| Case | Avalonia's own path (default) | Software |
|---|---|---|
| Cold start | 774.31 ms | 750.82 ms |
| Warm start | 717.09 ms | 750.71 ms |
| 1000-page file | 737.43 ms | 752.79 ms |
| Second file | 59.99 ms | 87.57 ms |
| Scroll p99 | 27.91 ms | 44.84 ms |
| Memory with the 1000-page file | 112.30 MiB | 164.39 MiB |
| Published size / zip | 58.48 / 26.58 MiB | the same |

The first cold run of the default path took 2692 ms (the cause was not examined; the median does not carry it).
**Decision:** The macOS default stays Avalonia's own rendering path; `RAFFICA_RENDERING=software` stays an opt-in. The time and memory budgets of `macos` are the default medians rounded up as in M1 (time to 10 ms, memory to 5 MiB), the limit is budget x 1.25 once: cold 780 / 975 ms, warm 720 / 900 ms, 1000 pages 740 / 925 ms, second file 60 / 75 ms, scroll p99 30 / 37.5 ms, memory 115 / 143.75 MiB. They are in `s_pdfBudgets` in `bench/shell-probe/measure.cs` with the size budgets that were already there. The selftest pins every value, judges a value inside the limit ok and one above it over, and runs `check` on the committed CSVs of the default run (exit 0, every median at or under its budget); the software run fails the same check on second file, scroll and memory, which is the point of the table.
**Rationale:** The case for software on Windows was a halved start and a third of the idle memory. On the Mac the picture is the opposite where it counts: software costs 52 MiB more memory (+46 %), 28 ms on the second file (+46 %) and 17 ms of scroll p99 (+61 %); it is 3 % faster at the cold start and 5 % slower at the warm start, which is inside the spread of a laptop. Speed of start and low memory are the promise of the suite, and the default path serves both better here.
**Rejected alternatives:** Software as the macOS default (worse on memory, second file and scrolling); budgets taken from the hosted Apple-silicon record or from Linux or Windows (a different machine; the rule of M2 is that budgets are measured, not inherited); a Pagewing control run on the same day, which the entry "Raffica PDF: release readiness measurements (M2.7)" asks for before a time row is a verdict (the predecessor does not run on macOS, so there is no control; the limit carries the 25 % margin of the rule instead).
**Consequences:** `check --target pdf --platform macos` is now a verdict for time and memory on the owner's Intel Mac only; a run of a hosted or other Mac is not one. A laptop varies by up to 30 % between days (entry M2.7), so one failing row on a day of its own is a reason to repeat the run, not to raise a limit. **Confirmed on 2026-11-12:** `measure.yml` platform `macos`, `rendering=app`, run 43218489749 on `dev` at 824bf59, power adapter and `caffeinate`: the check step is green; medians cold 649.3 ms, warm 628.6, 1000 pages 671.7, second file 63.8, scroll p99 24.9 ms, memory 112.8 MiB, all inside the limits (the second file is above its budget of 60 ms and inside its limit of 75 ms); CSVs in `Docs/measurements/pdf-viewer-2026-11-11-macos/confirm-2026-11-12/`. The Mac was about 15 % faster that day than on the evening before, which is the day-to-day spread the 25 % margin is for. **Open:** the measuring conditions of the first run of 2026-11-11 (power source, display) are not recorded; the Apple-silicon speed stays a record without a limit.

## 2026-11-11 – Raffica on macOS: the first checks on a real Mac (M3.1 to M3.3)
**Context:** The owner ran the manual checks of M3.1, M3.2 and M3.3 (`packaging/README.md`) on a Mac with macOS 15.8.2 and an Intel Core i5, with the Intel image of CI run 42885692478 and, after two fixes, of CI run 43076744033 (head of PR #73). The results are in `Docs/roadmap.json` (evidence of each criterion).
**Decision:** The results are recorded as proof for the criteria they answer and as findings for what they showed. Findings, all fixed: the menu bar had only an application menu "Avalonia Application" and Ctrl+Cmd+F did nothing (PR #72, "Raffica PDF on macOS: the menus belong to the window"); a PDF had another icon than the app (PR #73, "Raffica PDF on macOS: the PDF document type shows the app icon"); Cmd+A in the page list took the page text on the first try and selected the pages on the second, after the menu fix and with the organize plan open (the checklist now says to click Organize first; which of the two made the difference is not known). The headless tests had not seen the first two: they read the `NativeMenu` object and the plist, not what the system does with them.
**Answered by the Mac:** `LSSetDefaultRoleHandlerForContentType` sets the default app on macOS 15.8.2; the self-update from a test build 0.0.1 to 0.0.2 completes with no Gatekeeper prompt and no App Management prompt, the bundle has no quarantine attribute before or after (`xattr -l` printed nothing) and `/Applications` holds only `Raffica PDF.app` afterwards; the mounted-image and the write-protected-folder refusals show their cards; the `raffica` command line works through a symbolic link (`/usr/local/bin` did not exist and was made with `sudo mkdir -p`); the text recognition loads the Tesseract library of the bundle.
**Still open:** the Apple-silicon image (not run by hand); the Intel time and memory budgets and the rendering default (`measure.yml` platform `macos`, needs the owner's Intel runner online); the manifest job with `upload=true` and the first release.
**Consequences:** M3.1 is done, and M3.2 too once the owner confirmed that a page printed on a real printer (box h; the Mac has no PDF printer). M3.3 stays in progress for the budgets and the first release. A check that only reads a UI object (a menu, a plist) says nothing about what macOS shows; a change to either wants the owner's Mac again.

## 2026-11-11 – Raffica PDF on macOS: the PDF document type shows the app icon (M3.3 follow-up)
**Context:** The owner's first check on a real Mac (`packaging/README.md`, "Manual check on a Mac (M3.3)", box b) showed that a PDF opened by Raffica had a different icon than the app. The `Info.plist` declared the PDF document type (`com.adobe.pdf`, Editor, Alternate) without `CFBundleTypeIconFile`, so macOS composed an icon itself. On Windows the file type of the installer carries the app icon.
**Decision:** The document type names the same icon file as the app: `CFBundleTypeIconFile` is `RafficaPdf`, the `RafficaPdf.icns` that `macos-app` already makes (no second file). `package.cs selftest` checks that the document type names the same file as `CFBundleIconFile`; the check was seen to fail with the key removed.
**Rationale:** One icon, as on Windows, and no new artwork to maintain. The icon shows for PDFs only where Raffica is the default app for the type; PDFs that another app opens keep that app's icon, and the Finder shows a page preview instead of an icon in some views.
**Rejected alternatives:** A page-shaped document icon with the Raffica mark (the usual Mac look, but new artwork to design and review; can be done later as a replacement of the one key). No document icon (what macOS composes is not the app's own icon).
**Consequences:** **Open (only a real Mac answers):** that the Finder shows the Raffica icon for a PDF when Raffica is the default app (list view and Get Info; the Finder may need a moment or `killall Finder` to refresh its icon cache), and what the icon looks like at the sizes of the list and icon views.

## 2026-11-11 – Raffica PDF on macOS: the menus belong to the window, the app menu to the application (M3.2 follow-up)
**Context:** The owner's first check on a real Mac (macOS 15.8.2, Intel; `packaging/README.md`, "Manual check on a Mac (M3.2)", box a) found that the menu bar showed only an application menu titled "Avalonia Application": no File, Edit, View, Window or Help. `App.AttachMenu` had set one `NativeMenu` with all six menus on the application. Avalonia's documentation says that on macOS the menu set on the **Application** is the application menu only (the leftmost one), and the other menus of the bar come from the `NativeMenu` set on a **Window**. All our menus were therefore nested inside the application menu, never top-level. The headless tests read the `NativeMenu` object and could not see where the system puts it. The title "Avalonia Application" is Avalonia's fallback when `Application.Name` is not set.
**Decision:**
- **Two parts, one model.** `MacMenuModel.AppItems()` (About, Check for updates, Settings, then Open) is the content of the application menu; `MacMenuModel.WindowMenus()` (File, Edit, View, Window, Help) is what a window brings. `MacMenuModel.Build()` stays as the whole bar for the tests. `MacMenuBinder` takes a `MenuPart` (`All`, `App`, `Window`; default `All`).
- **One binder per window.** `MacWindowMenu.Attach(window, menuWindow)` makes a `MenuPart.Window` binder bound to that window and sets its menu on the window with `NativeMenu.SetMenu`. `App.CreateWindow` does it for every window on macOS outside a measurement run (`_menusOnWindows`); the system shows the menus of the window in front. A binder follows its own window only and lives with it.
- **The application binder follows the host.** `App.AttachMenu` makes the `MenuPart.App` binder, bound to `WindowHost.Current` as before, and sets it on the application. Its items are flat (no submenu), so macOS shows them in the application menu with its own Services, Hide and Quit items.
- **Open in the application menu, only without a window.** With no window the File menu is gone with its window, so Open (which makes a window first through the host) is an item of the application menu, visible only while no window exists (`NativeMenuItem.IsVisible`), so Cmd+O never belongs to two items.
- **The name.** `Name="Raffica PDF"` on the `Application` in `App.axaml` gives the application menu its title.
**Rationale:** The fix follows what the framework documents instead of a workaround, and keeps the model, the catalog labels and shortcuts and the binder logic as they were: only the place where the menu is set changes. A per-window binder keeps the states of that window exact (a window never reads another's commands) and needs no active-window bookkeeping beyond what the system does.
**Rejected alternatives:** One window-level menu shared by all windows (a `NativeMenu` has one owner, and the binder's states are per window). Keeping everything on the application and renaming the first menu (the framework puts only the application menu there). A hidden helper window to keep File alive without a window (a window with no content is a second thing to keep in step with the host).
**Consequences:** Without a window the bar shows the application menu only (About, Check for updates, Settings, Open, and the system's items); Preview-like File and Help menus with no window are not possible with Avalonia's two attach points. Proven by tests: the shape of both parts and that a window gets File through Help and no application items (`MacMenuBindingTests`, `MacMenuModelTests`). **Open (only a real Mac answers):** that the bar now shows "Raffica PDF  File  Edit  View  Window  Help"; that Ctrl+Cmd+F, which was dead while the Enter Full Screen item sat in the application menu, works (box f); that Cmd+A in the page list of the organize mode reaches the list (box m; the owner's first try may also have been without the plan open, which the organize mode needs); the German wording of the menus (box a, second half).

## 2026-11-10 – Raffica PDF on macOS: published sizes and the hosted record (M3.3)
**Context:** "Raffica on macOS: packaging, default app and updates (M3.3)" left the `macos` entry of `s_pdfBudgets` empty until the first real runs, and "Raffica on macOS: speed checks and CI (M3)" (and the plan, Task 12) said how to fill it: the size limits come from the hosted runner for both architectures, the time and memory limits only from the owner's Intel Mac, and the Apple-silicon speed numbers are a record without a limit (a hosted VM is no measuring machine). This entry records what was measured and what was derived from it. Everything below comes from real GitHub-hosted `macos-15` runs (Apple silicon; the Intel build is only built there and started under Rosetta), nothing from the owner's Mac.
**Published sizes (bytes), CI run 42865447655:** workflow CI, job `build-macos`, three attempts of the same head (698dce5), step "Sizes of the published app (recorded, not judged yet)" (a step without `--check`, so nothing was judged). The app publish folder had the OCR files (library and language data), the same shape as the release. "total" is the published folder as `size --check` counts it (no `.pdb`, `.dbg` or `.dSYM`), "zip" its zip, "app" every file of `Raffica PDF.app` (symbols included, symlinks skipped), "dmg" the image file.

| arch | metric | attempt 1 | attempt 2 | attempt 3 | median | MiB |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| osx-arm64 | total | 69973348 | 69973348 | 69973348 | 69973348 | 66.73 |
| osx-arm64 | zip | 31456955 | 31456958 | 31456958 | 31456958 | 30.00 |
| osx-arm64 | app | 76437261 | 76437261 | 76437261 | 76437261 | 72.90 |
| osx-arm64 | dmg | 36898909 | 36898899 | 36898899 | 36898899 | 35.19 |
| osx-x64 | total | 71801220 | 71801220 | 71801220 | 71801220 | 68.47 |
| osx-x64 | zip | 33029879 | 33029879 | 33029878 | 33029879 | 31.50 |
| osx-x64 | app | 78880445 | 78880445 | 78880445 | 78880445 | 75.23 |
| osx-x64 | dmg | 39247678 | 39247693 | 39247687 | 39247687 | 37.43 |

The native files (arm64 37149772, x64 37869652 bytes) and the fonts (3027784 bytes) were identical in all three attempts. The published folder is deterministic; only the compressed files (zip, dmg) move, by up to 15 bytes.
**Decision (the budgets):** the rule of M1 and M2.5 (median rounded up to the next MiB, limit = budget x 1.25 once, `Stats.Limit`) applied to the median of the **larger** architecture per metric, which is `osx-x64` for all four. Both architectures are judged against the one `macos` entry. The values stored in `s_pdfBudgets["macos"]` (MiB, as the table stores them) and the resulting limits:

| metric (key) | x64 median | budget (MiB) | limit (MiB) | limit (bytes) |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| size | 68.47 MiB | 69 | 86.25 | 90439680 |
| zip | 31.50 MiB | 32 | 40.00 | 41943040 |
| app | 75.23 MiB | 76 | 95.00 | 99614720 |
| dmg | 37.43 MiB | 38 | 47.50 | 49807360 |

The Apple-silicon sizes are below these limits (66.73, 30.00, 72.90 and 35.19 MiB). The step "Sizes of the published app" of `build-macos` now runs `measure.cs size --target pdf --check <rid> --app ... --dmg ...` for both architectures and fails the job above a limit (it checks both before it fails). The macOS entry has **no time and no memory limit on purpose**: a metric without a limit is `unrecorded` (a check that asks for it exits 1), and the size check asks only for size, zip, app and dmg. The selftest pins the four budgets, that every time and memory metric stays unrecorded on macOS, and the verdicts at and one byte above the limits on both architectures (`dotnet run bench/shell-probe/measure.cs -- selftest`, 369 checks). The Windows and Linux limits and verdicts are unchanged.
**Three size bases.** `total` (the published folder without symbols), `app` (every file of the bundle, symbols included) and the `dmg` file are different quantities on purpose; do not compare one with another or with the Windows and Linux sizes. Only `total` and `zip` are comparable across systems.
**Apple-silicon record, without a limit:** workflow Measure (`measure.yml`), platform `macos-hosted` (GitHub-hosted `macos-15`, Apple silicon, `osx-arm64`), run 42863871851, artifact `measure-macos-hosted-42863871851`, branch head e1ca1b3, target `pdf`, 10 runs per case, **no OCR files in that publish** (so its sizes differ from the table above). Medians: cold 685.1 ms, warm 554.5 ms, second file 52.4 ms, 1000 pages 546.5 ms, memory 133357568 bytes (127.18 MiB), scroll max 64.7 ms and p99 36.6 ms; size total 59835208 bytes (57.06 MiB), zip 26524623 (25.30 MiB), native 27011632, fonts 3027784. It carries **no limit and no verdict**: a shared VM varies a lot from day to day and is no binding machine, so the numbers say only that the app starts and scrolls on Apple silicon. The artifact expires after 7 days, so the numbers are copied here. The first dispatch (run 42863725956) was refused by the ref guard of `measure.yml` because a 40-hex commit that is on neither `main` nor `dev` is not accepted; a branch name works (`-f ref=feature/m33-package-and-release`), and the workflow file itself must exist on the dispatch ref.
**Rosetta first start:** `dmg-smoke` of CI run 42860216762 (head e1ca1b3) started the Intel app under Rosetta on the hosted Apple-silicon runner: the first start took 2.43 s against the ceiling of 5 s; the arm64 start took 0.06 s. One observation of one run, not a budget; the ceiling stays a coarse guard against a hang. The same run was green on Linux, Windows and macOS: the tests, the licence check, `macos-app` with `codesign --verify --deep --strict`, the Mach-O check, the command line smoke (a real OCR run on the bundle's CLI), `dmg`, `dmg-smoke` for both architectures and the artifact `raffica-pdf-macos-x64-dmg`. That answers the open items "strict signature with tessdata in `Contents/Resources`", "`licenses.cs check` on the `osx` publish folders" and "the Rosetta first start" of the entry "Raffica on macOS: packaging, default app and updates (M3.3)".
**Flaky tests seen on the hosted runner:** two macOS UI tests each failed once in earlier attempts and passed on a re-run, `OrganizeListTests.FocusStaysOnItsRowAfterSelectAllAndMoves` and `TextEditRobustnessTests.EmptyTextThroughEnterInTheRealWindowRemovesTheLineAndUndoBringsItBack`. Whether they were flaky before this milestone was not examined (not proven pre-existing); they are a backlog item, not a skip.
**Open (the owner's Intel Mac only):** the runner `runner-macbook-benni` (labels `self-hosted`, `X64`, `raffica-measure`, `macOS`) was **offline**, so these could not be decided and nothing was guessed: (1) the time and memory budgets of `s_pdfBudgets["macos"]`; (2) whether macOS gets software rendering as its default (`RAFFICA_RENDERING=software` exists and is opt-in; the macOS default is unchanged). Commands, once the runner is online: `gh workflow run measure.yml --ref feature/m33-package-and-release -f platform=macos -f target=pdf -f rendering=default -f note="<machine>"` and the same with `-f rendering=software`; apply the rule of "Raffica PDF: software rendering is the Windows default (M2.1 follow-up)" to choose the default, fill the time and memory entries by the M1 rule (time to the next 10 ms, memory to the next 5 MiB) from the chosen variant, change the macOS default in `Program.BuildAvaloniaApp` if software wins, and record both variants in a new entry. Until then a `measure.yml` check on the platform `macos` prints every time and memory row as `unrecorded` with its median and exits 1; that is the intended "not decided yet", not a regression.
**Consequences:** a published size above the limits fails `build-macos` from now on; the limits are never raised to make a run pass, a change is a new entry. A leaner OCR payload or a smaller runtime lowers them by a new measurement.
**Rejected alternatives:** a limit for the hosted Apple-silicon speed numbers (a shared VM is no measuring machine); time and memory budgets copied from Linux or Windows (the Mac is a different machine; the rule of M2 is that budgets are measured, not inherited); a separate size entry per architecture (the spec chose one entry; the Apple-silicon sizes are smaller, so the larger architecture binds both).

## 2026-11-10 – Raffica on macOS: packaging, default app and updates (M3.3)
**Context:** Milestone 3.3 ships Raffica PDF on macOS: a bundle, a disk image per architecture, the default-app offer, a self-update and a macOS job in `release.yml` (spec `Docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-09-raffica-macos-design.md`, sections 5.1, 5.7, 5.8, 6 and 7; plan `Docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-10-milestone-03-3-package-and-release.md`). The code was written on Windows. Everything that calls `hdiutil`, `ditto`, `codesign`, `plutil`, `xattr`, `sips`, `iconutil` or CoreServices is not proven by the Windows tests: the bundle and image steps were proven by `build-macos` (CI run 42860216762, green), everything else needs the owner's Mac (`packaging/README.md`, "Manual check on a Mac (M3.3)"). The measured numbers are in the entry "Raffica PDF on macOS: published sizes and the hosted record (M3.3)".
**Decision:**
- **Names and files.** `Raffica PDF.app` with the bundle id `io.github.benjo-code.raffica.pdf`; the executable is `RafficaPdf`, the command line tool `raffica` sits next to it in `Contents/MacOS`. Release files: `RafficaPdf-<v>-macos-arm64.dmg`, `RafficaPdf-<v>-macos-x64.dmg` and `SHA256SUMS-macos.txt`. No universal binary (two builds, two images).
- **Amendment of "x86_64 only" (M2.6):** macOS ships `arm64` and `x64`; Windows and Linux stay x86_64.
- **`Info.plist`.** Written by `MacApp.InfoPlist` from the version and from `MACOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET` of `packaging/native/versions.txt` (`LSMinimumSystemVersion`, never typed twice): identifier, executable, `CFBundleName` and `CFBundleDisplayName` "Raffica PDF", `APPL`, both version keys, `CFBundleIconFile` `RafficaPdf`, `NSHighResolutionCapable`, category `public.app-category.productivity` and `CFBundleDocumentTypes` for `com.adobe.pdf` (role Editor, `LSHandlerRank` Alternate). Only stable `X.Y.Z` versions are accepted: a prerelease tag has no numeric form in the plist, so `release-macos` fails on purpose for a `-rc` tag, and a `-rc` dry run ends red while Windows and Linux succeed. The tag `v0.1.0` is unaffected.
- **Signing order.** Ad-hoc only (`codesign --force -s -`, as the project rule says): first every Mach-O (`RafficaPdf`, `raffica`, each `.dylib`), then the bundle, then `codesign --verify --deep --strict` must pass or the step fails. The icon is made at packaging time from `Docs/design/icons/brand/pdf.png` (`sips`, `iconutil`), so no binary joins the repository. The CLI's native files must be byte-identical to the app's (no silent overwrite).
- **Risk that materialized: tessdata is in `Contents/Resources`.** The first plan kept tessdata and the libraries in `Contents/MacOS` and named the strict signature check as the deciding run. The first `build-macos` run (CI run 42843259224, step "Self-test of the packaging tool", the macOS-real `macos-app run`) signed every Mach-O, then failed to sign the bundle: `code object is not signed at all / In subcomponent: .../Contents/MacOS/tessdata/eng.traineddata`. macOS treats a folder inside `Contents/MacOS` as a nested code component. The fallback was taken: `macos-app` puts the language data in `Contents/Resources/tessdata` (mode 0644), whether it comes from the app publish folder (CI, where the csproj copied it next to the executable) or from `--native`, and the dylibs stay in `Contents/MacOS` (they are Mach-O files and are signed one by one). `TessdataStore.ShippedDirectoryFor` finds it through one bundle-specific rule: a base directory that is `<Something>.app/Contents/MacOS` with an existing `../Resources/tessdata` gets that folder (an own `tessdata` folder still wins, no other folder reads a `Resources` folder next to it, and the rule for `cli/` is unchanged). Both the app (`PdfWindow.Ocr.cs`) and the command line (`OcrCommand`) ask this method. `macos-app` also refuses a publish folder that holds any other folder than `tessdata` (and the skipped `*.dSYM`), naming it, and refuses to end with a folder in `Contents/MacOS`, because codesign would reject the bundle for it. Windows and Linux layouts are unchanged. `tools/smoke-cli.cs` looks in `../Resources/tessdata` too. What the run showed: the test suite, the Native AOT publish and the smoke tests passed; the bundle step was where it failed. CI run 42860216762 (head e1ca1b3, green) answered it: the bundle signs and `codesign --verify --deep --strict` passes, also on the copy that `dmg-smoke` takes out of the image with `ditto`; the real OCR run of the bundle's CLI works from `Resources/tessdata`; `dmg-smoke` passed for both images, the Intel first start under Rosetta took 2.43 s against the 5-second ceiling (the ceiling catches a build that cannot run, not speed). The updater's own use of `ditto` on a real Mac is still unproven.
- **Optional manifest kinds, as a pair.** `UpdateFileKinds.MacOsArm64Dmg` (`macos-arm64-dmg`) and `MacOsX64Dmg` (`macos-x64-dmg`); `update-manifest` takes a third sums file and refuses one macOS architecture without the other. The manifest `format` stays 1 (an older app skips an unknown kind), a manifest without the macOS files is byte-identical to before, and `testdata/updates/update-manifest.json` with its signature is unchanged.
- **Two package forms by process architecture.** `PackageForm.MacAppArm64` and `MacAppX64` (appended, persisted by name). The form follows `RuntimeInformation.ProcessArchitecture`, not the hardware, so an Intel build under Rosetta on an Apple-silicon Mac takes the Intel image. A `dotnet run` build (no `.app/Contents/MacOS/` in the path) is `Unknown` and shows the download page only.
- **The swap script instead of `--finish-update`.** The helper mode of the Windows installer form is Windows-only, and a copy of a running bundle cannot be the helper. On macOS the app (`MacAppUpdater`) downloads the `.dmg`, verifies the signed manifest, the size and the SHA-256 (`UpdateApplierSupport.VerifyAsync`), attaches the image read-only at an explicit mount point (no output is parsed), copies the app next to the installed one with `ditto`, always detaches (a second try with `-force`), checks the copy with `codesign --verify --deep --strict`, reads version and identifier with `plutil`, removes the quarantine attribute only after those checks and only when present, and then starts a detached `/bin/sh` (`MacSwapScript`). The script waits until the process has ended, re-checks the staged bundle with `codesign`, moves the old bundle to a backup, moves the new one into place (any failure puts the old one back and reopens it), deletes the backup and reopens the app (with `--restore-session` when a session was written). Every path is a positional argument of the script, never part of its text, so spaces and quotes in a path are harmless. The app verified the image, the signature, the size and the SHA-256 right before, so the window between the check and the move is the same-user write window the Windows helper also has.
- **A script that does not start is `InstallFailed`, not `NotStarted`.** The wording of `NotStarted` says that the new version is installed, which is false here: nothing is swapped when the script does not run.
- **Mounted image and translocation.** A bundle at `/Volumes/<name>/<App>.app` or under `/AppTranslocation/` cannot replace itself; `MacAppUpdater` refuses at apply time with `UpdateApplyProblem.RunsFromDownload` (worded in both languages, the download page stays available) before anything is touched. Known limit: the update UI still offers Install and the image is downloaded before the refusal; no new card API was added for it.
- **Default app: the C API.** `MacDefaultAppService` over `ILaunchServices` and `LaunchServicesNative` (`LibraryImport` of CoreServices and CoreFoundation, no Objective-C runtime) uses `LSCopyDefaultRoleHandlerForContentType` and `LSSetDefaultRoleHandlerForContentType` for `com.adobe.pdf`. The latter is deprecated; that it still works on macOS 15 is an assumption recalled from Apple's headers, not verified. If the owner's check shows that it fails or that the Finder ignores it, the fallback is an NSWorkspace-based service (an Objective-C use that its own plan must name); nothing else changes because the service sits behind `IDefaultAppService`. The status compares the bundle id ignoring case (LaunchServices hands it back in lower case) and treats no handler as "not default". The offer exists only from an installed bundle (not from the mounted image, not translocated, not a dev build).
- **Metrics and records.** The size metric `app` of `measure.cs size --app` counts every file of the bundle, symbols included, and skips symlinks, so it differs from `size/total`; `--dmg` is the file size. `s_pdfBudgets["macos"]` holds the four size limits (size, zip, app, dmg) since CI run 42865447655 and no time or memory entry: those metrics are `unrecorded` (exit 1) until the owner's Intel measurement (`measure.yml`, platform `macos`), and the size limits bind both architectures through the one entry. The `measure.yml` platform `macos-hosted` runs on the hosted `macos-15` (Apple silicon, `osx-arm64`) and records without judging. `RAFFICA_RENDERING=software` selects `AvaloniaNativeRenderingMode.Software` (type `AvaloniaNativePlatformOptions`) on macOS; the macOS default is unchanged until the Intel measurement decides (open, the Mac runner was offline).
- **CI and release.** `build-macos` runs the selftest of the packaging tool and, for both architectures, `macos-app`, the Mach-O check on the bundle, `licenses.cs check`, `smoke-cli.cs` on the bundle's CLI, `dmg` and `dmg-smoke`, then the step "Sizes of the published app" (the size check against the `macos` budget entry), and uploads the Intel image `raffica-pdf-macos-x64-dmg` (3 days). `release.yml` has the job `macos` (name `release-macos`, hosted `macos-15`, both architectures; it uploads the two images and `SHA256SUMS-macos.txt` only with `upload=true`); the job `manifest` waits for it and signs a manifest of six files.

**Open (only a real Mac or runner answers):** the deprecated `LSSetDefaultRoleHandlerForContentType` on macOS 15; the real macOS path of the updater (`hdiutil attach` at the mount point, `ditto`, the swap script, App Management); the update without a Gatekeeper prompt and the quarantine result; the Finder, icon and default app (boxes a to g of "Manual check on a Mac (M3.3)"); the Intel time and memory budgets and the macOS rendering default (the Mac runner was offline, see "Raffica PDF on macOS: published sizes and the hosted record (M3.3)"); the manifest job of `release.yml` with `upload=true` (it was skipped in the dry run), the real upload and the first release. Answered by the dry run of `release.yml` (`upload=false`, run 42883424963, commit 79efc9b, `release-preflight`, `release-linux`, `release-macos` and `release-windows` green, `release-manifest` skipped as expected): the macOS job's licence check, bundles, Mach-O check, CLI smoke with a real OCR run, images, `dmg-smoke` for both architectures and checksums. Answered by CI run 42877461391 (attempt 2): the size check `size --check <rid>` for both architectures (osx-arm64 66.73 MiB, osx-x64 68.47 MiB against the 69 MiB size basis). Answered by CI run 42860216762: the strict `codesign` check with tessdata in `Contents/Resources`, the Rosetta first start, `licenses.cs check` on the `osx` publish folders, `ditto` for `dmg-smoke`.
**Consequences:** The app stays ad-hoc signed, so every first launch (and, to be checked, possibly an update) needs the user's "Open Anyway"; the README says so and never claims the opposite. A change of the plist keys, the signing order or the file names is a new entry. A `-rc` tag cannot pass `release-macos` until the plist has a numeric form for it.

**Final review fixes (whole-branch review of M3.3):** (1) `third-party/licenses.json` owns `libAvaloniaNative.dylib` (`Avalonia.Native`), `libSkiaSharp.dylib` and `libHarfBuzzSharp.dylib` (the two `NativeAssets.macOS` entries, no longer `shipped: false`, so their licences are in the notices); `tools/licenses.cs` ignores every file below a `*.dSYM` folder (the symbols of an AOT publish) and its selftest pins the three owners; `build-macos` runs `licenses.cs check` and `smoke-cli.cs` on the bundle's CLI for both architectures (both passed in CI run 42860216762, so the publish folders hold nothing else). (2) `tools/check-binary.cs` reads `CA FE BA BE` and `CA FE BA BF` as Mach-O: a universal `*.dylib` (the three NuGet libraries are universal) passes when it holds the expected slice and is read on that slice (`otool -arch`, `vtool -arch`); `RafficaPdf` and `raffica` stay exactly one architecture; a file that is neither ELF nor Mach-O is a failure instead of an ELF check. (3) A disk image a crashed update left mounted in `<config>/updates/<version>/mnt` blocks the recursive delete of that folder, so every later download failed as a storage failure until a restart: `UpdateFlow.BeforeStagingDelete` (`MacStagingMount.ReleaseAsync`, wired by `UpdateServices.StagingReleaseFor` on macOS only) runs `hdiutil detach <mnt> -force` first, best effort, and only when the `mnt` folder exists. (4) `MacSwapScript` reopens the old bundle with `--args --restore-session` on a failed verify or a failed move when the session was written, like the success path. (5) `MacAppUpdater` fails with the folder (`FolderWriteProtected`) when a stale staged or backup entry stays after the delete, instead of copying into it; a null outcome of `hdiutil attach` is `InstallFailed` and still detaches. (6) The README and the CHANGELOG say that the default-app offer works from an installed copy (not from the disk image or a translocated copy), which is what `MacDefaultAppService.IsSupported` accepts. Open: whether App Management (macOS 13 and later) lets an ad-hoc signed app replace itself in `/Applications` is unverified; box d of the owner's checklist asks for the prompt and the leftovers.

## 2026-11-10 – Raffica on macOS: menu bar, keys and life cycle (M3.2)
**Context:** Milestone 3.2 makes Raffica PDF behave like a Mac app (decision "Raffica on macOS: scope and Mac conventions (M3)", spec `Docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-09-raffica-macos-design.md`, sections 4 and 5). The code was written and reviewed on Windows; the macOS-only parts are proven by `build-macos` where a test can show them and otherwise by the owner's Mac (`packaging/README.md`, "Manual check on a Mac (M3.2)").
**Decision:**
- **Menu bar:** `MacMenuModel` (`Platform/MacOS`, no platform attribute, tested everywhere) is the whole menu as data: `CommandNode` (an item that runs a catalog command; header and shortcut are the catalog's own, on macOS the `macShortcut` of the command), `ActionNode` (an item that only macOS defines: About, Cut, Paste, Minimize, Zoom, Bring All to Front, Enter Full Screen, plus the recent-files fill) and `SubmenuNode`. `MacMenuBinder` builds the Avalonia `NativeMenu` from it and keeps it in step with the window: enabled and checked state come from the command (`CommandOf`, `IsCommandOn`) and are refreshed when the current window changes (`WindowHost.CurrentChanged`), when the window's commands change (`IMenuWindow.StatesChanged`) and when the menu opens (`NeedsUpdate`, on the bar and on every submenu's own `NativeMenu`, subscribed once when the submenu is built), plus one `Refresh` right after `NativeMenu.SetMenu` in `App.AttachMenu` so the first window is followed from the start. Native versus wired: Services, Hide, Hide others, Show all and Quit are added by Avalonia's native side to the first menu and are not in the model; everything else is wired by the binder. The app menu's header is the existing string `AppMarkName`; no second string for the same name. Cut and Paste have no catalog shortcut, so the binder gives them the system gestures Meta+X and Meta+V (without a key equivalent the macOS menu would not have them); Undo, Redo, Copy and Select all are catalog commands, and with a text box focused (comment box, text-box editor, search field) the binder routes the Edit items to that box (`TextBox.Undo`, `Copy`, `SelectAll`, ...), never to the document or the annotation history. In the page list of the organize mode Cmd+A selects pages and Cmd+C copies pages, not page text: `PdfWindow.OnShortcutKeyDown` exempts them with `FocusIsInOrganizeList()`, and the menu path does the same through `IMenuWindow.OwnsEditKeys` (the same call). While it is true the `text.copy` and `text.selectall` items are disabled, so AppKit passes the key to the list; a focused text box still has priority (its routing is checked first), and Undo and Redo stay enabled because the window sends them to the plan. While a card is open (`IMenuWindow.DialogOpen`, from `Host.IsOpen`) every command item, every Open Recent entry and About are disabled, as the window's own shortcuts are, except the edit items that act on a focused box; the window items Minimize, Zoom, Bring All to Front and Enter Full Screen are not gated.
- **A Help menu with one item (Help, F1):** the F1 card is the only help the app has; a second item would be a second way into the same card, and macOS's search field in the Help menu has nothing to search. The item is the catalog's `Help` command.
- **No window:** `WindowHost` (`Hosting/WindowHost.cs`, with `IHostedWindow` and `IReopenSource`) holds the app alive after the last window closes (macOS only; Windows and Linux keep `ShutdownMode` and the old path). With no window the menu runs only Open (the binder disables every command item when `current()` is null, Open Recent included; Open makes a window first through `host.Reopen()`), About and the system items stay. A Dock click (`Reopen` of `IReopenSource`) shows a new window, a Finder file with no window creates one, a file while a window is open becomes a tab. The dispatcher is captured on the UI thread in the constructor (pool-thread rule); `OpenFiles` and `Reopen` may be called from any thread and post to it.
- **Close questions and quit:** a plain window close runs the close questions and leaves the host alive. Cmd+Q (`WindowHost.Quit`) sets `QuitRequested` on the window and runs the same close questions; Cancel (or an exception) clears the flag, so a later plain close never ends the app; a quit upgrades a close that is already running, a plain close never downgrades it. A file that reaches a window while it is closing (queued behind the close card, or after the close was confirmed) is returned by `IHostedWindow.TakeUndelivered` and opens in the next window; a quit discards them. Known gap: a hand-over whose UI-thread step runs after the window has closed and `TakeUndelivered` has run has no taker (very narrow).
- **Per window, not per app:** the update flow and the dialog queue stay on the window, as before; they come back with each new window (the crash card goes to `WindowHost.Current`; `ShowCrashCard` reports the card closed once, also when its window closes first, so the crash guard never waits for a card that cannot return). An install that takes over ends the app through `App.EndAction`: `WindowHost.Quit` when macOS hosts the windows (nothing is made afterwards by a waiting file or a Dock click), else the plain shutdown; the menu's "Open" without a window runs under `PdfWindow.GuardAsync`. A ready update (downloaded, not installed) is dropped together with its window; the next window checks again. `WindowSettingsSource` (`Hosting/WindowSettingsSource.cs`) hands the first window the settings read at process start and re-reads both settings files for every later one, so a second window gets the placement and the compact-bar choice the first one wrote when it closed. `App.CreateWindow` is the one place that makes a window. The wiring is macOS-only and absent in measurement runs.
- **Host event instead of a window hook:** `WindowHost.CurrentChanged` (raised when a window is adopted, created or closed) tells the binder which window is current. The plan had the window announce `Activated`; with one window at a time that adds nothing (spec 5.3).
- **Pinch zoom:** `ViewCommands.ZoomByMagnify` is continuous between the limits of `ZoomSteps` and does not snap to its table (a trackpad pinch that jumped by steps would feel wrong); the zoom commands step on from the pinched value; the pinch is around the pointer; it ends a fit mode. Retina: tiles are asked for at the window's scaling (`AtScalingTwoTilesAskForTwiceThePixels`), no production tile code changed.
- **Title bar:** the 80 px inset for the traffic lights stays, except in full screen where the system hides them: `TitleBar.EffectiveInset` is 0 there on macOS, so no empty gap stays.
- **Shortcut audit:** `ReservedShortcuts.MacOs` lists what macOS keeps (Cmd+H, Alt+Cmd+H, Cmd+M, Cmd+Q, Shift+Cmd+Q, Cmd+Tab, Cmd+Space, Cmd+`, Ctrl+Cmd+F, Shift+Cmd+3/4/5) and `ReservedShortcuts.ClashesIn(true)` is the audit. The only clash was Recognize text, Ctrl+Shift+Q, which became Shift+Cmd+Q and is macOS Log Out: it moves to Shift+Cmd+R on macOS only (`macShortcut` of `ocr.run`; Windows and Linux keep Ctrl+Shift+Q). `NoCatalogShortcutClashesWithAReservedMacShortcutOnMacOs` pins the result and `TheUnmovedCatalogHasExactlyOneClash` pins that the unmoved table (`DefaultShortcut`) has exactly this one clash, so the audit cannot silently go empty; `NoTwoCommandsShareAShortcutOnEitherSystem` guards the two tables.
- **The page list's command modifier:** the organize list read Control for Ctrl+D and Ctrl+A and printed `Ctrl+D` in the macOS help. `OrganizeList.CommandModifier` now defaults to `KeyNames.CommandModifier` (Meta on macOS, Control elsewhere) and `NavigationHelp.Entries(bool macOs)` renders the lines for the system. In the list Cmd+A selects pages, not page text.
- **Key tips off (spec 5.4):** on macOS `KeyTips.Offered(macOs)` is false: no `FieldCodes` is attached to the window, no ribbon key tips, no Alt capture (so Alt types `@` and the euro sign on the German layout and nothing flashes in the ribbon), and `NavigationHelp` leaves out the field-code line.
