Windows and Linux are unchanged (`KeyTipPolicyTests`). Every command stays reachable through the menu bar, Cmd+K (title-bar search) and Tab through the ribbon. This closes the backlog entry "Option key handling on macOS", which moved into 3.2 at planning time (see "Scope changes" of the roadmap).
- **CUPS on macOS:** printing uses the own print card over CUPS as on Linux (`CupsPrintService`); `PlatformPrintServices.Choose` returns it for Linux and macOS and `CupsNative` binds `libcups.2.dylib` (found in `/usr/lib`) through a resolver for the logical name `cups`. The files stay in `Platform/Linux` for now; moving three files and their tests would be churn without a behavior change, and the folder is renamed when a second consumer needs a neutral home. `CupsLibraryLoadsOnMacOs` is the one test that loads the real library (skipped elsewhere with a reason). Real printers are the owner's check. The native print panel stays in the backlog ("macOS print panel").
- **German wording:** the system items (Services, Hide, ..., Window menu items) use Apple's own German terms as the author recalls them; they are not verified against a German macOS and the owner reads them on the Mac (`--lang de`). Every item that runs an app command shows that command's catalog label, in both languages. The German is in the "Sie" form like the rest of the app.
- **Avalonia assumption:** `NativeMenu` on macOS is expected to add Services, Hide, Hide others, Show all and Quit to the first menu by itself; CI cannot see it. The assumption is recalled from Avalonia's macOS menu code and was not verified in the session that wrote it. If the owner's check shows one of them missing, the fallback is one `ActionNode` per missing item that calls the application's shutdown (`TryShutdown`) or the `NSApp` equivalent; the model has the room for it and nothing else changes. Until then no such item is duplicated by hand.
- **CI and tests:** `build-macos` now also runs on a change under `src/Raffica.Pdf/Hosting/` and on `src/Raffica.Pdf/App.axaml.cs` (`macos-paths.sh`, `macos-paths.test.sh`), because the macOS-only wiring lives there. Tests: `ReservedShortcutTests`, `WindowHostTests` (fake windows), `WindowHostWindowTests` (real `PdfWindow`), `MacMenuModelTests`, `MacMenuBindingTests`, `KeyTipPolicyTests`, `PinchZoomTests`, `TitleBarInsetTests`, `PlatformPrintServicesTests`.
**Rationale:** The menu bar is data bound to the command catalog, so no command exists twice and a new command needs one model line, not new behavior. A host above the window keeps the life-cycle rules (alive without a window, Dock, quit, files in flight) testable with fake windows and away from UI code. Moving the one clashing shortcut on macOS only keeps Windows and Linux muscle memory and the documentation of the ribbon stable; the pinned unmoved table keeps the audit honest. Dropping key tips instead of remapping Alt keeps the German layout typing.
**Known limits (open until the owner checks them):** the live behavior of the native menu (state, key equivalents, the system items), the Dock and Finder paths, pinch and Retina sharpness, the full-screen title bar and real printers are the owner's checklist; double handling of a shortcut by the native key equivalent and the window's own shortcut handler (Cmd+S once, Shift+Cmd+D once, no repeat on a held key) cannot be seen headless. Deferred minors of the reviews, still open: **log out, restart and shut down** (`PdfWindow.OnWindowClosing` sets `e.Cancel = true` unconditionally, also for `ApplicationShutdown` and `OSShutdown` with nothing unsaved; macOS probably treats the cancel as aborting the whole log out or restart, and `CloseGuard` has no synchronous "nothing to ask" check, so the real fix is a follow-up and the owner checks it, `packaging/README.md`); the closed first window is still reachable through `desktop.MainWindow`; an exception from `_create` inside `WindowHost.OnClosed` propagates into `Window.Close`; the `CA1416` pragma on `PlatformPrintServices.Choose`; no Linux-CI test of the resolver load path; page-list tests that still press Control on macOS (macOS CI triage; the organize list tests that mean the command key use `CommandKey` now); the Ctrl+Cmd+F reserved entry cannot be detected by the `PdfShortcut` clash model, which has no Control-plus-Command base; a focus change does not refresh the menu (Cut and Paste are right on open); `ToggleWindowZoom` from full screen loses the saved state; the pinch over the inline field editor bubbles to the page zoom (confirm intended).
**Rejected alternatives:** a Help menu with search and several items (no second help); snapping the pinch to the zoom table; a window-level Alt remap instead of switching key tips off; keeping the app alive through a hidden window instead of a host; making the update flow and the dialog queue app-wide now (a bigger change with no gain until Write shares a host); Objective-C runtime calls for the menu (everything stays `LibraryImport` and Avalonia, Native AOT safe); moving the CUPS files out of `Platform/Linux` in this milestone.

## 2026-11-09 – Raffica on macOS: native libraries, paths and activation (M3.1)
**Context:** Milestone 3.1 makes Raffica PDF build, test and open files from the Finder on `osx-x64` and `osx-arm64` (decision "Raffica on macOS: scope and Mac conventions (M3)"). The owner's Mac is an Intel MacBook Pro 2016, hosted macOS runners are arm64 VMs, and the repository has no Apple account.
**Decision:**
- **Minimum macOS 15.0** (`MACOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET` in `packaging/native/versions.txt`). Source 1: .NET 10's oldest supported macOS is 15 (dotnet/core `release-notes/10.0/supported-os.md` lists "27, 26, 15"). Source 2: Avalonia 12 gives support tiers only (macOS 26 Tier 1, 15 and 14 Tier 2, docs.avaloniaui.net/docs/supported-platforms) and no number. The higher stated minimum wins; 15 is also the owner's Mac. The library build uses it as its deployment target and milestone 3.3 reads the same value for `LSMinimumSystemVersion`.
- **`PathRules` (Core)** is the one place that chooses how paths compare: Windows `OrdinalIgnoreCase`, Linux `Ordinal`, macOS an `Ordinal` match or an NFC-normalized `OrdinalIgnoreCase` match. 18 sites that chose by a Windows check now call it; `NoSourceFileChoosesPathCaseByWindowsCheck` guards that (it reads single lines only, so a check split over two lines is not seen). Limits: a case-sensitive macOS volume is still treated as case-insensitive by name, so two files that differ only by case count as one there; where a file system answer is available the identity checks decide (`FileIdentity.AreSameFile`); nothing but NFC is folded (no diacritics, no width, no compatibility forms). `OutputNames.cs` still compares with `OrdinalIgnoreCase` on every OS (deferred).
- **`FileIdentity` on macOS** is `st_ino` plus `st_dev` from `stat$INODE64` on x64 and plain `stat` on arm64: plain `stat` on x86_64 is the legacy variant with 32-bit inodes and a different struct layout, while arm64 only ever had the 64-bit one. The real calls are proven only by CI: `FileIdentityTests` run on the arm64 runner (plain `stat`), and the step "Smoke test of the hard-link guard" of `build-macos` makes a hard link of a PDF and requires the CLI to refuse it as "the same file under another name or path" for both RIDs, so the x64 build under Rosetta reaches `stat$INODE64` through `FileIdentity`; the older same-file smoke test (`Bericht.pdf` against `bericht.pdf`) only hits the case-insensitive `PathRules` branch of `OutputGuard` first. `OpenTargetGuard` treats a null answer as the same file on macOS too.
- **Single instance:** a Unix socket path is limited by `sun_path` (104 bytes on macOS, 108 on Linux, terminator included). A name is used as is under 100 UTF-8 bytes; for a long temp path the name is `r-` plus 8 bytes of SHA-256 as hex. Windows pipe names are always plain (`PipeNameFor(string)` returns the plain name there, pinned by `WindowsKeepsThePlainNameHoweverLongItIs`). The Windows test `SingleInstanceTests.SeveralSendersAreAllDelivered` failed in 2 of 4 runs of the pull request (42700451097, 42712926953), so it was not just a flake: the listener dropped a whole message when the end of the connection came after its 3 s read timeout (or the pipe broke first), even though the sender's write had been accepted (`TrySend` true). That is a product weakness that existed before this milestone, and it also exists on Windows and Linux. `SingleInstance.ReadMessageAsync` (commit f2f645a) now lets the parser decide: a message that arrived whole is delivered, a cut-off one is still dropped; the oversize limit, the stopped-listener behaviour and the socket and pipe access rules are unchanged. The read timeout is an `internal` `ReadTimeout` setting so that the tests can shorten it; two new tests assert both directions. Evidence after the fix is thin (a few green runs), so watch the Windows job.
- **File activation:** the Finder hands files over through `IActivatableLifetime` (`FileActivatedEventArgs`), not `argv`. `FileActivationBridge` with `AvaloniaFileActivationSource` queues files until the window attaches; the wiring in `App` is macOS-only and absent in measurement runs; `PdfOptions` ignores `-psn_*`. Reopen (Dock click without files) belongs to 3.2. The live behavior (first start and while running) is the owner's manual check.
- **Mac conventions found by CI:** the title bar draws no caption buttons on macOS (`ShowCaptionButtons` defaults to false, `LeadingInset` 80, the system decorations stay), so a test that needs the buttons sets `ShowCaptionButtons = true`; shortcut texts use macOS glyphs; frame captures are RGBA there (`PixelOrder` swaps by the capture's format). No render difference against the Windows-made reference set showed up, so no references, tolerances or macOS skips were added. `build-macos` lists every skip with a `macOS:` reason and fails on a skipped test with an empty reason; first green run 42709065751 (head 51606c5): 6307 total, 0 failed, 63 skipped (all with pre-existing reasons). Four headless tests then flaked on the slower runner (runs 42720705154, 42727720704) and were fixed by waiting for the real condition (first sharp page, a render tick before a hit test, the pending form walk), never by loosening an assertion (commits 66bbd5d, b107c48); green again on b107c48 (run 42731782508) and on 4a88c98 (run 42736005328), where the hard-link smoke passed for both RIDs. More such tests can show up on a loaded macOS runner.
- **Tesseract for macOS:** `packaging/native/build-macos.sh`, run through the new `systems=macos` input of `native-tesseract.yml`, builds `libtesseract.dylib` for both RIDs as the prerelease `native-tesseract-5.5.4-2` (run 42699294492); the Windows and Linux pins and release `-1` are unchanged. Assets: `libtesseract-osx-x64.dylib` (4847040 bytes, SHA-256 `4928b312945529d558fc95207e9437f55b0288d0b0e39c70f71ae375d9d0565b`) and `libtesseract-osx-arm64.dylib` (4499616 bytes, SHA-256 `922ec3f4377ac4926a63d3fa4228fe2b620a5bbe15212772814076a06d8d35f2`), both laid down by `tools/native-fetch.cs` as `libtesseract.dylib`. Flags: Leptonica static, no image decoders, `OPENMP_BUILD=OFF`, `DISABLE_CURL`, `DISABLE_ARCHIVE`, `GRAPHICS_DISABLED`, `ENABLE_NATIVE=OFF` (no `-march`), `-Wl,-headerpad_max_install_names`, `-dead_strip`, `strip -x`, install name `@loader_path/libtesseract.dylib`, deployment target 15.0, `CMAKE_IGNORE_PREFIX_PATH` for Homebrew.
- **Mach-O rules:** `tools/check-binary.cs` (`MachORules.Violations`): every dependency must be in `/usr/lib`, in `/System/Library` or an `@rpath`/`@loader_path`/`@executable_path` file inside the bundle; a dylib's own install name (`otool -D`, the first entry of `otool -L`) is not a dependency and is dropped, nothing else is; unparsable `otool` output fails closed; switches `--arch`, `--max-macos`, `--bundle`; `check-binary selftest` pins the rules (the step runs in `build-macos`).
- **`build-macos` triggers:** pull request into `main`, push to `main`, the label `ci:macos`, or a platform-near path (`.github/scripts/macos-paths.sh`, tested by `macos-paths.test.sh`; the script reads its whole input so that a long file list cannot kill the producer with SIGPIPE under `pipefail`, and `global.json` counts as platform-near); never a draft or a docs-only change. The `labeled` pull request type is added to `ci.yml`, so adding any label re-runs the whole CI and cancels a running one (concurrency). The job runs on the arm64 `macos-15` runner and builds the x64 variant under Rosetta (`arch -x86_64`); it uploads `raffica-pdf-osx-x64` (3 days) for the owner's manual check. The aggregate `ci` treats a skipped macOS job as passed.
**Rationale:** One path rule in one place keeps the macOS case question out of 18 call sites and out of review. The identity checks, not the spelling, are the only honest answer for "same file". Hashing only long names keeps short names readable and the rule testable. A separate release `-2` leaves the Windows and Linux pins byte-identical.
**Lessons from the Tesseract build:** (1) macOS ships bash 3.2, where `source <(cmd)` reads nothing and does not fail; use `eval "$(...)"`. (2) The x86_64 library is cross-built on the arm64 runner, and Tesseract's CMake takes `CMAKE_SYSTEM_PROCESSOR` from the host: a plain `-DCMAKE_SYSTEM_PROCESSOR` is ignored in non-cross mode (the log showed arm64 and `HAVE_NEON` TRUE, then a link error for NEON symbols). The script writes `target-processor.cmake` and passes it as `CMAKE_PROJECT_INCLUDE`; setting `CMAKE_SYSTEM_NAME` would switch CMake to cross mode and break Tesseract's unconditional `try_run` for `LEPT_TIFF`. The script asserts the configure log, `lipo -archs` and that the x86_64 library holds no NEON symbol. If a cross-build problem returns, build x86_64 on the hosted Intel runner `macos-15-intel`. (3) The workflow refuses a rerun with an existing release tag ("a rebuild needs a new number"), and a failed run leaves an empty prerelease behind: delete it (`gh release delete native-tesseract-5.5.4-2 --cleanup-tag --yes`) before the next dispatch.
**Known limits:** the double-click and `open -a` paths and OCR on a real Mac are manual checks of the owner (`packaging/README.md`, "Manual check on a Mac (M3.1)"); the build without a RuntimeIdentifier (the shell-probe bench) now also resolves the osx natives, which must be checked against the M3 size budgets in 3.3; `tools/package.cs` is Windows and Linux only until 3.3; Apple silicon is covered by arm64 CI only, the x64 build is the one that runs under Rosetta there and says nothing about arm64.
**Rejected alternatives:** a plain `-DCMAKE_SYSTEM_PROCESSOR` for the x64 library (ignored, see lesson 2); case folding by culture or NFKC (changes names the file system keeps apart); a per-OS path comparer chosen at each call site (the 18 sites are what this replaces).

## 2026-11-09 – Raffica on macOS: speed checks and CI (M3)
**Context:** Speed is the core promise, so the Mac build must be measured like the other two. But the only Mac the owner can test by hand is an Intel MacBook Pro 2016, hosted macOS runners are VMs whose timings vary too much, and the owner wants as few macOS CI runs as possible without losing a check. The repository is public, so standard hosted runners cost nothing.
**Decision:**
- **Size budgets are binding** and come from hosted-runner measurements (the size of the app, the `.app` and each `.dmg` does not depend on the machine), by the M1 rule (median rounded up, limit = budget × 1.25 once), in `s_pdfBudgets` of `measure.cs`.
- **Time and memory budgets are binding for the owner's Intel Mac only,** measured through `measure.yml` (`target=pdf`, `platform=macos`, the owner's self-hosted runner) when the owner dispatches it. The same runs decide the Mac rendering default (Avalonia's own path or software, as for Windows); `RAFFICA_RENDERING` stays the opt-out.
- **Apple silicon is recorded without a limit** in `Docs/DECISIONS.md`; the CI and release smokes carry a coarse start ceiling (a few seconds, value set in 3.3) that catches gross faults such as the x64 build running under Rosetta.
- **`ci.yml` gets a `build-macos` job on `macos-15`** that runs only on pull requests into `main`, on pull requests that touch platform-near paths, and on pull requests with the label `ci:macos`; the aggregate `ci` check counts a skipped macOS job as passed. `release.yml` builds, signs and smokes both architectures on a hosted macOS runner.
- Standard hosted runners are used (free in the public repository); larger runners are never used.
- A miss against a budget is a finding for the owner; budgets are never loosened to make a check pass.
**Rationale:** Only what a machine-independent number can bind is bound everywhere; time and memory bind where the machine is known and stable enough. A sparse CI job keeps the quality gate for platform code and for releases without a macOS queue on every pull request.
**Rejected alternatives:** macOS on every pull request (slow queue, little gain for changes that cannot touch the Mac build); binding time budgets from hosted VMs (they vary too much); loosening a budget to pass.

## 2026-11-09 – Raffica on macOS: scope and Mac conventions (M3)
**Context:** Raffica PDF `v0.1.0` ships for Windows and Linux. Subproject 4 brings it to macOS before Write, so every later app inherits the platform work. Spec: `Docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-09-raffica-macos-design.md`. It builds on the existing decision "macOS without paid Apple account" (ad-hoc signing, `.dmg`, documented first launch).
**Decision:**
- **Scope:** parity with `v0.1.0` on `osx-x64` and `osx-arm64`. Apple silicon is released with automatic checks only and marked "not tested by hand" in the README.
- **Packaging:** two `.dmg` files (one per architecture), bundle id `io.github.benjo-code.raffica.pdf`, the CLI `raffica` inside the bundle (not on `PATH`).
- **Menu bar:** a native menu bar bound to the command catalog, next to the ribbon.
- **Keyboard:** no ribbon key tips and no ⌥ capture on macOS (this decides the backlog entry "Option key handling on macOS"); every command stays reachable through the menu bar, ⌘K and Tab through the ribbon.
- **Life cycle:** the app outlives its last window; Dock reopen shows a new window; the close questions run on window close and on ⌘Q. Files from the Finder arrive through activation events, not `argv`.
- **Data:** settings, recent files, signatures, the crash log and update staging live under `~/Library/Application Support/Raffica`, caches under `~/Library/Caches/Raffica`.
- **Paths:** on macOS two spellings of one file are the same file (case-insensitive volumes); `FileIdentity.AreSameFile` answers from device and inode.
- **Printing:** over CUPS with the app's own print card, as on Linux; the native print panel goes to the backlog ("macOS print panel").
- **Default app:** offered through LaunchServices for `com.adobe.pdf`.
- **Updates:** the app swaps its own `.app` from the downloaded `.dmg` after the signature, size and SHA-256 checks, with the download page as fallback when it cannot write next to its bundle or runs translocated.
**Rationale:** Mac users expect a menu bar, Finder and Dock integration and ⌘ shortcuts, and the German Mac layout types characters on ⌥ (@ on ⌥L, € on ⌥E), so taking it would break typing. One build per architecture keeps each download small.
**Rejected alternatives:** a universal binary (doubles the download for every user); the native macOS print panel (needs Objective-C interop for NSPrintOperation, backlog); ⌥ key tips (break typing on the German Mac layout); quitting with the last window (not Mac-like, Preview does not).

## 2026-11-09 – Raffica PDF: Windows start time at the final head and before the ribbon work (M2.7)
**Context:** The final review of M2.7 asked whether the Windows excess over the budgets (see "Raffica PDF: release readiness measurements (M2.7)") comes from the ribbon work of this milestone (the full band is built once and then the compact one at the default width), and noted that those numbers were taken at `1db9043`, not at the final head.
**Decision:** Two more `measure.yml` runs on the Windows PC (target `pdf`, 10 runs per case, `check` passes in both): the final dev head `3bf52c8` (run 42625828393) and `d77e784` (the merge of #50, the last commit before any ribbon change of M2.7; run 42625887333, input `ref`).

| Case | `d77e784` (before the ribbon work) | `3bf52c8` (final head) | Budget / limit |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cold start (ms) | 225.33 | 218.24 | 200 / 250 |
| Warm start (ms) | 219.09 | 225.47 | 210 / 262.5 |
| 1000-page file (ms) | 213.9 | 217.45 | 200 / 250 |
| Second file (ms) | 19.08 | 19.3 | 20 / 25 |
| Scroll p99 (ms) | 18.05 | 18.1 | 20 / 25 |
| Idle memory (MiB) | 39.63 | 41.79 | 40 / 50 |
| Published size (MiB) | 59.87 | 60.09 | 66 / 82.5 |

The start rows are the same before and after the ribbon work, inside the run-to-run spread of about 15 ms: the ribbon change costs no measurable start time, and the excess over the budgets (cold 195 ms, warm 202 ms and 1000 pages 191 ms at M2.1) was already there at `d77e784`, so it comes from M2.2 to M2.6 and the first part of M2.7 (not bisected further because no limit is exceeded). Idle memory grows by 2.2 MiB through the work of #51 to #53 (39.63 to 41.79 MiB, budget 40 MiB, limit 50 MiB): the ribbon with its icons, the status bar and the cards are the candidates; this was not narrowed down. The size is under its budget in both.
**Rationale:** One run before and one after the suspected change, on the same machine and the same day, separates the change from the drift. The result is a finding for the owner, not a loosened budget: if the budgets are to be met again, bisect from M2.1 (`measure.yml ... -f ref=<40-hex sha>` over the merge commits of `dev`).
**Rejected alternatives:** choosing the layout before the first ribbon build (it would fix a cost that was not measured); raising the budgets.

## 2026-11-09 – Raffica PDF: release readiness measurements (M2.7)
**Context:** The review of 2026-11-08 found the Linux measurement run 42502138965 red (cold start 334 ms against the limit of 287.5 ms, second file 58 ms against 50 ms) and a control run of the predecessor (42503638604) that suggested the laptop was 31 to 37 % slower than on 2026-11-04. Milestone 2.7 therefore had to answer: is that a regression of the app, or of the machine? `measure.yml` got the input `ref` (a commit or branch to measure, validated like the update-check workflows) so that a bisect would have been possible.
**Decision:** On 2026-11-09 `measure.yml` ran on `dev` (head `1db9043`, the state with Tasks 1 to 14 merged) on the two binding machines (Windows: `runner-dotnet-windows-gpu`, Linux: the HP ProBook `runner-probook-benni`), target `pdf` and the predecessor Pagewing v1.0.0 (pinned by SHA) as the control, each with 10 runs per case and the machines idle. Result of `check --target pdf`: **every case is `ok` (inside its limit) on both machines.** No bisect was needed and none was run.

| Case | Windows (run 42608090757, repeat 42620074198) | Windows budget / limit | Linux (run 42608099486) | Linux budget / limit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Cold start (ms) | 234.65, 219.72 | 200 / 250 | 237.34 | 230 / 287.5 |
| Warm start (ms) | 219.16, 218.76 | 210 / 262.5 | 234.2 | 230 / 287.5 |
| 1000-page file (ms) | 205.7, 207.39 | 200 / 250 | 248.21 | 220 / 275 |
| Second file (ms) | 19.65, 19.34 | 20 / 25 | 46.81 | 40 / 50 |
| Scroll p99 (ms) | 18.29, 18.11 | 20 / 25 | 38.29 | 40 / 50 |
| Idle memory (MiB) | 41.7, 41.7 | 40 / 50 | 165.08 | 155 / 193.75 |
| Published size (MiB) | 60.08, 60.08 | 66 / 82.5 | 57.01 | 65 / 81.25 |

Control (Pagewing v1.0.0, medians of the same session): Windows cold 161.8 to 169.0 ms, warm 160.5 to 167.3, second file 31.3 to 31.6; Linux cold 206.2 to 210.5, warm 193.5 to 207.0, second file 34.6 to 39.6. The control of 2026-11-08 had measured warm 275 to 286 ms on the same laptop, so the red run was a slow day of the machine (the predecessor was 30 % slower too), not a regression of the app. On 2026-11-04 the app measured cold 227.51 and warm 227.83 ms on that laptop (M2.1); today 237.34 and 234.2, about 4 % apart, inside the spread of the machine.
**Findings for the owner (not loosened, not hidden):**
- On Windows the rows cold (200), warm (210), 1000 pages (200) and idle memory (40 MiB) are above their budgets by up to 17 % (cold, first run; the repeat showed 9.9 %) and by 4 % (warm, 1000 pages, memory), but inside the limits, which is the rule of M1 (limit = budget × 1.25). Cold start varies by about 15 ms between two runs of the same commit, so a single cold figure is not a verdict; warm, 1000 pages and memory repeat within 2 %. The cause of the excess over the budgets was not investigated, because no limit is exceeded; if the budgets are to be met again, the next step is `measure.yml ... -f ref=<sha>` over the merge commits of M2.2 to M2.7.
- Against the predecessor on the same Windows machine the app starts about 40 % slower (cold 220 to 235 ms against 162 to 169 ms), while its second file is faster (19 ms against 31 ms). The predecessor has no ribbon and no OCR payload.
- Linux idle memory is 165 MiB, 6 % above its budget of 155 MiB and inside the limit of 193.75 MiB; second file 46.8 ms is 17 % above its budget of 40 ms and inside 50 ms. The laptop varies by 30 % from day to day, so a Linux budget miss needs a control run of the predecessor on the same day to mean anything.
- The Linux and Windows sizes are under their budgets (57.01 and 60.08 MiB).
**Rationale:** A red run with a control that is red too points at the machine. The control run of the predecessor, taken in the same session, separates the machine from the app; a single run on a laptop with power management is not a measurement of the app.
**Rejected alternatives:** raising a budget or the limit (a budget change is the owner's decision, see M2.5); bisecting a pass (it would cost hours of two idle machines for no verdict); reading one cold figure as a regression (it varies by 15 ms between runs).

## 2026-11-09 – Raffica PDF: platform fixes (M2.7)
**Context:** The review of 2026-11-08 found four platform problems in the default-app feature and the OCR languages: an `APPIMAGE` variable inherited from a parent AppImage could make the Linux service write a desktop file that starts a foreign file (01-S2); the Windows check read only HKCU, so an installation for all users asked to be made the default again and wrote a second registration below HKCU (06-B-2); the languages command was locked only for the active tab's run (06-B-3); and "Not now" brought the card back at every start (08-UI-16).
**Decision:** (1) `LinuxDefaultAppService` treats `APPIMAGE` as this program only when `PackageFormDetector.IsInsideAppDir(ProcessPath, APPDIR)` holds, in the status query and in setting the default; otherwise the non-AppImage path runs (a desktop file of a package must exist, else `NotAvailable`). (2) `WindowsDefaultAppService` reads the open command of `Raffica.PDF` through HKCU first and HKLM second (the order of the merged view); it writes the HKCU registration only when that effective command is not this program, so an all-users registration is left alone, while a stale HKCU value that shadows it is still repaired. (3) `ocr.languages` is disabled with `ModeOcrReason` while any run goes (`ocrRunning`, the window's `OcrBusy`), and `LanguagesAsync` returns early then. (4) "Not now" stores today plus 30 days in `AppSettings.DefaultAppPromptSnoozedUntil` (`DateOnly?`, additive; a file without it loads with no snooze); the card stays away through that day and is offered on the first start after it (`DefaultAppFlow.IsSnoozed`, also checked by the window before the flow is made, so nothing is created while snoozed). "Don't ask again" is still `DefaultAppPrompt = Never`. Scope change recorded in the roadmap (2026-11-09, "Milestone 2.7").
**Rationale:** The environment of a program is not evidence about the program: the variable names a file only where the program is known to run from it. HKLM is where the installer for all users writes; a copy below HKCU would shadow later updates of that installation. The mode lock is per tab but a run is per window and holds the language files. A card that returns at every start trains users to dismiss it blindly; 30 days is a month's quiet without a permanent choice.
**Rejected alternatives:** Checking only `APPIMAGE` for existence on disk (a foreign file exists); writing HKCU always and relying on shadowing (stale after an all-users update); snoozing for the run only (the old behavior); a settings dialog entry for the snooze length (no UI wanted). Follow-up of the review: when the effective registration comes from HKLM (nothing below HKCU) the settings page is opened with `registeredAppMachine=Raffica`, otherwise with `registeredAppUser=Raffica` (the documented parameters of the `ms-settings:defaultapps` URI); a snooze day further away than 30 days (a wrong clock when it was stored) counts as expired; ticking "ask at start" again in Settings, or "Set as default now", clears the snooze. Open (owner check): on a machine with an all-users install, open Settings > Default apps through the command and confirm that Raffica is preselected. Open: a corrupt snooze date in a hand-edited file makes the whole settings file corrupt like any other bad value (see the settings entry); real registry (HKLM), a real AppImage and real desktops are owner checks.

## 2026-11-09 – Raffica PDF: card footers, disabled reasons and navigation help (M2.7)
**Context:** The review of 2026-11-08 found that the buttons of the cards stood in a different order from card to card (Cancel first on some, last on others, in Settings and on the signed-file question), that many commands were greyed out without a word, that the help card did not list the keys that belong to no command, that the hints of the cards were silent for a screen reader, and that Windows named the program "RafficaPdf".
**Decision:** (1) Every card footer is built by `CardFooter.Build(primary, secondary, cancel)` of the shell: primary first, then the secondary actions, Cancel last, right-aligned by the dialog; the primary is `IsDefault` (Enter) and the cancel `IsCancel` (Esc). A card whose safe answer is its cancel (the unsaved-changes question of a tab that cannot be saved, the signed-file question, the recognition question, a running update download) passes no primary: the cancel is then both, and `Dialog.FocusFirst` focuses the default button of the footer when the body has no control, so the focus stays on the safe answer wherever the order puts it. A card with one button uses `CardFooter.Only`. Cards whose Esc answers their primary (the OCR keep-pages question, the required-fields card, "keep" of the language replace card) have no `IsCancel`. `CardFooterTests` walks every card factory (an exhaustive list, a reflection guard fails when a factory is not listed) and the three states of the update progress card. `JobRunner`'s dialog has no footer (the job card carries its own Cancel) and is outside the rule. (2) `PdfCommandRules.ReasonFor` keeps the lock reasons it had and, with a document open, gives a reason for every other disabled state (`DisabledReason`): "Turn on "Redact" first" (also Organize pages and Edit text, with the names of their commands), nothing to save, undo, redo, apply or delete, only one document open, select pages first, the pages are still loading, and the save-busy reason for Print, Save and Save as. Without a document nothing is told (the start view shows no commands). `PdfCommandRulesTests` walks every id of `PdfCommandIds` in 22 states and fails for a command that is off without a reason, in both languages. (3) The help card has a fixed "Navigation" section (`NavigationHelp`): Ctrl+K, F6, Alt, Ctrl+Y, Enter and Shift+Enter in the search field, the keys of Organize pages and forms, Ctrl+Enter in text boxes and comments and Ctrl+Shift+G, written through `KeyNames`; each key was checked against its handler. (4) Every danger-colored hint of a card is made by `ValidationHint.Mark` and is an assertive live region (`CardHintTests` checks every card). (5) The version resource of `RafficaPdf.exe` says "Raffica PDF" (`Product`, `AssemblyTitle`, `Description`) and "Raffica" as company; the assembly name, the installer and the update helper keep `RafficaPdf`. The print job was already titled with the file name (`PrintJobRequest.DocumentName`), a test now pins it.
**Rationale:** One order is learned once and matches the Windows convention; Enter and Esc then mean the same on every card. A reason on every lock is the product rule ("every lock shows its reason"), and a walk over the commands and states keeps it true when a command is added. The version resource is what Windows shows for the program in the print dialogs, the queue and Task Manager; the Win32 print dialog and `DOCINFO` have no field for an application name.
**Rejected alternatives:** A per-card footer order (what grew the inconsistency); a reason for the start view without a document (the commands are not shown there and tests pin the null); a custom print dialog on Windows to show a name (the system dialog is the one the owner's printers know); renaming the assembly (the installer, the update helper and the file type registration use the file name).

## 2026-11-09 – Raffica: forms editing and contrast fixes (M2.7)
**Context:** Editable combo boxes of PDF forms could not be typed into (the shell's ComboBox theme had no text box); the caret and the edge of the editors over a page used theme tokens although the page paper is white (1.07 : 1 in high contrast, 2.0 : 1 in the dark theme); the selection of the page and outline lists (1.24 : 1) and of text in a field (selection.tint on the field) was nearly invisible; the lock mark of a locked field sat over the value.
**Decision:** (1) `ComboBox.axaml` has `PART_EditableTextBox`, the shell text field without its own edge and fill, shown only when `IsEditable`, text bound two-way, automation name taken from the combo box; the box edge turns `Text.Primary` while the text box has the focus. (2) New tokens in Light, Dark and HighContrast, each classified in `ContrastPolicy` and listed in `TokenValuesTests` and the gallery's `FoundationsSection`: `Paper.Caret` `#000000`, `Paper.Edge` `#1A1D21`, `Paper.Selection` `#5F6670` and `Paper.OnSelection` `#FFFFFF` (the same in every theme, new kind `OnPaper`: caret, edge and selection at least 3 : 1 on white, the text on the selection at least 4.5 : 1) used by the text box annotation editor and the form field editors (`PageView.axaml`); `Selection.Field` (`#5F6670` Light, `#8F96A1` Dark, `#FFFF00` HighContrast, kind `Fill`: at least 3 : 1 on `Surface.Page` and `Surface.Band`, `Text.OnInk` on it at least 4.5 : 1) is the text selection brush of the shell text field (selected text `Text.OnInk`); `Selection.Tint` stays for list rows and segmented controls. (3) Items of `nav-list` get a 2 px left edge bar in `Text.Primary` when selected (always 2 px, transparent otherwise, padding reduced by 2 so nothing moves), like `mark-edge`. (4) `FormMarkers.LockChipAt`: the lock chip is left of the widget (2 DIP gap), right of it when the left leaves the view, above it when neither side has room; the star stays inside the widget at its top right.
**Rationale:** A fixed pair for things drawn over paper, because the paper does not follow the theme; a separate selection token for text fields so that list selection keeps its quiet tint. Limits: the popup list of a form combo box is themed (not paper); the text edit field of M2.5 (`RunFieldEditor`) keeps its computed selection; the star chip over the value of a required field is unchanged; whether the lock chip has room is judged against the view width, not the page.
**Rejected alternatives:** Raising `Selection.Tint` to 3 : 1 (it would turn every list row and segmented control heavy); styling the page editors with a theme variant forced to Light (a second token set for one control, and the focus ring would still follow the app theme).
**Amendment (review of the same day):** the typing edge of the combo box (`Text.Primary` while the text box has the focus) is scoped to `IsEditable=True`, a non-editable combo box or select box keeps its own edge on focus (the keyboard ring); the marks list (`nav-list marks-list`) keeps its own `mark-edge` bar and its 6 px padding, so it gets no second bar from the nav-list edge and does not shift; the mouse opens an editable combo box on the chevron area (the chevron is not hit-testable, the box behind it takes the click and the control toggles the list) while a click on the text places the caret, pinned by `ClickOnTheChevronOfAnEditableComboOpensTheListAndAClickOnTheTextDoesNot`.

## 2026-11-09 – Raffica shell: status bar, scrolling cards and screen-reader basics (M2.7)
**Context:** The review of 2026-11-08 found the status bar cutting "Unsaved changes", the page size ("2 pages · 141 × 141 mm" at 1280 and 1920 DIP) and "Page 1000 of 1000"; cards that do not scroll (Settings is cut off at 150% scaling or in a small window, header and footer out of reach); and a page view and status bar that a screen reader does not find.
**Decision:** (1) Status bar (`StatusBar.axaml`): the save-state cell shows the new short string `StatusUnsaved` ("Unsaved", "Nicht gespeichert"; the tab and its automation name keep "Unsaved changes"); the position and count columns are Auto with a minimum (128, 144) and size to their text; the focus or hint column is the star column (it takes the free room and the shortfall of a narrow window, its text then ends in an ellipsis before another cell is cut); the language (96) and the zoom cluster sit at the right edge. (2) Cards: the `Dialog` template wraps the body in a `ScrollViewer` (`PART_BodyScroll`, vertical Auto, horizontal Disabled, not focusable); `DialogHost` caps the active card at its own height less `DialogHost.CardMargin` (48 DIP) and follows size changes; `Dialog` brings the control that Tab focuses into view. `Dialog.Content` is untouched (the wrap is in the template, so apps and tests that read the content see no change); inner scroll viewers of the cards (help, extract, split, About) stay and scroll first. (3) Screen readers: `PageView` has its own automation peer (control type Document, control and content element, keyboard focusable) named "<file>, page <n> of <m>" ("<Datei>, Seite <n> von <m>"); the name is rebuilt only when the current page or the page count changes (not while scrolling inside a page), a change is raised as a name property change through the peer, and a focused form field still names the view as before (`FocusedFieldName`). The text cells of the status bar are control elements named by their text (Text control type, live setting off, an empty cell is left out), so the save state, position, count, focus text, language and zoom value are found.
**Rationale:** A cell that is cut is a defect users see at once; Auto-with-minimum keeps the cells in place for ordinary texts and grows only for the long ones. A scroll viewer in the template fixes every card at once instead of each card growing its own. The page text for screen readers is a text pattern of its own and stays a backlog item; the name and role make the view findable and tell where the user is.
**Rejected alternatives:** Shortening the position and count texts (they are the information); fixed wider columns (the longest German text would still eat the free room at 1024 DIP and the next language would break them again); a `ScrollViewer` in each card (about a dozen cards, easy to forget); making the status cells live regions (every scroll step would be read out).
**Limits:** At the minimum width of 1024 DIP the longest position, the longest count, the language and the Alt hint together leave the hint less than its full German text (it ends in an ellipsis; the PDF app never sets the language cell, and `GermanLayoutTests` checks the long cells with the F6 text and the hint with ordinary cells). Nested scroll viewers (a card with its own list): the wheel scrolls the inner one first. Narrator and NVDA were not run; the peers are checked through Avalonia's peer API in headless tests (`PageViewAutomationTests`, `StatusBarTests`), not through a screen reader.

## 2026-11-09 – Raffica PDF: About card, narrow panes and tab overflow (M2.7)
**Context:** The review found the About card's notices list wider than the card (a fixed 560 DIP box in a 520 DIP card), the intro not wrapping, the section toggles filled with the toggle's checked fill, the multi-component sections without a licence in their title, the project address as plain text and "enthaelt" without the umlaut; check boxes and buttons of the 256 px redact pane cut off in German; tabs of many files cut in the middle of ".pdf", with no way to reach the tabs outside the strip; and two leftovers of the status bar and card work (an empty language cell that kept 112 DIP, a card capped at 2 DIP in a tiny host).
**Decision:** (1) About card: the list stretches to the width of the card; the intro box wraps and the licence boxes scroll sideways (they are fixed-width text); a section row is a `ToggleButton` with the shell class `disclosure` (`ToggleButton.axaml`: no fill when checked, the page text colour, hover fill, grows with a wrapping title, the chevron shows the state) and its title is `AboutCard.SectionTitle`: the first component and, when the section holds several, "and N more", then " - " and the licence (the last line of the section head that `tools/licenses.cs` writes); the project address is a button with the shell class `link` (`Button.axaml`, ink colour; its `TextBlock` takes the class `link` of `Typography.axaml` for the underline) that closes the card and then asks through `PdfWindow.OpenAddressAfterConfirmationAsync`, the same confirmation card as a link in a document and as the download page, so there is no second launch path; the notices intro says "enthält" (`tools/licenses.cs`, UTF-8 without BOM, read as UTF-8 by `ThirdPartyNotices.Read`, pinned by a self-test of the tool and by `AboutCardTests`). The sections still fill on their first open. (2) Narrow panes: the shell class `wrap` (`Button.axaml`, `CheckBox.axaml`; `ToggleButton` is based on `Button`) sets a `ContentTemplate` for a string content, a wrapping `TextBlock`, so the control grows in height (a button gets 3 px of vertical padding and a minimum height of 26) and keeps its string as `Content`. The redact pane and its Find panel put it on every check box, button and toggle; the placeholder of the overlay text box is "Optional text" (the label above and the automation name say what it is for), because a placeholder cannot wrap. `RedactPaneGermanTests` shows the pane in a 256 px host in both languages, with the Look rows, the Find panel in both modes (with hits, failed checksums and the not-searched list) and the marks list, and requires that no label, check box or button text is wider than its control (`LabelFit`). (3) Tabs: `MiddleEllipsis.FitFileName` (used by `MiddleTrimTextBlock`) shortens the name in the middle before its extension (the last dot with one to five letters or digits, not a leading dot); the extension stays only while at least eight characters of the name stay beside it, otherwise it goes and the name takes the room. When the tabs do not fit at their minimum width (96 DIP) `DocumentTabs` shows a left arrow before the strip and a right arrow and a list button after it, each as wide as the "+" button (the strip gets the rest, "+" and the title bar's search stay where they are); an arrow scrolls the next hidden tab on its side fully into view and is dim at the end; the list button opens a menu of every tab (the selected one with a radio mark, a dirty one with a bullet) and a click selects the tab and brings it into view. The automation peer of a tab reports `IsOffscreen` for a tab outside the strip's window (a partly visible tab counts as on screen). (4) Carry-overs: the language cell of the status bar and its gap are there only while it has a text (it shares a column with the zoom cluster), so the free room goes to the hint column; `DialogHost.CapHeight` is `max(MinCardHeight = 200, host height less 48)`, so the margin gives way in a small host and the card is as tall as the host, never taller.
**Rationale:** One style for every disclosure row keeps the notice list and later lists alike; the About title is derived from the head the tool already writes, so the generated file and its `check` stay as they are. A class on the shell controls (`wrap`, `disclosure`, `link`) lets a pane opt in with one word and keeps the strings as strings for names and tests. The strip with arrows and a list is what browsers and editors do, so users know it; a menu is the one place where every name is readable at once.
**Rejected alternatives:** A `ToggleButton` local style in the card (not reusable); the link opening the address directly (an address from the app is confirmed like any other); a title line written by the tool into every section (the self-tests and the file format would change for a derived value); wrapping content by replacing the strings with `TextBlock`s in every pane (tests and automation names read the string); shortening the middle of the whole file name as before (it cut ".pdf" in half); a scroll bar on the tab strip (it takes a row of the title bar).
**Limits:** Closing the About card to show the confirmation means the user comes back to it by the command again; the link text is wrapped, not shortened. The text of a hit or a mark row of the redact pane is the document's own: a word wider than the pane (an e-mail address, an IBAN) is broken in the middle in the Find list and ends in an ellipsis in the marks list on purpose, so those rows are exempt from the word check and held to the pane width only. `IsOffscreen` is read when asked; no property-changed event is raised while the strip scrolls, and the peers were checked in headless tests, not with Narrator or NVDA. The arrows scroll by tab, not by pixel, and the wheel and the selected tab still scroll the strip as before.

## 2026-11-09 – Raffica shell: ribbon overflow (M2.7)
**Context:** At the default window width of 1280 DIP the full band of several tabs of Raffica PDF is wider than the window (Home needs about 1370 DIP in English, the German Start tab about 1600): the band clipped whole groups with no way to them, so Undo, Redo and Help of the Home tab and Extract, Split, Export and Delete of the Pages tab could not be clicked.
**Decision:** `RibbonView` (`src/Raffica.Shell/Frame`) decides the layout of the band. `IsCompact` stays the user's setting and forces the compact layout at any width; `IsEffectivelyCompact` (read-only) is that setting or "the band is narrower than the full band of the active tab". (1) What is measured: only the width of the full band of the active tab, `StackPanel.DesiredSize.Width` of its groups. The band on screen is its own measure when it is the full band; when the band is compact (or shows another tab) a throw-away full band is built inside the band, measured with an infinite size (the styles apply only inside the tree) and removed. (2) When: lazily and never before the band is in a window and has a width, so nothing is measured at start beyond the full band that is built today. At the default width (1280 DIP, and in German) the first layout therefore builds the full band and then rebuilds it compact once, one extra build of 15 to 25 buttons at start, not before the first sharp page; a `measure.yml` run in Task 15 must confirm that it costs no measurable time, and if it does the layout is chosen before the first build. The decision runs when the width of the band changes, when the tab changes, when `IsCompact` changes and when the definition is replaced; a height change (the row is 82 or 40 px) never starts it. (3) Caching: one value per `RibbonTabDefinition` instance, dropped when `Definition` is replaced. A language is a new definition (the names come from the definition), so a tab is measured once per language and a visit to a tab already seen costs nothing (`FullMeasureCount` in `RibbonOverflowTests`). The decision is a pure function of the band width and the cached full width, so the layout it picks cannot change the input of the next decision: no flicker and no loop (`TheDecisionDoesNotChangeWithTheLayoutItPicks` sweeps the width down and up). (4) The frame: `AppFrame.IsAutoCompact` (additive) is set by the ribbon whose band sits in the frame and, with `IsCompact`, makes the band row 40 px; the app and the gallery keep setting `IsCompact` for the user's setting only. (5) Chevrons: the band is a `ScrollViewer` (`PART_BandScroll`) between two slots (`PART_ScrollLeft`, `PART_ScrollRight`). When even the compact band is wider than the window both slots take 32 DIP and keep it, so the viewport never changes when a chevron shows or hides and a button scrolled into view stays in view; a chevron shows only while there is more in its direction; a click moves 60 percent of the viewport (at least 96 DIP) and the mouse wheel moves the band. The chevrons are no tab stop and not focusable (focus stays in the document); every command stays reachable by keyboard because focus on a button (not by pointer) calls `BringIntoView`. Field codes and tab letters run commands without focus and are not affected. (6) Names: `RibbonScrollLeft` and `RibbonScrollRight` in `Strings.en.resx` and `Strings.de.resx` of the shell (automation name and tooltip).
**Rationale:** A hidden command is the worst failure of a toolbar; the compact layout is the lossless fallback the design already has (every command is a button or an item of a group menu), scrolling is the last resort for a window narrower than that. Measuring once per tab and language keeps the cost off the start and off every resize.
**Rejected alternatives:** Rebuilding the band on every size change to see whether it fits (a build per pixel of a drag); hysteresis between the two widths (the decision is stable without it and a gap would show the clipped band); measuring the visible band in both modes (the compact band says nothing about the full one); an overflow menu for whole groups (a second, different way to reach a command, and the compact layout already is one); chevrons that overlay the band (they would cover the buttons they lead to); a horizontal scroll bar (takes height of the 40 px band).
**Limits:** The measure assumes the band does not change its font or scaling after the first measure of a tab; a change of the system font scale at run time is picked up by the next definition (a restart). The key-tip badges of a button that is scrolled out of sight are not checked. The PDF app's compact bands fit 1024 DIP in English and German without chevrons (`PdfRibbonLayoutTests`), so the chevrons are the guard for narrower windows and other apps.

## 2026-11-09 – Raffica shell: ribbon labels, on state and key tips (M2.7)
**Context:** German ribbon labels broke in the middle of a word ("Anmerkungsmod/us") or clipped, the buttons of a switch or choice (dark page, page layout, zoom fit mode, pane) never showed whether they were on, the group labels sat at different heights, a key tip covered the text of its button, the zoom buttons of the status bar had no tooltip, and the Help group of the Home tab lacked Document info and Check for updates.
**Decision:** (1) A tile label wraps at spaces and hyphens only (`RibbonButton.RibbonLabel`): up to 96 DIP it wraps as before; a word (or the part between two hyphens) that is wider is never cut, the label and with it the tile take the width of that word. The German names are the short ones of the task ("Anmerken", "Änderungen anwenden", "Felder hervorheben", "Rückgängig", "Wiederholen", "Verwerfen", "Als Bilder exportieren …", "Aus Datei einfügen …"); the long wording is the `Description` of the command (strings `Desc<Name>` in both languages for the Pages tab, Annotate mode, the form highlight, Redact discard and Undo and Redo of Home): the tooltip shows it, and the title search shows it in place of the shortcut for a command without one, so commands with the same short name ("Rückgängig" of Home and of Pages, "Verwerfen" of Pages and of Redact) can be told apart. (2) `CommandDefinition.CheckedWhen` (`Func<bool>?`, read again with `EnabledWhen` in `RibbonView.RefreshCommandStates`; `IsToggle` and `IsChecked` read it) makes a command a switch or a choice: its button gets the class `checked`, drawn with the ink fill and text.onInk of the toggle (semantic tokens only, `Styles/Frame/Ribbon.axaml`), and exposes the UIA Toggle pattern (`RibbonToggleAutomationPeer`; `Toggle` runs the command like a click); a plain command stays a plain button. The ribbon tabs are `TabItem` peers with the SelectionItem pattern. (3) The PDF catalog marks the toggles (`CatalogCommand.Toggle`: zoom fit width, whole page, actual size; layouts continuous, single, two pages, two pages with cover; dark page; pages and outline pane) and `PdfWindow.IsCommandOn` reads the state from the active page view (`ZoomMode`, `Layout`, `PageTheme`) and the window (`LeftPane`); the ribbon is asked again only when that tuple changes (`UpdateCheckedStates`), so scrolling costs nothing. The layouts, fit modes and panes are choices: pressing the one that is on does nothing (a pane is closed by `CloseLeftPane`, no ribbon command), so they are pressed, not toggled off. Annotate mode, Organize, Redact, Edit text and the form highlight are modes with their own badge or status and are not part of this change. (4) The commands of a group are as high as a tile, so every group label sits on one row. (5) Key tips (`FieldCodeBadge.Show` with a `BadgePlacement`) cover no text and stay inside the band: a large tile has its tip in the top right corner over the icon, a small or icon-only button has it beside it, vertically centred, 4 px over its right edge (its padding is 6 px) and the rest beyond (over the padding and the icon of the next button; the margin follows the width of a two-character code), a group has it flush right in its label row, which keeps 20 px free on each side of the label text (the groups are as wide as label plus 40 where that is more than their commands). Tab letters, the search field and the pane codes keep the bottom right corner of the spec. `BadgeFit` (shared by the Shell and Pdf tests) checks every tip of every tab, full and compact, English and German: no visible text of the band is covered and the tip lies inside the band vertically. (6) The zoom buttons and the zoom slider of the status bar have tooltips with the strings of their automation names. (7) The Home Help group holds Help (71), Document info (72, `doc.info`, icon info) and Check for updates (73, `app.update`, icon refresh-cw); the title search lists an extra result only when no command of the same name is listed, so these two do not appear twice. Check for updates is always enabled and does nothing in a window without an update flow (a test or measurement window), as the palette entry did. (8) The dismiss button of the form notice can take the focus (a keyboard user could not close the notice), Esc on it closes the notice, and the focus returns to the page view; the counter and the highlight switch stay out of the tab order. (9) `layout.twocover` uses `book-copy` (it looked like `book-open` at 16 px), the find bar uses the arrow icons of the catalog.
**Rationale:** A label that is cut or a toggle without a state is a defect users see at once; every change keeps the semantic tokens and the one source of commands.
**Rejected alternatives:** Shortening every German label to fit 96 DIP (the long ones are real words); `ToggleButton` for the ribbon button (it flips itself on a click, the state belongs to the document); a checked state kept in the ribbon (it would drift from the window); hiding the key tips of small buttons in a stacked column (they would be unreachable by sight).
**Limits:** A choice that is on (layout, fit mode, pane) does nothing when it is pressed again and the Toggle pattern on an On choice leaves it On; the SelectionItem pattern would describe a choice more exactly. "Check for updates" is enabled although the command may return without a word (no update flow in a test or measurement window, or a check that is already running). A key tip beside a small button hides the left edge of the icon of the next button while Alt is on. The key tips were checked by geometry only, not on a real display (the gallery has no switch for the Alt mode). The pressed look of a disabled checked button is grey (not measured for contrast in every theme, as the toggle). Whether a screen reader announces the toggle state and the selected tab needs a check with a real one (Narrator, NVDA); the tests read the patterns through the automation peers only.

## 2026-11-09 – Raffica PDF: window placement and hand-over behavior (M2.7)
**Context:** On a 1366 x 728 work area the fixed 1280 x 860 window reached under the task bar, the window never remembered where it was, a second launch within its one second could miss a listener that only started after the first sharp page, a hand-over or the close question met an open card and either moved the tab under it or was answered Cancel without a word, a dead share removed a recent entry by a `File.Exists` on the UI thread, twenty bad files were twenty cards, an update restart activated the last tab instead of the one that was active, and End on short pages showed the second to last page.
**Decision:** (1) `ShellSettings.WindowPlacement` (`WindowPlacement(X, Y, Width, Height, Maximized)`, null until a window closed once; X and Y are screen pixels, Width and Height are DIPs) is read with the settings that are read at start anyway, and `WindowPlacement.FitTo(workArea, scaling, stored, defaultSize)` shrinks the size to the work area less 24 DIP on every side and moves the position inside it (no stored placement: the default size centred). `PdfWindow` applies it in its constructor, before the window is shown, on the screen under the stored top left corner (the primary screen when that is gone), and stores the restore bounds (the last bounds seen in the normal state) plus the maximized flag when it is closed, writing the shell file once and only when the value changed. A measurement run applies and stores nothing. The settings dialog keeps the placement (`current with {...}`). (2) The single-instance listener starts when the window has opened (`Opened`), still with the 1 s hand-over timeout of `Program`; showing what arrives waits for the UI as before. (3) A hand-over that arrives while a card is open or the close questions run is queued (`PdfWindow.PendingHandover`) and opened when the host is free; the window is brought to the front at once, and `ShowActiveView` does not move the focus while a card is open. The close and plan, redaction and recognition questions go through the dialog queue (`AskAsync`), so they wait behind an open card instead of answering Cancel and bring the window to the front (the card of a running file job, recognized by the `SaveJobCard` in the open dialog, is no question the user can end: a close that meets it is still answered Cancel after the window was brought to the front, the tab stays and the job runs on; any other card makes the question wait); a second request to close while the first one runs (`IsCloseRunning`) does not start a second set of questions; a hand-over that arrives behind a queued question (a tab close, an update) waits behind the card and then behind the question. (4) `OpenFlow.OpenAsync` reports the reason (`onError`); a recent entry leaves the list only for `PdfOpenError.FileNotFound`. (5) `OpenFlow.OpenManyAsync` collects the failures; one failure stays an ordinary error card, two or more are one `OpenFailuresCard` with the first five names and "and N more". (6) After an update restart `RestoreSession` names the tab that was active (`PdfOptions.ActiveFile`) and the window selects it after the files are open, unless a file was given on the command line (that one stays the active tab). (7) `PageView.CurrentPage` is the last page whenever the view is scrolled to its end and the document is taller than the view.
**Rationale:** The numbers come from the settings read at start and the screens the window system already knows, so the start budget has no new file, no new thread and no new wait. Queueing keeps every answer the user gave and every file the user sent; nothing is dropped or silently refused.
**Rejected alternatives:** A separate placement file (one more read at start); storing the maximized bounds (a restore would be a screen-sized window); answering Cancel behind a card as before (the user pressed Close and nothing happened); removing a recent entry for every open error (an offline share would lose its entry); one card per failed file (twenty cards).
**Limits:** The restore bounds are tracked by `PositionChanged`, `Resized` and the window state. A position or size that arrives up to 300 ms before a change to Maximized or Minimized (Win32 raises it first) is dropped in favor of the newest older snapshot, and the minimized sentinel position and an empty size are never snapshots; a drag that ends less than 300 ms before a maximize is therefore stored one step early. How a real window system orders these events is only checked with simulated sequences, so a real display with several monitors, other scalings and the Windows snap layouts needs the owner's check. A window that is minimized when the app is killed (not closed) stores nothing new. A hand-over that arrives after the window confirmed its close is dropped (the app is ending; the second launch then starts its own window only if its one second ran out). `CurrentPage` at the end of a scroll is the last page that is on screen (the shown page in the single page layout, the last page of the document in the other layouts). A close question that waits behind a card the user can neither answer nor the app end would wait for ever; the only such card is a file job's, which is excluded. The hand-over of paths that do not exist still shows one error card per path (at most three).

## 2026-11-09 – Raffica PDF: crash handling (M2.7)
**Context:** An exception nobody caught on the UI thread ended the app without a word, and the user lost the unsaved marks of every tab.
**Decision:** `CrashGuard` (`src/Raffica.Pdf/Hosting`) logs every uncaught exception to `crash.log` in `PdfSettingsFiles.ConfigDirectoryFor(settingsFile)` (time, type, message and stack of the exception and its inner exceptions, appended, capped at 1 MiB by dropping the older half). A UI-thread exception (`Dispatcher.UIThread.UnhandledException`) is marked handled and answered with one card at a time (repeats are only logged while a card shows; `CrashGuard.CardClosed` re-arms it when the card is dismissed) ("Something went wrong", save under a new name, restart, details in `crash.log`, a button for the folder). More than three UI exceptions within ten seconds (measured on the monotonic clock) mean a loop: the guard logs and ends the app through the injected `exit` (no card, no loop). `AppDomain.UnhandledException` and `TaskScheduler.UnobservedTaskException` (then `SetObserved`) are logged only; the process may end as before. `OutOfMemoryException` is logged and left unhandled. The log never holds a path or the text of a document: absolute paths (drive, UNC, `/home/`, `/Users/` and similar roots) in messages and stacks (also `~/`, `../`, `..\` and the roots `/opt`, `/srv`, `/data`, `/mnt`, `/media`, `/var`, `/tmp`, `/root`; addresses such as `http://` and `avares://` stay) are replaced by `<path>` up to the next double quote or the end of the line (a path may contain spaces and apostrophes, so a little text after it is lost, on purpose); every message and stack is cut at 8 KiB and an entry at 64 KiB, so one entry never fills the 1 MiB log. Nothing is made before the first exception (no file, no folder); the app only subscribes the events, and not in a measurement run. The guard never reads `Dispatcher.UIThread` itself: `App` captures the dispatcher on the UI thread and posts the card and the shutdown through it. The folder button hands only the directory to `FolderLauncher` (`explorer.exe` from the Windows folder, `open` or `xdg-open`, no shell, one argument).
**Rationale:** A visible answer and a log let the user save and report; the cap on repeats prevents an endless card loop; keeping paths out of the log means a log can be attached to an issue.
**Rejected alternatives:** Ending the app on every exception (loses work); handling `OutOfMemoryException` (nothing can be promised after it); a crash dialog per exception without a limit; a log with the full `ToString()` (messages carry file names).
**Limits:** A faulted fire-and-forget task reaches `UnobservedTaskException` late (after finalization) and shows no card; it is only logged. The log text of a message that mentions a document by name only (no path) is kept as the exception wrote it. A UI exception inside an `async void` handler reaches the guard only through the dispatcher's own path; the headless test (`ACommandThatThrowsLeavesTheWindowOpenAndTheOtherTabDirty`) posts a failing delegate to the UI dispatcher, the same path a click takes, because no ribbon command throws on demand.

## 2026-11-08 – Raffica: leftover path of the save fallback, CLI console encoding, text-edit check size (M2.7)
**Context:** Three small limits that come with the Core fixes of M2.7 and are worth knowing later.
**Decision:**
- `SaveResult.LeftoverPath` (the `.pw-<12 hex>.old` file the Windows fallback replace could not delete, after four attempts over 300 ms) is shown by Save, the page-organizer Apply and the redaction Apply. It is not shown by Extract and Split, image export and `ocr --force` of the CLI. The risk is low: such a file holds the previous content of a file the user chose to overwrite (for a redaction the card names the unredacted original; Extract, Split and OCR write other files than the open original).
- The CLI sets `Console.OutputEncoding` to UTF-8 without BOM when it writes to an unredirected Windows console and does not restore it on exit; this also changes how `Console.Error` is encoded for the rest of that console session (the other direction, the ANSI or OEM code page, showed umlauts as mojibake). Redirected output is untouched.
- The picture of the text-edit check is held to 32,768 px per side and `PdfiumRenderWork.MaxPixels` in area on top of the 4 Mpx scaling budget; a thinner page is refused as "not rewritable" (the edit is not possible).
**Rationale:** Each is the smallest change that closes the finding; none changes a file format or a public contract.
**Rejected alternatives:** Restoring the console encoding on exit (the process ends right after the output, and a Ctrl+C path would need the same handler); wiring the leftover card into Extract/Split (they never replace the user's open file).

## 2026-11-08 – Raffica: saving through a symbolic link (M2.7)
**Context:** `DocumentSaver` wrote a temporary file next to the target path and renamed it over that path. For a target that is a symbolic link the rename replaced the link by a regular file and left the file behind it unchanged (the "known limit" of M2.1 and of "Raffica PDF: no write onto a file another tab has open", call 8). For a redaction that is a trap: the link was gone and the linked original still held the unredacted content.
**Decision:** `DocumentSaver.RunAsync` resolves the target with `FileInfo.ResolveLinkTarget(returnFinalTarget: true)` before anything is written; the temporary file lives next to the final target and replaces it, so the link stays a link and the file behind it is what is saved. This holds for every chain of the saver (incremental save, rewrite, redaction apply). The "original changed" comparison of the target with the original compares the resolved paths; the stamp of the original is still taken at the path it was opened by.
**Known limits:** a hard link is another name of the same content, not a pointer: the replace gives the saved name a new file and the other names keep the old one (no cheap, portable way to tell the user's intent; writing in place would give up the atomic replace). A dangling link is written through (the file at its destination appears). A link that cannot be followed (a cycle, no access) is treated as a plain path, as before.
**Rationale:** The link was the user's way to name the file; saving through it keeps what they set up and never leaves old content behind a name that looked saved.
**Rejected alternatives:** Refusing a link target (a save would fail where it worked); writing in place (loses the atomic replace and the checked-then-moved guarantee).

## 2026-11-08 – Raffica PDF: no write onto a file another tab has open (M2.7)
**Context:** PDFium opens files share-delete, so the atomic replace of a save succeeds under a tab that has the target open. Save as, Apply (page organizer and redaction) with Save as and Extract never asked the other tabs; two tabs could end with one path, and a stale one could later write old (for example unredacted) content back.
**Decision:**
- `OpenTargetGuard.FindOtherTabAsync(workspace, self, target, token)` returns the other tab (never `self`) whose path is the target by spelling (`JobRunner.SamePath`) or another name of the same file (`FileIdentity.AreSameFile`, off the UI thread). A hit ends the flow with the note "<name> is open in another tab. Close it first." (English and German) on the flow's own notice card; nothing is written.
- It is asked after each picker and after the last re-check, immediately before the write: `SaveController` (after the picker, again after the signed-file card, and after the second picker of the changed-original card), `ApplyFlow` and `RedactApplyFlow` (after the plan-version re-check, only for the Save-as choice, because "Save in the original file" is the tab's own path), `SplitFlow.ExtractCoreAsync` (after the picker and the lock re-check). Save in the close question now says `SaveBusy` while a save of the tab runs instead of returning silently.
- Null rule: `AreSameFile` answers null for "cannot tell". On Windows and Linux that is a real failure and counts as the same file (as for the original in the redaction flow). On any other OS (macOS) it is the answer for every pair, so the guard falls back to the spelling alone there; otherwise every target would be refused. The "null counts as the same file" semantics for the tab's own original are unchanged.
**Known limits:** a file that is still loading is not yet in `Workspace.Tabs`; `Workspace.Find` dedupes by spelling only, so a link spelling can give two tabs one file, and Save in the original file never checks that; the check and the end of the write are not atomic (`OpenFlow` does not know a job is writing), so a tab opened in that window is not noticed; Split part folders and Export images are not guarded (Core's non-overwrite naming makes a clash low risk); on macOS only the spelling is compared.
**Rationale:** A refused write costs a click, a replaced open file costs data; the check runs where the user cannot change the target any more.
**Rejected alternatives:** Making `OpenFlow` refuse to open a file while a job writes it (a wider change to the open flow, left for later); an exclusive lock on open tabs' files (PDFium opens share-delete and the platform dialogs confirm replacements themselves).
**Process note:** the tests were written after the implementation (the test rigs needed the new parameter to compile); the red phase was shown by neutering the guard and the later fixes.

## 2026-11-08 – Raffica redaction: page /AF and /B refuse the file, the read-back counts embedded files (M2.7)
**Context:** The cleaning rebuild copies pages with `FPDF_ImportPagesByIndex`, which keeps every page key except `/Type` and `/Parent`. A page `/AF` (associated files with embedded streams, for example the XML of a PDF/A-3 invoice) and `/B` (article beads leading to a thread with `/I` title and author) therefore survived a redaction, and the report listed "Attached files" as cleaned although the read-back never looked at embedded files reachable from pages.
**Decision:**
- `PageKeyScanner.Keys` is `/Thumb`, `/PieceInfo`, `/Metadata`, `/AA`, `/AF`, `/B`. A page with `/AF` or `/B` is refused like one with `/Thumb`, also when that page becomes an image (the rule of M2.4 for page keys is unchanged: whether a fresh page really sheds a key is not proved). `/SeparationInfo`, `/VP`, `/ID` and `/PZ` were not added; they are not known to carry document text and the brief for this task did not ask for them.
- `CleanupReadBack` fails the check when the written file has more embedded files than paperclip annotations. The count is `PageKeyScan.EmbeddedFiles` (the larger of the streams with `/Type /EmbeddedFile` and the file specifications with `/EF`) against `PageKeyScan.AttachmentAnnotations` (`/Subtype /FileAttachment` objects), taken from the plain objects of the written bytes. "Attached files" counts as cleaned only after this read; the analysis drops it when the file is refused for page keys or when another object (an annotation with `/AF`) carries associated files (`OtherAttachments`, listed under "Not cleaned"; the apply stops while one is left).
- Sound, movie, screen, 3D and rich media annotations are counted (`MediaAnnotations`) only as the value of `/Subtype` and listed under "Not cleaned" as "up to N".
**Known limits of the count:** a paperclip whose `/FS` has no embedded file next to a non-paperclip embedded file is not noticed; an inline file specification whose stream has no `/Type` is not counted by its stream (the `/EF` count covers plain objects only); a paperclip with two typed streams is refused although both are its own; a media annotation with its own file specification fails the apply; an annotation written inline in a page's `/Annots` array is not seen as the holder of `/AF` (its stream still counts in the read-back); a file with object streams has no `OtherAttachments` preview (the read-back refuses an unreadable written file).
**Rationale:** Refusing is the conservative option: the user is told the cause and nothing is written, instead of a file that still holds the attachment. The count is cheap and fails closed in the cases that matter (a missed attachment makes the apply fail, a false match refuses a file).
**Rejected alternatives:** Dropping `/AF` and `/B` from the rebuilt pages (PDFium has no API to remove a page key); resolving the names tree to count only its files (needs reference resolution and object streams make it unreliable); rebuilding pages in an empty document without keys (breaks forms).

## 2026-11-08 – Raffica PDF: Extract and Split name what stays (M2.7)
**Context:** A part of Extract or Split is the working copy of the document minus the dropped pages, so the information dictionary, the XMP, the whole outline, the names tree (attached files, document scripts) and the form with the values of fields on dropped pages stay in it. The cards said only that pages are written into a new file.
**Decision:**
- Parts stay working PDFs (forms and bookmarks keep working). The confirm card of Extract and Split now names what stays: attached files, scripts, form fields that have a widget only on pages outside the part, and bookmarks that point to pages outside the part, one line per item that is there, plus a pointer to the Redact tab. For a split the field and bookmark counts are the highest of any part.
- `DocumentLevelContent` (Core, `Pages`) counts attached files and scripts with the raw scanner of the redaction (`RawContentScanner.Inventory`) on the file on disk. This is a scan of the bytes: it can overstate (superseded objects of earlier incremental revisions, files or scripts only a dropped page refers to), so the wording says "up to" and "possibly". The scanner's own "complete" flag is not used (it concerns text hiding in streams and is false for ordinary files); only a file that cannot be read at all adds the note that more may stay than is listed.
- The confirm card now also appears when the loss list is empty but something stays (any document with an outline, form, attachment or script when a subset is written; before it appeared only for losses). A document with nothing of it still goes straight on.
- The counts are read once per card inside the cancelable check job that already reports the losses (off the UI thread, behind the job card), and only when the Extract or Split flow reaches the confirm card; nothing exists before that. A failed read shows no lines and blocks nothing.
**Rationale:** The user learns what a part carries before sending it, without claiming it is removed or safe; rebuilding parts in an empty document would break their forms.
**Rejected alternatives:** Cleaned parts (import the chosen pages into a new document with a filtered outline and the fields of the kept pages): backlog item "Extract and Split write cleaned parts". A new scanner in `Core/Pages`: the existing counts are enough.

## 2026-11-08 – CI on hosted runners once public (M2.7)
**Context:** The release-readiness review found that a fork pull request could reach the self-hosted runners that also build the release: a fork controls the workflow file of its own pull request, so a `runs-on` expression in `ci.yml` is no boundary, and the runners keep `~/.dotnet`, `~/.nuget` and the file-based tool cache between jobs. "CI on self-hosted runners, as few runs as possible" (2026-11-03) was written for a private repository, where minutes cost money; hosted runners are free for a public one.
**Decision:**
- `ci.yml` and `roadmap.yml` route every job to GitHub-hosted runners (`ubuntu-24.04`, `windows-2025`) when `github.event.repository.private == false` or the pull request comes from a fork; while the repository is private they keep the self-hosted runners. No edit is needed at the flip.
- The security boundary is the runner group, not that expression: the group `raffica-ci` is restricted to `release.yml` on `refs/heads/main` and `refs/heads/dev` (the `main` entry can only be added once `main` has the file, that is after the first release pull request is merged, because GitHub refuses an entry for a missing file), the group `Default` stays private-only, and fork pull request workflows need approval for all external contributors (`packaging/README.md`, "Making the repository public"). The owner sets these before the flip.
- `release.yml` refuses a `ref` that is not `main`, `dev` or a commit reachable from one of them (GitHub compare status `identical` or `behind`, as for the tag) in `preflight`, `windows` and `linux`, before anything is checked out.
- The Windows and Linux release jobs use `DOTNET_INSTALL_DIR` and `NUGET_PACKAGES` under `RUNNER_TEMP` (emptied for every job), so a release is built with a fresh SDK and fresh packages.
- The image of the Linux build is pinned by digest: `ubuntu:22.04@sha256:076e7bf82319619f28dd0aa9c02ab34b055ad37100395b4f49a2ffba04fb5e11` (the multi-platform index, read with `docker buildx imagetools inspect ubuntu:22.04` on 2026-11-08), set as `UBUNTU_IMAGE` in `release.yml` and as the default of `packaging/native/container-build.sh`. To update it, run that command, put the new `Digest:` of the index in both places and in `packaging/README.md`, and let the dry run of the release workflow and `native-tesseract.yml` pass before merging. The floor stays glibc 2.35, so only the 22.04 line is ever used.
