**Rationale:** Free minutes and no foreign code on the owner's machines; each protection stands without the others, because the workflow file of a fork is under the fork's control.
**Rejected alternatives:** Keeping CI on the self-hosted runners after the flip (the setting that gates fork runs is a click, not a boundary); trusting the `runs-on` expression alone; building releases in the same SDK and package folders as pull requests (one poisoned cache would reach the signed files).
Supersedes: "CI on self-hosted runners, as few runs as possible" (2026-11-03) for the routing of a public repository.

## 2026-11-07 – Raffica PDF: packaging and release measurements (M2.6)
**Context:** "Raffica PDF: packaging and release (M2.6)" promised that nothing of the updater, the default-app card or the About card costs anything before first use, and that the published size stays inside the limits of "Raffica PDF: published size budgets after the OCR payload (M2.5)". This entry records what was measured and what was not. Every number comes from **one development machine** (AMD Ryzen 7 3800X, Windows 11 Pro, software rendering, 10 runs per case, scroll 5 runs, `dotnet run bench/shell-probe/measure.cs -- run --target pdf --exe <exe> --case <case>` against a Native AOT publish of the commit, 2026-11-07). That machine is slower than the owner's binding machines, so a time row over its budget is a property of the machine, not a verdict: the pre-2.6 commit `dd13ced` (published and measured on the same machine in the same session) is equally "over". The budgets are those of "Raffica PDF speed budgets (M2.1)" (Windows: cold 200 ms, warm 210, 1000 pages 200, second file 20, scroll p99 20, idle memory 40 MiB), unchanged and not loosened.
**Decision:** Start, scroll and memory with the update flow (cold and warm medians in ms, scroll p99 in ms, idle private bytes of the 1000-page file in MiB). Rows 1 and 2 are one session, rows 3 to 5 another:

| Variant | cold | warm | 1000 pages | second file | scroll p99 | idle memory |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Base before 2.6 (`dd13ced`) | 310.8 | 307.9 | 299.7 | 50.9 | 32.3 | 38.62 |
| After the update flow (release build; a measurement run keeps the flow off) | 304.4 | 309.3 | 312.4 | 49.1 | 31.7 | 38.77 |
| Flow off (release build of the fix-round commit, same session as the next two rows) | 297.2 | 250.5 | | 61.1 | 32.2 | 38.62 |
| Flow on, loopback test build, server answering (`UpdateServices.MeasurementKeepsUpdatesOff` lets the loopback address through) | 257.8 | 253.8 | | 60.4 | 31.1 | 39.23 |
| Flow on, loopback test build, nothing listening | 310.5 | | | | 30.7 | 38.98 |

Cold-start medians differ by about 50 ms between series and sessions on this machine, so there is no measurable start cost of the check (it starts at background priority after the first sharp page); scroll p99 is unchanged within noise. The enabled flow adds about 0.4 to 0.6 MiB of idle memory: 39.23 MiB against the budget of 40 MiB. This harness cannot show the TLS cost. By hand against the real `api.github.com` address (release build, no measurement switch, `hello_world.pdf`, 8 s after the start, 4 runs each; the repository has no release, so the check ends without an offer): check on, private bytes 34.3 to 34.4 MiB and working set 77.9 MiB; check off (`pdf-settings.json` with `UpdateCheck` false), 33.5 to 33.6 MiB and 73.7 to 73.8 MiB. The real check costs about 0.8 MiB private and 4.2 MiB working set (the TLS and HTTP stack, mostly shared pages). Attributed to its case: with the loopback server the 1000-page file ended at 39.23 MiB, 0.77 MiB under the budget of 40 MiB; adding the 0.8 MiB of the real check to the 38.62 MiB of the flow-off row gives about 39.4 MiB, about 0.6 MiB under it. That second figure is an estimate from two different files and has to be confirmed on the owner's machines.

Published size (`dotnet run bench/shell-probe/measure.cs -- size --check win-x64 --target pdf ...`, win-x64 Native AOT, measured after the update flow was added and before the About card was split): **68.32 MiB** against the budget of 66 MiB (limit 82.5 MiB) and a zip of **29.5 MiB** against the budget of 29 MiB (limit 36.25 MiB). The check passes (it fails only above the limits), but both are above their budgets: this is a **finding for the owner**, not a loosened limit. The causes in this milestone: the 438 272 bytes (about 428 KiB) of third-party notices that `RafficaPdf.exe` embeds (32 929 280 to 33 367 552 bytes) and the code and strings of the updater, the default-app flow and the About card. The size was not re-measured after the later commits of the milestone, and the Linux size was not measured at all (no Linux machine).

About card (headless, real text engine, 2026-11-07): one read-only `TextBox` with all 467 290 characters of the notices needed 425 to 1428 ms until laid out (30 000 characters 29 ms, 100 000 characters 110 ms), a visible freeze. The card now cuts the text at the separator lines of the generated notices file into the introduction and one toggle per component and creates a section's box on its first expansion (`AboutCard.SplitNotices`): the card opens in 50 to 98 ms warm (188 ms with a cold JIT) and, on a real display (1920 × 1080), was fully drawn within 200 ms of Enter. Expanding one of the three biggest sections (138K, 122K and 78K characters: the PDFium and HarfBuzz native-asset bundles and the .NET runtime notice) costs 0.2 to 0.4 s once. Not fixed; the next cheap step would be to page those three.

**Open (needs the owner's machines or a Linux host):** `measure.yml` on the two binding machines (`gh workflow run measure.yml --ref <branch> -f platform=<windows|linux> -f target=pdf -f note="<GPU, resolution, scaling, power>"`) for the verdict on the time rows and the memory headroom, and the same A/B with a loopback test build there; the size check on Linux and a new one on Windows for the final commit (`dotnet run bench/shell-probe/measure.cs -- size --check <rid> --target pdf --publish-dir <dir> --fonts-dir src/Raffica.Shell/Assets/Fonts`); the dry run of `release.yml` has run since (run 42422270038, see "Raffica PDF: packaging and release (M2.6)", section "Dry run and Linux build"); it measured no sizes or times, so the budgets above stay as open as before.
**Rationale:** The numbers show where the cost is: nothing at start, a small amount of memory once the check runs, and a size that is about 2.3 MiB over its budget. The pre-2.6 comparison on the same machine separates the machine from the milestone.
**Rejected alternatives:** raising the size budget (a budget change needs the owner's decision, see M2.5); a budget from one run on a development machine; keeping the update flow off in every measurement run (the harness would then never see the check; a release build still keeps it off on purpose, and only a loopback test build turns it on).

## 2026-11-07 – Raffica PDF: packaging and release (M2.6)
**Context:** Milestone 2.6 turns the PDF app into something a user can install and keep up to date: Windows installer and portable ZIP, Linux `.deb` and AppImage, registration as the PDF handler, the first-start card, signed updates behind a settings switch, the About card with the licence notices, and a release workflow. The logic below the UI (`UpdateChecker`, `ManifestVerifier`, `ChecksumVerifier`, `UpdateTrust`, `PackageFormDetector`, `SessionStore`, `FileTypeRegistration`, `DesktopEntry`) came with the core in M0; the platform and tool side did not. The rules of behaviour come from the predecessor's updater design (Pagewing 1.0, read only).
**Decision:**
- **One plan, no split.** Each task ended in an independently testable deliverable, so the roadmap keeps the single id 2.6.
- **Names.** Product "Raffica PDF"; executable `RafficaPdf` (`.exe` on Windows); the command line tool `raffica` ships next to it (folder `cli` on Windows, `/usr/bin/raffica` in the `.deb`). Windows installs to `{autopf}\Raffica PDF`, per user; Linux files are `/usr/lib/raffica-pdf/RafficaPdf`, `raffica-pdf.desktop` and a link `/usr/bin/raffica-pdf`. Release files: `RafficaPdf-<v>-win-x64-setup.exe`, `RafficaPdf-<v>-win-x64-portable.zip`, `RafficaPdf-<v>-x86_64.AppImage`, `raffica-pdf_<v>_amd64.deb`, `update-manifest.json` and its `.sig`, `SHA256SUMS-windows.txt`, `SHA256SUMS-linux.txt`. The Inno Setup `AppId` is a new GUID (`E2602600-DBDA-4741-8773-A1D1284E2562`); the selftest pins that it differs from the predecessor's. `StartupWMClass=RafficaPdf` is set in the desktop file and in `DesktopEntry`; it matches the real window class on X11 (`xprop WM_CLASS` under Xvfb, see "Dry run and Linux build"), the Wayland `app_id` is not tested.
- **Imported tools, adapted.** `tools/package.cs`, `tools/licenses.cs`, `tools/update-testserver.cs`, `tools/smoke-cli.cs`, `packaging/windows`, `packaging/linux` and `.github/workflows/release.yml` were imported from Pagewing `v1.0.0` unchanged in one commit and renamed, translated and trimmed in the next (`check-binary` and `native-fetch` of the old `package.cs` are gone: this repo has `tools/check-binary.cs` and `tools/native-fetch.cs`). The security rules of the workflow (tag and ref input rules, key preflight, `workflow_dispatch` only, environment `release-signing`, glibc ceiling 2.35 on Linux) are unchanged in meaning. `tools/make-placeholder-icon.cs` was new and is gone since the brand icons (entry "Brand icons").
- **Test vector.** `testdata/updates/update-manifest.json` and its `.sig` were regenerated with a throwaway key whose private half was deleted (never stored); the app never trusts the vector key. The selftest reads the vector key from `ReleaseToolTests.cs`.
- **Trust and addresses.** The app trusts only the public keys in `UpdateTrust.Keys` and the three addresses compiled into `UpdateTrust`. No environment variable, switch or setting changes them in a release build. The test build (`-p:RafficaUpdateTestBuild=true -p:RafficaUpdateTestPort=<port> -p:RafficaUpdateTestKey=<public key>`) swaps `UpdateTrust.cs` for `UpdateTrust.TestBuild.cs` (trusts only that key and a server on `127.0.0.1`); the properties come from the command line only, and `Directory.Build.props` refuses the same names as environment variables. A measurement run keeps the update flow off unless the compiled-in address is the loopback of a test build (`UpdateServices.MeasurementKeepsUpdatesOff`). With the setting off, the app opens no connection (`StartupCreatesNoPackagingWorkTests.TheRealFactoryMakesNoHttpClientWhileTheCheckIsOff`).
- **Nothing before first use.** The default-app card, the update flow, the About notices and the HTTP client do not exist before the first sharp page or first use (`StartupCreatesNoPackagingWorkTests`, `GitHubUpdateSource.ClientsCreated`). The check runs at background priority after the first sharp page.
- **Title bar slot.** `TitleBar` got an additive `Trailing` content slot left of the caption buttons (`TitleBarTrailingTests`); the update button sits there, only while an update is known (`PdfWindow.UpdateButton` is created together with the flow). The gallery and the other apps are unchanged. To be recorded under "Scope changes" in the roadmap.
- **Applier per package form** (`PlatformUpdateApplier.Create`): the installer form downloads, verifies, and after "Install" starts a helper copy of the app (`--finish-update <staging>`, with `--after-process <pid>` and `--restore-session`) that runs the Inno setup silently, shows its progress and starts the new version; the AppImage writes the new file next to itself as `.part`, verifies it, sets `0755` and replaces itself with a rename; the `.deb`, the portable ZIP and any unknown form get no applier (the factory returns `null`) and show "update available" with the download page, opened through the card that confirms external addresses. The signature of the release information and the size and SHA-256 of the file are checked at download and again in the helper (`UpdateApplierSupport.VerifyAsync`). The tabs come back after the restart through `SessionStore` (the files in order; the view of each tab is not restored). The UI never says "safe" or "guaranteed"; it names what was checked.
- **Install guard.** Before an install every tab is asked its usual questions (OCR run, redact plan, page plan, unsaved marks; `CloseGuard.AskForUpdateAsync`, `UpdateInstallGuard`); Cancel anywhere keeps all tabs and writes nothing, the answers run only after the last one, and an install is refused while a file job runs (`UpdateBusyNote`). Staging keeps the downloaded file for the next try.
- **Default app.** The first-start card (`DefaultAppCard`: Not now, Don't ask again, Set as default) is offered once per run after the first sharp page; a failed set ends in one note, "Don't ask again" is remembered. Windows: the app registers itself and opens the system's "Default apps" page; Linux: `xdg-mime`, and for the AppImage a `.desktop` file and icons in the user's data folder.
- **Linux package.** The `.deb` is built from the upstream-built files with their prebuilt native libraries and has no Debian changelog; `deb-smoke` therefore accepts exactly the lintian errors `embedded-library`, `unstripped-binary-or-object` and `no-changelog` (`AcceptedLintianErrors` in `tools/package.cs`) and fails on every other one.
- **x86_64 only,** as the predecessor: no arm64 build. macOS has no package; it comes with its own milestone. *(Amended for macOS by "Raffica on macOS: packaging, default app and updates (M3.3)": macOS ships arm64 and x64.)*

**Pagewing 1.0 inventory check (acceptance criterion 4).** Compared by hand on 2026-11-07 with the marks `(P<n>)` of milestones 2.1 to 2.6 in `Docs/ROADMAP.md` and the scope changes of 2026-11-04 (context "Milestone 2.1"), by reading the roadmap proofs and checking that every named test class exists in `tests/`; the app was not run for this and the tests were not re-run per mark. A mark is **done** when its feature is built and the named tests exist; the last full runs of this milestone were green (Pdf 1655 total, 1645 passed, 10 skipped; Shell 686 passed; Core 3377 total, 3356 passed, 21 skipped; 0 failed).

| Mark (Pagewing milestone) | Status | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| P3 shell, open (argument, dialog, drop, password), single instance, scroll with tiles, zoom, speed budgets | done | `OpenFlowTests`, `InstanceHandoverTests`, `HandoverWindowTests`, `PageViewScrollTests`, `PageViewRenderTests`, `ZoomTests`; budgets in "Raffica PDF speed budgets (M2.1)". The manual check of a second file in the running instance was skipped on 2026-11-04 and is in the backlog. |
| P4 tabs, zoom modes, go to page, layouts, pages pane and outline, document info | done | `DocumentTabsTests`, `TabListTests`, `DocumentLayoutTests`, `LayoutTests`, `ZoomStepsTests`, `DocumentInfoCardTests` |

A real printer on each platform needs the owner's machines. The Linux card has no paper size row (Core's `PrintSettings` has no paper field). |
| P8 installer, portable ZIP, `.deb`, AppImage, default app, licence notices, release workflow, README, CHANGELOG | this milestone | Windows: `package.cs installer-smoke` (install, install over, uninstall; passed on the development machine) and `package.cs selftest` (599 checks passed). Linux files and the workflow: built and smoke-tested in a container and in the release dry run (run 42422270038), see "Dry run and Linux build"; installing on a real desktop is open. Default app: `DefaultAppFlowTests`, `DefaultAppCardTests`, `DefaultAppServiceTests`. Notices: `AboutCardTests`, `licenses.cs check`. |
| P8.1 automatic updates | this milestone | `UpdateFlowTests`, `UpdateInstallGuardTests`, `GitHubUpdateSourceTests`, `UpdateServicesTests`, `WindowsUpdateInstallerTests`, `AppImageUpdaterTests`; Windows end to end: `package.cs update-smoke` through the helper mode with a throwaway key (0.1.0 to 0.2.0, passed). The AppImage updater is unit-tested only, now on real Linux too (`AppImageUpdaterTests` 10 of 10 in an Ubuntu 22.04 container); the run with a real FUSE mount on a desktop is open. |

| P12 visible signature | done | `SignatureLibraryTests`, `SignatureCardTests`, `SignaturePadTests`, `SignaturePlacementTests` |
| P12.1 public repository and security review | **open** | The owner's decision; the steps are in `packaging/README.md`, section "Making the repository public". |

Result: every mark is done or open with its reason. Open by decision: tabs between windows and "open in new window", "reduce animations" (all in the backlog), P12.1 (the owner's). Not rebuilt on purpose: the density setting and the separate high-contrast switch. The manual checks that the owner skipped in 2.1 to 2.4 stay in the backlog.

**Risks carried over from the predecessor's updater decision.** (1) Loss of the signing key means no update for installed copies: they trust only keys compiled into the program, so the fix is a new release that people install by hand; the key is kept as the CI secret `RAFFICA_UPDATE_SIGNING_KEY` and in the owner's password manager, with rotation by adding the new key next to the old one first (`packaging/README.md`). (2) A compromise of the release runner that holds the secret can sign a malicious manifest; the signing job therefore runs on a GitHub-hosted runner behind the environment `release-signing` with a required reviewer. (3) The helper's `request.json` in the staging folder is not signed; it is reachable only with write access of the same user, and the helper checks the staged file again (manifest signature, size, SHA-256) before it runs it. (4) The helper does not hand `--settings-file` on, so a run started with that switch cannot complete an update (and `package.cs update-smoke` cannot use it). (5) The OCR-fetch steps of `release.yml` carry `GITHUB_TOKEN` in their environment because the repository is private (`tools/native-fetch.cs` needs a token while it is); they need none once the repository is public.
**Brand icons.** `Docs/design/icons/brand/` holds one 1024x1024 master per app (`suite`, `write`, `sheets`, `slides`, `pdf`): a squircle in the app's accent (the light-theme `Raffica.Color.Ink.*` value), two-letter monogram in off-white (`Wr`, `Sh`, `Sl`, `Pd`; the suite has a large `R`), and wind streaks in the dark-theme ink of the app (four streaks in the four app colours for the suite) that run into the letters. The letters come from generated artwork supplied by the owner; the canvas (transparent outside the squircle), the colours, the streaks and the placement were rebuilt so that all marks share one geometry and every streak meets the letter contour without a gap. No Pagewing artwork is used. `src/Raffica.Pdf/Assets/raffica-pdf.ico` (16, 24, 32, 48, 64, 128, 256 as PNG entries) and `Docs/design/icons/linux/hicolor/<n>x<n>/apps/raffica-pdf.png` (16 to 512) are the sizes of `brand/pdf.png`, resampled with Lanczos (a light sharpening below 48 px); only the PDF mark is packaged until the other apps exist. The same `.ico` is the installer icon (`SetupIconFile`) and, through `<ApplicationIcon>` in `src/Raffica.Pdf/Raffica.Pdf.csproj`, the icon resource of `RafficaPdf.exe` (file icon, taskbar, Start menu; `FileTypeRegistration` writes `DefaultIcon` as the exe, resource 0). Known limits: below 32 px only silhouette and colour read, not the letters (a simplified small-size variant without streaks is possible later); `Sl` reads as `S/` because the italic l is a plain stroke. The placeholder generator `tools/make-placeholder-icon.cs` is gone.
**Dry run and Linux build (verified).** *Linux packages in Docker (ubuntu:22.04, Linux SDK 10.0.403, clean `git archive` export), before the dry run:* the build found and fixed three defects in the packaging code: commit ae05f31 (the glibc check now allows `libfontconfig.so.1` of Skia through a new `--allow-needed` option of `tools/check-binary.cs`), commit 422c5b5 (the AppImage smoke passed no `--settings-file`) and commit ed6caba (the AppImage smoke waited for `first-sharp` instead of `first-page`). It then went green end to end: glibc check ok for `RafficaPdf`, `libHarfBuzzSharp`, `libSkiaSharp`, `libpdfium` (app and cli), `cli/raffica` and `libtesseract`; layout 12 files; `.deb` with 21 entries (`/usr/bin/raffica` and `/usr/bin/raffica-pdf` links, `/usr/lib/raffica-pdf/*`, the desktop file, hicolor icons 16 to 512, copyright); `deb-smoke` passed; `desktop-file-validate` code 0; lintian errors only of the classes the smoke accepts (`embedded-library`, `no-changelog`, `unstripped-binary-or-object`; warning `no-manual-page`); sizes: `.deb` 30,894,280 bytes, AppImage 32,864,760, `fuse-3.15.0.tar.xz` 4,589,124, `type2-runtime-20251108.tar.gz` 31,045, `SHA256SUMS-linux.txt` 374 bytes; `appimage-smoke` under xvfb passed (the app draws `text-basic.pdf`, `first-page`, exit 0 with `APPIMAGE_EXTRACT_AND_RUN=1`; without the variable the container has no FUSE and the runtime says so, which the smoke accepts as designed). MIME handling is `MimeType=application/pdf;` in the desktop file only. `AppImageUpdaterTests` passed 10 of 10 on real Linux (including the Mode755 assertion skipped on Windows), and the real window class under Xvfb with no window manager is "RafficaPdf", "RafficaPdf" (`xprop WM_CLASS`), so `StartupWMClass=RafficaPdf` matches on X11. *Release workflow dry run:* `gh workflow run release.yml --ref dev -f tag=v0.1.0 -f ref=<40-hex sha of dev> -f upload=false` works once `release.yml` exists on `dev` (the runner group accepts `--ref dev` with a commit SHA as `ref`). Run 42422270038 (https://github.com/benjo-code/raffica/actions/runs/42422270038) on `dev` commit 4a31a74b81563f6015dc773ebe07a7b12ab16ca9: `release-preflight` success; `release-windows` success (layout, ZIP with 13 files, installer, `installer-smoke: all steps passed`, checksums on the owner's runner); `release-linux` success (selftest 622 checks passed, container build, `.deb`, AppImage, smokes); `release-manifest` skipped as designed for a dry run, nothing uploaded. The first dry run (run 42418844306 on 841cfd6) had failed in `release-linux` at the Linux-only part of the packaging selftest (a stale fixture path `Raffica PDF` instead of `RafficaPdf`; fixed by PR #37); its Windows job had passed. Lesson: a Windows-only selftest does not cover the Linux part, and the packaging code needed a real Linux build to be right.
**Open (not verified; each with its check):**
- Real Linux desktops: the AppImage with a real FUSE mount (run it, then the manual update check, `packaging/README.md`, "Manual update check", step 4); `sudo apt install ./raffica-pdf_<v>_amd64.deb`, open a PDF and `sudo apt remove raffica-pdf` on a Debian or Ubuntu machine; the Wayland `app_id` against `StartupWMClass=RafficaPdf`.
- The real UAC prompt of a per-machine install through the helper (the smoke is per user); the exit code Inno returns for a declined prompt is treated as any non-zero.
- Default app on real desktops (the real `ms-settings:` page, a policy-locked `HKCU`, `xdg-mime`, an AppImage) and real printers.
- The real mark: replace the placeholder files (`Docs/design/icons/README.md`); the exe already carries the placeholder icon. Check the icon of an installed exe and the PDF file icon in Explorer on a real machine.
- The owner gates: the path of an update with the real key (the key `release-1` and the secret exist since 2026-11-08, the public half is in `UpdateTrust.cs`; no signed release exists yet), the reviewer and branch policy of the environment `release-signing` (not available while the repository is private), repository visibility, the first release (the `upload=true` path and the job `release-manifest` have not run), SmartScreen and Authenticode (the installer is unsigned), and the size finding above (68.48 MiB against the budget of 66 MiB, limit 82.5) with the time budgets and idle-memory headroom through `measure.yml` on the owner's machines.
- The three largest notice sections of the About card take 0.2 to 0.4 s on their first expand (see the measurements entry).
- Flaky tests seen in CI, green on re-run each time. (1) The test host crash in `Avalonia.Threading.Dispatcher.RequestProcessing`: addressed in PR #38 (merged), to be confirmed by watching CI (seen in a full run and once in CI, run 42419846708): a `NullReferenceException` called from `PageView.OnPagesInvalidated` or `PagesPane.OnTileReady` on a pool thread. Cause identified from the stack trace and the Avalonia 12.1.5 source, not reproduced: each headless test has its own dispatcher and the end of a test clears the static `Dispatcher.UIThread`; a late callback of a leftover window that posted through the static would make a new dispatcher on the pool thread, published before its platform part is set, and a second poster would throw. The handlers no longer touch the static `Dispatcher.UIThread`: a handler, timer or progress callback that can run on a pool thread posts through the dispatcher its owner captured on the UI thread (`Dispatcher` of a control, a `_dispatcher` field of other owners, the captured `SynchronizationContext` of the sessions), and `TextEditSession` keeps its flush synchronous on the UI thread. `TeardownRaceTests` pins this for PagesPane, PageView and OrganizeList; the other sites follow the same pattern. The real app is not affected, its UI dispatcher lives until exit. (2) `Raffica.Core.Tests.Ocr.OcrRunnerTests.PdfiumThreadIsNeverBlockedByRecognition` failed once on the shared Windows runner (`Assert.True(clock.ElapsedMilliseconds < 200 ...)` with 667 ms): a timing assertion on a loaded runner; Core code was untouched by that PR and the check was not changed. To watch; the owner decides whether to widen it or move it.
**Rationale:** Porting the tools and the platform code and rebuilding only the UI keeps the proven install and update behaviour of the predecessor and the spec's rule that UI code is never ported. One plan with small tasks was enough because the pieces are independent. The update button and cards reuse the shell's frame; the only shell change is an additive slot.
**Rejected alternatives:**
- *Writing `UserChoice` on Windows 10/11 to become the default app:* Windows does not allow a program to set that choice (the key is protected). The app registers itself and opens the settings page for the user's confirmation.
- *One read-only text box for all notices in the About card:* measured at 0.4 to 1.4 s of layout (see the measurements entry); replaced by sections created on first expansion.
- *Reusing Pagewing's artwork for the icon:* it is another product's mark. A generated placeholder stands in until the real mark exists.
- *An environment variable or switch that overrides the update addresses or keys* (convenient for tests): it would also be a way to redirect the updater of an installed copy. Tests use the compile-time test build instead, whose properties only the command line can set.

## 2026-11-06 – Raffica PDF: published size budgets after the OCR payload (M2.5)
**Context:** The CI step "Size of the published PDF app" (`measure.cs size --check --target pdf`) of pull request #34 failed after Milestone 2.5: Windows 65.33 MiB published size and 28.3 MiB zip against the limits 63.75 and 27.5 MiB; Linux 64.65 MiB and 29.19 MiB against 60 and 27.5 MiB. The M2.4 state was 50.81 MiB on Windows (entry "Raffica PDF: software rendering is the Windows default (M2.1 follow-up)"). The growth has two causes. The OCR payload that M2.5 puts next to the program: on Windows `tesseract.dll` 3 684 864 bytes and the `tessdata` folder (English and German, `tessdata_fast`) 5 638 524 bytes, together about 8.9 MiB; on Linux `libtesseract.so` 6 948 376 bytes plus the same language data (5.4 MiB). The rest, about 5.6 MiB on Windows, is the code and strings of redacting, text recognition and text editing (Native AOT code and the new strings in English and German).
**Decision:** The owner decided on 2026-11-06 to **count the OCR payload**: the check keeps measuring everything a user downloads, because the library and the bundled English and German data are part of the product. The size and zip budgets of the PDF target are replaced by the measurement rounded up to 1 MiB (rule of M1): Windows size 66 MiB and zip 29 MiB, Linux size 65 MiB and zip 30 MiB (before: 51 and 22, 48 and 22). The limits are the budgets times 1.25 once (`Stats.Limit`): Windows 82.5 MiB and 36.25 MiB, Linux 81.25 MiB and 37.5 MiB. This is a loosening decided by the owner, not made by CI to get a check green; **every other budget is unchanged** (Windows cold 200, warm 210, 1000 pages 200, second file 20, scroll 20, memory 40; Linux cold 230, warm 230, 1000 pages 220, second file 40, scroll 40, memory 155). The single copy of the numbers is `s_pdfBudgets` in `CheckCommand` of `bench/shell-probe/measure.cs`; the selftest pins every value (`dotnet run bench/shell-probe/measure.cs -- selftest`, 287 checks).
**Measurement basis:** one run on the self-hosted CI runners (the publish of pull request #34, Native AOT, published without debug symbols as the check does); Linux was measured on the Linux runner, Windows on the Windows runner. The owner's runs on the two binding machines are not repeated for this change: the measurement is the CI publish. A local publish with debug symbols on the development machine passes the new Windows limits (65.33 MiB size, 28.3 MiB zip, as the check handles the symbols the same way as in CI).
**Rationale:** Not counting the library and the data would make the check measure a program the user does not get; a lower budget would need the payload out of the package. Rounding up and the factor 1.25 keep the rule that every earlier entry used.
**What would change them:** the optional language-data download of Milestone 2.6 or later (English alone, more languages on demand) would take the data (5.4 MiB) out of the package, and a leaner OCR build or a stripped library would take part of the library out; either is a new entry with a new measurement.
**Rejected alternatives:** leaving the OCR files out of the check (the check would no longer say what is downloaded); keeping the old budgets and failing CI (the payload is intended); raising the budgets without the owner (the numbers are the owner's call).

## 2026-11-06 – Raffica PDF: redaction, OCR and text editing measurements (M2.5)
**Context:** "Raffica PDF: redaction, OCR and text editing conventions (M2.5)" promised that the three features cost nothing at startup and stay usable on large input. This entry records the first numbers of the two heaviest paths. They are **single runs on one machine** (the dev machine of the session: AMD Ryzen 7 3800X, `Environment.ProcessorCount` = 6 as the test host sees it, about 16 GB RAM, Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26200, Release build of the tests, headless Avalonia window, **software rendering**, hot file cache, 2026-11-06). They carry **no budget**: `s_pdfBudgets` and the measurement switches of the app are untouched, and the runs on the owner's two binding machines (method: "Raffica PDF measurement method (M2.1)") are still open.
**Decision:** `RedactMeasurementTests.ApplyOnAThousandPageFile` and `OcrMeasurementTests.RunOnTwentyScannedPages` (both skipped unless `RAFFICA_MEASURE_OUT` is set; re-run with `RAFFICA_MEASURE_OUT=<folder> dotnet test tests/Raffica.Pdf.Tests -c Release --filter-class "*RedactMeasurementTests"` and `"*OcrMeasurementTests"`, they write `redact-measurements.txt` and `ocr-measurements.txt`, never assert a time; the OCR one needs the library and fails under `RAFFICA_REQUIRE_OCR=1` without it). **Redaction:** `thousand-pages.pdf` (265 KiB), one area mark on each of the 1000 pages, applied through `RedactApplyFlow` into a temp file: making the 1000 marks 78 ms; from "Apply" to the analysis card 13 759 ms; from the card's "Save as…" to the result card (rewrite, check, adopt) 15 242 ms; the written file 441 KiB; peak working set of the test process 191 MiB. **OCR:** 20 scanned pages (the three pages of `ocr-scan.pdf` repeated, built with `PageEditApplier`), language `eng`, 3 jobs, the real library and the shipped language data, through `StartOcrRunAsync`: 1 234 ms from the start of the run to the report card (62 ms a page), 485 words, mean confidence reported by the engine 96 %, peak working set 226 MiB. Text editing has no time here: its reads are pinned as "none before the first use" (`TextEditModeTests.NothingIsReadAtStartOrBeforeTheFirstUse`), and `StartupCreatesNoRedactOcrOrEditWorkTests` pins that start creates no session, flow, badge, engine or language folder, and that only the scan-hint probe runs after the first sharp page, once, and only when the library is there.
**Rationale:** The numbers say where the time goes, not whether it is fast enough. The two redaction steps are each about 14 to 15 seconds for 1000 pages in a headless run (the analysis reads and measures every page, the rewrite then writes and checks them), which is why both run as cancelable jobs behind a job card; whether that is acceptable and where it could be cut is for the owner's machines and a later milestone, not a budget set from one run. The OCR run is far from the limit of the 20 pages. A real window with a graphics device, a long scan and the other languages stay manual checks.
**Rejected alternatives:** a budget from one run on a development machine; asserting a time in the test (it would be flaky across machines).

## 2026-11-06 – Raffica PDF: redaction, OCR and text editing conventions (M2.5)
**Context:** Milestone 2.5 puts the redaction, OCR and text-run engines that Core already has (`RedactionPlan`, `RedactionHistory`, `RedactionSearch`, `RedactionPatterns`, `RedactionExecutor.AnalyzeAsync`, `RedactionApplier`; `OcrRunner`, `TessdataStore`, `AnnotationEditor.ApplyOcrAsync`; `PdfDocument.GetTextRunsAsync`, `TextEditCheck`, `AnnotationEditor.ApplyTextEditAsync`) behind the PDF app's UI and builds the OCR library in this repository. The predecessor's designs (M14, M14.1, M15, M16) fixed the models; the calls below are the author's calls while planning (`Docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-06-milestone-02-5-redact-ocr-line-editing.md`); the owner can overturn them. Research for the plan (public guidance on redaction failures, Acrobat's workflow, Tesseract builds and font subsets) agreed with the predecessor's rules.
**Decision:**
1. **Redaction is mark, review, apply** with a pending plan per tab and a full rewrite on Apply; marks are drawn by an overlay and never written before Apply.
2. **Cleaning is not a switch** (owner, 2026-11-06; Core already works this way): every Apply removes the cleanup list; the Apply card lists it by field name only and the result card lists what was checked and what was not.
3. **One class decides which modes exclude each other** (annotate, forms, organize, redact, OCR run, text edit); the reason is shown, controls are not silently dimmed.
4. **A Redact tab (R) and an Edit tab (E)**; the redact pane takes the marks-pane slot while the mode is on.
5. **Appearance is per plan, overlay text per mark;** a caution is shown whenever pixelate or blur is chosen.
6. **Rescue pages are explicit:** a refused page is ticked or removed before Apply is possible; encrypted files and unremovable page keys offer nothing.
7. **Picture pages point to OCR:** a search that cannot see text in images says so and offers the command; it never starts OCR itself.
8. **Apply defaults to "Save as…";** the original is armed and locked for 400 ms.
9. **OCR is an options card plus a non-modal run** (badge and status text with Cancel); a finished run is one unsaved history step; cancelling with finished pages asks to keep or discard.
10. **Language data:** English and German bundled, more by copying a file from disk with a trust warning, never a download; an imported file beats the bundled one; the choice is stored in the settings.
11. **Text editing is a page-view mode** with run outlines, an inline field and a hint bar; an empty text removes the run; an open draft counts as an unresolved edit.
12. **The old text stays in earlier revisions** of an incrementally saved file and the status bar says so once per tab and document; no forced rewrite.
13. **The OCR library is built here** by a dispatch-only workflow on hosted runners from pinned commits (Tesseract 5.5.4, Leptonica 1.87.0) and published as the prerelease `native-tesseract-5.5.4-1`; because the repository is private, `tools/native-fetch.cs` reads the assets through the GitHub API with a token; the pinned size and SHA-256 stay the trust anchor. Running the workflow is an owner gate.
14. **CodeQL returns guarded for private repositories; `measure.yml` is the hardware-measure workflow.**
**Rationale:** (1, 2) Boxes over text and forgotten sanitizing are the usual redaction failures; a pending plan with an always-on cleanup and an honest "not checked" list removes both. (3) Five modes that must not touch each other need one place that knows the matrix. (7) Acrobat's search cannot see text in images and leaves users to find out. (9) A scan can take minutes; the document must stay readable. (12) The predecessor's decision: a forced rewrite would change files the user only wanted to correct. (13) The installers must not depend on another repository; a private repository cannot serve unauthenticated downloads, the pinned hash does not care where the file came from.
**Rejected alternatives:** a sanitize checkbox (the failure it invites); marks saved as `/Redact` annotations (backlog); QPDF post-processing (no new dependency); in-app language downloads (no network access besides the updater); a modal OCR job card (blocks reading for minutes); deskew or OSD (predecessor's anti-goal, backlog); building Tesseract on the self-hosted runners (third-party source on owner machines); a second hardware-measure workflow (`measure.yml` exists).

**Amendment (end of M2.5, 2026-11-06): what was built differently from the 14 calls.**
- **Call 6 (rescue):** `redact-structure` and `redact-objstm` are not redacted but refused by Core for unremovable page keys (`/AA`, `/Metadata`, `/PieceInfo`, `/Thumb`); the UI shows the refusal and offers no rescue, and the acceptance test asserts the refusal. The planning text that listed them as redactable was wrong. `redact-hidden` and `redact-everywhere` need the page-as-image rescue, `redact-fonts` needs an extra area mark for the line a text search does not find. The ticked rescue pages join the plan after the picker and the plan-version re-check, just before the write; Undo removes them.
- **Call 1 (marks):** text marks are built as `Kind.Text` marks directly.
- **Call 3 (lock matrix):** `ModeExclusion.CanEnter` is the strict pair table; `CanStart` skips the one pair "Annotate active, starting Organize" because entering Organize ends the tool (the 2.4 behaviour). Form clicks and keys, the output commands and Organize ask the same class.
- **Call 8 (original armed):** the arming also applies when the picked path is an alias of the open file (relative or `..` spelling, capitals on Windows, symlink, `\?\` prefix) through `FileIdentity.AreSameFile`; a null answer counts as the same file. A confirmation card ("Replace the original file?") sits behind it. Writing to a symlink path replaces the link itself and leaves the file behind it unchanged. The result card is shown after the tab rebuild, not before.
- **Call 9 (OCR):** the history-full retry was dropped: Core writes the result anyway and reports `Undoable = false`, which the report card says plainly. The app words failures through the new Core type `OcrFailure` (`OcrFailureKind`, `OcrFailure.From`) because `AppProjectTests.TheAppReferencesOnlyCoreAndShell` forbids a reference to `Raffica.Ocr`. `ocr.languages` is locked while a run goes, the options card has a "current page" option, and Save, Print and Undo wait for a run.
- **Call 4 (keys):** Esc in the redact mode unwinds in this order: a drag in progress, the mark selection, the text selection, then the mode. The key handlers of the modes are tunnel handlers on the page view (the view's class handler would consume the arrows first) and entering a mode focuses the view. Ctrl+Shift+G toggles the whole-line switch of the text-edit field.
- **Call 13 (library):** `Raffica.Pdf.csproj` copies the OCR library and tessdata only when a `RuntimeIdentifier` is set and `artifacts/native/<rid>` exists; a plain build or the test project never copies them (the Pdf tests use `OcrTestLibrary`, which copies only the library at first use). The workflow run is 42198001684 (prerelease `native-tesseract-5.5.4-1`, merged to `dev` as part of PR #33).
- **Call 7 (picture pages), final review:** the "Recognize text…" button of the Find panel's picture row could never be enabled in the real window, because Redact and OCR exclude each other (call 3) and the Find panel exists only in the redact mode. The offer is therefore an instruction: the row keeps the page count sentence and adds "Recognize text first: leave Redact mode (apply or discard the marks), recognize the text, then search again." (English and German). The command button is shown only when its command is really enabled (a fake in the tests); `InTheRealWindowTheRowIsAnInstructionBecauseRedactAndOcrExcludeEachOther` reads the real command state. Item 7 above stays as written (history); its "offers the command" now means this instruction.
- **Call 3 (lock matrix), final review:** the annotate tool is the window's, the other modes are the tab's. The window now ends the tool whenever the active tab's modes do not allow it (`ModeExclusion.CanEnter(Annotate)`: Organize, Redact, Edit text, a text recognition; forms may stay), when the tab changes and when a tool is chosen by a path around the disabled commands. The marks pane is locked while Edit text is on (a click does not choose a tool), and `ToggleOrganizeAsync` asks `CanStart` itself like the other two modes. While a text recognition runs in any tab, a disabled mode command names the run, not a save.
- **Call 13 (library fetch), final review:** `tools/native-fetch.cs` sends the token only to `https://api.github.com`: an asset address in the release answer that points elsewhere, is not https, carries user info or is no URL is refused before any second request (selftest cases for each).
- **Call 14 (CodeQL):** the guard for private repositories stays; the CodeQL job is skipped there rather than failing.

## 2026-11-05 – Raffica PDF: page organizing measurements (M2.4)
**Context:** "Raffica PDF: page organizing conventions (M2.4)" promised that the organize mode stays usable on a large file and costs nothing at startup. This entry records the first and the test of the second. The numbers are **one run on one machine** (AMD Ryzen 7 3800X, `Environment.ProcessorCount` = 6 as the test host sees it, Windows 11 Pro, Release build of the tests, headless Avalonia window, three runs after one warm-up run, each in a fresh window, 2026-11-05). They carry **no budget**: `s_pdfBudgets` and the measurement switches of the app are untouched, and the runs on the owner's two binding machines (method: "Raffica PDF measurement method (M2.1)") are still open.
**Decision:** `OrganizeMeasurementTests.OrganizeAThousandPagesIsMeasured` (skipped unless `RAFFICA_MEASURE_OUT` is set; re-run with `RAFFICA_MEASURE_OUT=<folder> dotnet test tests/Raffica.Pdf.Tests -c Release --filter-class "*OrganizeMeasurementTests"`, writes `organize-measurements.txt`, never asserts a time) uses `thousand-pages.pdf`. Median / min / max in ms; every step of the list includes the pump of the UI queue. Opening the file, the window and the list with its 1000 items (the mode entered) 24.7 / 24.1 / 27.4; scrolling the list through 50 steps from top to bottom, the whole run 518.4 / 498.4 / 646.5 (about 10 ms a step); rotating a selection of 100 pages 29.1 / 25.6 / 32.6; deleting 100 pages 36.0 / 31.0 / 38.2; moving 100 pages 45.6 / 29.0 / 45.9; `PageEditExecutor.AnalyzeAsync` of a rotation of all 1000 pages 7.5 / 7.3 / 7.8; `PageEditApplier.ApplyAsync` of that plan (rewrite, check, adopt the written file, called directly without the cards) 524.9 / 493.6 / 537.3. `StartupCreatesNoOrganizeWork` (not skipped) pins that a tab that was opened and shown has no `OrganizeSession` and that the window made none of the insert, apply, extract, split and export flows.
**Rationale:** The numbers say where the time goes, not whether it is fast enough: a plan edit is cheap (tens of milliseconds for 100 pages including the list update) and the rewrite of 1000 pages is the one half-second step, which is why Apply is a cancelable job on a pool thread. The headless window has no graphics device and no real thumbnail pipeline: how the list feels while thumbnails arrive and the time to the repaint on screen stay manual checks.
**Rejected alternatives:** a budget from one run on a development machine; asserting a time in the test (it would be flaky across machines).

## 2026-11-05 – Raffica PDF: page organizing conventions (M2.4)
**Context:** Milestone 2.4 puts the page engine that Core already has (`PageEditPlan` and `PageEditHistory`, `PageEditExecutor.AnalyzeAsync` with its `PageEditReport`, `PageEditApplier`, `DocumentSplitter`, `PageImageExporter`; the CLI commands `rotate`, `merge`, `split` and `export` use it) behind the PDF app's UI. The predecessor's design (M13) fixed the model "a plan, nothing written until Apply" and the rule "say what is lost before, never lose silently"; the calls below are the author's calls while planning (`Docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-05-milestone-02-4-organize-pages.md`); the owner can overturn them.
**Decision:**
1. **One pending plan per tab, nothing written until Apply** (kept from the predecessor). The page view shows the unchanged document meanwhile; the organize mode of the pages pane edits the plan.
2. **Locks while a plan is open:** annotate tools, Sign, form filling, Extract, Split and Export images are disabled with the reason "Apply or discard the page changes first"; print stays; Save and Save as with an open plan open the Apply card.
3. **Unsaved marks or form values are saved first** (a question, "Save first" default); the history is not flowed into the working copy.
4. **The Apply card** shows the summary, the localized loss list per `PageLossKind` (the signed-file warning is the loss `SignatureInvalidated`), the sentence that a rewrite cannot be undone and that no backup copy is made, and the choices "Save as…" (default), "Save in the original file" (armed) and "Back"; a refused report shows only the reason.
5. **After the rewrite the app rebuilds the tab's view and panes** over the adopted document (the page list and page sizes changed; the annotation and form sessions start clean), at the page `PageEditPlan.FollowPage` gives; the fallback is to reopen the written file in the same strip position.
6. **Inserted pages show real thumbnails** from one `ThumbnailSource` per inserted file; blank pages are drawn.
7. **Merge is "Insert file…"** (range and position) into the plan; the first file is the main file. No merge dialog with a file list.
8. **Reorder:** drag with an insertion marker and list-local keys (Alt+Up/Down/Home/End, Delete, `[` and `]`, Ctrl+D); no new window-level key capture; Tab never traps focus.
9. **Undo and redo go to the plan history while the mode is active.**
10. **Extract, Split and Export images are cards with a range field** that read the open document without a plan, write only new files (never replace one unasked, a clash is named), show losses of the parts and run as cancelable jobs.
11. **Image export:** PNG, 72, 150, 300 dpi or custom, marks on or off, the light page, names as the CLI writes them, a page over `PageImageExporter.MaxPixels` refused before the first file.
12. **A Pages ribbon tab (letter P)** with organize, edit, history, apply, output and (set apart, last) destructive groups.
**Rationale:** (1) Annotations, search, forms, layout and caches hang on page numbers of the open file; moving pages live would rebuild the whole view. (2) A plan that disagrees with the shown document must not be annotated or exported by mistake, and a rewrite cannot run beside an open edit. (3) The predecessor's own rule, and the cheapest one that loses nothing silently. (4) Predecessor's rules: the loss list is the honest part, and "Save as…" is the only safe copy. (5) The pieces of a tab compare page counts and sizes of the old document, so a rotation of the same page count would show stale sizes. (6) The predecessor's design asked for it and Core supports it. (7) The same Core call serves both, with fewer surfaces. (8) The predecessor's keys, without a second window-level key table. (9) Two histories on one key would surprise. (10) Pagewing's rule "only new files, never replace silently". (11) The CLI's behaviour, so both tools write the same names. (12) The canvas has one separated destructive group.
**Rejected alternatives:** moving pages live in the page view (rebuilds every pane); a plan-to-new-file writer (a second path to the rewrite, backlog); an automatic backup copy (a silent write); a merge dialog with a file list (backlog, no new capability); placeholders instead of thumbnails for inserted pages (cheaper, but the predecessor's design and Core allow real ones); JPEG export (no encoder, a new dependency).

**Amendment (end of M2.4): what was built differently from the plan.**
- **Locks follow "mode active", not "plan changed":** a tab in the organize mode locks the annotate tools, Sign, form clicks, form keys and form commands, marks-pane comments and the output commands (Extract, Split, Export images) even while the plan is the identity; form clicks and marks-pane comments were added to the locks in a fix round (the reason text is the same). Print stays. Leaving the mode is one command; `pages.organize` leaves at once for an identity plan and keeps the mode for a changed one (a leave with changes goes through the plan question below).
- **Output commands read the open document without the plan** (as call 10 said); they are disabled in the mode and read the file as shown.
- **Save and Save as hand over to Apply:** `SaveController.ApplyPlan` is a delegate `Func<PdfTab, bool, Task<bool>>` set by the window (no reference from `SaveController` to `ApplyFlow`); the bool is the Save-as flag, so Save as offers only "Save as…" and "Back" on the Apply card. It is used only for a non-identity plan; unsaved marks are saved first through `SaveMarksAsync` (the normal incremental save, which does not take the hook) and the tab stays dirty while a plan is open.
- **Job cards are modal** (`DialogQueue`): the analysis and the rewrite cannot overlap an edit of the plan. Apply re-checks the plan version after the Apply card and after the save picker and tells the user ("plan changed") instead of writing a plan that was not shown. The "Save in the original file" arming and the changed-original loop (`ChangedOriginalCard`) are in `ApplyFlow`; the system picker confirms replacing a file in Save as, so the request keeps `ReplaceExisting` at its default.
- **One shared `JobRunner`** (`Organizing/JobRunner`) runs Apply, Extract, Split and Export images: one modal job card with progress and Cancel, `Task.Run`, the work exception always reaches the caller, a failed dialog is swallowed, a tab that is closed cancels the job. The jobs refuse each other (an Apply while a split runs and the reverse), and the window's busy and cancel lists name all four flows.
- **Closing and discarding:** `CloseGuard.PlanClose` asks a changed plan its own question (`UnsavedChangesCard` with `UnsavedKind.Plan`: Apply…, Discard, Cancel; closes only after a successful Apply); an identity plan closes silently. Discard ends the plan and then continues with the generic save question, so unsaved marks next to a plan are still asked (never lost silently). In close-all, a Discard on tab 1 followed by Cancel on tab 2 leaves tab 1's plan open, as "Don't save" leaves marks.
- **Insert file:** a file with zero pages is refused as unreadable (a card, nothing registered); any unexpected failure after the picker ends in a card, not silence. No blank-page card: a blank page takes the size of the page before the position (A4 for the first page). `OrganizeSession.TooManyFiles` counts every file ever registered in the session, not only those the plan still uses, because undo can bring a removed file's pages back; after 20 inserts the user must leave the mode (and apply or discard) to insert another file.
- **Drag and keys:** a non-contiguous selection is gathered at the drop target (a drag moves them as one block); the brackets `[` and `]` match on the typed symbol first, so AltGr layouts (German) work.
- **Output wording:** a part that fails to write is named with its cause (`PartFailed` is mapped to opening, damaged and I/O or access wording); the losses of the parts use the same `PageLossWording` as the Apply card (a test pins that every `PageLossKind` has wording in both languages).
- **Added members:** `OrganizeSession.Ending` (raised before the session ends, so the list lets go of its state), `PdfTab.OrganizeCreated` (raised when the lazy session is made, so a pane built earlier can attach) and the static `OrganizeSession.Summarize(PageEditPlan)` (the summary of any plan, used by the Apply card).
- **Open (owner's machines):** the bracket keys on a real German keyboard layout (headless tests cannot press AltGr) and the feel of the list while thumbnails arrive; see "Raffica PDF: page organizing measurements (M2.4)".

**Amendment (final review of M2.4): corrections to the amendment above and rules added by the review.**
- **Leaving the mode:** `pages.organize` leaves at once for an identity plan; a changed plan asks Apply / Discard / Cancel, and the mode stays only on Cancel (this replaces "keeps the mode for a changed one" above).
- **Closing never ends a plan before the tab goes:** `CloseGuard` only remembers "Discard"; the plan ends with the tab's dispose when the tab really closes. A Cancel at the question about the marks, or at a later tab of close-all, therefore keeps every plan (this makes the close-all sentence above true, and replaces "Discard ends the plan and then continues"). After a Discard only the marks decide whether the generic question is asked (`AnnotationSession.IsDirty`, not `PdfTab.IsDirty`, which the plan sets by itself), and its Save writes the marks alone (`SaveController.SaveMarksAsync` through `PlanClose.SaveMarks`), never the plan.
- **A running save locks the page commands:** the incremental save is not modal, so `Apply`, Extract, Split, Export images and entering the mode wait for it (command rules with the reason "A save is running.", and the flows refuse with the busy notice if they are reached around the rules).
- **Window-level keys:** Ctrl+A and Ctrl+C stay with the organize list while the focus is in it; the other list keys (Delete, Ctrl+D, Alt+arrows, brackets, Esc) are no catalog shortcuts and reach the list (`OrganizeWindowKeysTests` presses them in the real window).
- **Locks are read again after every wait:** Extract, Split and Export images flush an open edit first (a value just typed counts as an unsaved mark), and read the locks again after the outline or size read, after the cards and pickers and before the write. The plan version is re-checked after every card and picker of Apply, including the Save-as picker behind the changed-original card.
- **Same-file check:** inserting the open file is refused by the spelling of the path and then by `FileIdentity.AreSameFile` (8.3 names, links, hard links), off the UI thread.
- **Wording after a save-first:** on the Save-as path the save-first card names the open file and says it is changed; a failure after a save-first says the marks are saved and the page changes still open, not "the file is unchanged".

## 2026-11-05 – Raffica PDF: forms and signing measurements (M2.3)
**Context:** "Raffica PDF: forms and signing conventions (M2.3)" left two things to check in the plan: that printing carries form values (call 10) and what a form with many fields costs in the window. This entry records both. The numbers are **one run on one machine** (AMD Ryzen 7 3800X, `Environment.ProcessorCount` = 6 as the test host sees it, Windows 11 Pro, Release build of the tests, headless Avalonia window, nine runs after one warm-up run, each run in a fresh window, 2026-11-05). They carry **no budget**: `s_pdfBudgets` and the measurement switches of the app are untouched, and the runs on the owner's two binding machines (method: "Raffica PDF measurement method (M2.1)") are still open.
**Decision:** (a) **Print.** Core prints with the values the document holds now, saved or not, and the app adds one thing: `PrintCommand` flushes the tab's annotation session (`AnnotationSession.FlushAsync`, the call the save path uses) before it reads the print info, so an open valid field edit is committed before the pages are drawn; a draft the rules refuse stays in its editor and the pages carry the last accepted value. A tab that never had an annotation session is not flushed and none is made, and a document without a form starts no form work (`PrintFormValuesTests`). A tab with unsaved changes passes no path to the service, so the service writes a copy from the document in memory: `PrintedPageCarriesUnsavedFormValues` prints through "Microsoft Print to PDF" on Windows and compares the printed page before and after an unsaved value (it skips where that printer is missing); on Linux `CupsPrintService` hands CUPS that same copy (`PdfDocument.SaveDecryptedCopyAsync`), and `TheCopyForCupsCarriesAValueThatWasSetButNotSaved` pins what is testable without CUPS: the copy holds the unsaved value while the file on disk keeps the old one. **Not checked:** a real CUPS job with a form (no CUPS on the development machine; `CupsPrintServiceTests` skip without `RAFFICA_TEST_CUPS_PRINTER`), the check is the owner's. (b) **Measurement.** `FormOpenMeasurementTests.OpenAndLeaveAFieldAreMeasured` (skipped unless `RAFFICA_MEASURE_OUT` is set; re-run with `RAFFICA_MEASURE_OUT=<folder> dotnet test tests/Raffica.Pdf.Tests -c Release --filter-class "*FormOpenMeasurementTests"`, writes `form-open-measurements.txt`, never asserts a time) opens one page with 400 text fields in a headless window. Median / min / max: opening to the first sharp page shown and the widgets of page 0 read 200.7 / 130.6 / 236.9 ms; leaving a field to the value written and the tab's state current 142.1 / 100.9 / 151.4 ms; one full render of the page afterwards (scale 1.0) 5.5 / 5.0 / 9.3 ms.
**Rationale:** The numbers say where the time goes, not whether it is fast enough: leaving a field costs more than a render because the value goes through the shared history, the form engine and the required-field refresh, and the test's settle loop waits up to three rounds, so the figure is an upper bound of what a user waits for. The headless window has no graphics device and no real tile pipeline: the time to the repaint on screen and high-DPI sharpness stay manual checks. A budget needs the owner's runs on the binding machines, as for M2.1.
**Rejected alternatives:** a fix for printing in the app (Core already prints the current values); a budget from one run on a development machine.

## 2026-11-05 – Raffica PDF: forms and signing conventions (M2.3)
**Context:** Milestone 2.3 puts the form and signature engine that Core already has (PDFium form fill, `AnnotationEditor.SetFormValueAsync` in the shared history, `FormNavigation`, `FormValueRules`, `FormInfo`, `SignatureStore`, `SignatureCutout`, `SignaturePlacement`, image and drawn signatures, printing of form values) behind the PDF app's UI. The predecessor's designs (M11 forms, M12 visible signature) fixed a separate fill mode and a spike-first plan; Core has since settled the spike questions. The calls below are the author's calls while planning (`Docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-05-milestone-02-3-forms-and-signing.md`); the owner approved the approach on 2026-11-05 and can overturn single calls.
**Decision:**
1. **No separate fill mode.** Fields are live whenever the document has fillable fields and no annotate tool is active; a click on a field starts editing, any other click behaves as in 2.1. An annotate tool makes the fields inert and ends an open field edit first.
2. **Highlight** is a per-tab switch on the form badge, on by default for a document with fillable fields, not stored. PDFium draws it; the app draws stars (required), locks (read-only or unsupported) and the focus ring, never relying on colour alone.
3. **Required fields:** a counter on the badge, a "Next required field" command and the card before saving ("Go to first empty field" default, "Save anyway"); the card never blocks.
4. **Notices** are one dismissible line naming only what applies (calculations and format scripts, XFA, signature fields, buttons), shown once per tab.
5. **Keyboard:** Tab and Shift+Tab walk the fields in `FormNavigation` order and pass at the ends (no focus trap); Enter or Space acts on the focused field; Esc leaves it. Check boxes and radio buttons have no element, the overlay draws the focus ring.
6. **Core permission:** `PdfDocument.MayFillFormsAsync` (fill-forms bit or annotate bit, or unencrypted or owner-opened), tested in Core.
7. **Slots on `PageView`:** the field editor reuses the `Field` slot, a new `FormOverlay` slot draws markers before `Overlay`, the `Badge` slot hosts a `BadgeHost` that shows the annotate badge while a tool is active and the form badge otherwise.
8. **Signing in one step:** the Sign command (Annotate tab) opens the New-signature card when nothing is saved, else the picker; a pick is placed in the centre of the visible part of the page and selected with the select tool. A drawn signature is an ink annotation with the subject "Signature"; an imported image offers no colour or width.
9. **Signature folder** next to `--settings-file` when that switch is given, else the user's data folder; tests never touch the real one.
10. **Printing** needs almost no app work (Core prints strips with the current values): the app adds one thing, the print flush, see "Raffica PDF: forms and signing measurements (M2.3)"; the plan checks the flush before printing and the Linux path.
**Rationale:** (1) Chrome and Edge fill on a click; a mode with its own hint and toolbar is an extra step, and the text selection and links of the viewer are unaffected because the press is taken only over a fillable widget. (2) to (4) the predecessor's honesty rules kept with fewer surfaces. (5) WCAG 2.1.2 and keyboard-only use. (6) A document that allows only filling must stay fillable. (7) The slots exist and a form edit never coexists with a text box edit. (8) Fewer clicks; the select tool already offers handles and arrow keys. (9) Spec rule: measurement runs and tests never touch the user's data. (10) The decision belongs to Core, which has the tests.
**Rejected alternatives:** a separate fill mode with its own toolbar (extra step, a second mode next to annotate); a placement ghost under the pointer (extra interaction code for little gain, the select tool already moves the placed signature); a bar per notice (clutter); typed-name signatures and initials (lookalike of a signature, font licences, out of the predecessor's scope); writing values from the app instead of the form engine (two writers would disagree in other viewers).
**Amendment (closing the milestone, 2026-11-05):** what was built differs from the calls above in these points.
- **Core additions** (each with a Core test): `PdfDocument.MayFillFormsAsync`, the overload `GetFormFieldsAsync(int, PdfPriority, ...)`, `AnnotationEditor.HasFormChanges` and `PngEncoder.EncodeWithAlpha`. `NativeBindingsTests` was fixed along the way: `PermissionFillForms` is a bit of the PDF specification and is mapped in `SpecificationValues`.
- **Required-field card and signed-file wording (call 3):** the card is asked for any form change (`HasFormChanges`). It stays true after the only form step was undone until the next save clears the history ("touched until save"), so the card can ask for a document whose values are back at the saved ones. The form-aware wording of the signed-file card stays tied to `HasOnlyFormChanges`.
- **Pasted text:** every field-editor `TextBox` has `AcceptsReturn = true` and no `MaxLength`, so Avalonia never shortens a pasted text; the rules of the field decide, and a pasted break is refused with the one-line hint. The one exception is the inner text box of an editable combo box, which keeps Avalonia's default: a multi-line paste into it may still be cut (an open item of milestone 2.3, to check on a real display). This replaces the plan's `MaxLength` from the field.
- **Added beyond the plan:** hover tooltips for locked and required widgets and composed automation names (`FormInteraction`, `FormAutomation`); `FormFocus.Move` never jumps an unloaded page (`PageToLoad`) and failed page reads are remembered; the badge buttons are `Focusable = false`; a failed read of the signature library shows a card; the Sign command sits in the group with digit 5 and field code 51; image signatures can be moved and scaled (`SelectController.CanChange` tests `SelectionShape`); Cancel is disabled while a signature save is in flight.
- **Internal test seams:** `PdfWindow.UseSignatureLibrary`, `SignaturePlacer.SelectStep`, `FormSession.PageReader` and `FormSession.PageCountSource`.
- **Defects of the final review, fixed in the closing wave.** (N1) A form editor that came back when the text box field freed the `Field` slot sat at zero width without focus: `TextBoxController.Close` and `FormInteraction.Close` now reset `FieldRect` before clearing `Field`, and `OnFieldSlotChanged` lays the editor out again and posts the focus itself. (N2) Undo did nothing while a refused form draft was parked hidden and an annotate tool was active: `DiscardForHistory` reveals the draft only when no tool is active, otherwise the draft ends and the undo goes on, and `RevealDraft` no longer scrolls while a tool is active (so the form commands end without moving).

## 2026-11-04 – Raffica PDF: annotation conventions (M2.2)
**Context:** Milestone 2.2 puts the annotation engine that Core already has (`AnnotationEditor` with one undo history, hit-testing, handles, the save wrapper) behind the PDF app's UI. The spec names a 256 px marks pane and a 300 px comments pane; the approved canvas shows one right pane. The calls below are the author's calls while planning (`Docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-04-milestone-02-2-annotate.md`); the owner can overturn them.
**Decision:**
1. **One right pane, the marks pane (256 px).** The empty comments pane slot of 2.1 becomes the marks pane, listing every mark with its comment; the comment of the selected mark is edited in the pane.
2. **Annotate mode as in the predecessor.** A tool in the Annotate tab starts the mode; choosing it again, "Done" or Esc ends it. Outside the mode the viewer behaves as in 2.1. Tool letters work only inside the mode and never in a text field.
3. **Dirty state:** `PdfTab.IsDirty` stays the flag the UI reads; a per-tab `AnnotationSession` sets it (editor dirty or busy, or a draft or stroke in flight).
4. **Core gaps are closed in Core** with a Core test, only where found: hit-testing of text boxes, the "may annotate" permission.
5. **Marks list:** no document-wide listing in Core; the app reads pages at background priority after the first sharp page and refreshes only the pages of `PagesInvalidated`.
6. **Comments are `/Contents`** of a markup or drawing; no sticky note, no popup, no replies; an annotation with replies is read-only.
7. **Foreign marks** are listed; locked ones (Core: `GeometryEditable == false`, or not `Editable`) offer comment where editable, and delete, with the lock reason shown.
**Rationale:** (1) the canvas is the approved design and the pane list in it contains notes, so a second pane would duplicate it. (2) The mode keeps text selection and links of the viewer unchanged for readers. (3) A second dirty source would let the tab and the editor disagree; the flag stays one place. (4) The UI must not carry engine rules. (5) Reading 10 000 annotations at open would break the speed budgets. (6) The model Core implements and tests. (7) Same rules as the predecessor, no damage to foreign marks.
**Rejected alternatives:** a second right pane for comments (no room, not in the canvas); tools always on without a mode (text selection and links would fight the tools); `PdfTab.IsDirty` computed live from the editor (the 2.1 tests and the tab binding use the flag); autosave (contradicts "never damages the original").

**Amendments while building (2026-11-05):** the calls above held except for these, found while building and recorded as scope changes in the roadmap (context "Milestone 2.2"). (a) Call 2: the tool letters I, R, E, L, A and T are not implemented; the Annotate tab's key tips and the Ctrl+Shift shortcuts exist instead, which avoids another key-capture surface. Esc is the only window-level key offer of the mode. (b) Colour and width are chosen in an app-side style strip (`Annotating/StyleStrip`) beside the mode badge, not in the ribbon, because the shell ribbon has no checked or swatch control; a text box gets colours only on the strip for a selection. (c) The text-box default font size is 16 pt (Medium, as in Core and Pagewing 1.0), not 12. (d) The ribbon has no checked state for the active tool; the mode badge names the tool and "Esc to finish". (e) Touch input is ignored in annotate mode (the page view has no touch scrolling yet). (f) `AnnotationSession` keeps unresolved edits dirty: a refused text-box draft or an unapplied marks-pane comment keeps the tab dirty and is never dropped implicitly; a flush applies or commits the valid ones. (g) An annotation save skips the Core write when the editor itself is not dirty (a tab that is dirty only for an open draft writes nothing and keeps the draft).

## 2026-11-04 – Raffica PDF: software rendering is the Windows default (M2.1 follow-up)
**Context:** The reference runs showed the PDF app about 2.2 times slower than the predecessor 1.0 on every Windows start case, and the experiment with the opt-in `RAFFICA_RENDERING=software` proved the cause: Avalonia's default path on Windows (ANGLE over Direct3D 11) costs about 190 ms at start and about 90 MiB of idle memory. Same machine (Ryzen 7 7800X3D, RTX 4070 SUPER, 1920 × 1080, 100 %), same commit, medians of `measure.yml` `target=pdf` (runs 41892985596 for the default path and 41893002871 for software, CSVs in `Docs/measurements/pdf-viewer-2026-11-04/rendering/{default,software}/`): cold 386.57 against 195.17 ms, warm 393.92 against 202.38, 1000 pages 405.08 against 190.68, second file 29.10 against 17.36, idle memory 126.65 against 37.27 MiB, scroll p99 13.53 against 18.01 ms, size 50.81 MiB in both. The predecessor 1.0 was at 175.5 / 174.7 / 183.1 ms on the same files. The owner decided on 2026-11-04 to take the faster path as the default.
**Decision:** On Windows the PDF app renders in software by default: `Program.BuildAvaloniaApp` sets `Win32PlatformOptions.RenderingMode = [Win32RenderingMode.Software]` (no Direct3D device, no ANGLE display, no composition). The environment variable `RAFFICA_RENDERING` stays and is the opt-out: `default` (trimmed, any case) selects Avalonia's own path for comparison and for a user with a problem; `software`, unset, empty or anything else gives software (`RenderingChoiceParser.Parse`; the enum values are `Default` = Avalonia's own, `Software` = the app's default). Linux and macOS are untouched; there is no setting and no UI; the code uses no reflection (AOT-safe). `measure.yml` gets the input `rendering` with `app` (default: nothing set, what a user gets), `software` and `default`; `machine.txt` and the job summary say "Rendering override: app", "software" or "default" for every `target=pdf` run.
The Windows budgets of the PDF target are re-derived from the software medians (rule of M1, as in "Raffica PDF speed budgets (M2.1)": budget = median rounded up, time to 10 ms, memory to 5 MiB, size and zip to 1 MiB; limit = budget × 1.25 applied once). Linux is not measured again; its budgets stand.

| Case | Software median | Windows budget / limit (new) | Old Windows budget (GPU path) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cold start | 195.17 ms | 200 / 250 ms | 400 ms |
| Warm start | 202.38 ms | 210 / 262.5 ms | 390 ms |
| 1000-page file | 190.68 ms | 200 / 250 ms | 400 ms |
| Second file | 17.36 ms | 20 / 25 ms | 30 ms |
| Scroll p99 | 18.01 ms | 20 / 25 ms | 20 ms |
| Idle memory | 37.27 MiB | 40 / 50 MiB | 130 MiB |
| Published size | 50.81 MiB | 51 / 63.75 MiB | 51 MiB |

*Superseded for size and zip by "Raffica PDF: published size budgets after the OCR payload (M2.5)".*
| Zip | 21.6 MiB | 22 / 27.5 MiB | 22 MiB |

The old Windows budgets (400 / 390 / 400 / 30 / 20 ms, 130 MiB) belonged to the GPU path and are replaced; the budgets are tightened, never loosened (scroll, size and zip stay the same). `s_pdfBudgets` in `bench/shell-probe/measure.cs` is the single copy; the selftest pins the values and runs the Windows check on `rendering/software` (the Linux check stays on `linux`). The CSVs of the first Windows run (`Docs/measurements/pdf-viewer-2026-11-04/windows`) were measured on Avalonia's default path and are kept as history; they no longer pass the new limits, by design.
**Consequences:** The scroll p99 rises from 13.5 to 18.0 ms and is now close to its budget of 20 ms (the limit is 25 ms; the five-run median varies by a few ms). Frames are drawn in software with no GPU compositing, so a very large window or a 4K display may scroll worse than the 1080p measurement shows. Not verified and recorded as manual checks (milestone 2.1, "Manual checks on real machines"): the scrolling feel on a real display, the sharpness at fractional scaling and high DPI, tile seams and the dark page, German and other locales unaffected. The `measure.yml` run with the new limits on the owner's Windows machine is open (`gh workflow run measure.yml --ref dev -f platform=windows -f target=pdf`; the check step must pass). A user with a problem sets `RAFFICA_RENDERING=default`.
**Rationale:** Speed of start and a third of the idle memory are the core promise of the suite; the one cost is a few milliseconds of scroll p99 that stay inside the budget. The predecessor reached the same conclusion on its own measurements.
**Rejected alternatives:** Keeping Avalonia's default path (ANGLE) as the default (twice the start time, three times the idle memory, a 2.2 times gap to the predecessor); choosing software only when no hardware GPU is detected, which was the predecessor's first attempt (it first chose software only without a hardware GPU and made it the default for every machine after measuring a PC with a GPU: its entry "Software-Rendering als Windows-Standard (M3)"; the measurement here shows the GPU path slower at start also on a machine with a strong GPU); a setting or UI switch (the environment opt-out is enough until a user needs more).

## 2026-11-04 – Raffica PDF speed budgets (M2.1)
**Context:** "Raffica PDF measurement method (M2.1)" fixes what is measured; this entry records the first real numbers and the budgets from them. `measure.yml` ran with `target=pdf` on the owner's two binding machines on `dev` at commit db90246 (run 2026-11-04), 10 runs per case (the scroll case 5 runs, one p99 and one maximum each), no invalid-measurement flag. The CSVs and machine notes are in `Docs/measurements/pdf-viewer-2026-11-04/`. The machines are those of the M1 entries ("Speed targets of the frame (M1)", "Gallery frame baseline (M1.2)"):
- Windows (binding): AMD Ryzen 7 7800X3D, 30.9 GiB, NVIDIA RTX 4070 SUPER, 1920 × 1080 at 60 Hz, scaling 100 %, Windows 11 build 26300, balanced power plan, mains power.
- Linux (binding): HP ProBook, Intel Core i5-8265U, 15.4 GiB, Intel UHD Graphics 620, 1920 × 1080, Wayland session with XWayland, CachyOS (kernel 7.3.0-4-cachyos), balanced profile, mains power.
- macOS: not measured here (indicative only, later; the macOS subproject and the Apple-silicon numbers come after).

**Measured (median, minimum to maximum):**

| Case | Windows | Linux |
|---|---|---|
| Cold start, one page (ms) | 392.53 (387.52 to 555.01) | 227.51 (210.59 to 831.06) |
| Warm start, one page (ms) | 382.84 (374.56 to 396.20) | 227.83 (216.96 to 252.89) |
| 1000-page file to the first sharp page (ms) | 394.86 (371.14 to 404.84) | 217.63 (210.79 to 268.94) |
| Second file in the running instance (ms) | 25.46 (23.93 to 32.91) | 33.82 (32.16 to 37.41) |
| Scroll, p99 of the frame gaps (ms), median of 5 runs | 10.70 (10.04 to 11.62) | 36.25 (36.21 to 37.77) |
| Idle memory, 1000-page file (MiB) | 126.68 (126.01 to 129.11) | 151.37 (149.62 to 154.25) |
| Published size (MiB) | 50.80 | 47.61 |
| Zip (MiB) | 21.60 | 21.19 |

**Decision:** The rule is that of M1: the budget is the median rounded up (time to 10 ms, memory to 5 MiB, size and zip to 1 MiB), the limit is the budget × 1.25 applied once (`Stats.Limit`). The scroll metric is the median of the p99 values; the zip has a budget as in the gallery table because `size --check` judges size and zip together (a metric without a budget would be `unrecorded` and fail). The single copy in code is `s_pdfBudgets` in `CheckCommand` of `bench/shell-probe/measure.cs`; its selftest pins every value and runs `check` on the committed CSVs.

| Case | Windows budget / limit | Linux budget / limit |
|---|---|---|
| Cold start | 400 / 500 ms | 230 / 287.5 ms |
| Warm start | 390 / 487.5 ms | 230 / 287.5 ms |
| 1000-page file | 400 / 500 ms | 220 / 275 ms |
| Second file | 30 / 37.5 ms | 40 / 50 ms |
| Scroll p99 | 20 / 25 ms | 40 / 50 ms |
| Idle memory | 130 / 162.5 MiB | 155 / 193.75 MiB |
| Published size | 51 / 63.75 MiB | 48 / 60 MiB |
| Zip | 22 / 27.5 MiB | 22 / 27.5 MiB |

*Superseded for size and zip by "Raffica PDF: published size budgets after the OCR payload (M2.5)".*

macOS has no budgets: `check` prints the verdict `indicative` and exits 0. Re-measure with `gh workflow run measure.yml --ref <branch> -f platform=<windows|linux> -f target=pdf -f note="<GPU, resolution, scaling, power>"` (the check step must pass), or locally `dotnet run bench/shell-probe/measure.cs -- check --target pdf --platform <windows|linux> --in "<CSVs>"`; both exit 0 on the recorded CSVs. CI checks the published size with `size --check <rid> --target pdf --publish-dir out/pdf --fonts-dir src/Raffica.Shell/Assets/Fonts` (step "Size of the published PDF app"); a local win-x64 Native AOT publish of the same commit was 512 bytes smaller than the owner's run (53271080 against 53271592 bytes). Budgets are never loosened to make a check pass; a change is a new decisions entry.

**Findings for the owner.** A finding is not a miss of a limit (the limits come from these medians); it is what the spec's reference rule calls "slower than Pagewing 1.0 on the same machines".
- **(a) Reference runs of the predecessor 1.0 (2026-11-04, same two machines, same day).** `measure.yml` with `target=reference` built the predecessor's `v1.0.0` tag (commit 0373bf6, pinned by SHA) in the job and ran its own startup protocol (`bench/startup/startup.cs`: first sharp page painted plus one frame, from just before the process starts, 10 runs, the same measurement point as the PDF app). Runs 41888200195 (Windows) and 41888206538 (Linux); CSVs, summaries and machine notes in `Docs/measurements/pdf-viewer-2026-11-04/reference/`. Medians in ms (the predecessor with the files of the PDF target, `hello_world.pdf`, for cold and warm, `hello_world.pdf` then `outline.pdf` for the second file; its own protocol with `text-basic.pdf` in brackets; the 1000-page file is the same file in both):
